Things, she understood, were certainly good if you thought they were, and if you thought they were poor they could be entertaining, sometimes. She was glad Jimmy was different. He was not an intellectual. You could hold on to him﻿—more like a coarse man. She had mocked his beard, but after all it was the only one in the room. Just under the reddish cheekbones it was golden, but it was grizzled already by the sides of the mouth, and under the lower lip. She had not noticed this before. When he turned his head to young Walcott﻿—they seemed very friendly this evening﻿—a muscle stretched like a strong cable from his ear to his throat. He looked solid, and as if he would last. There he was. The evening could be a success after all.

But when Colet chanced to see her face Helen had turned it, in the idleness of contentment, to the Russians. She was an admirer of that critic, he thought. Used to recommend his stuff to him. She was part of this place. He was an outsider. Better be off. Most of these people were a little queer, like the pictures painted on the walls. Over their table was a puzzle of heterogeneous yellow and crimson geometry, in which he could make out a one-eyed woman who would have been nude but for the chance intervention of a greenish rhomb. There were no vitals to the room. It was heartless. Night was outside, and you could wander there alone, and would not have to listen to anything clever. He rose, and squeezed the shoulder of the biologist. “I’ll be off. I’ll leave you to it.”

Outside, the look of the stars above the parapets of the houses opposite, and even the smell, on a still night, of London’s pavements that had been heated all day by the sun, were better. Nothing ingenious about that, even if it had no meaning. No false contact. He stood by the kerb, free again, deciding which way he should turn.

===Chinese===
一个姑娘懒洋洋地在桌间穿行起舞，仿佛这是派定的差事，她正在把它应付过去。她避开用餐者的目光；她走近时，也只有寥寥几个男人抬眼看她，年长的女人们则要等她走过自家的桌子，才挑剔地朝她的背影瞟一眼。科利特望着她过去，愈发觉得屈辱。海伦看见了他的疏离，也看见那舞女游鱼般滑过时他眼里的嫌恶。那位批评家没能逗乐她。她明白：事情嘛，你若认为好，那自然是好的；你若认为糟，它们有时候也能逗人一乐。她很高兴吉米不一样。他不是个知识分子。你抓得住他——更像个粗豪的男人。她取笑过他的胡子，可说到底，那是满屋子仅有的一把。微红的颧骨正下方，它是金色的，可在嘴角两侧和下唇底下，已经灰白了。这个她先前不曾留意。他转头朝年轻的沃尔科特那边时——今晚他们俩似乎很友好——一条肌肉像一根结实的缆索，从他耳根绷到咽喉。他看上去敦实，像是经得起年月的人。他到底来了。这个晚上终究还成得了事。

可当科利特碰巧再看海伦的脸时，她已经怀着餍足的慵懒，把它转向那桌俄国人了。她是那位批评家的仰慕者，他想。从前还常把他的文章推荐给他。她是这地方的一部分。他是个外人。还是走的好。这些人多半都有点怪，就像墙上挂的那些画。他们头顶上，是一幅杂色的黄与红拼成的几何谜题，他从中勉强辨认出一个独眼女人，若不是一块发绿的菱形碰巧一挡，她就是全裸的了。这间屋子没有脏腑。它是没有心的。外面是黑夜，你可以独自在那儿游荡，不必听任何聪明话。他站起身，捏了捏生物学家的肩膀。「我走了。你们尽兴。」

外面，星星悬在对面房屋女儿墙上方的那副样子，甚至无风的夜里伦敦人行道的气味——被太阳晒了一整天的气味——都要更好些。这里面没有半点机巧，纵使它没有意义。没有虚假的接触。他站在路缘，重获自由，盘算着该往哪边走。

===Original===
“I’m coming too, Jimmy, I could see you were bored. So was I. Come along.” Helen laid her hand on his arm.

“You were?” He hesitated.

“Of course. Did you think you were going to escape like this?” She laughed quietly, in confidence. She could rely on Jimmy.

He, though, was suspicious that the friendly night was being taken from him as soon as he had found it. He was reluctant to share the street with anyone. It surprised him that she had left her friends. Why was that? He could trust himself, when alone. There was safety in the night, but he knew he could not be sure of himself if she were close to him. Then he was largely in abeyance. It was as if most other human creatures were inimical. They were so remarkably not the same that they were uncanny. He felt strongly drawn to that clever, supple woman beside him, and resented her for that reason. There was no privacy with a woman. The soul got mauled about.

===Chinese===
「我也走，吉米。看得出你闷了。我也闷。来吧。」海伦把手搭上他的手臂。

「你闷了？」他迟疑道。

「当然。你以为你能就这样溜掉？」她轻轻地笑出声，带着推心置腹的神气。吉米是靠得住的。

然而他起了疑心，疑心这个友善的黑夜刚一到手，就要被人夺走。他不愿同任何人分享这条街。她竟离开了她的朋友们，这叫他意外。这是为什么？独处时他信得过自己。黑夜里有安全，可他知道，她一旦挨近，他就拿不准自己了。那时，他的大半个自己便悬置起来。仿佛其余的人类大多是怀敌意的。他们同他悬殊得那样显著，显著得近于怪异。他强烈地被身边这个聪明柔韧的女人吸引，也正为这一点怨恨她。同女人在一起，没有独处可言。灵魂会被人撕来扯去。

===Original===
Besides, she had not left that dinner-table because its talk was glib and sparkling. She liked that. She’d brought that atmosphere with her. She admired those people in there. They were all clever, and he felt a slow fool. But if they were clever, perhaps that only meant they could justify their hollow insides. They could make their dry and dusty cavities seem more like nature than having guts. Lord, they could make a heart feel ashamed, compared with an interior that had a thick settlement of knowledge on its hard ledges. If that was Bloomsbury, give him Billiter Avenue. You knew there where you were.

“It’s better out here, Jimmy.”

He found it hard to believe she meant that. She meant it at the moment; that was all. But what an autocrat she was in that cloak. He wanted to believe her. If he could do that he would surrender. Here was luck, for a woman like this to show she wanted him. Helen was as clever as they were made. Then why did she want him? Even the pictures she painted were malicious, as if her insight were diabolical. Sometimes her designs and figures were as though she was contemptuous of the world, and wanted to expose it. He would sooner look at the traffic now, and have no reason to talk. He would not accept her; she did not belong to him. It didn’t do to look at that full throat of hers, and then at her eyes. Common sense went then. Was it time it did?

===Chinese===
再说，她并不是因为餐桌上的谈话油滑光鲜才离席的。她喜欢那一套。她是把那气氛随身带来了。她钦佩里头那些人。他们个个聪明，他自觉是个迟钝的傻瓜。可他们若是聪明，那也许只说明，他们有本事给自己的空膛内里辩护。他们能让自己那些干燥多尘的腔洞，看起来比有血有肠更近于自然。天哪，同一副内里相比——那内里的硬质岩架上积着厚厚的知识沉垢——他们能叫一颗心羞愧。倘若那就是布卢姆斯伯里，那他宁要比利特大道（Billiter Avenue）。在那儿，你至少知道自己身在何处。

「外头这儿好些，吉米。」

他很难相信她说的是真心话。此刻她是真心的；仅此而已。可她裹在那件斗篷里，多么专擅。他愿意相信她。若能做到，他就投降了。这可是造化，这样一个女人竟露出意思，说她要他。海伦的聪明，是女人里顶了尖的那一种。那她为什么要他？连她画的画都存着恶意，仿佛她的洞见里有魔鬼。有时她的构图和人物，透着对世人的轻蔑，仿佛存心要把世界揭穿。眼下他宁可去看车流，省得找话说。他不肯接受她；她不属于他。她那丰润的喉颈是看不得的，看了喉颈再看她的眼睛，更看不得。一那样，常识就溜走了。也该到常识溜走的时候了么？

===Original===
As they walked, and she stepped in unison with him no matter how in irritation he broke his stride, for she was nearly as tall as himself, he felt her intended touch now and then, and was stirred. She pointed to something comic in the upturned faces of a crowd that was watching an electric sky-sign, a baby’s feeding-bottle that constantly emptied and refilled to the joy repeated as intermittent jerky lights in the face of a gigantic cherub, and Jimmy stopped and laughed aloud. The crowd might have been watching the heavens unroll as a scroll.

They got into a taxicab. Helen could see his profile, salient and thoughtful, in an occasional light, and his nearness was evident to her. He suggested faintly﻿—what was it?﻿—tonka beans. That was Perriam’s warehouse. Or his tobacco. She remembered it. She broke into gaiety over what they had heard at dinner. He heard, in surprise, his own dubiety expressed in positive wit. Was that what she was thinking while listening to the critic with such apparent respect? Poor man of letters! Perhaps women were like chameleons, and could swiftly assume the colour which circumstance required. But he liked it. It was pleasant to feel a woman so close who could be as comically shrewd as that over people who had mocked his verities.

Helen knew he was coming over to her. “How’s the ogre? How’s old Perriam?” she asked. “You haven’t said a thing yet. Talk to me.”

===Chinese===
两人一路走着，任他烦躁地怎样踏乱步子，她总能同他合上步调，因为她的个子同他几乎一般高。他不时觉出她有意的触碰，心神一荡。一群人仰着脸看一幅电气楼顶广告，她指给他看那些仰起的面孔里可笑的地方——广告上，一只婴儿奶瓶不住地倒空、再斟满，欢喜便像断续闪跳的灯火，一遍遍点亮一张巨型小天使的脸——吉米站住脚，放声大笑。那群人看的，简直像是天幕正像书卷一样铺展开来。

他们上了一辆出租汽车。偶尔一盏灯亮过，海伦看得见他的侧影，棱角分明，若有所思；他的切近，她感得分明。他身上淡淡地叫人想起——是什么呢？——零陵香豆（tonka beans）。那是佩里亚姆（Perriam）货栈的气味。要么就是他的烟丝。她想起来了。她为晚餐上听到的一切迸出一串欢笑。他惊讶地听见，自己心里的疑虑，竟被她说成了笃定的妙语。她带着那副显然的敬意听批评家讲话时，心里转的就是这个么？可怜的文人！女人也许就像变色龙，境遇要什么颜色，她们就能飞快换上什么颜色。可他喜欢。挨得这样近的一个女人，对那些嘲弄过他所信真理的人，能这样可笑地精明，这感觉是愉快的。

海伦知道，他这就要倒向她这边来了。「那妖魔怎么样？老佩里亚姆怎么样？」她问。「你还一句话没说呢。跟我说说。」

===Original===
He outlined the latest manifestation in the city. He put his hand on hers. “So, you see, if I’m to go on, they’re to get out.”

She took possession of his fist. “Don’t let those people trouble you. That’s what you always do.”

He did not answer.

“You are ridiculous. You want to treat a crude earth as if it were porcelain. You waste feeling on what will never know it. No doubt about it, men are the sentimentalists. Haven’t you learned yet that the art of commerce is the art of doing without more feeling than you need for luck?”

===Chinese===
他把城里的近事约略讲了一遍。他把手放到她的手上。「所以你瞧，我要想干下去，他们就得滚蛋。」

她把他那只拳头收进掌中。「别让那些人搅扰你。你回回都是这样。」

他没有作声。

「你可笑。你想拿一片生蛮的大地当瓷器待。你把感情浪费在永远不会有知觉的东西上。这错不了，男人才是感伤主义者。你还没学会么——商业这门艺术，就是除了碰运气不得不有的那点感情之外，一分不多备的感情的艺术？」

===Original===
His fist was clenched on her knee. She opened his hand, and laid it limply flat.

“If it were daylight, I’d read your fortune. You’re too easy with those men. No daylight wanted to read that. If they hurt you, get others who won’t.”

The cab bumped. His hat fell to the floor. He withdrew his hand to pick it up, and then folded his arms.

“Those men knew well enough, of course, that either they would win, or else you would. They asked for it. Why let them win?”

He could not answer that. Such an argument came from a different order of assumptions. That was the way Perriam looked at it.

===Chinese===
他的拳头攥在她的膝上。她把那只手掰开，让它软软地摊平。

「若是白天，我就给你看手相。你对那些人太宽了。不过这个，不用天光也看得出来。他们伤了你，就换一批不会伤你的。」

车子颠了一下。他的帽子滚落在车板上。他抽回手捡起来，随后抱起了双臂。

「那些人心里当然明白，不是他们赢，就是你赢。这是他们自讨的。为什么放他们赢？」

他答不上来。这样的论辩，出自另一套假设。佩里亚姆看事情就是这个路数。

===Original===
They went up to her rooms. There she was, cool, clever, and luxurious, with her books and pictures about her, the best that London could offer a man. And here he was, like a grey long-eared one, out of sympathy. She welcomed him with a restrained little gesture, and for a second met his eyes in candour and intimacy. They might have been alone in the city. He was sure her eyes could look the Lohan serenely in the face, though that disciple of Buddha were in the flesh. That would give the Lohan something to do.

He did not sit down. He stood with an elbow on the mantelpiece, and examined a Tanagra figurine. It was not unlike Helen in miniature.

“There you are, Jim. Where’s your pipe?” She lifted an arm, which would have delighted him in Grecian marble, and pressed his shoulder. He noticed the turquoise on her white hand. He sank into the chair. She sat on the arm of it, and he did not hear what she was saying, for her voice was as far as something just remembered. The bold curves of the thigh beside him, instead of satisfying him, as would that of a statue, so disturbed him that its proximity gave him anxiety. It was dangerous; and she had said “get rid of them.” He could not forget that. He was not going to blaspheme life. There was no fellowship here. He stood up and met her glance. She was patiently watching him in enchanting perplexity.

“Why, aren’t you going to stay?” She looked down, and paused. “You’ve only just come,” she said very quietly. He did not answer, and she said more. He vaguely wondered whether he rightly understood her. The courage of this woman! He dared not look at her. His own sensations were baffling, but somehow he remained rigidly outside himself, so that his body could not act, as though he were afraid, not of her, but of coming too close to himself. There was something more important. She took a step back, and her arm, which had been raised towards him, fell to her side, as though she had forgotten it was raised. He had no sense.

===Chinese===
他们上楼进了她的寓所。她就在那儿，冷静、聪明、奢华，身前身后围着她的书和画，是伦敦所能给一个男人的一切里最好的。而他站在这儿，像一头灰色的长耳朵牲口，两不相入。她用一个克制的小手势迎他，有那么一秒钟，坦然而亲密地接住他的目光。他们尽可算这座城里仅有的两个人。他确信，她的眼睛能同那尊罗汉（Lohan）安详地对视，哪怕佛陀（Buddha）的这位弟子血肉俱全地立在面前。那倒够那罗汉忙一阵子的。

他没有坐下。他撑着一只手肘立在壁炉架前，端详一尊塔纳格拉（Tanagra）小陶俑。它活脱是缩小了几号的海伦。

「来了，吉姆。你的烟斗呢？」她抬起一条手臂——这条手臂若装在希腊大理石像上，准会叫他心醉——按了按他的肩。他注意到她白皙的手上的绿松石。他沉进椅子里。她坐在椅子扶手上，她说了什么，他没有听进，因为她的声音远得像一件刚刚记起的事。身边那条大腿丰满的曲线，非但不像雕像的曲线那样叫他餍足，反而搅得他方寸大乱，那切近使他焦虑。这是危险的；而她说过，『把他们打发掉』。这句话他忘不掉。他不愿亵渎生活。这里没有情谊。他站起来，迎住她的目光。她正怀着迷人的惶惑，耐心地注视他。

「怎么，你不留下了？」她垂下眼，顿了顿。「你才刚来。」她极轻地说。他没有回答，她又说了几句。他模糊地疑心，自己是否真听懂了她。这个女人的胆量！他不敢看她。他自己的感觉纷乱难解，可不知怎的，他始终僵僵地置身己外，叫他的身体动弹不得，仿佛他怕的不是她，而是离自己太近。还有更要紧的事。她退后一步，那条朝他抬起的手臂垂落到身侧，仿佛忘了它曾经抬起。他毫无知觉。

## V / 五

===Original===
At Brixton on Monday evening, Mr. Perriam was trying to leave his house. It was his address, or his house; he never called it his home. He had but just come from Manchester, and the fact that the train had been late gave him the impression that he was an overtasked man to whom even time was an enemy. But he could do it all. He was a strong man. He could continue till he had steeled the indecision into which his affairs had softened in his absence. But it was imperative that he should go to Billiter Avenue at once. He was incensed by the impediments placed by the stupid in the straight course of a just man single-minded in his devotion to good order and common sense. His menservants, with an air of solicitude, and in swift obedience to his peremptory exactions, were silently cursing him, and doing things awry. Mr. Perriam had been in a hurry when he arrived, he was in desperation to leave, and was moving about the hall with an abrupt and heavy celerity which could have been mistaken for craziness, or at best black temper, except that he was so evidently controlling with dignity his righteous impatience over the follies of inferior creatures.

His wife was not there. She had withdrawn unnoticed to a secluded upper room at the first wave of disturbance sent before Mr. Perriam’s car as it entered the outer gates of his residence, as it passed in fact between the two giant pineapples in stone which guarded their Brixton privet hedge. Mrs. Perriam was represented in the hall by the silently protesting surrogation of some Chinese silk tapestry, and a few comforting rugs and prints. They did not accord with the magnificent Indian furniture of Mr. Perriam’s importation, but they did give something on which the eye could rest. But Mr. Perriam’s eye did not rest upon them. He was unaware that his wife was in any way represented. The reproach he felt because she was not there to assuage for an anxious man the torment of the foolishness about him gave his countenance a shadow of proud resignation. His thoughts concentrated on his grave decision, that he must ignore his dinner, and go instantly to his office to examine his letters. He knew his fear was both natural and scrupulous, that ignorance and folly, while he was away, had deflected the orderly directions of his authority.

Jimmy was wondering when his chief would come. The offices of Perriams were deserted. It was past six o’clock. The only light was in his room. Jimmy had to wait. The church clock in St. Mary Axe chimed a quarter-past, half past; and its echo in the empty office where the shadows were deepening was like the memory of things gone in a place where the stir of men would be seen no more. Colet’s surviving light might have been a meaningless obstinacy in the face of advancing night. The desks in the big room were cleared of their books, and the bare mahogany surfaces gleamed in cold patches in the dusk. One of the cats of the building strolled across the linoleum. Jimmy stood up and nervously stretched himself. He saw that cat. Ah! another creature was alive there. He called to it. But the cat only twitched her tail and went on. She was nothing to him; she was only a familiar, native to the desert.

===Chinese===
星期一傍晚，在布里克斯顿（Brixton），佩里亚姆先生正竭力要离开他的宅邸。那是他的通讯地址，或者说他的房子；他从不称它为家。他刚从曼彻斯特（Manchester）回来，火车误了点，这个事实给他的感觉是：他是个操劳过度的人，连时间都在与他为敌。但他应付得了一切。他是个坚强的人。他可以继续干下去，直到把自己不在期间松软成犹豫不决的事务重新锻硬。可有一件事势在必行：他必须立刻赶往比利特大道（Billiter Avenue）。愚人们在一个一心笃信良好秩序与常识的正直之人的笔直道路上横设障碍，令他怒不可遏。他的男仆们一脸殷切的关切，对他的专横催逼唯命是从、手脚麻利，心里却在暗暗咒骂他，把事事做得颠三倒四。佩里亚姆先生到家时已是匆匆，此刻更是心急如焚地要走，在门厅里来回疾走，那突兀而沉重的迅疾，本可以被误认作疯癫，往好里说也是一股黑火似的脾气，亏得他分明在庄严地克制着一份义愤——对下等生灵之愚蠢的、理直气壮的不耐。

他的妻子不在跟前。佩里亚姆先生的座车驶进宅邸的外门——事实上，正从守卫着布里克斯顿女贞树篱的那两颗巨大石雕菠萝之间穿过——它先行送出的第一波动荡刚一传来，她便乘人不备，退到了楼上一间幽僻的屋子里。大厅里代表佩里亚姆夫人的，是几幅中国丝织挂毯无声抗议着的代役，外加几条予人慰藉的地毯和几幅版画。它们与佩里亚姆先生进口来的堂皇印度家具并不协调，却确实给了眼睛一点可以栖止的东西。但佩里亚姆先生的眼睛并没有栖止在它们上面。他没有意识到妻子以任何方式在场。他因她不在——不能为一个忧心忡忡的人排解四周蠢行的煎熬——而感到的那份责怪，给他脸上蒙了一层高傲的隐忍。他的心思凝在一个庄重的决定上：他必须置晚饭于不顾，即刻去写字间查验信件。他知道自己的忧惧既出于天性、又出于审慎：他不在的时候，无知与愚蠢已经把他权威的井然指令引偏了。

吉米（Jimmy）正在纳闷，上司几时才会来。佩里亚姆商行的写字间空无一人。已经过了六点。唯一的灯光在他那间屋里。吉米只好等。圣玛丽斧街（St. Mary Axe）教堂的钟敲了一刻，又敲了半点；那钟声回荡在暗影渐浓的空写字间里，就像在一个再也见不着人烟搅动的地方，对既往事物的记忆。科利特（Colet）那盏残灯，面对节节进逼的黑夜，简直像一种毫无意义的固执。大间里办公桌上的账册都已撤去，裸露的桃花心木桌面在暮色里泛出一块块冷光。楼里养的一只猫踱过油地毡。吉米站起身，神经质地伸了个懒腰。他看见了那只猫。啊！那里还有一个活物。他唤它。可那猫只抽了抽尾巴，径自走它的路。它与他毫不相干；它不过是一头精怪，这片荒漠的土著。

===Original===
But why should he wait? There was really nothing to wait for. He did not want to see Perriam. And perhaps the boss was not coming after all. It was impossible to do any work. If Perriam came there was no report he could make which could be called good. He could give nothing to the place; and it had nothing for him except a cat which considered he was a stranger to the time and the occasion. By going now he could save London from one little eddying turmoil, make one quarrel the less in its vast meaningless jangle. That was worth thinking over. It was impossible to know by how much the air was kept sweet through saving it from but one quarrel.

Jimmy, in abstraction, was placing Kuan-yin so that he could consider her from various angles. Then the telephone bell menaced. (Yes, yes.﻿ ﻿… Certainly﻿ ﻿… He was waiting.)

The office no longer seemed so abandoned now he had heard Perriam’s voice. But he was not thinking of his chief. He was considering the Chinese image. Kuan-yin was meek and passive, however she was viewed. She accepted just what happened to her. At whatever angle she was seen her grace was distinguished only by its gentleness and composure. She was not like the cat, which flicked an insulting tail. Kuan-yin was possibly a mistake. This passive acceptance might be all right in the East, or in Jerusalem, but it was a poor substitute for assertion among western steam-engines. He had been passive all his life. He had never felt himself other than an outsider, watching the show. Somehow, the show never seemed to have much to do with him. He had taken the place in it to which chance had led him. He was sitting in that chair because his father pushed him that way. One place was as good as another. If he had followed his instinct ten minutes ago he would not have been there when Perriam was at the telephone. Which was right, the cat or Kuan-yin? There he was now, waiting for something unpleasant to happen, through a sense of duty no more admirable than the reason the cat had for crossing the floor.

===Chinese===
可他为什么要等呢？实在没有什么可等的。他并不想见佩里亚姆。再说，老板说不定压根就不来了。做什么工作都是不可能的。佩里亚姆若是来了，他也拿不出一份称得上好的报告。他给不了这个地方任何东西；这地方也拿不出任何东西给他，除了一只猫——那猫认定，对于这个时辰和这场合，他是个生人。此刻一走了之，他可以使伦敦少一小股打旋的骚乱，使它那浩大而无谓的嘈杂刺响里少一场口角。这值得想一想。单是省下一场口角，空气能因此多保住几分清甜，这是无从知道的。

吉米心不在焉地挪动着观音（Kuan-yin），好从各个角度端详她。这时电话铃威胁似的响了。（是的，是的。……当然……他在等着。）

听过佩里亚姆的声音之后，写字间似乎不再那么像遭人遗弃的了。但他心里想的并不是上司。他在琢磨那尊中国人的造像。观音无论从哪个角度看，都是温顺而被动的。落到她头上的一切，她都照单全收。无论从什么角度去看，她的风姿惟以温婉与沉静见胜。她不像那只猫，轻蔑地甩着尾巴。观音说不定是个错误。这种被动的接受，在东方，或在耶路撒冷（Jerusalem），也许尽可以成立；可在西方的蒸汽机中间，它是主张自我的一个蹩脚替身。他一辈子都是被动的。他从来不曾觉得自己是别的什么，始终是个局外人，看着那台戏。不知怎么，那台戏似乎从来与他没有多大干系。他在戏里占的这个位置，是机缘把他引去的。他坐在这把椅子上，是因为他父亲把他往那条路上推。哪个位子都一样。十分钟前他若是依了本能，佩里亚姆打电话来的时候他就不在那里了。哪一个对呢，猫，还是观音？瞧他现在，正等着一件不愉快的事情发生，凭的是一种责任感——并不比那猫穿过地板的理由更可敬佩。

===Original===
And there was Helen. To her he had, without knowing why he did it, casually declined life. He had got out of its way. Actually, in its most adorable form, he had refused it. That would be hard to explain, when the sense of his distance from what was warm and living, from what was shaping the world, was like a drouth. The outer office was the picture of what he had done; cold and empty. But it is not always easy to tell whether one is accepting or declining, whether one is going with the tendency of life, or against it.

Perriam was late. He would be glad to get this over. Then he would be free from two perplexities. He would escape into another existence which, whatever it might prove to be, would be free from the worst consequences of the past. He would be born again. That Chinese image of acceptance had her back turned to him. Should he turn his back on her? Perhaps not. These little things might mean a lot. She might represent something better than he knew. Perhaps the damned steam-engine was on the wrong line, after all.

Eh? There at last was Perriam. He was coming up the stairs in heavy deliberation, like destiny. No escape now; might kill the beggar, though. Jimmy smiled at the thought. But to fling a bomb into Moloch’s fiery belly and do in the brute god would be decrepit backsliding. Not much spiritual acceptance, in that act, of the ultimate unimportance of material bellies, fiery or otherwise. Let the fiery belly burn itself out.

===Chinese===
还有海伦（Helen）。对她，他曾经不知何故，漫不经心地回绝了生命。他闪身让开了它的路。实际上，生命以它最可爱的形态现身时，他拒绝了它。这很难解释清楚，当他感到自己远离一切温热而有生气的、远离一切正在塑造世界的东西时，那感觉就像一场干旱。外面的大间正是他所做之事的写照：又冷又空。可一个人究竟是在接受还是在拒绝，究竟是在顺着生命的趋势走、还是逆着它，这并不总是容易分清的。

佩里亚姆来迟了。把这桩事了结掉，他会高兴。那样他就从两桩烦难里脱了身。他将遁入另一种生存，不管它到头来是什么，总可免于往昔的最坏后果。他将获得再生。那尊象征接受的中国造像背朝着他。他该把背转向她吗？也许不必。这些小事也许关系重大。她也许代表某种比他所知更好的东西。说不定那台该死的蒸汽机，到头来才是开错了线路。

咦？佩里亚姆总算来了。他正迈着沉重而从容的步子拾级而上，像命运一样。这下逃不掉了；不过，杀了那家伙也未尝不可。吉米想到这里笑了。可是，往摩洛（Moloch）火烫的肚腹里扔一颗炸弹、结果掉那头畜生神，那不过是老朽的背道反驰。那一举动里，没有多少灵性的接受——承认物质的肚腹、火烫的也罢、别的也罢，终究无关宏旨。就让那火烫的肚腹自己烧尽吧。

===Original===
Mr. Perriam was filled, in fact, with resolute calm. He was not burning. He was content, for now he knew that control of his affairs was in his hands again. He and they were safe. He walked slowly to his door. Jimmy heard it close. The reflections of another light confused the darkness of the outer office.

Jimmy considered it. Should he go in? No. Better to wait until he was called. He heard his principal moving about. Then there was silence, a long silence. Then his bell rang. Jimmy was glad to hear it.

Mr. Perriam was sitting at his table, magisterial, but at his ease. His hands were spread on the arms of his chair. He did not look at his assistant. He was as if inspecting the central air, his eyes half-closed, in the sad knowledge that there could be no right answers to his searching inquisition; as if slovenly men could never satisfy demands that were so austere and irrefragable. He was anticipating, in weariness, a coming dissatisfaction.

===Chinese===
其实，佩里亚姆先生满怀的是坚定的平静。他并没有燃烧。他很满足，因为此刻他知道，他的事务又握回在自己手里了。他和它们都安全了。他缓缓走向自己那扇门。吉米听见门关上了。另一盏灯的反光，把外面大间的黑暗搅乱了。

吉米掂量了一下。要不要进去？不。还是等传唤的好。他听见老板在里面走动。随后是一片寂静，长长的寂静。随后他的铃响了。吉米很高兴听见它响。

佩里亚姆先生坐在他的桌子后面，威仪凛然，却又舒展自如。他两手摊开在椅子扶手上。他不看他的助手。他仿佛在检视屋子中央的空气，双眼半阖，带着一种悲哀的认知：对他那刨根究底的盘问，不可能有正确的答案；仿佛懒散的人永远满足不了这般严苛、这般不容置辩的要求。他正疲惫地预支着一桩即将到来的不满。

===Original===
He asked some questions about the drift of the office; and, as no fault could be found with the answers, he made no comment. He merely took his eyes from their inspection of the invisible to look at his signet ring. He rubbed his nose. He leaned forward, with his arms on the table, and he himself began to surmise that he had wasted his time. He might have left all this till the morning. Jimmy began to feel more at his ease. The boss seemed almost human, after all. He had been exaggerating this problem.

“See, now. I’d forgotten. There’s another little thing. When do the men go at our warehouse﻿—the fellows who don’t want to stay? This week or next?”

Jimmy did not reflect. “Haven’t heard,” he said brightly. Let chance answer for him.

===Chinese===
他问了几个关于写字间动向的问题；由于答案里挑不出岔子，他未置一词。他只是把目光从对无形之物的检视上收回来，看了看自己的图章戒指。他揉了揉鼻子。他俯身向前，两臂撑在桌上，自己也开始疑心，这一趟是白费工夫了。这一切他本可以留到明天早晨。吉米开始觉得自在些了。这位老板看上去终究几乎还算个人。他先前把这个问题夸大了。

「你瞧，我这会儿倒忘了。还有一件小事。我们仓库里那些人什么时候走——就是那些不想留下的家伙？这星期还是下星期？」

吉米没有细想。「没听说，」他爽快地说。且让机缘替他作答。

===Original===
Perriam was drumming on the table with his fingers, but he stopped. It seemed a long time before he spoke again.

“When will you know?”

“Well, they haven’t told me, and I haven’t asked.”

===Chinese===
佩里亚姆用手指在桌上敲着鼓点，随后停住了。似乎过了很久，他才又开口。

「你什么时候能知道？」

「这个，他们没有告诉我，我也没问。」

===Original===
The principal pushed his chair back noisily, paused, and then rose in pointed slowness. He began to pace the room, his head bowed in thought. As he walked, he snapped his fingers once or twice, and his resentment began to glow anew at the frivolity of this frustration of reason. He considered, with his back to Jimmy, a picture of a ship on the wall. Jimmy knew it, the old *Chrysolite*. Important once; now that rare lithograph. Without turning, Mr. Perriam asked, “What is your reason for saying that?”

“No reason for it. I merely report the fact.”

“What are you going to do about it?”

“Nothing.”

===Chinese===
老板哗地把椅子向后一推，顿了顿，然后刻意缓慢地站起身。他开始在屋里踱步，垂头沉思。走着走着，他捻响了一两次响指，面对理性遭此轻慢的挫败，怨愤重新炽了起来。他背朝吉米，端详墙上的一幅船的画。吉米认得它，老『克里索利特号』（*Chrysolite*）。当年何等要紧；如今是幅罕有的石版画。佩里亚姆先生没有回头，问道：「你那样说，是什么道理？」

「没有什么道理。我只是照实报告。」

「那你打算怎么办？」

「不怎么办。」

===Original===
Here was a man Mr. Perriam admired. He had not expected this. It was very good. Colet was a stouter fellow than he had imagined. Anyone who coolly ignored the aggressiveness with which Mr. Perriam disguised his own simple hesitances was sure of his secret approbation. A sly smile moved round his set mouth, but Jimmy did not see it. Still, this young man would have to be disciplined, to get him back to his place. When Mr. Perriam swung about his face was flushed and grim, and even fanatical in its assumed determination. The principal of that important house began, with sonorous sententiousness, for his task was not easy, to advise his assistant what young Colet was, when he came there, and what he had become in that fostering office. Mr. Perriam had all the command of rhetoric of a romantic man of affairs luxuriating in the waywardness of fools. He was solemn, and eloquently reasonable. He was enjoying this. He moved hither and thither with the energy of his warm periods, as if this was a meeting, and he could not help an appeal to the better feelings of a thoughtless generation, which might, nevertheless, do well, if it would but listen to him.

Colet hardly heard him, after the initial outburst. There was but a continuous and strenuous noise. He was meek and enduring. The room grew hot. This must end some day. But Perriam, he could see, was a figure of lasting power, able to continue, and the logic of his monomania was unanswerable. Jimmy merely waited patiently for silence to fall. It did not occur to him that he might laugh and walk out of the room and away. Nothing occurred to him.

But his meek submission to ill-luck, which to Mr. Perriam seemed but a show of proud and enduring reserve, caused his chief to believe that this appeal for gratitude and common sense was in vain, a further offence that made Mr. Perriam flounder in his periods. He was convinced by his own eloquence. His sense of an injustice became genuine, and too quick for his words. They were not ready for his heartfelt sincerity. He began to accuse Colet with an emphasis which he felt was all too weak. He saw that this was because he was not near enough for his assistant to get a full impression. He approached Colet, with his voice raised. Jimmy looked at him then, in dreary apprehension of a puerile but menacing apparition.

===Chinese===
这可是个佩里亚姆先生暗自佩服的人。他没有料到这一手。好得很。科利特比他想象的是条更硬气的汉子。谁要是能冷冷地不理会那副咄咄逼人——佩里亚姆先生正是拿它来掩饰自己那点单纯的迟疑——谁就准能赢得他私下的赞许。一丝狡黠的笑意在他紧闭的嘴边漾动，只是吉米没有看见。不过，这个年轻人必须受一番管束，好叫他归回本分。佩里亚姆先生猛地转过身来时，脸涨红了，铁青着，装出的决绝里甚至透着狂热。那家重要商行的老板开腔了，声如洪钟，箴言迭出——他的差事本不容易——告诫他的助手，年轻的科利特当初来的时候是何等样人，在这间栽培后进的写字间里又变成了何等样人。佩里亚姆先生使出了一个浪漫实业家的全套修辞功夫——这号实业家最能在愚人的任性妄为里如鱼得水。他神情庄重，说得头头是道。他正享受着这一刻。他随着一个个热烈段落的劲头在屋里东走西移，仿佛这是一场集会，他忍不住要向一代没心没肺的年轻人发出呼吁——不过那代人只要肯听他的，也未尝不能成器。

开头那一阵爆发过后，科利特几乎没有在听。那不过是一片连绵而费力的喧响。他温顺地忍着。屋里热了起来。这种事总有一天要了结。可佩里亚姆，他看得出来，是个后劲悠长的人物，能够一直继续下去，他偏执狂的逻辑无从置答。吉米只是耐心地等着寂静降临。他没有想到过，他本可以哈哈一笑，走出那间屋子，一走了之。他什么都没有想。

可是他这种对厄运的温顺屈服——在佩里亚姆先生看来，那不过是一副高傲而坚忍的矜持——倒叫上司认定，这一番唤起感恩与常识的恳求全属徒劳；这又是一重冒犯，害得佩里亚姆先生的辞章磕绊起来。他被自己的雄辩说服了。他的委屈感变得真切，来得比言辞还快。话语跟不上他发自肺腑的真诚。他开始指控科利特，用的语气连他自己都觉得太欠分量。他看出来，这是因为距离不够近，助手领受不了完整的印象。他逼近科利特，嗓门抬高了。吉米这时才看他，凄惶地注视着一个孩子气却来势汹汹的幽灵。

===Original===
“A man like you,” Mr. Perriam was saying, “has no right to be here. There are better men. I’ll tell you what it is to take a place you can’t fill. It’s swindling. You are a fraud. That damned quietness and good-nature cheats the people who pay you.”

Jimmy was not listening. His principal, close to him, raised an arm in trenchant reprobation. Colet glanced at the threat with indifference, and then an uncalled surge of abhorrence turned him black. He saw Perriam’s near mask as the front of all arrogant swinishness. He struck it.

Mr. Perriam behaved as though he had no bones. He dropped, face downwards, and his unexpected falling weight, which his assistant tried to catch, sent Colet floundering. He sat on the floor, legs spread out, deferentially waiting, as it seemed, for Mr. Perriam to rise first. But Mr. Perriam did not move. Colet eyed his chief in astonishment. The room was silent. Mr. Perriam remained on the carpet, with one arm awkwardly folded under him. His bald head, resting on the Axminster roses, was absurdly out of place. His boots with their spats were spread unnaturally. The assistant scrambled to his chief’s aid, and turned him round. Some effort was necessary; and Jimmy was as surprised as if, succouring the figure of a man, he found it had the head of a tailor’s dummy. Mr. Perriam’s face was a bad parody in wax. His mouth was open, and his teeth looked dry. His tongue was large and fatuous. Mr. Perriam stared at the ceiling.

===Chinese===
「像你这样的人，」佩里亚姆先生正说着，「没有权利待在这里。比你强的人有的是。我告诉你，占上一个你填不满的位子算什么。那是诈骗。你是个骗子。你那该死的安分和好脾气，坑的是付你薪水的人。」

吉米没有在听。他的上司逼近他，扬起一条手臂，摆出斩钉截铁的痛斥之势。科利特漠然瞥了那威胁一眼，随即一股无端涌起的憎恶使他满脸发黑。他看见近在咫尺的佩里亚姆那张假面，正是集一切傲慢猪性之大成的一张前脸。他一拳打了上去。

佩里亚姆先生的样子，仿佛他浑身没有一根骨头。他面朝下倒了下去；他那出乎意料的下坠分量，助手想接一把，反倒被带得踉跄失据。他坐在地板上，两腿摊开，一副恭恭敬敬的样子，仿佛在等佩里亚姆先生先起身。可佩里亚姆先生一动不动。科利特惊愕地打量他的上司。房间里静悄悄的。佩里亚姆先生伏在地毯上，一条胳膊别扭地压在身下。他那颗秃头枕在阿克斯明斯特（Axminster）地毯的玫瑰花纹上，显得荒唐地格格不入。他那双套着鞋罩的靴子不自然地撇开着。助手手忙脚乱地赶去搀上司，把他翻转过来。这需要费些力气；吉米又是那样吃惊，仿佛搀起一个人的身形，却发现它安着一颗裁缝铺人体模型的头。佩里亚姆先生的脸成了一幅蜡制的拙劣戏仿。他嘴张着，牙齿显得干燥。他的舌头肥大而呆滞。佩里亚姆先生直瞪着天花板。

===Original===
Jimmy shook him, and called to him, in the sudden anger of dismay. Mr. Perriam continued to stare at the ceiling. Jimmy loosened his chief’s collar in fumbling haste, swore at the knot of the neckcloth, tore roughly at the starch which held the collar-stud; but Mr. Perriam did not object. His big rough chin was warm but docile. His limp submission was horrible. Jimmy saw that he was dead; and waited on his knees, hoping that someone would come in. The church clock chimed nine. Only the cat looked in at the door, in round-eyed surprise, but did not enter.

Jimmy went to his own room, grabbed his hat to hurry for assistance, yet returned irresolutely to his principal’s room, because, naturally, one would expect to see Mr. Perriam in his chair. But he was still on the floor. Colet left the office, in the confused intention to escape from that object, to get help, to think it over, to call the police.

===Chinese===
吉米摇他，喊他，带着惊惶中骤起的怒气。佩里亚姆先生继续瞪着天花板。吉米慌乱而急促地去解上司的领子，一边咒骂领巾上的结，一边粗暴地撕扯那浆得硬挺、咬住领扣的领子；佩里亚姆先生却毫不抗议。他那粗大的下巴温热，却柔顺。他那种瘫软的任人摆布，可怕极了。吉米明白他死了；于是跪着等，巴望有人进来。教堂的钟敲了九点。只有那只猫在门口探进头来，圆睁着眼，一脸惊奇，却没有进来。

吉米回到自己那间屋，抓起帽子要赶去搬救兵，却又踌躇地折回上司的房间，因为照常理，人们总以为会看见佩里亚姆先生坐在他的椅子里。可他还躺在地板上。科利特离开了写字间，心怀一团纷乱的打算：躲开那个东西，去找人帮忙，把这事细想一遍，去报警。

## VI / 六

===Original===
Colet was surprised to find that the night outside was in cool and spacious repose. Its indifference stopped his rush. The Avenue was empty. He could hear the traffic as usual in Leadenhall Street. It was still there. And then he could hear also the lonely sound of his footsteps quickly following him. That sound startled yet steadied him. As he approached Billiter Street a policeman strolled into view, paused, and yawned. Jimmy was looking for a policeman, but not for one who yawned. That sign of boredom confused him, for he was nearing the constable. His distress would have checked him with an impulse to confide, but his legs did not know that, and so he was carried on.

He found himself in Fenchurch Street. He was walking east, but without any reason. He had merely turned to the left. He was just walking, and somewhat too hurriedly, so he slowed down. Then he came to Aldgate Pump, which is the starting-point in London for all solitary and extravagant adventures. He stopped, though not because he recognised a starting-point. He knew that pump. He was astonished to see it there. It had not changed. It was the first impartial and certain landmark to show distinctly since he took his eyes off the Axminster carpet.

What should he do? He thought of this as he continued to walk eastwards. He did not know what he expected to find in that direction, but the vista ahead, he had seen, was more friendly with a larger crowd. The crowd, somehow, looked helpful. He wanted to get into it. One more does not seem to matter so much when the crowd is large. Nobody looked at him. This steadied him still more. He did not want to be looked at.

===Chinese===
科利特（Colet）惊讶地发现，外面的夜正处于清凉而开阔的静谧之中。这份漠然止住了他的疾奔。比利特大道上空无一人。他能照常听见利德贺街上的车声。它还在那儿。接着，他还听见了自己脚步那孤零的声响，正急急地跟在他身后。那声音叫他吃惊，却也叫他定住了神。走近比利特街时，一名警察踱进视野，停了停，打了个哈欠。吉米（Jimmy）正在找一名警察，但要找的不是打哈欠的那种。那副倦怠的表示把他的方寸搅乱了，因为他正在向那名警员靠近。他的苦楚本会让他停下，生出倾诉的冲动，可他的两条腿不知道这个，于是他只管被带着往前去了。

他发觉自己到了芬乔奇街。他在往东走，却毫无缘由。他不过是朝左拐了个弯。他只是在走，而且走得有些太急，于是放慢了脚步。然后他到了奥德门水泵（Aldgate Pump）——在伦敦，一切孤独而狂放的奇遇都从这里启程。他停了下来，倒不是因为认出了什么起点。他认得那座水泵。看见它还在那里，他很惊讶。它没有变。自从他的目光离开那块阿克斯明斯特地毯（Axminster carpet）以来，这是头一件不偏不倚、确凿分明地显现在他眼前的地标。

他该怎么办？他一边继续往东走，一边想着这个。他不知道自己指望朝那个方向找到什么，但他已经看到，前方的景象人更多，也更友善。那人群，不知怎么，看上去像是肯帮忙的。他想钻进人群里去。人群一大，多一个人似乎就不那么要紧了。没有人看他。这更让他定了神。他不想被人看。

===Original===
Something ought to be done. Should he telephone to Mrs. Perriam? “Is that Mrs. Perriam? I have just killed your husband. I couldn’t help it.” Seemed rather silly. She might be upset.

Was there anything he could do? He considered that, and continued his easterly drift. Perhaps there was nothing he could do. Now and again the image of that yawning policeman came before him, to be instantly expunged. That fellow would not understand; he didn’t know Perriams, and never saw the boss with his arm up, bullying, and that look on his big flushed face. The look wasn’t on his face now. Where had it gone? No good trying to produce it in evidence. The little things which really count can never be shown in evidence. They do the trick, and then they vanish.

Nobody could help Perriam now. He ought not to have died like that. Too idiotic. A man who could die so easily should have kept quiet. Bad as a swindle. He would never have believed it. Anyone would think the heart was just waiting for an excuse to stop. Heavens, you couldn’t stop a decent heart like that.

===Chinese===
总得做点什么。要不要打电话给佩里亚姆（Perriam）太太？『是佩里亚姆太太吗？我刚杀了您丈夫。我实在没法子。』未免太傻了。她也许会心乱。

有什么他能做的吗？他思量着这个，继续向东漂移。也许根本没有什么他能做的。那个打哈欠的警察的形象时不时来到他眼前，随即被抹去。那家伙不会懂的；他不认得佩里亚姆商行，也从没见过老板抬着胳膊、盛气凌人的样子，没见过他那张涨红的大脸上的那副神气。那副神气此刻不在他脸上了。它到哪儿去了？想把它当证据摆出来，没有用处。真正起了作用的那些小事，永远没法作为证据出示。它们做成了事，然后就消失了。

现在谁也帮不了佩里亚姆了。他不该就那样死了。太荒唐。一个这么容易就死掉的人，本该安分些。恶劣得像一场骗局。他本来是绝不会相信的。谁都会以为，那颗心脏只是在等一个借口好停下来。天哪，那么一颗体面的心脏，你休想让它停摆。

===Original===
Had he really hit his chief? He did not remember doing it. He could not recall the feel of the contact. The violent old fool just dropped. Poor old fellow. A pity he waited till that telephone bell rang. Perriam would be alive now if he hadn’t. It was odd that he couldn’t remember the blow. But that wouldn’t do. No good, that. Either he hit the man, or else God knocked him out. Perhaps a bit of both. All the same to the police. Easy for God to prove an alibi.

He found himself by the stalls of Aldgate. There was a distraction of hissing naphtha flares, and illuminated trams which interlaced on many tracks like short lengths of lighted streets on the move; and a confused slow tide of faces, masks that were vacant, foreign, indifferent, which expected nothing. They seemed to be upborne on shadows. They went slowly past, bobbing on the surface of nothing, and had no names, and were going nowhere. Each face had but a brief existence by the favour of a chance light, and then was gone.

That made the matter worse. It was meaningless. The faces just glanced once, and then went out. Eyes in a never-ending stream, that came into existence with one look of indifference as they passed into a light, and then were done. He went into a tavern to get out of it. There were many eyes floating past, a ceaseless drift of stares.

His thoughts would not stop, and yet they did not help him. Perhaps the morning would help him. It would be all cut and dried by then. No escape. He could stand up to it then. But to what? What would there be? Only the usual cold and compelling logic of the old confusion, and those eyes all round looking on indifferently.

===Chinese===
他真的打了他的老板吗？他不记得打过。他回想不起那一击上手的感觉。那个凶暴的老傻瓜就那么倒了下去。可怜的老家伙。可惜他偏要等到那电话铃响起来。要是没有那一响，佩里亚姆现在还活着。他居然记不起那一拳，这很奇怪。可那样不行。那样可不行。要么是他打了那人，要么是上帝把他撂倒的。也许两样各占一点。对警察来说全都一样。上帝要证明自己不在场，倒是容易。

他发觉自己到了奥德门的货摊旁。那里有一片撩人眼目、叫人分神的景象：嘶嘶作响的石脑油灯火；亮着灯的电车在多条轨道上交错往来，像一段段亮着灯的街道在移动；还有一股纷乱而迟缓的人面潮流，一张张空洞、陌生、冷漠的面具，什么都不指望。它们仿佛被暗影托浮着。它们缓缓漂过，在虚无的表面上起伏，没有名字，也不去往哪里。每张脸只凭一星偶然的灯光之惠，短暂地存在一下，随后便没了。

这让事情更糟了。这毫无意义。那些脸只瞥一下，随即熄灭。一双双眼睛汇成不绝的长流，在走进一片灯光时带着漠然的一望而生，随即完结。他走进一家酒馆，好躲开这一切。里面仍有许多眼睛漂过，一阵无休无止的凝视的漂流。

他的思绪停不下来，却帮不了他。也许早晨能帮他。到那时，一切都已成定局。无处可逃。到那时他就能挺住去面对。可是面对什么呢？那里会有什么？只有那场旧日混乱惯常的冰冷而有强迫力的逻辑，还有四周那些漠然旁观的眼睛。

===Original===
He was not sure what he wanted in a public-house. A brisk potman appeared to know what, and served him. The potman had a squint. That was a good squint. It made the chap seem polite. He sat on a bench near a tough who was thumping a table with a heavy hand to emphasise a matter which had to be whispered, though huskily, to a companion who listened with his eyes shut, while sucking a pipe. “I arst yer. What would you ’ave done?” The lean man did not open his eyes. He nodded his head solemnly.

The talker glanced furtively at Jimmy beside him, who was gazing in evident abstraction at a glass globe in its haze of tobacco smoke. The man had no collar, and he eased his thick moist neck from a constricting shirt-band with a finger, and grimaced in impatient discomfort. “I’d ’ad enough of the bitch. Too much of it. But that stopped her jaw. An’ there you are, Bill. I shan’t turn up in the mornin’.”

The other fellow removed his pipe. “Police know?” he asked.

===Chinese===
他拿不准自己在酒馆里想要什么。一个利落的酒馆伙计倒像是知道，便给他端了上来。那伙计斜视。那是种好斜视。它使这家伙显得客气。他在长凳上坐下，旁边是个粗汉，正用沉重的手捶着桌子，以强调一件不得不压着嗓子、却仍沙哑地讲给同伴听的事，那同伴闭着眼睛听，嘴里嘬着一支烟斗。

「我问你。换了你，你会怎么办？」那瘦子没有睁眼。他郑重其事地点了点头。

另一个家伙把烟斗从嘴里拿开。「警察知道吗？」他问。

===Original===
Jimmy moved instantly at that word to look at them. The tough felt his movement, and swung sharply upon him with his great hands clenched on the greasy knees of his trousers. He contemplated Jimmy with lowering insolence in silence, head thrust forward, for some seconds.

“ ’Ere, you﻿—you with the whiskers. You listening to us? Know anything? By cripes, you shift your ear, or it’ll get thick.”

Jimmy felt a change of thought. It went over him with a glow of pleasure. He smiled kindly at the tough. Good, good, that fellow was a weight.

===Chinese===
一听见这个词，吉米立刻动了动，朝他们看去。那粗汉察觉了他的动静，猛地朝他转过身来，两只大手紧攥在油亮的裤膝上。他探着头，带着阴沉的横蛮，一声不响地打量了吉米几秒钟。

「喂，你——说你呢，留络腮胡的。你在听我们说话？知道些什么吗？老天在上，把你那耳朵挪开，不然管叫它肿起来。」

吉米觉得念头换了一副样子。它裹着一股快意的暖流从他身上淌过。他朝那粗汉和气地笑了笑。好，好，那家伙有分量。

===Original===
“Don’t be alarmed,” he said. “I don’t know what you are talking about.”

“Alarmed!” The big fellow inclined his head to his friend. “ ’Ear ’im, Bill? Arsts me if I’m alarmed.” His face came round with decision. “Don’t you wait ’ere any longer than you must, whiskers. This pub is unhealthy. Understand what I mean? You got anything else to do, go and do it.”

The distraction grew still more pleasing, though Jimmy thought it might be better to go. Yet not too soon. He maintained his friendly smile, and took a drink.

===Chinese===
「别慌，」他说，「我不知道你们在谈什么。」

「慌！」大块头朝他朋友偏了偏头。「听见没有，比尔（Bill）？他问我慌不慌。」他毅然把脸转了回来。「络腮胡，别在这儿多待一刻。这家酒馆伤身。明白我的意思吗？你还有别的事要办，就办去。」

这个排遣越来越叫他受用了，虽然吉米觉得也许还是走的好。可也别急着走。他保持着友好的微笑，喝了一口。

===Original===
“Plenty of other things to do, when I feel like it. Don’t let me keep you from your interesting conversation with your friend.”

The man steadily took stock of Jimmy, hesitated, and turned away, to mumble to his companion. Jimmy presently rose, wished him good night, and left the tavern. He paused, when in the shades beyond, to watch the door. The two men came out, surveyed the traffic carefully, and walked towards him. This would never do. He kept close to the wall, and continued. He took a dark and handy byway, and lost himself in it. Those two fellows did not appear to enter it. No use having more trouble. He had an unlucky fist.

Where was he? But it did not matter where he was. Any circumstance would do now, for he had lost the old set. Lost it? Not so easily lost as that. Anyhow, he might as well walk off his feelings till morning, when he would have to own up.

===Chinese===
「等我想做的时候，别的事有的是。别让我耽误你和你朋友那番有意思的谈话。」

那人不动声色地把吉米估量了一番，迟疑了一下，转过身去，朝同伴咕哝起来。吉米随后便起身，向他道了晚安，离开了酒馆。走到外面的暗影里，他停下脚步，望着酒馆的门。那两个人出来了，仔细打量了一遍街上的动静，朝他走来。这可不行。他紧贴着墙，继续走。他拐进一条又暗又凑手的小路，把自己隐没在里面。那两个人似乎没有进来。再惹麻烦没有用处。他这只拳头不吉利。

这是哪儿？可这是哪儿不要紧。如今什么境遇都使得，因为他把旧的那一套丢了。丢了？不会那么容易就丢了。不管怎样，他不妨走着把这腔情绪走散，一直走到早晨，到那时他就得坦白了。

===Original===
Queer place, this. There was a wall beside him which was Cyclopean. A straight section of primordial night, like the beginning of the way down to Erebus. The just and predestined path for him. He would follow it to whatever was at the bottom of it. He did not know that it was, anciently, but the beginning of Ratcliffe Highway. The night was brooding and overcast. He could only guess that he was still going east. There were no stars. Why go east? Well, when the stars have fallen out of the sky, of course they are not there for use.

When he came to a street lamp he could see the wall was only dingy brickwork, but it looked like the palpable residue of old chaos, something which had never seen daylight. It ranged upwards beyond the glim in the street. No end to it, above. The glass of the lamp was broken, and its flame, shaken by a draught, caused irresolution in the revealed area of the wall, which contracted and expanded, as though immemorial night were resilient, but too vast to be moved by a little light except as a local jest.

He continued along by the wall, which was so vague that sometimes his hand knocked it. Then he remembered he had a body. Damn! He was still there. He was not a disembodied spirit yet walking in a chaste nightshirt down to Hades. But there was no need of a grave-cloth for him, to get the appropriate feeling. Was Apollyon anywhere about? It would be a pleasure to meet him. Give a fellow something to do. The trouble with Belial is that you can get no nearer to him than when in abstraction you bark your knuckles. Pretty lonely, that kind of conflict, in the valley of the shadow. The worst thing in Hell is that nobody else is there, no devil, no fire that has the merit of being everlasting, no pal, no light, no way out, and the way in behind you gone like yesterday morning.

===Chinese===
这地方真怪。他身旁有一堵墙，巨石累累，如出库克罗普斯（Cyclopean）之手。像太古之夜笔直的一截，像通往厄瑞玻斯（Erebus）的下坡路的起头。对他来说，这恰是公道而前定的路。他要顺着它走下去，走到它底下的无论什么东西那里去。他并不知道，这条道在古时候，不过是拉德克利夫大道（Ratcliffe Highway）的开端。夜阴沉沉地笼罩着。他只能猜自己还在往东走。天上没有星。为什么往东？唉，星星既然都从天上掉了出去，自然就不在原处供人取用了。

走到一盏街灯下，他看出那墙不过是脏污的砖工，可它看上去像古老混沌那可以触摸的残滓，是某种从未见过天日的东西。它向上延伸，超出了街上那一点灯光。上头没有尽头。灯的玻璃罩破了，灯焰被穿堂风摇撼，使墙上被照亮的那一片迟疑不定，忽缩忽胀，仿佛太古之夜自有弹性，却又浩瀚得不会被一点灯火撼动，至多被它逗出一星本地的小玩笑。

他顺着墙继续走，墙是那样模糊，有时他的手会撞上它。这时他才记起自己还有一副身体。该死！他还在。他还不是一缕离体的魂灵，穿着贞洁的睡衣一路走进哈得斯（Hades）。不过他要讨那种相称的感觉，也用不着裹尸布。亚玻伦（Apollyon）就在左近吗？遇上他会是件乐事。也好叫这家伙有点事做。彼列（Belial）难缠的地方在于：你出了神磕破指节，也仍旧近不了他的身。那样的交战，在死荫的幽谷里，实在太孤单。地狱里最糟的是里头没有别人，没有魔鬼，没有那点以不灭见长的火，没有伙伴，没有光，没有出路，而身后的来路，也像昨天早晨一样没了。

===Original===
Were they houses, opposite? They might be houses. They were more like that than anything else. Some were the complement of the wall beside him, and the roofs of others came down almost to his level. The irregular penumbra opposite was sprinkled with lighted squares. The squares showed, surprisingly, that there might be others beside himself in this abiding-place of night; he could see into their lighted caves. Who and what were they? At times the shadow of a colossal and distorted head would appear on a window-blind, a protean shape which confused a newcomer with its grotesque mockery of order and shapeliness, reduced itself to a sudden knob, and faded off. The others here had that scope. They could take any shape they liked, and diminish to nothing suddenly while you watched. Occasionally there were wanderers like himself on the other side of the way, figures with no character, in no hurry. They only moved. Perhaps they were shapes which had come off the blinds for a change.

At times he heard voices, but they belonged to nobody. Nobody was there to speak. Once there was a single eldritch cry. Jimmy stopped. It came from a narrow opening in the dark on the other side, to which depth was given by a distant bracket lamp on a wall. He could see nothing but the lamp up there. Its light flattened and turned blue in a gust, and then flared again, as though it had got over that trouble. Maybe the lamp had shrieked in its loneliness. A figure, which reminded Jimmy of a man, leaned against a post at the bottom of the turning. It did not stir. It did not move to look at the lamp which had screamed. Perhaps it was used to the cries of loneliness in the dark.

Jimmy felt it was time to make out what the shapes were that haunted this region. He crossed over to see. But the figure did not look at him. Its head was on its breast, studying the road, perhaps trying to see daylight on a path that was far below the reach of daylight. There came along three other forms which appeared to be two women and a man, and they shambled together past him, singing with drawling and doleful remorse. The yowling whine of the women was almost human in its discordance with the subjugating dark to which it was addressed.

===Chinese===
对面那些是房子吗？可能是房子。比起别的什么来，它们更像房子。有的正好补全他身旁这堵墙，另一些的屋顶几乎垂到他这一层高。对面不规则的半影里，撒着一个个亮着的方块。这些方块出人意料地表明，在这夜的栖止之地，除了他或许还有别人；他望得进他们那些亮着的洞穴。他们是谁，又是什么？有时，一个庞大而歪扭的头影会映在窗帘上，一个善变形的形体，用它对秩序与端方那怪诞的嘲弄，把新来的人弄得晕头转向，随即缩成突然的一颗疙瘩，淡了下去。这里的其他存在有那样的能耐。他们爱变什么形就变什么形，还能在你眼看着的时候骤然缩成乌有。偶尔路的另一边也有像他一样的游荡者，一些没有面目、不慌不忙的身影。他们只是移动。也许它们是从窗帘上下来换换花样的形体。

他不时听见人语，可那不属于任何人。没有人在那里说话。有一次，响起了一声孤零零的怪叫。吉米站住了。声音来自对面黑暗中一道狭窄的开口，远处墙上一盏壁灯给那深处添出了纵深。他只看得见高处那盏灯。一阵风里，它的光变得扁而发蓝，随即又旺了起来，像是挺过了那场麻烦。也许是那盏灯在孤独里尖叫了一声。一个让吉米想起人的形体，斜倚在拐角底下一根柱子上。它不动。它不挪动去看那盏尖叫过的灯。它大概听惯了黑暗中孤独的喊叫。

吉米觉得，该弄清楚出没于这一带的那些形体究竟是什么了。他穿过去看。可那形体不看他。它的头垂在胸前，端详着路面，也许想在那条远非日光所能及的路径上看见日光。又有三个人影走来，看样子是两个女人和一个男人，他们一同拖拖沓沓地从他面前走过，用拖长了调子的、哀悔的歌喉唱着。那两个女人呜呜咽咽的哀鸣，冲着那压服一切的黑暗发去，其不谐之处几乎带上了人味。

===Original===
Jimmy, nearing another lamp, was thoughtfully regarding a truncated monument which stood under it. What did that commemorate? The top of it moved, and turned a human face to him. It was a policeman. “Good night,” said the policeman, as Jimmy got within that brief circle of knowledge.

“Good evening,” said Jimmy, and stopped.

“Having a midnight prowl, sir?” asked the policeman.

“Yes,” said Jimmy. “This is a strange parish.”

===Chinese===
吉米走近另一盏灯，正若有所思地端详灯下一座截了顶的碑。那是纪念什么的？碑顶动了动，朝他转过来一张人脸。是名警察。吉米一走进那一小圈认知的领域，警察便道了声「晚安」。

「晚上好，」吉米说，停了下来。

「半夜出来游荡吗，先生？」警察问。

「是啊，」吉米说。「这个教区真怪。」

===Original===
“Oh, I dunno. Not to us. We’re used to it. Not so bad as it’s painted.”

“I thought it looked like a place where everything was hidden away.”

“Not it. Don’t you believe it. No good for hiding in. Too many looking on. No good to come here, after a little upset like, thinking that you can get lost. Though some people do say so. I read a book the other day﻿ ﻿… funny things get into books. All about this place. But not like it is when you know it, same as I do.”

===Chinese===
「哦，我可不觉得。对我们来说不怪。习惯了。不像传说的那么糟。」

「我看这儿像是个把一切都藏起来的地方。」

「才不是。别信那个。藏身可不成。旁观的眼睛太多。遇上了点小乱子什么的就跑到这儿来，指望就此把自己走丢，那可没用。不过也真有人这么说。前几天我读了本书……书里什么怪玩意儿都有。写的全是这地方。可跟我这样摸熟了之后的实际情形，不是一回事。」

===Original===
Jimmy turned over the keys in his pocket. There wasn’t much to talk about, if you didn’t want to say anything.

“I heard an unpleasant scream just now, at the turning above.”

“Yers; they’re always nasty to hear. But there’s nothing in it. Take no notice, that’s the thing to do. No screams, guv’nor, take it from me, when someone’s really getting it in the neck. They take good care of that. As a rule. Only amateurs let ’em scream.” The constable was amused. “If they began with a loud noise our job would be as easy as kiss your hand. Go straight to it, couldn’t we? But they don’t oblige us. We have to find ’em afterwards.”

===Chinese===
吉米在口袋里翻弄着那串钥匙。要是你什么也不想讲，可谈的事就不多了。

「我刚才在上面那个拐角，听见一声吓人的尖叫。」

「是啊；那种声音听着总是不受用。可里面没什么名堂。别去理会它，这才是正经理。听我的没错，长官：真有人被干掉的时候，是不喊叫的。他们把这一层照顾得很周到。照例如此。只有外行才让他们喊出声。」警员自得其乐。「要是他们一开场就闹出大动静，我们这份差事就易如反掌了。照直奔过去就是了，是不是？可他们不肯成全我们。我们只好事后把他们找出来。」

===Original===
The officer seemed glad of someone to talk to. He eased his helmet.

“Take it from me, sir.” He jerked his left thumb over his shoulder. “Why, only last week a young feller up there, he tried it on. Came from another part of London. People always think they’re safe when they dunno where they are, like. Reckoned, I suppose, that anything could happen here and nobody would notice it. God bless me, the fact that he was here gave him away. What was he doing here? Of course, everybody asked that. Wouldn’t come here for pleasure, as you might say.” The constable chuckled again. “And there you are.”

“Where does this road lead to?”

===Chinese===
这位警员像是有人说话，很高兴。他松了松头盔。

「听我的没错，先生。」他左手拇指朝肩后一扬。「喏，就在上礼拜，那边有个小伙子，就来了这么一手。从伦敦别处来的。人一到不知自己身在哪儿的地方，就总当自己安全了，就是这么回事。我估摸着，他认定这儿出什么事都没人会留意。老天爷，偏偏是他到了这儿这件事，把他给卖了。他到这儿来干什么？当然，人人都这么问。可以说，谁也不会为了取乐上这儿来。」警员又嘿嘿笑了起来。「这不就得啦。」

「这条路通到哪儿去？」

===Original===
“Same sort of thing all the way along. Comes out by Stepney Station. Go on far enough, and you’ll have to walk back, this time o’ night. Far to go?”

“I think I’ll be getting along, then.”

“Well, speaking for myself, I’d sooner be indoors. But, of course, if it’s the first time you’ve done it, it’s an experience. I hope you’ll enjoy it, sir.”

===Chinese===
「一路全是这一路光景。走到头是斯特普尼车站（Stepney Station）。再往前走远了，这个钟点你就得走回头路。还要走远吗？」

「那我想我这就走吧。」

「唉，替我自己说话，我宁可待在屋里。不过当然了，要是这是您头一回干这个，也算一番阅历。祝您尽兴，先生。」

===Original===
Jimmy hesitated, but then went his way. He strolled away from the light, but without knowing whether he was continuing in the same direction. He was not thinking of that. He took a side alley without knowing it, and continued to take whichever opening in the obscurity was the next one. No good trying to believe morning would ever come to that precinct. But he wanted the morning, he wanted it as soon as it could come.

This place looked like the forgotten lumberyard of creation. Objects that could not be published had been abandoned there. They held together because they had never been disturbed. The echoes of his footsteps might shake them down, so he made less noise. This was the very bottom of the night, and he had sunk to it by his own weight. One byway left him in a narrow passage, under a gas-jet, where he had to choose between right and left. He could see what used to be there. It used to be warehouses. He looked above, as if in appeal, for a suggestion of sky. There might have been one, but the ancient walls were close, and leaned towards each other, as if the weight of night with its density would bury that foundered corner. Jimmy felt that he was sunk profoundly from all communion with his fellows. The gas-jet made hardly any hollow in the gloom. It but selected for illumination a worn iron post, a scatter of chaff on cobblestones, horse droppings, and a few barrel-hoops. Then, almost melted into the dusk beyond the chaff on the cobbles, he saw a dog watching him. He saw its yellow eyes. It was a dog? Here, old fellow! When he moved that way it became only an ugly little noise, and was not there. An unseen hoop sprang from under his tread and bit him on the hand. But he did not cry out. Almost immediately he saw it was only a hoop.

As though it had only opened in the darkness since he came, he noticed before him a cleft in the wall. It could have been an outlet there. It was a lighter patch. While he wondered whether it was an outlet a green planet moved across it, midway, from side to side. The planet appeared suddenly, was bright for a few seconds, and then was eclipsed. As though that green light had caused it, he felt a cool draught blow steadily from across the way. What was there? Then a red star appeared midway, in the midst of a travelling cluster of white stars. Lord, a ship!

===Chinese===
吉米迟疑了一下，随后还是上了路。他信步离开灯光，却不知自己是否还循着原来的方向。他没有想那个。他不知不觉拐进了一条小巷，之后凡是晦暗中下一个遇到的口子，他都照拐不误。想叫自己相信这样的地界也会来早晨，是徒劳。可他要早晨，他要它来得多早就多早。

这地方像是造化遗忘了的杂物场。没法公之于众的东西都被丢弃在这里。它们还聚而不散，是因为从没被扰动过。他脚步的回声也许会把它们震落，所以他放轻了声音。这里正是夜的最底层，他是凭自身的分量沉到底来的。一条小路把他留在一条窄巷里，头顶一盏煤气灯，他必须在左右之间作个选择。他看得出这里原先是什么。原先是一排货栈。他抬头望，像是在恳求，想觅得一点天空的示意。也许本来有一个，可古老的墙靠得近，彼此倾侧，仿佛夜的分量挟着它的浓密，要把那个塌陷的角落埋掉。吉米觉得自己深深沉陷，与同胞的一切交往都已断绝。那盏煤气灯在幽暗里几乎没能掏出一个坑。它只挑出几样东西来照亮：一根磨旧的铁柱，鹅卵石上一摊谷壳，马粪，几只桶箍。随后，在谷壳之外几乎融进暮色里的地方，他看见一条狗在望他。他看见它黄色的眼睛。那是条狗吗？过来，老伙计！他朝那边一动，它只变成一小声难听的响动，就不在了。一只看不见的桶箍从他脚下弹起，咬了他的手一口。可他没有喊出声。几乎紧接着他就看清了，那不过是只桶箍。

仿佛是自他来之后才在黑暗里裂开的，他注意到面前墙上有一道裂口。那可能是个出口。那是较亮的一块。他正琢磨着它是不是出口，一颗绿色的行星横越而过，正在半途，从一边到另一边。那颗行星突然出现，亮了几秒钟，随即被蚀掉了。仿佛是那点绿光招来的，他觉出一股清凉的风从对过平稳地吹来。那边有什么？随后，一颗红星在半途出现，处在一簇移动的白星当中。天哪，一条船！

===Original===
He listened rigidly. He could hear the plunging of a propeller. He made a guess. A red light? Then she was going east. She was bound outwards. He crossed over and walked down that slit in the dark till he felt only outer space was before him. There were remote points of light in a void. He stopped, and fumbled with his hand. Yes, this was the edge of his world.

He sat down on it. In a little while he could hear water talking quietly somewhere below him. It might have been near or far. It was invisible. Perhaps that was the tide running by the Southern Cross. That was a long sheer cold drop.

“Ahoy!” It was a clear but minute call from straight before him. “Aho‑o‑y!”

===Chinese===
他僵住身子细听。他听得见螺旋桨击水的起落。他猜了一猜。红灯？那她是往东去。她是出海去的。他穿过去，顺着黑暗中那道细缝走下去，直到他觉得面前只剩下外层空间。虚空里有遥远的点点光。他停下，用手去摸索。是的，这是他的世界的边缘。

他在那边缘上坐了下来。过了一会儿，他听见水在下面什么地方安静地说话。也许近，也许远。它看不见。那也许是从南十字号（Southern Cross）旁边流过的潮水。那是一段又长、又陡、又冷的坠落。

「啊嗬！」一声清晰却细微的呼唤，从正前方传来。「啊——嗬——嗬！」

===Original===
“I’m here,” said Jimmy to himself. “I’m coming.”

That caller would take some finding. How far to go? He sat looking at that idea till his plight, the monody of the waters, the far points of light, and a thin drizzle which began, all blurred into a stillness within which his waking mind became like one of the stars sunk deeply in the void. He was hardly there.

Had he been asleep? It had been raining. He was wet. When he stood on the edge, he heard, as if from across the river, a clock strike three. Three tiny ones. Not much longer to wait now. Better get moving. Nobody about yet.

===Chinese===
「我在这儿，」吉米对自己说。「我来了。」

那个呼唤的人，可有的找了。还要走多远？他坐着凝望这个念头，直到他的处境、水声的单调悲吟、远处的点点灯光，还有这时落起来的一阵细雨，全都晕成一片静止，在这静止里，他醒着的心智变得像深深沉入虚空的群星中的一颗。他几乎不在那里了。

他睡着过吗？下过雨了。他湿透了。他站到边上时，听见一座钟敲了三下，像是隔河传来的。三下小小的钟声。要等的不多了。最好动身。周围还没有人。

===Original===
The same old walls continued in a city that was dead. Funny name that, over a shop. Couldn’t be right. Perhaps he was getting lightheaded. Wu Fu Li. Better not see people about when they had such names. This ashy solitude was interminable, and morning never came to it.

He rambled up to the centre of a bridge which seemed to rise above the shadows, and saw beyond him the inky grotesques of chimneys and house ridges against a low pallor. He leaned over the parapet. So there it began, that day for him. Below the bridge was a stream, soundless and raven, which became outlined in the bottom of night even as he watched. Its banks were of mud. They were livid like the water, but they did not move. The water uncoiled slowly and so it could be seen. A careened barge was below, a lump melting into the sludge. It would take old Charon some time to shift that. But this was his river, all right. The old boy was probably waiting asleep under that gasometer.

A group of men passed him, going the same way. But they were brisk. Their noisy footsteps meant purpose and direction. Something was ahead of them, and they were going to it. That was more like life. Where they could go, so could he. He followed them, more like life. People were about, glum but purposeful. This was an early world, where railway lines were mixed with the streets, factories with the homes, and an unborn ship stood immense in her skeleton womb above the tenements. The day was broad, when, surmounting grey fields and sheds with low roofs of iron, the scarlet funnel of a liner stood up like a noble beacon. Beyond her was a blue funnel with yellow bands. The vista of low buildings was overtopped by a long diminishing array of cranes and jibs, masts, and the vivid colours of smokestacks, one beyond the other. A broad new world this, but with some smells he knew. Where did this road end? Some lascars in blue muslin and red turbans were crouched under a railway station. A clock was suspended over the deserted platform. It proclaimed a quite impossible hour. Time, perhaps, had lost its way. Or there was no time here. He might have got beyond the range of the schedules. He looked up at the clock, and saw a sparrow’s nest in its works. Time was stopped here to let the birds nest. At the other end of the platform was a name-board above the palings, its letters big and positive enough to announce that locality to a great distance. Gallions.

===Chinese===
一样的老墙延续着，穿过一座死去的城。一家铺子上方那个名字可真逗。不会是真有的吧。也许他有点神思恍惚了。吴福利（Wu Fu Li）。顶着这样的名字，还是别撞见周围有什么人更好。这灰烬似的孤寂没有尽头，早晨永远不到它这儿来。

他信步登上一座桥的中央，那桥仿佛在暗影之上耸起，他望见前方烟囱与屋脊墨色的怪影，衬着一片低低的灰白。他俯身伏在栏杆上。他那一日，便由此开始了。桥下是一道水流，无声而乌黑，就在他注视之下，在夜的底部显出了轮廓。它的两岸是淤泥。它们像水一样青白，却不动。水缓缓舒展开来，这样才能看见它。下面倾侧着一条驳船，一团正消进泥淖里的东西。老卡戎（Charon）要想把它挪走，得费上些时候。不过，这确实是他的河，没错。那老伙计多半正在那座煤气罐底下打着瞌睡等呢。

一群人打他身边走过，与他同路。可他们脚步轻快。他们闹哄哄的脚步声意味着目的与方向。前面有什么在等着他们，他们正朝它去。那更像活气。他们能去的地方，他也能去。他跟了上去，就更像活气了。四处有了人，郁郁的，却各有目标。这是一个早年的世界，铁轨混在街道里，工厂混在家宅间，一条尚未出世的船，以她骨架的子宫，赫然耸立在成片的廉价住宅之上。天已大亮，一艘班轮的猩红烟囱越过灰色的田野和覆着低矮铁皮顶的棚屋，像一座高贵的灯塔矗立起来。她后面还有一条箍着黄道的蓝烟囱。低矮房屋的长景之上，压着一列渐远渐缩的起重机和吊杆、桅杆，还有烟囱的鲜色，一座挨着一座。这是一个开阔的新世界，可有几股气味是他认得的。这条路到哪里为止？几个穿蓝细布、缠红头巾的拉斯卡尔水手（lascar）蜷在一座火车站底下。一面钟悬在空荡荡的站台上。它报出一个绝无可能的时刻。时间，也许，迷了路。或者，这里根本没有时间。他也许已经走到了时刻表的范围之外。他抬头看那面钟，看见钟的机件里有一窝麻雀。时间在这里停了，好让鸟儿做窝。站台另一头，栅栏上方挂着一块站名牌，上面的字母又大又笃定，足以把这地名宣示到很远的地方去。加利恩斯（Gallions）。

## VII / 七

===Original===
After a little respite of sleep in the hotel at the dockhead, Jimmy went down through a dull corridor to the coffee-room. He was surprised, when he opened its door, by the attack of an interior light which was theatrical in its early brilliance. Four or five men at a table near the window looked as if they were beginning the day. Breakfast then? He got an impression of a room which was set, in a surprisingly good imitation of morning, for an act in a play. The dour figures of the men at coffee and newspapers were very like life. One of them looked up at him over his spectacles in critical fixity, as if he had interrupted a private rehearsal. In his embarrassment Jimmy shut the door at once, without going in. Thought it was time for tea. Perhaps his watch had stopped.

===Chinese===
在码头头那家旅馆里稍得片刻睡眠的喘息之后，吉米穿过一条晦暗的走廊，下楼往咖啡室去。推开门时，一屋子的光猛地袭来，那份清晨似的明亮带着舞台气，叫他吃了一惊。窗边一张桌旁坐着四五个男人，看样子正开始他们的一天。这么说，是在用早餐了？他得到的印象是：这屋子布置得像一出戏里的一幕，把早晨摹拟得出奇地像。喝咖啡、读报纸的那些阴沉身影，活脱脱就是生活本身。其中一个从眼镜上方抬眼看他，目光挑剔而凝定，仿佛他打搅了一场私下的排练。吉米窘迫之下立刻带上了门，没进去。他还以为到了喝下午茶的时候。兴许是他的表停了。

===Original===
“Who was that?” asked the spectacled man of his neighbour.

“Don’t know. Didn’t see him.”

“Thought it might be the man who got the *Altair*. He was supposed to join her yesterday.”

Another man lowered his cup. “The *Altair* is my ship,” he said.

“So that’s that. Pleased to meet you, sir. She’s anchored astern of mine, the *Harlow*.” His paper went aside. “Nothing in that,” he grumbled, “I always go to it as if it could no more be missed than the chronometer, but somehow I can never get the time by it. Anything in your radical rag, doctor? And don’t keep those rolls.”

The elderly doctor smiled sideways. “Why, yes, Captain Bennett, plenty in it. You are unjust. You must have missed a whole page of bargains in Oxford Street. And I see our owner’s horse is fancied for the Derby. You didn’t see that? It struck me as strange that racing stables should be run on ships that never will pay.”

===Chinese===
「刚才是谁？」戴眼镜的人问邻座。

「不知道。没瞧见。」

「还以为是拿到牵牛星号（Altair）的那一位。他本该昨天上船的。」

另一个人放下了杯子。「牵牛星号是我的船。」他说。

「原来如此。幸会，先生。她就在我那条哈洛号（Harlow）的船尾后面下锚。」他把报纸撇到一边。「里头没啥名堂，」他嘟哝道，「我一向把它当作同天文钟一样须臾离不得的东西去看，可不知怎么，我从它那儿就是对不出钟点来。你那份激进破报上有什么吗，大夫？还有，别把面包卷都攥在自己跟前。」

年长的医生侧过脸笑了笑。「哎哟，有的呢，贝内特（Bennett）船长，多着呢。你这话不公道。你准是漏看了一整版牛津街（Oxford Street）的廉价广告。我还瞧见咱们船东那匹马，在德比大赛（the Derby）里被人看好了。你没瞧见？我一向觉得奇怪，永远赚不着钱的船上，居然还养着赛马房。」

===Original===
“Get away. If that horse is like the *Harlow*, he’ll want some stoking to raise the knots out of him. But I know what you mean. I don’t like your talk. You’re too fond of showing notions by the arse-end.”

Jimmy went out of the hotel. The look of that room had lessened his specific gravity. Quite a hopeful hint in the air, that day, of Rip van Winkle. Perhaps he had not, like Rip, secured a very long advantage on the dear old home; he could not have left it so securely far behind. For his beard was about the same. It was not venerable. How much of the calendar had he dodged? Through a slip in the celestial cogs he might have been wangled into another year. What year was this? It was a buoyant thought, it ascended as a morning grace, and at least he could continue to enjoy it till he reached a newsboy and the truth. There was enough about him to justify brief enjoyment of the idea. Evidently Gallions was outside the world which used to have him. Its railway station clock was timed to a sparrow’s nest. If time is one of man’s devices, like fish-knives and drains, then alter the clock when its current hour is unsympathetic. Choose your own time, if the local duration feels untimely. To the devil with Greenwich, if it is out of your date. Settle on your own meridian, and stick to it. He paused to take in a noisy group of lascars.

The station was busy with a life that was foreign to him. This was a boundary where diversities melted, Hindus, white men, Chinese, negroes, as if Gallions served as a common denominator of men; turbans, woolly knobs, silk hats, and caps. No wonder the clock was abandoned to nesting-time. A rough and dusty enclosure at the back of a shed was cumbered with vans that were loaded with packages port-marked for coasts that were only names in London. Even the vans here had more faith than the far thought had a reality. What you had to do was to follow it up. Only custom and timidity prevent us from stepping over the last row of the cabbage plot.

===Chinese===
「去你的。那匹马要是同哈洛号一个德行，想从它身上逼出那几节速度来，可得好好给它添煤。不过你的意思我明白。你这种话我不爱听。你这家伙，就是太爱把高见从屁股那一头亮出来。」

吉米走出旅馆。那间屋子的光景减轻了他的比重。那一天，空气里颇有几分吉兆，透着点瑞普·凡·温克尔（Rip van Winkle）的意思。也许他还不曾像瑞普那样，从亲爱的老家身上稳稳赚下那么长一段便宜；他不可能把老家那样稳当、那样遥远地甩在身后。因为他的胡子同先前差不多，还当不起令人起敬。他躲掉了日历上多少日子？说不定天界的齿轮打了个滑，把他蒙混进了另一个年头。今年是哪一年？这念头轻飘飘的，像晨间的恩典一般冉冉上升，至少，在撞上卖报的孩子、撞上真相之前，他尽可以接着受用。他身上那点光景，也尽够把这念头先享用片刻。加利恩斯分明在从前那个装得下他的世界之外。此地火车站的钟，是对着麻雀窝校准的。倘若时间也不过是人的诸多发明之一，同鱼刀和下水道一路，那么钟上的时辰不合心意时，尽管拨它一拨。本地的光阴若不对路，就自择其时。格林尼治（Greenwich）若是出了你的日期，就让它见鬼去。定下你自己的子午线，咬定了不放。他停住脚，打量一群喧闹的拉斯卡尔水手。

车站里熙熙攘攘，忙着一种他陌生的生计。这里像一道边界，万般异质在此消融——印度教徒、白人、中国人、黑人，仿佛加利恩斯是各色人等的公分母；缠头巾、绒毛小圆头、大礼帽，还有便帽。怪不得那面钟被弃给了筑巢的时令。棚屋背后一片粗粝多尘的围场，塞满装货的篷车，货包上打着口岸的戳记，运往那些海岸——它们在伦敦只是几个名字。连此地的篷车，都比渺远的念头多着几分实在。你要做的，是把念头一追到底。唯有积习与怯懦，拦着人不肯跨过菜畦的最后一垄。

===Original===
A newsboy offered him a paper. Had he better take it? It was certain that paper did not belong to the day he hoped he had reached. Cowardly to step back. That boy was handing him a line of return to Billiter Avenue. But perhaps there was no choice. The boy was destiny right enough, though destiny ought to wipe its nose. He took the paper, opened it.

Nothing was in it. Not a name he knew, not a name which concerned him. No big type for such as he. Useless for the unenterprising to kill anybody. He looked at the date. He had lost a day. This was tomorrow morning. He would have to keep to his own time. The night before last was with the Kings of Memphis. He dropped the paper, returned to the hotel, and went into the coffee-room with a spurious confidence which was almost complete that he knew where he was, and when. Jimmy took a seat beside the master of the *Altair*.

===Chinese===
一个报童递给他一份报纸。接不接为好？可以肯定，那报纸并不属于他盼望自己已抵达的这一天。退回去是怯懦。那孩子递来的，是一条把他拽回比利特大道的缆绳。不过兴许别无选择。这孩子分明就是命运，虽说命运也该擦一擦自己的鼻涕。他接过报纸，翻开。

报上什么也没有。没有一个他认得的名字，没有一个与他相干的名字。没有为他这号人预备的大字标题。没出息的人杀了谁也是白杀。他看了看日期。他丢了一天。这已是明天早晨。他只好守住自己的时间了。前天夜里，他还同孟菲斯的列王在一起。他丢下报纸，回到旅馆，走进咖啡室，怀着一种差不多完满的、冒牌的笃定，仿佛他知道自己身在何处、又是何时。吉米在牵牛星号的船长身旁坐了下来。

===Original===
At that moment, on the other side of the captain, the diffident doctor was contemplating the master furtively, for the doctor wished to speak to him, and this bearded stranger who was just sitting down had changed the atmosphere a trifle, and he had not yet spoken to the captain. The *Altair* was to make an interesting voyage. The doctor sighed. It was years since he himself coasted in the China Sea. Out there were the coasts of youth. Probably he would never sit again in the verandah of that place he knew in Singapore, and watch the various and unaccountable East go by, at sunset. Never smell tropical overgrowth again. He would like another chance to visit the ruins of Angkor. The *Altair*’s captain was staring absently across the table to the window light, which was broad from the river. That light gave him away. The elderly and experienced doctor wondered, for a moment, while he judged his neighbour, what the merchant service was coming to, when a man like that could have command of a ship. A negative figure; thin hair, an insignificant mouth and nose; even his moustache was trifling. A lot of interest Bangkok, or the ruins of a forgotten civilisation, would be to him. No character. The doctor had long ago decided that England was decadent, for an unassailable reason; he had found it impossible to get an appointment ashore better than the quackery of humouring the willing victims of bad habits and unoccupied minds, and as a ship’s surgeon he was sent on uninteresting routes.

===Chinese===
正在此时，船长的另一侧，那位讷于言辞的医生正偷眼打量着船长，因为他有话想同他说；这位刚刚落座的大胡子陌生人略微改动了席间的空气，而他还没有对船长开过口。牵牛星号将要做一次有意思的航行。医生叹了口气。他自己在中国海（China Sea）沿岸跑船，已是多年前的旧事了。那边是青春的海岸。他多半再也坐不回新加坡（Singapore）那处他熟识的游廊，再也看不见日暮时分那千姿百态、无从究诘的东方打眼前流过了。再也闻不见热带草木疯长的气息了。吴哥（Angkor）的古迹，他倒还想再讨一个机会去走走。牵牛星号的船长正失神地隔着桌子望向窗光，河上来的光线正宽宽地铺进屋来。那光线出卖了他。这位年长而阅历深的医生一面品评着邻座，一时暗暗纳闷：商船界究竟成了什么光景，这样的人竟然也执掌得了一船。一副毫不出挑的形象；头发稀薄，一张嘴、一个鼻子都无足轻重；连唇上的胡子也无精打采。曼谷（Bangkok）也好，湮灭的文明的废墟也好，于他能算得一桩多大的兴味。毫无性情。医生早就断定英格兰在衰败了，理由无可辩驳——他发现在岸上谋不到一份胜过庸医营生的差事，那营生不过是哄着恶习缠身、脑子空虚的自愿受害者混日子；而当船医呢，又被派去跑些无趣的航线。

===Original===
“You know Bangkok, Cambodia, those places, sir?” he asked.

The skipper started nervously. “Eh? No, well, I haven’t been that way since I was a junior.”

“An interesting coast.”

“Yes? You know it? Any coast has to be that, though, when one is there.”

===Chinese===
「您熟悉曼谷、柬埔寨（Cambodia）那些地方吗，先生？」他问道。

船长不安地一怔。「呃？不，这个，打从我还是低级驾驶员那阵起，就没再走过那条线了。」

「那片海岸很有意思。」

「是吗？您到过？不过随便哪片海岸，人一到了那儿，也就非有意思不可了。」

===Original===
Captain Bennett laughed rudely. “Interesting! That’s it. That’s the way my surgeon talks. You ought to sail with him.” He shook a rebuking fork at the doctor in pride. “I tell you he’s even interested in the cockroaches. Keeps ’em in bottles. He’d measure the head of any bumboatman who came alongside. The interest of a coast is to keep off it. It’s a fine coast when you’re clear of it.”

“It’s only a point of view, captain.”

“Point of view! Five fathoms, and a draught of twenty-six feet. There’s a point of view. You always talk as if a ship were a peepshow or Noah’s Ark. You ought to know by now it’s more like a pawnshop owned by a Welshman. No Cardiff man here? Every damned rivet is tallied. Doctor, you are too late. You should have signed articles with Noah.”

“Well, captain, don’t you think Noah would be more interested in your ship than you would be in his old ark?”

===Chinese===
贝内特船长粗声笑了起来。「有意思！对喽。我们船医说话就是这个腔调。你真该同他跑一趟船。」他得意地朝医生摇着一柄叉子，算是申斥。「我告诉你，他连蟑螂都感兴趣，一只一只养在瓶子里。靠舷兜售的小贩船主，他个个都要量量人家的脑袋。海岸的妙处，就在于离它远远的。等你把它甩在身后，那才是一片好海岸。」

「这不过是各人看法不同罢了，船长。」

「看法！水深五英寻，吃水二十六英尺，这才叫看法。你说话总好像一条船是拉洋片的西洋镜，或是挪亚方舟（Noah's Ark）。到如今你该明白了，它更像威尔士人开的一家当铺。这儿没有加的夫（Cardiff）人吧？每一颗该死的铆钉都记着账呢。大夫，你来得太晚了。你早该同挪亚（Noah）签下船员合同。」

「哦，船长，您不觉得吗——挪亚对您这条船的兴趣，怕要胜过您对他那条老方舟的兴趣呢？」

===Original===
Captain Bennett was entangled for a moment. He frowned at the doctor while getting this notion free. Jimmy took a look at him. A rosy but truculent old dog. This was one of his favourite pastimes, to quarrel in play. The sly doctor enjoyed pulling his leg. Bennett grunted.

“That ark, dirtier than a cattle-ship, what with monkeys and elephants. Didn’t her old man have to beat about because the only port was under water? Weather as thick as hell. All the same, no trouble with soundings. Yes, doctor, I guess old Noah would have been glad of a gin and bitters on the *Harlow*. But you knew all right what I meant. Our world isn’t new, but Noah’s was the first voyage, wasn’t it? You’d have seen everything for the first time with him.”

The doctor was offensively quiet and kind. “Do you think we ever see anything at all? There’s nothing but names in the world, captain. Most of the names are old. They hide the things. We look at the names and see nothing.”

“Now what’s he getting at? That’s the way he goes on, quietly pushing the soup off the table to start a nice little conversation with me. Him and our engineer. If you could hear the pair of them at it, you’d think the earth was only a fog, as near as I can make out. Not enough solid rock in it to scrape the heads off wet matches.”

===Chinese===
贝内特一时被这话缠住了。他皱着眉瞪住医生，一面把这个念头解开来。吉米打量了他一眼。一条面色红润却好斗的老狗。斗嘴取乐，正是他心爱的消遣之一。狡黠的医生也乐于撩他。贝内特哼了一声。

「那条方舟哇，比运牲口的船还脏，又是猴子又是大象的。她那位老船长可不就只得抢风折航地折腾，因为唯一的口岸淹在水底下？天气又浓得同地狱一样。话虽如此，测深倒是半点不费事。是啊大夫，我想老挪亚在哈洛号上讨一杯金酒加苦精喝，一定求之不得。不过我的意思你心里有数。咱们这世界不算新鲜，可挪亚那回是开天辟地头一遭航行，对不对？跟着他，样样东西你都是头一回见识。」

医生那副安静温良，安静得叫人窝火。「您以为我们当真看见过什么吗？这世上除去名字什么也没有，船长。名字多半都旧了。名字把东西遮住了。我们望着名字，什么也没看见。」

「瞧他又要扯到哪儿去了？他就是这样没个完，不声不响把汤推开，好跟我起一场漂亮的小小清谈。还有我们的轮机长，同他一个鼻孔出气。你要是听得见他们俩凑在一块的那一套，照我琢磨，你准会觉得这地球不过是团雾。里头实心的石头不够多，连湿火柴的头都蹭不下来。」

===Original===
“Oh, come…”

“I say yes. All very well for a doctor to talk like that, when his job’s just guesswork, but it beats me to hear an engineer doing it.”

“Playing with words, doctor?” suggested the *Altair*’s master; “taking soundings with words, and finding no bottom?”

Hullo, thought the doctor, more in this chap than I supposed. He felt more at home.

===Chinese===
「哦，得了……」

「我说的没错。医生这么讲还情有可原，他那行当本就是瞎猜；可连轮机长也来这一套，我就想不通了。」

「拿词语做游戏呢，大夫？」牵牛星号的船长提了一句，「拿词语测深，却探不着底？」

嚯，医生心想，这家伙肚里的货色比我料想的多。他随即自在多了。

===Original===
“It can be a very dangerous game,” he said. “Find the right set of words, and you can make almost anything with them, a steam-engine, God, a war, or a pleasant little suggestion programme than he did of one of God’s own creatures. Three ladies in particular knew that he was a villain who did not believe they had seen a man fall into the sea.”

“More like a ghost story than anything else,” grumbled Captain Bennett.

“It is a ghost story,” answered the doctor.

“But,” asked Jimmy, “something must have been seen by those ladies?”

The doctor admitted it. “Yes, sir, I daresay. People do see things, then give names to them.”

===Chinese===
「这游戏可以玩得非常危险。」他说。「找对了一组词，你几乎拿它们什么都造得出来：一台蒸汽机、上帝、一场战争，或者一档小小的、叫人受用的暗示节目——比他拿上帝的一件造物摆弄出来的还要受用。尤其有三位女士认定他是个恶棍：她们分明看见一个人落进海里，他却硬是不肯相信。」

「怎么听怎么像个鬼故事。」贝内特船长嘟哝道。

「它本来就是鬼故事。」医生答道。

「可是，」吉米问道，「那几位女士总归是看见什么东西了吧？」

医生认下了这一点。「是的，先生，多半是吧。人确实会看见些东西，然后再给它们安上名字。」

===Original===
After they had solemnly considered the prospect of a world of intangibilities in which names and portents permuted in a dumb and dizzy flux, names and meanings differing for all who were looking on, Bennett spoke.

“Right you are, I give it to you, doctor. I won’t argue. But thank God one thing with a name is all right—money. You can’t deny that. You just let me have that, and you can keep the rest of the words, or what you call ’em. The only voyage I hanker after more than the usual charter is the one in the books, one of those treasure island hurroos. I’d sign on for that like a cabin boy with his first bolster.

“But if treasure is not the same as money…” the surgeon began.

“None of that, now. Of course it is. No good trying to pretend I don’t know the ace of trumps when there’s a name to it. Doctor, you can count money, which is treasure, and what more do you want?”

===Chinese===
众人郑重其事地悬想了一阵那样一幅前景——一个无形之物的世界，名字与兆象在喑哑而眩晕的流变中互相易位，名字与含义在每一个旁观者眼里各不相同——随后贝内特开了口。

「好好好，算你有理，大夫。我不争了。可是感谢上帝，有一样有名字的东西是实打实的——钱。这个你否认不了。你把这个留给我，其余那些词儿，随你们管它们叫什么，都归你。比寻常的租船合同更叫我心里痒痒的航程，只有书里写的那种，金银岛式的狂欢。那种航程，我会像头一回领到软枕的小舱童那样，欢天喜地签约上船。

「可要是财宝同钱不是一回事呢……」船医开了腔。

「打住，别来这套。当然是一回事。那东西既然有名字，想装作我不认得这张王牌，可没有用。大夫，钱数得清，钱就是财宝，你还想怎么样？」

===Original===
“We’ll call it a go, captain. But I hope I’m with you when you count treasure and stow it. I’d like to see it done. But a chart for hidden bullion! That’s enough to poison any desert island! I thought that was only a yarn for pirates and boys.”

The surgeon rose. Jimmy looked round at him. A short fellow with a big bald head. A grey moustache. A sad but quizzical face. The surgeon paused with his hands on the back of his chair. He appeared to find inspiration in the seat of it. “I’ve never heard of a chart for the things I want. I don’t believe I can have it, unless I make it myself, and then the next man couldn’t read it. If he did, he’d want something else. Morning, captain, I’ll see you at the shipping office presently. Sign on at two-thirty, don’t we?”

The master of the *Altair* was listening, and smiling ironically, while idly balancing his spoon on the edge of his cup. Then he, too, left the table, but without a word.

===Chinese===
「就算你赢了吧，船长。不过你清点财宝、把它们装舱的时候，但愿我在场。我倒想开开眼。可是藏着金条的海图！那玩意儿够把随便哪座荒岛都毒坏了！我还以为那不过是讲给海盗和男孩子听的故事呢。」

船医起身。吉米回头打量他。矮个子，顶着一颗光秃秃的大脑袋。一撇灰白的小胡子。一张悲戚却带着打趣神气的脸。船医双手搭在椅背上顿了一顿，仿佛从那椅座上得了灵感。「我要的那些东西，从没听说有什么海图。我看我是弄不到的，除非自己画一张，可那样一来，下一个人又读不懂了。就算读懂了，他要的又是别的东西了。早安，船长，回头航运事务所见。咱们两点半签约上船，是吧？」

牵牛星号的船长一直听着，讥诮地微笑，一面懒懒地把茶匙竖在杯沿上取平衡。随后他也离了席，却一言不发。

===Original===
Colet soon followed. A clear decision had come to him to return to the office. There was no hurry, but he was going. Perhaps Perriam wasn’t dead. And if he were, that was an inquisition he could face. Just see what would happen. Bound to be interesting. He went up to his bedroom. That surgeon was a good doctor. There was no name to him, but he was one of the fraternity. How surprised he would be if he knew that his demeanour and chance words had prompted a decision in a stranger about a quite irrelevant matter. Colet was passing an open door and glanced in. The *Altair*’s master was there, considering, with his hands in his pockets, a large example of Kuan-yin. Jimmy was brought up. “Hullo,” he said to himself, and then would have gone on. But the man inside saw him. “That took my eye,” Colet apologised. “It’s a beauty. May I look?”

===Chinese===
科利特不久也跟了出去。一个清清楚楚的决定在他心里落了地：回公司去。并不急，可他就是要去。也许佩里亚姆还没有死。就算死了，那一场盘问他也担当得起。且看会有什么事发生。必定有意思。他上楼回自己的卧室去。那位船医是个好医生。名字没留下，可他是那一门里的人。他若是知道，自己的做派和随口的几句话，竟促成一个陌生人就一件毫不相干的事拿定了主意，该有多么惊讶。科利特路过一扇敞开的门，朝里瞥了一眼。牵牛星号的船长正在里面，双手插在口袋里，端详着一尊高大的观音。吉米收住了脚。「嚯。」他自语了一声，随即本想接着走。可屋里的人看见了他。「那尊像把我的眼睛勾住了。」科利特赔了个不是。「真是件尤物。可以让我看看吗？」

===Original===
“Come in, come in. It is a good one?”

“I like it. I’ve never seen a better one. It’s a beautiful figure. But only one or two men know this stuff.”

“Are you interested in it?”

“Yes, but I don’t know anything about it. It’s different from Staffordshire ware, that’s all.”

The stranger shyly confessed that, when in London, he himself had paid furtive visits to the British Museum, he did not quite know why, to look at Chinese bowls. “There’s something about them,” he ventured.

“There is. They’re the same as some music, I suppose. There’s no reason in it, but it means something.”

===Chinese===
「进来，进来。您说，这尊好吗？」

「我喜欢它。比这更好的我还没见过。真是件美的造像。不过懂得这路东西的，世间一两人而已。」

「您对这有兴趣？」

「有，不过我对它一窍不通。只知道它同斯塔福德郡陶器（Staffordshire ware）不是一路货色，就这么点。」

那位陌生人腼腆地承认，自己在伦敦时，也偷偷去过几回大英博物馆，他自己也说不清为什么，就为看看中国的瓷碗。「它们有点什么名堂。」他大着胆子说。

「是有点什么名堂。我想它们同某些音乐是一路的。里头讲不出道理，可它诉说着什么。」

===Original===
They approved each other, and showed it. They had confessed a common frailty. Colet handled the figure, while they speculated over her quality, and the nature of her attraction.

“We had better not look for reason in it,” said the stranger. “For instance, just now she’s a bit of a nuisance. I’m leaving here, but now I can’t go to my ship till this afternoon. I’ve been called by phone to town. And there it is. I can’t lug her about. I don’t like trusting rough hands with her, but I shall have to risk it.”

Jimmy was jolted by a thoughtless impulse. He might as well finish with a useless friendly act. Was it the word to say? “Where is your ship? Could I take it along? It would be safe with me. It’s all one what time I get to the city today.”

The sailor gave Jimmy a direct glance. Then he pouted at Kuan-yin. “That’s very good of you. But it’s too much to expect of a stranger.”

===Chinese===
两人彼此看得中意，并且显露了出来。他们已经相互招认了一桩共同的癖好。科利特把那造像拿在手里，两人一面推敲她的品第，一面推敲她那份魅力的根底。

「这上头咱们最好别找道理。」陌生人说。「比方说眼下，她就有点碍事了。我正要离开这儿，可这下得等到下午才能回船。刚来了电话，把我叫进城去。事情就这么摆着。我没法拖着她到处跑。把她托给粗手粗脚的人我不乐意，可也只能冒这个险了。」

吉米被一阵没过脑子的冲动撞了一下。不妨用一桩无用的善举来收个场。这话当讲吗？「您的船在哪儿？我能捎它同去吗？搁我这儿错不了。反正我今天几时进城都一样。」

水手直直地看了吉米一眼，又朝观音噘了噘嘴。「您真是太好心了。可这怎么好劳驾一位素不相识的人。」

===Original===
“Not a stranger,” said, Jimmy. “I mean, I know her. I’ve got one—I had one—rather like it. No trouble to see that good thing is safe. If it would help of course. Is your ship far from here?”

“I should be grateful, sir, I must say. The steamer *Altair*. But could you really do it? I got the thing only three days ago, in a Limehouse pawnshop. I couldn’t resist it. Now I’m suddenly shifted to this ship, and I shan’t see home yet awhile.”

Colet protested the simplicity of the task, if he could be trusted with it. His hurry was not great.

“It’s very good of you. And I can’t make any return. Sure you’ve the time for it? My ship is at Woolwich buoys. There will be a launch at the dockhead in half an hour. That would put you aboard, if you care to go. Could you wait aboard for me? If you would tell Mr. Sinclair, the chief officer, that you are a friend of mine, and that I shall be aboard pretty soon after lunch—! My name is Hale.”

===Chinese===
「不是素不相识。」吉米说。「我是说，我认得她。我也有一尊——曾经有过一尊——同这尊挺相像。看管好这么一件好东西，不费什么事。当然，要是帮得上忙的话。您的船离这儿远吗？」

「那我真要感激不尽了，先生，实在话。汽船牵牛星号。可您果真能办到吗？这东西我到手才三天，在莱姆豪斯（Limehouse）一家当铺里。我实在抵抗不了它。如今又突然把我调上这条船，一时半会儿还见不着家呢。」

科利特连声申说这差事再简单不过，只要肯放心交给他。他也没有多少要紧事要赶。

「您真是太好了。我也无以为报。您真有这工夫吗？我的船泊在伍利奇（Woolwich）的浮筒。半小时后码头头上有一班交通艇。您若愿意，它就能送您上船。您能替我在船上等一等吗？麻烦您告诉大副辛克莱（Sinclair）先生，说您是我的朋友，说我午后不久便回船——！我姓黑尔（Hale）。」

## VIII / 八

===Original===
Mr. Sinclair, the chief officer of the *Altair*, was on the navigating bridge, with the boatswain and a few men, and his voice was raised above the importance of the job, which was but adjusting the weather-cloth. He moved about abruptly in dispraise. Something, the boatswain thought, had stung him that morning. His foxy hair was so boisterous that it strongly resented the imposition of his cap. There was reproach in his eye, and his darting energy was but the whooping of his exasperation. He had expected to get this ship, for he had earned the post; he had brought her home. But another master was coming to take charge. If there hadn’t been a new baby this voyage, worse luck, they could have found another mate for her as well. “Bo’sun, why the hell!﻿   …” The boatswain indicated with a warning nod that a boat was at the gangway. Sinclair shot his head over the bridge-end, saw a man of his own age in a launch, nursing a package, and who was conning the ship with a bright appraising eye. Here was the old man, already looking for faults. Let him find ’em. Let the whole chromatic directorate run their indecorous noses over her. She was better than they had deserved, blast them. He flung down to the head of the ship’s ladder, prepared for any complaint, and hoping he would get it. He faced the newcomer with a look as direct and doubting as that of a challenging bulldog. “Well, what fault have you found so far?” But the thought was not spoken.
