“The old man? Yes. Well, this ship’s got a master. Colet, the old man’s all right, if anyone wants to know.”

“How is it now?”

“Don’t ask. She’s taking it easier, though. Sitting in it like a duck. Shouldn’t have thought it possible, but the old man was sure she would. Been rolling her bridge-ends under, too. Glad to see daylight.”

===Chinese===
辛克莱尔此刻正带着孩子气的惊奇，端详手上的一道伤口。「这是打哪儿来的？」他把手撇开了。

「老头子？是啊。哼，这条船到底有了一位当家的。科利特，老头子没事——谁若想知道的话。」

「眼下情形怎样？」

「别问。不过她挨得轻省些了。像只鸭子稳稳浮坐水里。我本想不到竟有这可能，可老头子拿准了她挺得住。连驾驶台两端都摇进水里去过。见着天亮，高兴。」

===Original===
Sinclair shut his eyes.

“I say, I think I’ll take my ten minutes here. Handy, here.”

He sought Colet again, with a grin.

“Nice job for this child presently, when it moderates. Coming with us? The rudder’s broken at the couplings. You know that? Must get her under control. There’s a boat to be got out. Wires from the steering chains to the rudder. That sort of thing. Fix it up. You’d better make for the bridge-house now, the old man says. Tell him where I am, won’t you?”

===Chinese===
辛克莱尔合上了眼。

「我说，我想就在这儿歇上十分钟。这儿便当。」

他又咧嘴一笑，望向科利特。

「等风浪一缓，这孩子马上就有好差事了。跟我们一起去么？舵在联结节那里断了。这你知道吧？总得让她重新听人使唤。得放出一条艇去。从操舵链到舵，接上钢缆。就那一类活计。把它弄妥帖。老头子说，你这会儿顶好上驾驶台去。告诉他我在哪儿，好么？」

===Original===
The chief officer’s head fell sideways; this time he was certainly asleep.

The purser adventured for the bridge-house. If you checked yourself from point to point, hand over hand, you were not hurled along. The wind was solid. The purser did not look at the seas, but some heights caught his eye as they fled past. They were aged. It was better not to look at them. Hale turned his head as Colet entered. He grimaced at him humorously. Was the old man enjoying it? A seaman was still at the useless wheel, as if apart, and in a trance, looking to futurity through the glass, and waiting. His jaws moved slightly, now and then, but that was all. Nobody else was there.

“Did you sleep, Colet? No sleep till morn, eh? Well, I think it moderates a bit. We shall be busy, soon. I want you with me in case. The lads are taking a rest now.”

===Chinese===
大副的头歪向一侧；这一回他是确确实实睡着了。

事务长冒险往驾驶台去。只要你一个把手一个把手地把自己扣牢，一手换一手地挪，就不会被整个抡出去。风是实体。事务长不去看那些海，可有几堵高墙掠过时，还是攫住了他的眼。它们苍老。还是不看为妙。科利特进去时，黑尔回过头来，朝他诙谐地做了个鬼脸。老头子竟还乐在其中么？一个水手仍守在那没用的舵轮前，仿佛置身事外，如在出神，透过玻璃眺望着将来，等着。他的下颌时不时微微嚅动，仅此而已。那里再没有别人。

「你睡过没有，科利特？没合眼熬到天亮，是吧？唔，我看风浪缓一点了。咱们很快就要忙起来。我要你守在我身边，以防万一。小伙子们这会儿正歇着。」

===Original===
The master resumed his vigil. His head was turned to the world beyond. But that world had contracted. One could not see far, but the intimidated eye could see all that it wanted. The sky had closed down on them, and they were circumscribed by a sunless incertitude. In that grey vacancy shadows appeared which were too high to be of water, but those ghosts darkened and emerged as seas which saw the ship at once and came at her in towering velocity. As they shaped, each of them threatened that it was the one which would finish her, but the *Altair* heaved into the sky out of it in time. Yet another was always coming when one had gone. The desolate head of the ship, condemned to dreary unrest, streamed with gauzes of water. Its stanchions and rails were awry and tortured. A length of the bulwarks of the foredeck was ruptured and projected outwards raggedly. The deck ventilators had gone. One lay in the scuppers as if dead, but sometimes turned over and then back in a spasm of unexpected life.

That slight figure of the master, standing in profile, so watchful of vague and immense powers, and so undisturbed by their onrush, was like a token of quiet faith untouched by calamity and overthrow. There it was. He was looking it in the face. He did not move. He was timing the Indian Ocean, that little man. Colet felt he would as soon be there as elsewhere. Who did Hale remind him of as he stood like that? Somebody. Somebody in a dream? No, good Lord, he was like that dim figure of an old man when coming up Gallions Reach in an evening long ago; standing just like that, and muttering something of other worlds. Beyond the captain Colet saw a dire spectre loom and bear down on them, an immense sea, its pale head raving with speed and fury. Other worlds! This one? Colet could not help swallowing on a choking laugh. At that Hale turned on him quickly, and knew what the purser had seen.

“Laughing at it? They do look fairly terrifying, some of them.”

===Chinese===
船长恢复了守望。他的头转向外面的世界。可那个世界已经收缩了。人望不远，可那只受了惊的眼睛，想看的全都看得见。天穹压拢下来，他们被圈在一片不见太阳的无定里。在那灰色的空茫中，有暗影现出，高得不像水物；可那些鬼影暗下来、涌出来，成了看得见船的海，立刻以冲天的疾速朝她扑来。它们每涌起成形，个个都扬言自己就是结果她的那一面；可『牵牛星号』每次都及时从它怀里腾上了天。然而一面去了，总还有一面又来。船那颗荒凉的头颅，被判了沉闷不宁的罪，披挂着一层又一层轻纱似的水。它的立柱与栏杆歪扭不堪，受尽酷刑。前甲板有一段舷墙破裂了，参差地朝外翻出。甲板通风筒都不见了。一只躺在排水沟里，像是死了，可偶尔翻个身，又翻回去，来一阵出乎意料的生命痉挛。

船长那瘦小的身影侧身立着，对那些渺茫而浩大的权能如此戒备，对它们的猛扑又如此不为所动，像一枚静谧信实的表记，未受灾殃与倾覆的沾染。它就在那里。他正与之直面相对。他一动不动。他在掐算印度洋的时脉，那个小个子。科利特觉得，待在这里和待在别处，他都无所谓。黑尔那样站着，让科利特想起了谁？某个人。梦里见过的某个人？不，天哪，他像许久以前某个黄昏驶上加利恩斯河段时，那个朦胧的老人身影；恰恰就是那样站着，喃喃念着别的世界的什么。船长身外，科利特看见一个狰狞的鬼影耸起，朝他们压下来——一面巨海，苍白的头颅挟着疾速与狂怒咆哮而至。别的世界！就这一个？科利特忍不住就着一口呛住的笑咽了口唾沫。黑尔立刻猛地回头，知道事务长看见了什么。

「在笑话它？有几面浪，模样确实相当吓人。」

===Original===
The two men had made that signal to each other. Colet lightened, flippantly elate. Chanticleer crowing at spectres, sure of the morning? The steward pranced in with a can of hot coffee and some food. Wonderful fellow. How had he managed all that from the galley? He had saved the grub, but he was like a cat out of a ditch. Coffee was good. Calamity could not even blow off the fragrance of coffee. Stewards could dance through it, carrying coffee.

The three men in that bridge-house had no more to say to each other. They stumbled, made abrupt movements to steady themselves, kept their eyes on the world without. Hour after hour of onset and uplift; screens of white which keened and hid the ship, and passed; vaulting masses which strode over the bows; occasional gusts which careened her, held her over, and hid the sea with wool.

===Chinese===
两人之间打过了这个暗号。科利特轻快起来，轻狂地得意。对着鬼影打鸣、笃信早晨必至的尚提克利尔（Chanticleer）？服务员昂首阔步进来了，端着一罐热咖啡和些许吃食。了不起的家伙。他是怎么从厨房里张罗出这一切的？他把吃的抢救出来了，可他自己活像一只从水沟里捞上来的猫。咖啡是好东西。大祸临头，连咖啡的香气也吹不散。服务员能踏着灾难跳舞，手里端着咖啡。

驾驶台里那三个人，彼此再没有话说。他们踉跄着，冷不丁伸手撑稳自己，眼睛盯着外面的世界。一个钟头又一个钟头的猛扑与腾举；一道道惨白的水幕呼啸着掩住船，又过去了；一垛垛腾跃的巨体跨过船首；阵阵骤风把她压侧，按住不放，拿羊毛似的漫天水沫把海遮没。

===Original===
But it was lessening? The worse of it seemed to have gone. One could see farther. The ceiling had lifted. Hale was lighting his pipe when the engine-room signalled. The master listened at the black cup of the speaking-tube, and then put his mouth to it. His mouth and ear dodged about.

“No﻿ ﻿… not yet﻿ ﻿… we shall try soon﻿ ﻿… let you know﻿ ﻿… thank your men for me, Gillespie﻿ ﻿… not over yet, though﻿ ﻿… who?﻿ ﻿… badly?﻿ ﻿… where is he?﻿ ﻿… all right.﻿ ﻿… I’ll do what I can﻿ ﻿… let you know.

“One of the greasers, Colet. Flung into the gear. Very nasty. He must stay there. We dare not bring him up yet.”

He walked up and down the enclosure for a spell. He paused, and contemplated the outer world.

===Chinese===
可是风浪在减退？最凶险的一段像是过去了。人能望得远了。天顶抬高了。黑尔正点烟斗，机舱来了信号。船长把耳朵贴上传话管黑色的听口，随后又把嘴凑上去。他的嘴和耳朵躲闪腾挪。

「不……现在还不行……我们很快就试……到时候告诉你……替我谢谢你的伙计们，吉莱斯皮……不过还没完……谁？……伤得重？……他在哪儿？……好。……我尽力……回头告诉你。」

「一个机舱加油工，科利特。被甩进机器里去了。伤得很糟。他只好待在那儿。我们还不敢把他弄上来。」

他在那圈围栏里来回走了一阵。他停下，凝望外面的世界。

===Original===
“We might,” he said to himself. “Colet, would you tell Sinclair I want him?”

A bunch of the people of the ship presently dared the open. They appeared together in haste, as if they had suddenly agreed to submit no longer. They had come out to rebel. But how could they fight that? They were seen at once and attacked. They were expunged. When the cataract had gone they were still all there, hurried and desperate. They were minikin in a precarious wild, in a light as hopeless as nullity, their foothold a bare raft of disappearing iron. They could not do it. But they were hauling gear about, refractory stuff in league with the enemy, in an obsession that wires would bind cyclones. Sinclair was there. It was a losing game but he was playing it. His arm was flung out directing; he toppled over, but his arm continued pointing at the crisis from the deck﻿—no time to get up﻿—and his mouth was open, bawling, though he seemed to make no sound. It was a shadow show. A play of midgets who endeavoured with foolish efforts to frustrate the fate imposed on them by majesty. Well done, Sinclair. Nearly had you then.

They made for a boat as if they meant to use it. That ought to be stopped. That was carrying matters too far. They could not leave the ship. But the boat was manned and swung out﻿—Sinclair, the third officer, the boatswain, and a seaman, were leaving them. They were determined about it. They were hanging outboard now, uncertain in a threatening void; their adversary was waiting for them below. Sometimes it swept along the topside of the ship, and then retired, for its next leap, to a sudden deep below the ship’s wall. For a torturing moment the suspensory boat fell straight to disaster. It checked. It shot up on a roller, afloat. Colet was looking in astonishment straight at the faces of its men. The snoring ship heeled away from them. The boat dropped into a hollow. She sped to a crest higher than the funnel, and was poised for a moment on the foaming summit; she was lost. The seas opened, and she was seen close astern, askew on a slope.

===Chinese===
「倒也未尝不可。」他自言自语。「科利特，你去告诉辛克莱尔，说我找他，行么？」

一伙船上的人转眼便壮起胆子出了露天。他们行色匆匆地一齐露面，仿佛突然商量定了，再不肯屈服。他们是出来造反的。可他们怎么斗得过那个？他们立刻就被发现、被攻击。他们被抹去了。等那道洪流过去，他们竟还全都原地都在，仓皇而决绝。他们是险恶荒野里的小不点儿，在一片像虚无本身一样无望的光里，脚下是一块正在消隐的铁的薄筏。他们办不成。可他们还在拖拽索具——那是不服管束、同敌手串通一气的家伙——一味执念着钢缆能捆住气旋。辛克莱尔在那里。这是一场必输的局，可他在打。他一条手臂挥出去指挥；他翻倒了，可那条手臂还从甲板上指着那处危局——没工夫爬起来——他嘴张着，在呐喊，尽管像是发不出一点声音。那是皮影戏。一出侏儒的戏，侏儒们以愚蠢的努力，想挫败威严强加给他们的命运。干得好，辛克莱尔。方才差一点儿就叫它得手了。

他们走向一条艇，像是要动真格用它。那该被拦住。那就太过分了。他们不能离船。可艇已上了人、摇了出去——辛克莱尔、三副、水手长，还有一名水手，正在离开他们。他们是铁了心的。这会儿他们悬在舷外，在一片咄咄逼人的虚空里悬而未决；他们的敌手正在下面候着。那东西时而沿着船舷扫过一遭，随即退下去，到船壁之下一处骤然出现的深处，蓄势作下一扑。有一刻折磨人的光景，那悬着的艇径直朝灭顶之灾坠去。它顿住了。它被一道涌浪送起，浮在水面。科利特正惊愕地直望着艇里那几张脸。打着鼾的船从他们身边侧开去。艇落进一道波谷。她窜上一面比烟囱还高的浪峰，在飞沫的峰顶悬停了片刻；她不见了。海面豁开，她又出现在紧挨船尾的地方，歪斜地挂在一面坡上。

===Original===
When Colet next saw Sinclair that officer was climbing the ladder amidships, as happy and unexpected a token as an angelic visitor. The purser put his arm round his friend’s shoulder.

“All right. Done it, Colet.”

She was steering again.

“See that out there?” Sinclair thrust out his arm.

Something was to be seen in the murk. What was it? Smoke?

“Yes. It’s a liner. She’ll be passing us in an hour.”

===Chinese===
等科利特再见到辛克莱尔，那位驾驶员正在爬船腰的梯子，像一个天使来访般可喜而出乎意料的兆头。事务长伸手搂住朋友的肩膀。

「成了。弄妥了，科利特。」

她又能转舵了。

「瞧见外头那个没有？」辛克莱尔把手臂伸出去。

昏冥中有个什么东西看得见。那是什么？烟么？

「是的。是条班轮。一个钟头后她就要从我们旁边过去了。」

===Original===
Colet went to his room in gratitude. He felt as though the sky had gone up to its friendly height. They were let off. They were getting out of it. The violence meant nothing now. His cabin seemed larger, more intimate. He was comforted by even the society of distant but brotherly smoke. They were not alone, either. He was thinking he would go out presently to watch their neighbour pass them when Sinclair entered, and something about Sinclair made him rise as though this were an important meeting.

“The liner’s almost abeam. Go up with the old man, Colet. He’s going to signal her. There’s something not right﻿—Gillespie says the furnace footplates are awash. That and our steering-gear together﻿ ﻿… he wants a tow, I expect. I’m going below now to find what I can.”

===Chinese===
科利特怀着感激回自己房里去。他觉得天空仿佛升回了它那和善的高度。他们被赦免了。他们正在脱身。那场狂暴如今什么也算不得了。他的舱室似乎大了些，更可亲了些。连那一缕遥远而手足般的烟的陪伴，也给他安慰。他们也不孤单。他正想着，过一会儿要出去看他们的邻船打旁边经过，辛克莱尔进来了，而他身上那点什么东西，叫科利特站起身，仿佛这是一次要紧的会面。

「班轮差不多正横了。陪老头子上去，科利特。他要打信号给她。有点不对劲——吉莱斯皮说，锅炉前的踏板都漫水了。这毛病，加上咱们的操舵装置……我看他是想要拖带了。我这就下去，看看能做点什么。」

===Original===
There was light enough to see the signals of the stranger. It was impossible for Colet to make out what was passing between the two ships. The tumbling of their wounded steamer was accentuated, it was sickening, when things were happening, and you did not know what.

“Did you make out her last signal, Lycett?” asked the captain.

“Yes, sir, distinctly. He says he cannot tow. Got the mails. He asks whether you will abandon.”

“No. Signal, no.”


===Chinese===
光已够亮，看得见那陌生船的信号了。两船之间来回传递了些什么，科利特却辨认不出。他们这条负伤的汽船翻滚得愈发起劲，令人作呕——当事态正在演变、而你不知道演变的是什么的时候，尤其如此。

「她最后一个信号，你辨出来没有，莱塞特（Lycett）？」船长问。

「辨出来了，先生，一清二楚。他说他不能拖带。载着邮件呢。他问您要不要弃船。」

「不。发信号：不。」

## XVIII / 十八

===Original===

Young Lycett faintly hesitated over that signal; or so Colet imagined. Never before had he watched his fellows so closely. But the lad turned to the rack where rolled flags waited in their pigeonholes to cue any fate that might come, and went out to his duty. There came another smear of bunting for them on the liner﻿—too late; the light was not good enough. But a star began to wink at her bridge, and they all watched it till it ceased. Lycett returned to them.

“He says he will report us, sir.”

The master nodded. “Of course.”

===Chinese===
年轻的莱塞特打那个信号时，隐隐迟疑了一下；或者说，科利特想象如此。他从不曾把自己的同类看得这么仔细。可那小伙子还是转向了旗架——卷好的信号旗在各鸽笼格里候着，随时提词给任何可能降临的命运——随后出去尽他的职守。又一面旗在那条班轮上为他们抹开——太迟了；光线已经不够好了。可一颗星开始在她驾驶台上眨眼，他们都望着它，直到它熄灭。莱塞特回到了他们中间。

「他说他会把我们的情形呈报上去，先生。」

船长点了点头。「当然。」

===Original===
That was ended. Nobody spoke. The seaman at the useless wheel was aloof. He had no part in this. He appeared not to have heard anything, not to be interested in what was passing. He was merely waiting for the wheel to show a better spirit, and he could wait forever. He did not even turn his head to look at the departing liner. Only Lycett did that. The master had his hands spread on a desk, and was considering, so it appeared, a diagram before him. He had dismissed the liner. He had forgotten her already? And there she went. Colet, like young Lycett, watched her fade. She was already becoming unsubstantial; the upheavals and the twilight were taking her. Through a side-window Colet noticed a stoker below, clutching the bulwarks, his grimy flimsies shuddering in the wind, and he, too, was peering after the ship that was leaving them.

“Take over,” said the captain to Lycett, and went out.

===Chinese===
这一段了结了。没有人说话。守着那只没用舵轮的水手冷然置身局外。他在这件事里没有份。他像是压根儿什么也没听见，对正在经过的事也毫无兴致。他只是在等舵轮回出一点较好的心性，而他能永远等下去。他连头也不回，不去看那条正在离去的班轮。只有莱塞特回了头。船长双手摊在一张桌上，像是在斟酌面前的一幅图样。他已经把班轮打发了。他已经把她忘了吗？而她就这么去了。科利特和年轻的莱塞特一样，望着她淡去。她已经开始失却形骸；动乱的海与暮色正在把她收走。透过一扇边窗，科利特瞥见下方一个司炉，攥着舷墙，一身污脏的薄衫在风里簌簌发抖，而他，也在目送那条抛下他们的船。

「你来接手。」船长对莱塞特说，便出去了。

===Original===
The boyish officer at that turned from the place on the seas where the promise of the other ship was dying, as if he had petulantly resolved not to look that way again, and instead stood gazing at the *Altair*’s head uneasy in the surge, though not as if he saw it. He was silent. The man at the wheel might not have been there. What were the others doing? There was no sound but that of the swash and parade of the ocean. Lycett became aware of the diagram of the ship before him, and with a new interest began to examine it. He addressed the image at the wheel without lifting his head.

“Wilson, I say, do you think she is a bit by the head?”

“Yes.”

“Oh, you have noticed it. How long since you first thought so?”

The image continued its fixed regard of nothing for a while, its jaws moving as though it were making its words. When they were ready, it spoke.

“About an hour.”

===Chinese===
那位孩子气的驾驶员闻言，从海上那一点正在熄灭的、另一条船的许诺上转过脸去，仿佛赌着气打定了主意再不朝那边看，转而立定，凝望『牵牛星号』在涌浪里不安的头，却又不像真的在看。他沉默着。守舵轮的那个人，如在无人之境。旁的人都在做什么？除了海水的冲溅与浩荡巡行，再无别的声响。莱塞特觉察到面前那条船的图样，带着新的兴味端详起来。他头也不抬，向舵轮前那个影像开了口。

「威尔逊，我说，你看她是不是有点头重？」

「是。」

「哦，你也留意到了。你头一回这么想，有多久了？」

那影像继续定定地凝望着虚无，有一阵子，下颌嚅动，像是在把词句备出来。词句备好了，它才开口。

「约莫一个钟头。」

===Original===
The youngster bent closer to the diagram, and ended his inspection with a calculation on an old envelope he took from his pocket.

“Why, but in that case she won’t last till morning.”

Wilson eased his position slightly, and rubbed his mouth with the back of his hand. His voice was so deep and effortless that it sounded like the easy impersonal utterance of the room itself.

“She may. You can’t tell.”

===Chinese===
年轻人朝那图样俯得更近了些，末了从口袋里掏出一只旧信封，在上面演算一笔，结束了这番查验。

「哎呀，可要是那样，她挨不到天亮了。」

威尔逊把姿势微微松了松，用手背蹭了蹭嘴。他的嗓音低沉而不费力气，听上去就像这间屋子本身从容而不涉己意的发声。

「兴许挨得到。这谁说得准。」

===Original===
He might have experienced a full life of such nights and occasions, and so could, if it were worth while, advise youth out of a, privy knowledge which was part of the nature of ships and the sea.

“Something may be done,” said Wilson.

Colet made as if he were about to leave them.

“Don’t go, Mr. Colet,” urged the officer in charge. “Wait here, will you, till somebody comes along.”

Colet let go the handle of the door, went over to look at the diagram with Lycett, and endured the quiet, while listening to the seas, which were now invisible. The boy began to whistle a tune softly in a hesitating way.

===Chinese===
他仿佛已过完整整一生这样的夜晚与场面，因此，倘若值得，尽可以凭一份私密的知解去指点年轻人——那份知解，本就是船与海的天性的一部分。

「兴许还有法子可想。」威尔逊说。

科利特做出一副要离开他们的样子。

「别走，科利特先生。」值更的驾驶员挽留道。「在这儿等等，行么，等有人过来。」

科利特松开门把，走过去同莱塞特一起看那图样，一面忍受着这份安静，一面听着此刻已看不见的海浪。那小伙子开始踌躇着轻轻吹一支曲子。

===Original===
What were the others doing? Sometimes Colet thought he felt the far thumping of human handiwork under their feet. Hale and Sinclair were there, anyhow, and old Gillespie and his men; it was comforting, that certitude. It warmed the world, that secure thought of its good men holding fast. And while his faith was sustained that they could save her, it was not altogether because he himself was there. The ship herself meant something. She had become important. That image at the wheel was admonishing; that figure might be, perchance, the secret familiar of their ship. It was more than human; it spoke out of the ship. Once the steamer plunged head foremost, and something gave in her body. They thrilled to it, as if it were the smothered parting of a piano wire in a house where all was quiet and suspect. Lycett glanced at Colet, and then at the helmsman.

“Was that a bulkhead, Wilson?”

“Couldn’t say, sir.”

===Chinese===
旁的人都在做什么？有几回，科利特觉得自己觉出了脚下远远传来的、人手劳作的闷响。黑尔和辛克莱尔到底在那里，还有老吉莱斯皮和他的伙计们；这份确凿叫人心安。世上好人坚守不退的这个安稳念头，把整个世界都焐暖了。而他这份「他们救得了她」的信心撑得住，倒不全是因为他自己就在这船上。这条船本身有了意义。她变得要紧了。舵轮前那个影像带着儆戒之意；那个身影，说不定正是他们这条船秘而不宣的守护精灵。他不只是个人；他是从船身里说话。有一回，汽船一头扎了下去，她体内有什么东西断了。他们听得心里一凛，像在满宅安静而处处可疑的屋子里，听见一根钢琴弦闷声绷断。莱塞特瞥了科利特一眼，又瞥了瞥掌舵人。

「刚才那是舱壁么，威尔逊？」

「说不上，先生。」

===Original===
Of course he could not. He was not going to give her away so easily. Colet winked at the boy, and began to pace their little prison; but paused and stretched his arms. No. Stop that. That worried the others. Better have a pipe.

“Are you allowed to smoke, Wilson?”

The seaman only smiled.

“Oh, he chews,” said Lycett, and tapped a cigarette on the desk.

Lycett had just struck eight bells when Collins, the second officer, put his head in the door.

“Leave this. You can go below now, the lot of you. When you’re wanted you’ll know.” He disappeared at once.

===Chinese===
他当然说不上。他不打算这么轻易把她供出去。科利特朝那小伙子眨了眨眼，开始在他们的这个小监狱里踱步；却又停住，伸展双臂。不。别这样。这叫旁人心焦。还是抽斗烟好。

「准不准抽烟，威尔逊？」

那水手只是笑了笑。

「哦，他是嚼烟的。」莱塞特说，把一支香烟在桌上顿了顿。

莱塞特刚敲过八击钟，二副柯林斯（Collins）就把头探进门来。

「这里撤了。你们这会儿可以下舱去了，你们全体。要用着你们的时候，自然会有知会。」他随即就不见了。

===Original===
Colet roamed the deck amidships, accompanied only by the sough of the dark. Their own familiar and confident chant had ceased, that song which used to issue boldly from the open door of the engine-room casing as you passed it. No message but an infrequent clanking came up from below. Her heart had stopped. A flare or two, while he waited for signs, passed deep under the gratings. They were busy, in the depth of her; but doing what?

There was no doubt about it; when one walked aft it was distinctly to walk uphill. Her head was heavy. He tried to convince himself that this was not so. But it was.

There were no stars. There was only the steady drive of the dark. She was responding to it as though she were tired, stumbling and sluggish. Now and then a sea mounted over her foredeck. He looked at the shadows of some bent davits, with the swaying remnants of their falls, and heard a block mewling as it swung. That whining voice was the very trivialality of outer desolation. Creak. Whine. The captain’s daughter was taking her degree. A bit extra for her. Tonight, who was dining at the Gridiron?

===Chinese===
科利特在船腰甲板上徘徊，只有黑暗的呜咽作伴。他们自己那支熟稔而自信的吟唱停了——从前你打机舱围壁敞开的门前经过，那支歌总要大胆地从门里涌出来。如今从下面上来的，除了一声偶尔的铿锵，再无音信。她的心停了。他候着征兆的当口，有一两团火光从格栅深处掠过。他们在她的深腹里忙着；可忙的是什么？

这是错不了的；朝船尾走，分明就是上坡。她的头沉了。他竭力叫自己相信并非如此。可事实就是如此。

没有星。只有黑暗沉稳的驱迫。她应着它，仿佛倦了，磕磕绊绊，迟滞不前。不时有一面海漫上她的前甲板。他望着几具弯折的吊艇架的黑影，吊艇索的残段犹自摇曳，听见一只滑车荡来荡去，发出猫叫似的哀音。那呜呜的声音，正是外间荒凉的琐屑本相。吱呀。呜咽。船长的女儿正在挣她的学位呢。对她是格外加码。今夜，「烤架」餐馆里是谁在用晚餐？

===Original===
Morning came. Colet went out, when his port-light had shaped, and saw the crew, for the first time that voyage. The men were assembled on the afterdeck, and they surprised him as much as would a miraculous visitation of quiet disinterested strangers. Most of them were squatting against the bulwarks, but a few stood gazing seaward, indifferently. It was a scene dim and unreal. The air was warm. Once clanking broke out below again, but did not last long. Neither the captain nor Sinclair were there. He could not see any of the engineers. Perhaps, though, they still hoped to pull her through. The cook appeared at his galley door above, and peremptorily called out that anybody who wanted anything could come for it. There was a cheer from the men, wavering but derisive, and they began to move up to the galley. They might have been ignorant, or they might have known they had plenty of time. The forward deck was level with the water; it could not rise; the head of the ship was a sunken warning. Its lowness prompted Colet at once to appraise the size of the heavy propulsions of the ocean; he looked beyond to see whether a sea higher than the rest was coming.

It was not coming yet. And the men were still murmuring about the galley. Nothing was in sight, but one could not see very far. The sun would be there soon. It was warm, but, when Colet was not thinking of it, he shivered. Yet the sky was rosy along the east. How long to wait?

There came the sun, broad to the ship. It saw them. Their case now was manifest to heaven. A seaman who was lying as though asleep by the coaming of a hatch below the galley rose to his feet, stumbled to the side, and began to shout at the sun. The man was in rags. His mates watched him in limp wonder. He raised his bare arms and raved at the bloody day.

“To hell with you﻿—you’re no good to us.”

===Chinese===
早晨来了。等舷窗显出形迹，科利特走了出去，这一航次头一回见到全体船员。人们聚在后甲板上，他们之叫他惊异，不亚于一班安详而漠然的陌生人奇迹般显圣。多数人蹲靠着舷墙，少数几个站着望海，漠不关心。这是一幅昏昧而不真的场景。空气暖和。底下又起过一阵铿锵，但没有持续多久。船长和辛克莱尔都不在。他一个轮机员也看不见。不过，兴许他们还指望把她拖过去。厨子在上头厨房门口露了面，不容分说地吆喝：谁想要点什么，自己来拿。人群里起了一声欢呼，摇摇晃晃却又带着讥诮，随后他们便开始朝厨房挪上去。他们也许一无所知，也许知道自己有的是工夫。前甲板已经与水面齐平；它抬不起头来了；船的头颅是一枚沉没的警告。它的低伏立刻促使科利特掂量起海洋那沉重推进的量级；他望向远处，看是不是有一面比其余都高的海正朝这边来。

它还没有来。人们还在厨房近旁嘁嘁喳喳。视野里什么也没有，可人也望不远。太阳快来了。天是暖的，可科利特一想别的事，就打起寒噤。然而东边的天已泛出玫瑰色。还要等多久？

太阳来了，朝着船摊开。它看见他们了。他们的案情如今已上达天听。厨房下方一道舱口的围板边，躺着一个像是睡着的水手，他站起身，踉跄走到舷边，开始朝着太阳叫喊。那人衣衫褴褛。他的伙伴们瘫软而错愕地看着他。他举起赤裸的双臂，对着那该死的白昼咆哮。

「见你的鬼去——你对咱们一点用处没有。」

===Original===
“Stop that man,” commanded a voice. There stood the captain at the companion aft. Everybody turned that way.

“Stop him,” cried Hale, and ran quickly forward. The watchers came to their senses; but the man had scrambled outboard and dropped. One of his mates leaped astride the bulwarks, but Hale got a firm hand on him, and looked over. They were all peering over.

“Useless,” said Hale, still with his restraining hand on the seaman. “You come down. One’s enough.”

Colet was overlooking that from the amidships section. Someone’s hand heartily slapped his shoulder.

“Nice morning, Colet,” said Sinclair, and went pattering down to the main-deck, and passed through the men. Everyone now listlessly eyed the conference of the master and his lieutenant. Gillespie came hurrying along to join them. While the rest had their eyes on the three, the deck lapsed. There could be no mistake about that jolt.

“Let’s get a move on,” muttered a seaman. The master did not appear to have noticed it. Then he moved one arm slightly, a gesture of abandonment, and they heard the end of the talk.

===Chinese===
「拦住那个人。」一个声音下令。船长就立在船尾升降口那里。人人都转了过去。

「拦住他。」黑尔喊道，飞快地跑上前去。看的人才回过神来；可那人已经翻出舷外，坠了下去。他的一个伙伴跨上舷墙，黑尔却一把牢牢攥住他，朝外望。众人都朝外探望。

「没有用。」黑尔说，那只按住水手的手仍不肯放。「你下来。一个就够了。」

科利特从船腰那一段居高临下望着这一幕。一只手热乎乎地拍上他的肩。

「早晨真好，科利特。」辛克莱尔说，随即噔噔噔下到主甲板，从人群中穿过去。此刻人人都恹恹地注视着船长与其副手的那场会商。吉莱斯皮匆匆赶来入伙。众人的眼睛都盯在那三个人身上的当口，甲板陷了一截。那一下颠踬，错不了。

「赶紧动起来吧。」一个水手咕哝。船长像是没有留意那一记。随后他微微动了动一条手臂，一个撒手的姿势，他们听见了那席话的结尾。

===Original===
“Man, ye can do n’more,” from Gillespie.

“The boats, Mr. Sinclair,” said the master aloud.

Sinclair took the men’s eyes with a glance. He swept his arm with a motion to gather. He strolled to the amidships ladder, and they after him. It was right to show her that they were not in a hurry to leave her. Gillespie briefly inspected his squad, which had gravitated around him, and jerked his head towards Sinclair.

“Job’s finished, laddies. Awa’ now.”

She settled again while they took their stations, but the men kept their own gait. Colet sought the master, who was obscured by the activity at the ship’s side.

===Chinese===
「伙计，你再使不出劲了。」是吉莱斯皮的声音。

「放艇，辛克莱尔先生。」船长朗声说。

辛克莱尔一眼收拢了众人的目光。他手臂一挥，作了个聚拢的手势。他踱向船腰的梯子，众人随在他身后。让她看见他们并不急着离她而去，这是对的。吉莱斯皮把那伙已然聚拢到他身边的人简短地点检了一遍，朝辛克莱尔那边一摆头。

「活儿完了，小伙子们。走吧。」

他们各就各位时，她又坐沉了一截，可人们各守各的步子。科利特去寻船长，船舷边的忙碌遮住了他的身影。

===Original===
“Ah, purser, I am just going aft for something I must have.”

“I’ll go with you.”

“There’s time. How good these fellows are!”

The captain, with a japanned dispatch box in his hand, appeared to know exactly where to find all that he wanted; he moved about his room with a methodical promptitude which gave Colet the impression that the foundering of a ship could be ordered to a common ritual. Hale opened a drawer of his clothes chest and took out a wrap.

===Chinese===
「啊，事务长，我正要往船尾去取一样非带不可的东西。」

「我陪您去。」

「来得及。这些人是多么好啊！」

船长手里提着一只漆面公文箱，像是分毫不差地知道他要的东西都在哪里；他在舱室里走动，井井有条而干脆利落，给科利特这样一种印象：一条船的沉没，尽可以按一套寻常的仪节来料理。黑尔拉开衣物柜的一只抽屉，取出一件罩衣。

===Original===
“We must leave her, Colet. It’s queer you should have brought her to this.”

There lay Kuan-yin. The master glanced round his cabin; at his desk, at the barometer, and last, at his pictures. “That’s all. Time to go.”

One of the two laden boats was waiting for them, close under a boom which had been rigged out, with a man rope.

“Now, my lad, off with you.” Then the master hesitated.

===Chinese===
「我们必须离开她了，科利特。说来奇怪，竟是你把她带到了这般田地。」

观音就躺在那里。船长环顾他的舱室；看了看他的书桌，看了看气压计，最后看了看他的画。「就这些了。该走了。」

两条载重的艇中，有一条在等他们，紧傍着一根已经甩出的吊杆之下，垂着一根扶手绳。

「来，小伙子，下去吧。」随后船长迟疑了。

===Original===
“That greaser who was killed, Colet. I had meant to bear it in mind. I have a little packet of his in my cabin. His people might like it. Another minute.”

“I’ll wait.”

“Get in, get in.”

“No.﻿ ﻿…”

“The boat, sir.” Hale flung out his arm.

“Come on,” bawled someone from the sea.

===Chinese===
「那个罹难的机舱加油工，科利特。我原打算把这事记在心上的。他有一只小包存在我舱里。他的家里人兴许愿意要它。再给一分钟。」

「我等您。」

「上艇，上艇。」

「不。……」

「艇要开了，先生。」黑尔挥出臂膀。

「快呀。」海上有人吼道。

===Original===
The captain paused by the ship’s side to con her. Then he called out seawards:

“Your boat, Collins, keep her away.”

Colet eased off along the beam, and dropped from his hold as the lifeboat rose to him. He scrambled up to see what had become of Hale. There was no sign of him. Gillespie, in the stern of the boat, was angry with alarm.

===Chinese===
船长在船边停了停，把这条船细细端详了一遍。随后他朝海的方向高声喊道：

「柯林斯，你那条艇，叫它离远些。」

科利特顺着船梁挪出去，等救生艇升到他脚下，便松手坠了下去。他攀起身张望，看黑尔怎么样了。不见他的人影。吉莱斯皮在艇尾，惊惶得直冒火。

===Original===
“There’s no time, there’s no time.” The engineer eagerly half-stood, as they fell away into a hollow, for a better view of that companion door within which the captain had vanished. It was unnaturally high and strangely tilted in a ship whose life seemed poised on a moment of time and the hesitation of a breath. It remained empty.

“Hale,” shouted Gillespie; “Hale.”

They waited. A sea lifted them swiftly and lightly, and Colet turned his head in measuring fear from the door aft to the head of the *Altair*. Her forecastle deck was isolated, a raft of wreckage flush with the swirls and foam. The seas were pouring solidly across her middle. Her funnel was bowed over the flood, and each dip of it to the declination of the ocean was, to the men in the boat, the prelude to the end. But it was her stern which rose and lowered her head.

===Chinese===
「没有时间了，没有时间了。」轮机长急切地半欠起身，趁他们落进一道波谷，好把那扇升降口门看个真切——船长就是消失在那扇门里的。在一条性命仿佛悬于一瞬光阴、悬于一息踌躇的船上，那扇门显得高得出奇，倾斜得古怪。它始终空着。

「黑尔！」吉莱斯皮喊道；「黑尔！」

他们等着。一面海轻捷地把他们托起，科利特怀着掂量分寸的恐惧，把头从船尾那扇门转向『牵牛星号』的头。她的艏楼甲板已孤悬独立，成了一片与漩涡和飞沫齐平的残骸筏。海从她的腰部整垛整垛地灌过去。她的烟囱朝洪水之上弓着身，它每一次随海洋的倾斜俯下去，在艇上的人看来，都是终场的序曲。然而，正是她的船尾一起一伏，牵动她的头沉落。

===Original===
“My God, she’s going.”

She gave a hollow rumbling groan, and to the silent awe of the watchers without a pause she went down. Colet saw the shape of the propeller over him and the bright sky through its frame. There was a confusion on the surface of the waters, which melted as a swell heaved over the place. The sea was bare.


===Chinese===
「天哪，她去了。」

她发出一声空洞的隆隆哀号，在守望者们无声的敬畏里，不停不留地沉了下去。科利特看见螺旋桨的形状从他头顶上方过去，看见明亮的天空嵌在它的框架里。水面上乱了一阵，一道涌浪漫过那处所，纷乱便消融了。海面空空荡荡。

## XIX / 十九

===Original===
The men in the boat continued to stare at the place where their ship had been as though they still saw her. They remained trance-like without a movement in an apparent refusal to believe their experience. They certainly heard Hale’s voice there just now. The peaceful brightness of the vacant ocean was a mistake.

It was a stupid little noise in the shining immensity which woke Colet from his far absorption with what had gone, and brought him back to notice what new thing had taken its place. Lycett, beside him, was crying, but was trying to hide it. Mr. Collins, in the stern-sheets, had also withdrawn his gaze from the sea. He indicated something to a seaman, who spat on his palms, and made a few slow strokes with his oar. Nothing remained of the past but a spreading defilement of ash and oil.

Sinclair called across to them, and Collins held up a hand in understanding. Both craft set their lugs, and, in company, began to withdraw their occupants into another extension of life. Lycett’s head was averted. He was watching the near water. An area of cinders drifted astern. He watched it go till the water was clear again. He sat looking for more cinders, but the sea continued to be pure, impersonal, and unconcerned. Colet crouched uncomfortably, without changing his position, as if this posture in an open boat were but briefly provisional, and he were waiting for a return to what was necessary and accustomed. The transition from one existence to the next had been so abrupt that he had not fully accepted the change. And big Gillespie crouching on the opposite bench, staring between his legs at the bottom-boards, was vague. It was hardly Gillespie, in that attitude and that place. Colet was still in a ship’s cabin of another time, for the last minute of that room had survived its clock.

===Chinese===
艇里的人仍旧盯着他们的船方才所在的地方，仿佛还看得见她。他们像被施了定身法，一动不动，分明是拒不相信自己方才的经历。刚才他们确实还在那儿听见了黑尔的声音。这片空荡荡海面安宁的明亮，是个错误。

那闪亮的浩渺里响起一声小小蠢响，把科利特从对已然逝去之事的深沉出神里唤醒过来，带他去留意如今取而代之的新东西。他身旁的莱塞特在哭，却竭力想瞒住。艇尾座里的柯林斯先生也把目光从海上收了回来。他朝什么东西向一个水手示意，那水手往掌心啐了一口唾沫，用桨缓缓划了几下。往事除了一片漫开的灰烬与油污的狼藉，什么也没有留下。

辛克莱尔隔着水面朝他们喊了一声，柯林斯心领神会地举起一只手。两条艇都扬起了斜桁帆，结伴同行，开始把艇里的人载离此地，撤进生命的又一段延展里去。莱塞特把脸别了过去。他望着近处的水面。一片煤渣漂向船尾。他望着它漂远，直到海水又澄清了。他坐在那里还想找到更多煤渣，可大海依旧纯净，无我无情，漠不关心。科利特蜷缩着，很不好受，却不肯变换姿势，仿佛在敞篷小艇里的这个姿势只是片刻的权宜，他正等着回到那必要而惯常的一切里去。从一种存在跨入下一种存在，过渡得太突然，他还没有完全接受这个变化。而大个子吉莱斯皮蜷在对面座板上，从岔开的两腿间盯着艇底板，神情恍惚。那样一副姿势，那样一个所在，简直算不得吉莱斯皮了。科利特还在另一个时刻的船舱里，因为那间舱房的最后一分钟，比它自己的钟活得还久。

===Original===
“That’s all, Colet. Time to go.” But he had not gone yet.

Through that immediate apparition Colet presently surmised, as in a dissolving view, a threatening incline of blue water above them, at a surprising height above them. It shut out the sky astern. Before he rightly knew it was there he was soaring on it giddily, and his hand, hanging over the side, was immersed. He hastily withdrew his hand, plucking it, the last bit of himself in that enchantment, out of one dangerous dream into the next. He was transferred. From the summit of that swell he looked round upon an ocean he had never seen before. It was a narrower place, but at its centre it was more intimate and overgrown. The water had touched his hand; it was in hurrying flux near to his eyes; and the seas had become steep, and ranged close round above their mast. They were imprisoned by waves. Their complete assurance of the company of the other boat was intermittent.

They thought they heard singing. They really heard it, when their neighbour was on a crest above them; chance fragments of a song blew over from windward. A quick ear in their own boat caught some odd notes and recognised them. That stoker in the bows began to respond in drawling sardonic sentiment, “I﻿—don’t﻿—care﻿—if you﻿—don’t love﻿—me﻿—I﻿—don’t﻿—care﻿—if you go﻿—to﻿—sea.”

===Chinese===
「就这些了，科利特。该走了。」可他还没有走。

透过那近在眼前的幻影，科利特这会儿才如在一幅渐隐的画面里似的，隐约觉出他们头顶上方——高得吓人的上方——一面威压下来的蓝色水坡。它遮没了艉后的天空。他还没弄明白它在那儿，就已经晕晕地随它腾空而起，垂在舷外的那只手浸进了水里。他慌忙缩回手，把它——他在那场魔咒里仅剩的一点自己——从一个险恶的梦里拔出来，送进下一个险恶的梦里。他已经被挪到别处了。从那道涌浪的峰顶，他环顾一片从未见过的大洋。这是个更逼窄的处所，但愈近中心，浪涛挨得愈亲，也长得愈繁茂。海水碰过他的手了；它在他眼皮底下奔流变幻；浪头变得陡峭，就在他们桅顶上方四周近逼环列。他们被波浪囚禁了。对另一条艇还在作伴这件事，他们十足的把握时断时续。

他们仿佛听见了歌声。邻艇升上他们头顶的浪峰时，他们真的听见了；一支歌的零星片段，从上风飘了过来。他们艇上一只灵敏的耳朵捉住了几个古怪的音，认出了它们。船首那个司炉开始应和，拖长了调子，讥诮里带着伤感：「我——不——在乎——你爱——不爱——我——我——不——在乎——你——出——海。」

===Original===
Wilson sat near Colet. He was triturating tobacco between his hands in a musing deliberation which hinted to Colet that there was plenty of this new time. When a movement of the boat threw him out of his balance, Wilson paused, patiently. No need to hurry.

The boat was rather crowded; there was a great variety of heads and caps forward. One fellow rejoiced to recognise a pal in the bows.

“ ’Ullo, Percy, I see you. Coming for a nice sail?”

===Chinese===
威尔逊坐在科利特近旁。他把一块嚼烟夹在两只手掌中间碾揉，一面出神，一面不慌不忙；这份从容向科利特暗示：这种新时间有的是。艇身一个晃动，颠得他坐不稳，威尔逊便耐心地停一停。不必急。

艇里相当挤；船首一片五花八门的脑袋和帽子。有个家伙认出船首一个老相识，乐了。

「哟，珀西（Percy），我瞧见你了。来兜个风？」

===Original===
There was a long silence; nothing was to be heard but the shrill swish and flight of the waters along the gunwale, and the creaking of the boat. When she mounted a sea and was exposed to the wind, she heeled and jammed into the broad round of the hilltop. Collins sat mute and observant, but occasionally made a request to a man:

“Keep watch by the halyards there.”

Presently Gillespie spoke to him:

“You and Sinclair agreed about it?”

“Yes, the old man gave us our orders. The only thing to do. We ought to be picked up, on this course.”

===Chinese===
好一阵长久的沉默；除了水沿舷缘掠过时尖利的嘶嘶声，和艇身的吱呀作响，什么也听不见。她爬上一个浪头，暴露在风里，便倾侧着扎进浪丘顶部宽圆的脊背里。柯林斯坐在那里，一言不发却时刻留意，偶尔向某个人叮嘱一句：

「看好那边的升降索。」

过了一会儿，吉莱斯皮对他开口：

「你跟辛克莱尔商妥了？」

「嗯，老头子给我们下的令。只有这一条路可走。照这条航向，我们总会有船把我们救起。」

===Original===
They heard, breaking another long interval of quiet, a plaintive voice in the crowd forward.

“Alf, ’ave you noticed where the gentlemen’s room is in this ship?”

It grew hot, but there was no shelter from the glare. They must keep still, and ache. They could not ease away from the white fire. Colet, like his fellows, watched the seas. There was no more singing. They had begun already to peer beyond intently for the chance which would take them out of this huddled discomfort. Their narrow foothold was as lively as a bubble, flinching from every minor torment of the ascent and the dive. The inclines of the ocean were mesmeric with the horror of bulk whelming in unrest. The waters never paused. Respite was not there, and Colet found himself sighing for an outlook that would keep straight and still, and let him have his thoughts in peace. The sun continued its fire from a cloudless sky on the shelterless and silent boatful; but, whenever they were superior on a summit, and could see beyond the shifting and translucent parapets of their prison, only Sinclair and his crowd were in sight. And Sinclair’s boat looked overladen and trifling. The inconsequence of their neighbour, when she was sighted below, as if fixed in a spacious hollow, was a warning to themselves; they, too, were like that. Colet spied Sinclair’s charge with relief, if it were but the top of her mast above an intervening ridge. All right, so far. Sinclair was still there.

===Chinese===
又一段长长的静寂被打破，他们听见船首人群里一个哀声哀气的嗓门：

「阿尔夫（Alf），你找见这条船上的绅士盥洗室在哪儿了么？」

天热起来了，强光之下却无处躲避。他们只好一动不动地待着，干疼。他们没法从这场白火里挪开半分。科利特像他的同伴们一样，望着海。歌声没有了。他们已经开始凝神瞭望远处，盼着那个能把他们从这一堆挤作一团的不适里带出去的机会。他们脚下那点窄小的立锥之地，活泼得像个气泡，每一次爬升与俯冲的小磨难，都叫它打一个缩。大洋那些坡面自有一种催眠的力量，其中透着庞然大物在动荡不休里倾覆的恐怖。海水从不停歇。喘息是没有的，科利特发觉自己渴望一片笔直而静止的远景，好让他安安静静地想自己的心事。太阳从无云的天空里，继续向这一船无处躲藏、寂然无声的人喷火；可是，每当他们高踞浪顶，能望出这座牢狱那变动不居、半透明的胸墙时，看得见的只有辛克莱尔和他那一伙。而辛克莱尔的艇看上去超载了，微不足道。每当望见邻艇在下方，仿佛钉在一处空旷的洼地里，那副全然无足轻重的模样，对他们自己也是一个警告；他们也是那副样子。隔着一道浪脊，哪怕只望见她露出的那截桅顶，科利特瞥一眼辛克莱尔管领的那条艇也会放宽心。到目前为止，一切都好。辛克莱尔还在。

===Original===
Gillespie sat noting the pursuit of the following seas. He exclaimed to the helmsman:

“Look out, Collins. Here’s a beauty coming.”

Collins smiled, but kept his back to whatever was after them astern. The boat went squattering on the running hill till it found the wind at the top of it, and the hill was swinging ahead from under them. Not that time. Gillespie shook his head with dislike of it; but his eyes went again up their wake to look for the next attack.

The seas quivered in their mass with the original eagerness of that impulse which first sent them rolling round the globe. They would never stop. Their glassy inclines were fretted with lesser waves and hurrying cornices. They were flanged by outliers which deceived with hidden valleys, and the boat, rising briefly, dropped unexpectedly under the shadow of the superior headlong hill.

===Chinese===
吉莱斯皮坐在那里，留意着尾浪的追袭。他朝掌舵的人喊：

「留神，柯林斯。来了个大家伙。」

柯林斯笑了笑，却背对着艉后无论什么在追赶他们的东西。小艇噗噗地溅着水花，顺着奔跑的浪丘赶路，直到丘顶兜住了风，那浪丘便从他们身下朝前荡出去了。这一回没砸着。吉莱斯皮厌恶地摇了摇头；可他的眼睛又顺着他们的尾流望回去，搜寻下一次进攻。

浪涛在它们整个庞大的躯体里颤动，带着当初驱使它们绕着地球滚起来的第一股冲动的原始热望。它们永远不会停。它们玻璃般的坡面上，细碎的小浪和疾驰的浪檐刻出纷乱的纹理。四缘有边浪如凸缘般围着，用藏起来的谷地骗人；小艇短暂地升起，又冷不丁跌进那高出一头、直扑下来的浪丘的阴影里。

===Original===
“Look out!” The startled watcher beside the steersman was compelled then to an involuntary shout of warning.

“It could be worse,” said Collins. “She’s not bad to steer, but it makes me sleepy. Here Wilson. Take a turn at it.”

Collins then superintended the distribution of some rations. A little water, a very little water, and some biscuit went round.

“And listen, you men,” he called out; “if you don’t want to go balmy, leave the seawater alone. Bear that in mind tomorrow. All loonies will be put overside.”

“Good for you, sir. We’ll watch it. But chase the cook along with the ham and eggs.”

===Chinese===
「留神！」舵手旁边那个受了惊的瞭望者，到这时憋不住了，不由自主地喊出一声警告。

「还可能更糟呢，」柯林斯说。「她不难操纵，就是叫人犯困。喂，威尔逊。你来掌一程。」

随后柯林斯监督着分发了一点口粮。一点水，很少很少的一点水，还有些饼干，轮着传了一圈。

「还有，听着，弟兄们，」他高声说；「谁不想发疯，就别沾海水。明天也给我记牢。疯子一律扔下海去。」

「您说得对，先生。我们一定当心。不过劳驾催催厨子，火腿鸡蛋怎么还不来。」

===Original===
The sunset suffused with red and gold the transparent crests of the heights roving about them, and reflected in flashes from every transient facet of that region of crystal, where the foam glowed as runnels of coloured fire. Their little craft was transfigured. Its sail and boards were of a radiant and filmy substance too aerial to be scathed any more by the winds of earth. Its company were shining immortals, who had passed through their tribulation and were released at last from the labour and the wrecks where time is, and the lower seas of a troubled world.

“I doubt it will be a cold night,” mused Gillespie, looking round on the brightness, “and my pants are wet now.” A noisy shower of rubies swept over the crowd forward.

“That’s it,” said the engineer, “and wet shirts for the laddies.”

===Chinese===
日落把红与金，涂满在他们四周游走的高浪那透明的峰顶，又从那水晶境域每一个转瞬即逝的棱面上闪出反光；在那里，泡沫燃亮，像一道道彩色的火溪。他们的小艇变了形容。她的帆和艇板成了一种辉煌而轻薄的材料，太超逸于尘世，尘世的风再也伤不着她了。艇上这一伙是光华灿烂的不朽者，他们已经历尽自己的苦难，终于从劳作与沉船之中、从时间所在之处、从这烦恼人世的低贱海域里，解脱了出来。

「我看今夜怕要冷，」吉莱斯皮环顾着这片光亮，若有所思，「我的裤子这会儿已经湿了。」一阵喧闹的红宝石阵雨，扫过船首的人群。

「可不是，」轮机长说，「小伙子们还得套上湿衬衫呢。」

===Original===
The group aft, about Wilson, murmured a conversation, in which Wilson learned the name of the star which was in the general direction of their course, and how he should use it. They continued some speculations about the stars, whispering their attempts at mysteries, while the navigator gave names, haunting and occult names, to the glittering points of night.

“We shall have to keep this man awake,” said Collins. “I was not quite all there all the time I was steering.”

Their gossip went back to the ship. They guessed at where her plates had parted. They spoke of their old ship, but they did not name her master. Collins explained his hopes of the course they were on, and they wondered how long it would be before a ship was sighted. Frequently they glanced to the spark which showed where Sinclair was in the night. Then Gillespie was left to keep the steersman company, and to call Colet at midnight to sit with the second officer.

===Chinese===
艉部围着威尔逊的一小伙人低声交谈，威尔逊在其中得知了大致对着他们航向的那颗星的名字，以及该怎么用它。他们继续对星星做种种推想，压低嗓子摸索其中的奥秘；那位识星导航的人，则给夜空里闪烁的光点一一命名——萦绕于心的、秘传的名字。

「我们得让这个人一直醒着，」柯林斯说。「我掌舵那阵子，自始至终魂儿都不怎么齐。」

他们的话题又回到船上。他们猜船壳钢板是在哪儿崩裂的。他们说起了他们的老船，却不提她主人的名字。柯林斯说明了他对眼下这条航向寄予的希望，他们则琢磨着还要过多久才望得见一条船。他们不时瞥一眼夜色中标出辛克莱尔所在的那一点微光。随后留下吉莱斯皮陪掌舵的人，到半夜好去叫科利特起来，陪二副坐一班。

===Original===
Colet tried to sleep, but he had no sooner forgotten the cramp and the cold than the boat kicked him awake again. He turned about, to try the other side, and so got a memory of Wilson’s head bent forward, a presiding head, austere and calm, isolated in the gloom. A fellow at the other end was retching. The hours stood still. He thought he would never sleep; but then again the boat jolted him into full consciousness of the cold, and in surprise he saw over them the dark wing of the sail. He turned back again. The bench was hard and wet, and gave nowhere. He could feel the slight timber vibrating under his arm; she was as giddy as an air-ball. Impossible to sleep, while listening to the fall of waters in the dark. When Gillespie gently pressed his knee, he sat up abruptly as if he had been dreaming of a crisis. Collins was taking Wilson’s place.

“Eight bells,” said Gillespie, “and all’s well. Change over.”

Colet’s teeth chattered on their own account. They got into full speed before they were checked. And nobody would have guessed that night itself could be so dark, when there was nothing in it but the sound of unseen waters in flight, and the thin protests of their frail security as it was hurled along through nothing.

===Chinese===
科利特想睡，可他刚把抽筋和寒冷忘掉，艇又把他踢醒了。他翻了个身，想试试另一侧，于是印下一幅记忆：威尔逊的头向前俯着，一颗主事者的头，严峻而沉静，孤悬在幽暗里。艇另一头有个家伙在干呕。时辰凝住不动。他以为自己再也睡不着了；可接着艇又是一颠，把他颠得彻底醒觉出冷来，他惊讶地看见头顶上帆的黑翼。他又翻了回去。座板又硬又湿，没有一处肯让一分。他能觉出臂弯底下细薄的艇木在颤动；她像只气球似的又轻又晕。听着黑暗里水的起落，怎么可能睡得着。吉莱斯皮轻轻按了按他的膝盖，他猛地坐起来，仿佛正梦见什么危局。柯林斯正在接威尔逊的班。

「八击钟，」吉莱斯皮说，「一切平安。换班。」

科利特的牙齿自顾自地打颤。不等谁去管，它们已经全速颤了起来。谁也不会想到，夜本身竟能黑成这样——里面什么也没有，只有看不见的水奔逃的声音，和他们那点脆弱的安全被抛掷着穿过虚无时，发出的细弱抗议。

===Original===
Colet took a seat beside the steersman.

“Well, what have you got to say. Something good?” asked Collins. “Get any sleep?”

“Tell him about featherbeds,” murmured a voice.

Then another voice piped up, with a quaver in it. “No. Tell him about all the pubs you know, sir. I know a nice warm little place kept by a widder.”

===Chinese===
科利特在掌舵人身边坐下。

「怎么样，有什么要说的么。有好事没有？」柯林斯问。「睡着过没有？」

「跟他说说羽绒床吧，」一个声音咕哝道。

接着另一个声音尖细地响起来，带着点颤。「不。跟他说说你知道的那些酒馆吧，先生。我知道一家又暖和又舒服的小店，掌柜的是个寡妇。」

===Original===

“Shut up. You’d better go to sleep,” said Collins.

“How can I sleep, sir? There’s a bloke’s boots in my mouth. Besides, she wants baling.”

“Is there much water there?”

“Only enough for a drop of gin, as you might say, sir. It’ll all soak in my shirt, the next time she heels.”

===Chinese===

「闭嘴。你最好睡觉去。」柯林斯说。

「我哪里睡得着，先生？有个家伙的靴子杵在我嘴里。再说，她该舀水了。」

「那儿水多吗？」

「也就够兑一滴金酒的，不妨这么说吧，先生。等她下一回倾侧，就全渗进我衬衫里了。」

===Original===
Someone drawled a protest.

“It’s a lie, Jim. You fellers on the lee side are as well orf as what we are. Our shirts ’ere got no more stowage.”

“I don’t wonder at it, Dave. It was Dave spoke, wasn’t it? I know you, Dave, and I know that shirt of yourn. It’s the same one, ain’t it?”

===Chinese===
有人拖长了声气，表示抗议。

「瞎说，吉姆。你们下风舷的家伙跟我们一样好过。我们这边的衬衫，可再装不下一滴了。」

「这不奇怪，戴夫（Dave）。刚才开口的是戴夫吧？我认得你，戴夫，也认得你那件衬衫。还是原来那一件，是不是？」

===Original===

There was a thumping on the boards forward.

“Put a stopper in it, aft. We’re trying to forget it, up here.”

“Then yore wasting yore perishing time, Alec, my lad. Only brass monkeys could forget it.” She lurched, and a heavy shower fell across her, by the mast. The men up there groaned and swore. But they heard a laugh in the dark at the after end.

“Got that lot, Alec? Try to forget it.”

===Chinese===

船首有人咚咚地捶起艇板。

「艉那边别作声。我们这儿正想法忘掉它呢。」

「那你就是白瞎糟蹋工夫了，亚历克（Alec）老弟。只有黄铜铸的猴子才忘得掉它。」她一个趔趄，一大片水兜头浇过桅边。上头那些人哼哼唧唧地咒骂。可他们听见黑暗里艉端有人笑了。

「接了那一大瓢吧，亚历克？想法忘掉它呀。」

## XX / 二十

===Original===
The interminable days merged for those open boats. Time lapsed into an uneventful fortitude, a thirsty desert, to which apathy could see no end. The sail of each boat was double-reefed and goose-winged, perhaps because Sinclair was afraid of running too far, or because he thought exhaustion would make his men careless. Smoke was sighted, one day. It was a smear which persisted for so long that the castaways thought they could make to windward till they were seen. They never lifted that steamer. And more than once a light had been glimpsed at night, when Collins’ boat was on the back of a high sea.

“Light ahead!”

The men waited hopefully for the next lucky impulse which would lift them to a clear view towards the horizon. Yes.

“There it is. A light!”

===Chinese===
对那些敞篷小艇来说，没完没了的日子混成了一片。时间淌成一种波澜不惊的坚忍，一片渴人的荒漠，麻木的眼睛望不见它的尽头。每条艇的帆都收了两道缩帆，张成蝴蝶帆，也许是因为辛克莱尔怕跑得太远，也许是因为他觉得疲惫会叫他手下的人疏忽大意。有一天，望见了烟。那道烟痕久久不散，落难的人们以为能够抢风赶上前去，直到被对方望见。他们始终没能追见那艘汽船。还有不止一次，夜里瞥见过一点光，那是在柯林斯的艇正骑着一道高浪的脊背时。

「前方有光！」

人们满怀希望，等着下一个幸运的浪势把他们托起来，望得清地平线。果然。

「在那儿。一点光！」

===Original===
But Mr. Collins had sighted it too. “That? That’s a star.”

The men huddled down again without another word.

“Better luck next time,” their officer assured them. “Keep a good lookout. We’re in the way of traffic.”

It was strange. Colet, if he stood, was now easily thrown out of his balance by a movement of the boat. He was a little surprised by that. It was not, of course, that he was weak. He wasn’t weak. He did not care much; that was all. But he ought not to fall over, though that would be the easiest thing to do. No good. Almost sure to knock against somebody. Pull yourself together, old son. Look at young Collins. Fine fellow, Collins; and he’d hardly had a word with him till after the ship went down. Never thought there was much in Collins. But that youngster’s pasture, wherever it was, was the place for mettle. And Wilson, too. The whole lot of them. Not a murmur. There was something damned fine in this ordinary stuff.

===Chinese===
可柯林斯先生也望见了。「那个？那是颗星。」

人们又缩了下去，再没说一个字。

「下回运气会好的，」他们的驾驶员宽慰他们。「好好瞭望。我们正在船舶来往的道上。」

事情有点怪。科利特这会儿只要一站着，艇身一动，就容易把他晃得失去平衡。他对这一点有点惊讶。这当然不是因为他身子虚。他不虚。他只是不大在乎了；仅此而已。可他不该摔倒，虽然摔倒是最容易的事。那可不行。几乎准会撞上什么人。振作起来，老伙计。瞧瞧年轻的柯林斯。好样儿的，柯林斯；船沉之前，他几乎没同他说过一句话。从来没想到柯林斯肚子里有多少货。可那小伙子放牧生长的地方，不管在哪儿，正是养胆气的地方。还有威尔逊。他们全伙人。没有一声咕哝。这些寻常材料里，有一种真他妈了不起的东西。

===Original===
If he could only keep seated he could last till domesday. He could steer that boat into the Styx, and save the passage money. Hullo, Charon, now watch a bit of real boat work. Beat that. He was only thirsty. Not hungry. It would be all right if that thick slime could be washed out of the mouth.

“You off biscuits?” asked Collins that morning. “So am I. I can’t make anything of ’em, except to spit dust.”

A few of the men lay as if dead on the bottom boards. If they were trodden on they did not move, and did not speak. You had to look at their faces again to make sure. The unshaven faces of the men were like those of destitute but bearded children. The purser sat considering vacancy, steering the boat. The way she was going, you kept the draught on your left jaw.

===Chinese===
只要能一直坐着，他能撑到末日审判那一天。他能把这条艇驾进斯提克斯（Styx）冥河，还省下一笔渡资。喂，卡戎，这就瞧瞧什么叫真正的驾船本事。你比得了么。他只是渴。并不饿。要是能把嘴里那层厚浆子漱掉，就什么事都没有了。

「你吃不下饼干了吧？」那天早上柯林斯问。「我也一样。我拿它们没一点办法，只能嚼出一嘴的土。」

有几个人直挺挺躺在艇底板上，像死了一样。踩着他们，他们也不动，也不说话。你得再看看他们的脸，才敢断定。这些人不曾修面的脸，像一张张穷苦潦倒却长着胡子的孩子的脸。事务长坐在那里凝望虚空，掌着艇的舵。照她行驶的方向，那股风老贴着你左边的腮帮灌。

===Original===
“We ought to see something any time,” Collins soliloquised, a little querulously. “No need to worry.”

The purser smiled, with his eye on the quivering luff of the sail. He felt resigned.

“I’m not worrying.” That was the strange thing about it. He imagined his mind had never been clearer. It was like a steady light inside him. Nothing could blow that out. No wind could flicker it. Never knew before he had a mind. Sure of it now. He felt pale and lucid inside, but he did not want to move. He could look on, a sort of lamp, till the last wave of the sea had unrolled. The sea and sky could pass away, if they liked. They were passing away. They had got more distant, and less impressive. They could no longer daunt with their show of grandeur and dominance, and so they were going. Their game was up. But this old boat, she could go on till they had sighted Helicon. They might beat to windward round the Last Hope. Something like seafaring, something like life, when you knew you could hold on till the dark was encircled. Get right round it. One more drink, and he could sit there till the sail was a film, the men were ghosts, and they had the Pleiades close abeam. He gave her a touch, and she nicely missed an ugly one.

===Chinese===
「随便哪会儿，我们都该望见点什么了，」柯林斯自言自语，微微带着点愠怒。「用不着发愁。」

事务长笑了，眼睛望着帆上颤动的前缘。他心里听天由命。

「我并不发愁。」这正是他身上显得奇怪的地方。他想象自己的头脑从未这般清明过。它像他体内一盏稳定的灯。什么也吹不灭它。什么风也吹不得它闪烁。从前不知道自己有一个头脑。现在确知了。他觉得自己内里苍白而澄明，却不想动弹。他可以就这样旁观下去，像一盏灯，直到大海最后一道浪舒展到头。海和天要消逝，随它们的便。它们正在消逝。它们变得愈来愈远，愈来愈不动人。它们再也不能拿堂皇与威凌的排场慑服人了，所以它们正在退场。它们的把戏到头了。可这条老艇，她还能走下去，直到他们望见赫利孔山（Helicon）。他们还可以抢风，绕过「最后的希望」（the Last Hope）。这才像跑船，这才像人生——当你知道自己撑得住，一直撑到黑暗被团团围住。把它整个儿绕过去。再喝上一口，他就能一直坐在那儿，坐到帆化成一层薄翳，人化成一伙鬼影，昴星团（Pleiades）正挨着正横。他轻轻一带舵，她便灵巧地避开了一个丑浪。

===Original===
“Purser, you might have been doing this all your life,” the officer told him.

Colet reflected. “I think I have,” he said. Quite true; all the life he had had. Collins glanced at him, with a trace of alarm.

“I say, Colet. Don’t you go lightheaded like some of ’em.”

“I’m all right.”

===Chinese===
「事务长，你简直像是干了一辈子这一行的。」那位驾驶员对他说。

科利特想了想。「我想我是干了一辈子。」他说。千真万确；他有过的这一辈子，全干的是这一行。柯林斯瞥了他一眼，带着一丝惊惶。

「我说，科利特。你可别像他们有些人那样，神志发起飘来。」

「我没事。」

===Original===
“I wish it would rain.”

“A drop would about save the worst cases. Lycett’s bad.”

“Yes. I can’t do any more, can I?”

“Collins, you’re fine. We’re lucky.”

“I wonder how Sinclair’s bunch is getting on?”

===Chinese===
「真希望下场雨。」

「一场雨差不多能把最重的几个救回来。莱塞特不行了。」

「是啊。我再没别的法子了，对吧？」

「柯林斯，你真好样的。我们算走运的。」

「不知辛克莱尔那一帮子过得怎样？」

===Original===
There she was, just on the round of a sea, a tiny model. They sighted her together.

“About the same, of course.”

“Well, we’ll hear when we’re picked up. I say, Colet, it wouldn’t do to give the fellows more water, would it?”

“No. Not the way we’ve reckoned it. Wouldn’t do. We must wait.”

“Yes. Take our chance. Colet﻿ ﻿… talking of drink. Lord. I was going to talk about it, but I won’t.”

===Chinese===
她就在那儿，恰在一道浪的圆背上，一个小小的模型。他们一同望见了她。

「当然还是老样子。」

「嗯，等我们被救起就会知道了。我说，科利特，再给弟兄们多分水，不合适吧？」

「不合适。照我们核计的量不行。不合适。只能等。」

「对。碰我们的运气。科利特……说起喝的。老天。我本来正想聊聊它，算了，不聊了。」

===Original===
“No. Keep off the drink, Collins.”

“I know. My mouth’s coated with gum.”

The quivering of the sail had a strange effect. It was like a ceaseless glittering. It was like sun-points on a milldam on a drowsy summer afternoon, when you could just hear the rumbling of the mill. Colet took his eyes off that hypnotising movement, and glanced to windward. A mass of smooth glass was about to pass under them, and deep in its body he saw a long phantom, a suspended monster, that writhed once, and faded. It had gone under the boat.

===Chinese===
「不。别谈喝的，柯林斯。」

「我知道。我嘴里都糊了一层胶。」

帆的颤动有一种奇异的效果。它像一种不停歇的闪烁。它像恹恹欲睡的夏日午后，磨坊坝上的日光斑点，那时你刚好听得见磨坊的隆隆声。科利特把眼睛从那催眠的晃动上挪开，朝上风望了一眼。一大片平滑如镜的水面正要从艇底下过去，在它深处他看见一个长长的魅影，一头悬着的巨兽，扭动了一下，便隐没了。它已经从艇底穿过去了。

===Original===
The steersman’s eyes went back to the sail. Collins was still talking, but his voice was only like the muttering of the mill. The men were very still. Somebody ought to cover up Lycett’s face. The sun was too bright.

“Wilson,” he said, “cover up Lycett’s face.” But he did not hear his own voice in that silence. It was impossible to break that silence. Wilson did not move. The seaman sat like a statue. He was the Sphinx, his hands on his knees, staring like that.

Nobody moved. Nobody. They couldn’t. They would never move again. They were dead. There was only a deep humming. That was the world. It was droning in space. That was the sound of its sleep. They were floating off. All their weight had gone. Their boat was under them, and so plain you could still see it. There it was, that shadow inside the sea, but it was fading, fading. The old world was sinking under them. That was why they could hear it. It was dwindling and droning away. Wilson was watching the world leave them, and it was all right. You could trust Wilson. They were getting near that star now. Light ahead! The star was coming their way, and it was growing, growing round, like the sun, growing bigger every minute: so bright that it was a white blaze, the white centre of eternity with time streaming from it in spears. That was God. His face was going to show in that white light. They must keep looking.﻿ ﻿…

===Chinese===
掌舵人的眼睛回到帆上。柯林斯还在说话，可他的声音只剩下磨坊的咕隆。人们静极了。该有人把莱塞特的脸盖上。太阳太毒了。

「威尔逊，」他说，「把莱塞特的脸盖上。」可是在那片寂静里，他听不见自己的嗓音。那片寂静是打不破的。威尔逊没有动。那水手像一尊石像似的坐着。他就是斯芬克斯（Sphinx），双手搁在膝上，那样凝望着。

谁也没有动。谁也没有。他们动不了了。他们再也不会动了。他们死了。只剩一阵深沉的嗡鸣。那是世界。它在太空中嗡嗡作响。那是它沉睡的声音。他们在飘离。他们所有的重量都去了。他们的艇还在身下，清楚得仍然看得见。它在那儿，海里那道影子，可它在暗下去，暗下去。这个旧世界正在他们脚下沉没。所以他们才听得见它。它在缩小，嗡嗡远去。威尔逊正望着这个世界离他们而去，没有关系。威尔逊是可以信托的。他们此刻正在靠近那颗星。前方有光！那颗星朝他们这边来了，它在长大，长圆，像太阳，一分钟比一分钟大：亮得成了一团白焰，永恒的白色中心，时间像一支支长矛从它那里射出来。那是上帝。祂的脸就要在那片白光里显现了。他们必须一直望着。……

===Original===
“Colet!”

“What’s that?”

“Were you asleep?”

“Not me﻿ ﻿… I dunno.”

“The sun’s cruel hot. I wish a squall would come along. Some rain.﻿ ﻿… Those men look pretty sick.”

===Chinese===
「科利特！」

「什么？」

「睡着了么？」

「我才没有……说不清。」

「太阳毒极了。真希望来一阵狂风。来点雨。……那些人瞧着很不济了。」

===Original===
They sat with their heads close together, their tousled hair grizzled with dry salt. They looked aged, with grey beards. Only the boat retained youth and eagerness. She was as buoyant as ever. They could find nothing more to say. Collins sighed, and stood up. He looked to Sinclair’s charge, a mile away to windward. His eyes circled round, and suddenly his hand gripped hard the steersman’s shoulder.

“Coming up astern! Colet, a ship.”

His voice was raised and confident.

“Sail-ho!”

The dead figures stirred. They came to life. Some of them rose, clutching the gunwale, crouching with a grip on the thwarts, or clasping the mast. They were staring aft.

===Chinese===
他们两个头凑得很近地坐着，乱蓬蓬的头发上结着干盐，显出灰白。他们显得老了，生着灰白的胡子。只有那条艇还保有青春和劲头。她还像从前一样轻快活泼。他们再也找不出话来说了。柯林斯叹了口气，站起身。他望了望上风一英里外辛克莱尔管领的那条艇。他的目光扫了一圈，突然，他的手死死攥住掌舵人的肩膀。

「艉后来船了！科利特，一艘船。」

他的嗓门提了起来，满有把握。

「见船喽！」

那些死人似的身影动了起来。他们活了过来。有几个站起身，攥着舷缘，抠住横座板蹲伏着，或是抱住桅杆。他们都朝艉后张望。

===Original===
“All over, boys. Here she comes.”

“It’s a liner, sir,” said Wilson.

“Of course it is. That’s what we want. Share round what’s in the breaker, Wilson. She can’t miss us.”

Sinclair had seen it too. His boat had luffed. Colet did not remember afterwards very much of what followed. Collins took the helm. She was black, the liner, with a long row of round ports, circles of gold. She was enormous, when she stopped. She was bigger than the sea; she blotted it out. Her upper works were white, and she hardly moved, though the waters were dancing beneath her. There was someone shouting from her bridge. Along her rail was a row of still figures, regarding them silently, from a great height. Colet sat in dazed astonishment. Women in white dresses were looking down at them.

===Chinese===
「熬到头了，弟兄们。她来了。」

「是艘班轮，先生。」威尔逊说。

「当然是。我们要的就是这个。威尔逊，把淡水桶里的水分给大家。她绝不会漏掉我们的。」

辛克莱尔也望见了它。他的艇已抢风转向上风。事后科利特对随后发生的事记不太清了。柯林斯接过了舵。那条班轮是黑色的，一长排圆形舷窗，一圈圈金色。她停下来时，大得吓人。她比海还大；把海都遮没了。她的上层建筑是白色的，虽然船底下的海水在跳舞，她却几乎不动。她的驾驶台上有人在喊话。她的栏杆边排着一列静静的身影，从极高的地方，默然注视着他们。科利特坐在那里，惊得发怔。穿白裙的女人们正俯视着他们。

## XXI / 二十一

===Original===
The liner’s deck was a neat road, a disciplined promenade, and the seams of its scoured woodwork ran so far and straight that they were as incredible as plain truth. It had garden seats. The extraordinary thing about that deck was that it was too solid and steady. Colet could only flop loose feet upon it. It was funny, trying to walk on a steady deck. The feet didn’t know it was steady.

“Purser, I can’t move,” complained Collins, in a whisper. “I can’t walk.”

A beneficence had come unexpectedly out of the blue, just as the apparitions of monsters had loomed beside the boat in the body of the sea, and as the hopeful lights of ships, in night watches, had declined into setting stars. But it had come. It enfolded them. They were in luck. Colet was tucked into a cabin, a luxurious place, not yet to be believed, but quite solid, for all that, and there he lay in the surrender of release, while yet his body was responsive to the soaring lightness of that boat. His body still had a ghostly apprehension of the swift lift and the descent. Couldn’t forget that. Never forget that; nor the swaying ridges of the great seas overshadowing them at dusk. Hale’s last words: “That’s all, Colet. Time to go.” And Wilson’s head at daybreak, watching; watching without a movement the march of the seas as if he knew all about them, but doubted the loyalty of their inferior nature. Nothing to do now but to snuff up the smell of cool linen again, and forget a book while listening to the soporific whirr of the electric fan. The surgeon would then come in, easy and bland.

===Chinese===
班轮的甲板是一条整洁的路，一处规行矩步的散步场，刷洗过的木活的板缝伸展得又远又直，直得叫人难以相信，像平平淡淡的实情一样难以置信。甲板上还安着花园座椅。这层甲板的古怪之处，在于它过于结实、过于安稳。科利特只能把两只虚浮的脚扑塌扑塌地踩上去。在一块安稳的甲板上学走路，实在滑稽。那双脚还不晓得它是安稳的。

「事务长，我动不了，」柯林斯（Collins）低声抱怨道，「我走不了路。」

一份恩惠不期然从天而降，正如巨怪的幻影曾在海腹之中逼临小艇旁，正如夜更中有望的船火曾渐渐沉落成西沉的星。可它到底是来了。它把他们裹进怀里。他们交了好运。科利特被安顿进一间舱房，一处豪华所在，一时还叫人不敢相信，却又结结实实。他便躺在那里，在解脱之中彻底缴了械，而他的身体仍旧应和着那条小艇高扬的轻盈。他的身体还幽灵似的提防着那急骤的腾起与跌落。那是忘不掉的。永远忘不掉；也忘不掉黄昏时分覆在他们头顶的大海摇荡的峰脊。黑尔最后的话：「行了，科利特。该走了。」还有破晓时威尔逊的那颗头，望着；一动不动地望着海水的行军，仿佛他对海的底细一清二楚，却又拿不准它们那低劣的天性靠不靠得住。如今无事可做，只消重新去嗅凉爽被单的气息，一面听着电扇催眠的嗡鸣，一面把一本书读到忘却。随后船医就会进来，安闲而和悦。

===Original===
“Morning, doctor. How’s the rest? How’s Lycett?”

“That’s how you feel, is it? All is well with the child.”

“And Sinclair, our chief officer? You’ve seen him, too?”

“Ah, the redheaded pirate. Couldn’t help seeing him. He’s picking up. He just told me to go to hell.”

“Gillespie?”

“Don’t know him. Oh, you mean the dear old Glasgow Highlander who keeps on asking for a long whisky and soda? Unless his anxiety exhausts him first, he’ll smell whisky again, some day.”

===Chinese===
「早，医生。其余的人怎么样？莱塞特（Lycett）呢？」

「你惦记的是这个，是么？那孩子一切安好。」

「还有辛克莱尔，我们的大副？你也见过他了？」

「啊，那个红头发的海盗。想不见他也难。他正在见好。他刚才还叫我去见鬼呢。」

「吉莱斯皮呢？」

「不认得。哦，你是说那位没完没了要大杯威士忌苏打的老格拉斯哥（Glasgow）高地人吧？除非他那份焦渴先把他熬干，总有一天他还能闻上威士忌的味儿。」

===Original===
“Don’t be hard on him. He’s all right.”

“So the engineers say. But he hates me. You all do, you know. I’ve never seen such a crew. Some of the women here are nervous. It’s as much as I can do to keep them out of your cabins.”

“They’re not coming, are they?”

“Not unless you don’t do what I tell you.”

===Chinese===
「别对他太苛刻。他没事的。」

「轮机员们也这么说。可他恨我。你们全恨我，这你们是知道的。我从来没见过这样一伙人。船上有些太太神经紧张。我费了好大的劲，才把她们挡在你们舱门外面。」

「她们不会进来吧？」

「她们不来——除非你不照我吩咐的做。」

===Original===
The youthful but white-haired surgeon, tall, deliberate, and gracious, who in his white uniform could have been a functionary, immaculate and revered, in a sphere where all was pure and noble, one morning took Colet, shrunk within a borrowed suit, which made him feel like an awkward mortal who had blundered into the abode of the blest, to the smoke-room. There was Sinclair, with some strangers about him. Sinclair came to meet him. He was much amused by something in Colet’s appearance. He held him off at arm’s length, and laughed.

“You look holy, purser. You look as if you were just coming out of the wilderness after turning down the devil. Come and have a pick-me-up.”

===Chinese===
那位年纪轻轻却已满头白发的船医，高个子，举止从容，态度亲切，穿上那身白色制服，俨然是某个纯洁高尚的境界里一位一尘不染、受人敬仰的官员。一天上午，他领着科利特往吸烟室去；科利特缩在一身借来的衣服里，自觉像个冒失闯进福人居所的笨拙凡人。辛克莱尔正在那里，身边围着几个生人。辛克莱尔迎上前来。科利特模样上的什么东西把他大大逗乐了。他伸直胳膊把科利特擎住端详，放声大笑。

「你一脸圣洁相，事务长。你像是刚在荒野里回绝了魔鬼，走出来了。来，喝一杯提提神。」

===Original===
The strangers made room for him, adjusting wicker chairs about one of the tables with an air of quickly providing for a welcome guest who was really invisible to them. Colet noticed that they observed him cautiously only when they supposed they were unobserved. They continued their conversation as though he had not come. They did not want to embarrass him by showing they were aware of his unusual presence. The shyness of Englishmen was so delicate and polite, he thought, and so encouraging, that a nervous kitten might be deluded into thinking that it had the room to itself, until it was trodden on. They evidently knew nothing about the rescue of any castaways. They had never heard of it. Luckily for him, no boats had been picked up in mid-ocean, so there was no need, if challenged, to confess to an episode which probably had never happened. They talked of rubber, of one or two important men who ought to be shot, of one or two unimportant women who had provided the ship, that voyage, with a little welcome unexpectedness, and of a fellow-passenger whose luck at cards was evidence of the existence of the devil. But presently, when the conversation became various with subjects discreet between pairs of these strangers, the man next to Colet tapped out his pipe and leaned over to him, as though with a chance private thought.

“Feeling all right now?”

“Fine.”

===Chinese===
那些生人给他腾出位置来，围着一张桌子挪动藤椅，那副神气像是赶忙招待一位受欢迎的客人，其实这位客人他们压根儿没看在眼里。科利特注意到，他们只有在自己以为没人瞧着的时候，才小心翼翼地打量他。他们接着谈他们的话，仿佛他没有来。他们不愿露出知道他来历特别的样子，免得叫他难堪。英国人的羞怯竟如此细腻、如此客气，他想，还如此鼓舞人心，一只胆怯的小猫会被哄得以为这间屋子归它独享，直到叫人踩上一脚。他们显然对搭救落难之人的事一无所知。他们从来没有听说过。也算他运气，大洋当中没有捞起过任何小艇，所以万一有人问起，也无须招认一桩多半压根儿没发生过的事。他们谈橡胶，谈一两个该枪毙的大人物，谈一两个无足轻重的女人——那一程航程里，她们给这条船添了点受人欢迎的小小意外，——还谈一位牌运好得出奇的同船客人，那手气足可证明魔鬼确实存在。可是过了一会儿，当谈话变得五花八门、这些生人两两之间低声谈起各自谨慎的话题时，坐在科利特身旁的那个人磕空了烟斗，朝他俯过身来，像是偶然起了一个私下想说说的念头。

「现在觉得好些了吗？」

「很好。」

===Original===
That was as far as it went. The stranger began to refill a beautiful briar with some rich tobacco which moved Colet with a sudden yearning. But the stranger was unaware of it. He lovingly loaded that ripe bowl, and Colet watched the rite with the happy knowledge that he had come back to the sun, that sights and smells were good, and that there were pleasant things to be done.

“What happens to you, may I ask, after an occasion of this sort? What do you do?”

“I’ve no idea. Sent home, I suppose.”

“But an official has to worry about it, presumably. They ought not to land you at Rangoon and just leave it at that.”

===Chinese===
话到此为止。那生人开始往一只漂亮的石楠木烟斗里装味道醇厚的板烟，那烟忽然叫科利特心头涌起一阵渴念。可生人并不察觉。他满心爱惜地装满那只熟透的烟斗钵，科利特望着这一套仪式，怀着快乐的确信：他回到了太阳底下，目之所见、鼻之所闻都是好的，而且还有种种愉快的事情可做。

「恕我动问，经过了这样一场事，往后你落个什么下场？打算怎么办？」

「不晓得。大概是遣送回国吧。」

「不过想必总有位官员要为这事操心。他们不该把你送上仰光（Rangoon）的岸就撒手不管。」

===Original===
“Rangoon?”

“Where we’re going. You knew that?”

“No. I forgot to ask.”

The stranger was amused. “I guess you’re right. Any old place would do for me, after an outing like yours.”

“Do you know Rangoon?”

===Chinese===
「仰光？」

「我们要去的地方。你不知道？」

「不知道。我忘了问。」

那生人觉得有趣。「我看你说得对。像你这样出门逛了一遭，随便什么地方，对我来说都行。」

「你熟悉仰光吗？」

===Original===
“Pretty well. All round from that purgatory to Bangkok.”

“The names sound very attractive.”

“They do? How one forgets!” His amusement was faint but provocative. “Yes, I suppose they sound attractive. Must have sounded so to me once. Must have.”

“And now they don’t. That’s the worst of disillusionment. The real thing goes.”

===Chinese===
「相当熟。从那座炼狱到曼谷，一路我都跑遍了。」

「这些地名听起来都很迷人。」

「是吗？人多么健忘！」他那点笑意淡淡的，却带着撩拨。「是啊，我想它们听起来是迷人。从前在我耳朵里想必也迷人过。一定迷人过。」

「如今却不迷人了。幻灭最坏的地方就在这儿。真的东西没有了。」

===Original===
“Eh? I’m not disillusioned. I’m busy. I heard you all right, didn’t I? Didn’t you say the real thing goes when we know the reality? Now, what on earth do you mean by that?”

“Well﻿ ﻿… of course it means nothing. Only a little doubt about the nature of the reality, perhaps.”

“You’ll feel stronger presently. We understand reality well enough, when we bark our shins on it. Now, I say that’s the fun of it, seeing what things are in good time, and treating them as they deserve; don’t you?”

“I expect you are right.”

===Chinese===
「嗯？我可不幻灭。我忙得很。你的话我听得清清楚楚，不是吗？你是不是说，真东西一等到我们认清了现实就没有了？你这话究竟是什么意思？」

「唔……当然，这话没什么意思。不过是对于现实的本性，稍许有一点疑心罢了。」

「过一会儿你气力足些就好了。现实这东西，等我们在它上面磕破了小腿，也就懂得够透彻了。要我说，妙就妙在这儿：趁早看清事物的底细，照它们应得的分寸去对付；你说是不是？」

「我想你说得对。」

===Original===
“Sure of it. Like testing a piece of rock. That’s my job. Most people would call it road metalling. Good enough for them. But if you know what to do with it, it might mean﻿—it might mean kissing your hand to those places with names you think so attractive, and getting that deer park at home.”

Colet laughed. “Have you come across that magic lump of road metalling yet?”

“Not so far. Only something a bit like it. Enough to keep me going on. Can’t have the park yet, but I could buy a fawn﻿—one or two nice little fawns.”

===Chinese===
「这还用说。就像检验一块矿石。那是我的本行。多数人只会管它叫筑路碎石。随他们去。可要是你晓得拿它怎么办，它就可能意味着——它可能意味着，向你觉得名字那么迷人的那些地方吻手作别，回家置起那座鹿园。」

科利特笑了。「那你至今碰上过那块有魔法的筑路碎石没有？」

「还没有。只碰上过一点略略像那么回事的东西。也够叫我接着干下去了。鹿园暂时还置不起，不过一只幼鹿还买得起——一两只漂亮的小幼鹿。」

===Original===
“Perhaps the fun is in the search, not in finding.”

“Say that again. It sounds interesting. Though I’d like to hear you say it after you’d ploughed through Siamese forests in the rains, punctured from head to foot by ticks and leeches, and no more to show for it than enough to buy quinine for the next bout of ‘the shakes.’”

“I think I’d say it then. The hunt’s the game. The quarry doesn’t matter so much.”

===Chinese===
「也许乐趣就在寻，不在于找见。」

「把这话再说一遍。听着挺有意思。不过我倒想听听，等你在雨季里跋涉过暹罗（Siam）的森林，从头到脚叫蜱虫和水蛭扎了个遍，到头来所获刚够买奎宁对付下一场『打摆子』，那时候你还能不能说出这句话来。」

「我想那时候我还是会这么说。打猎本身就是游戏。猎物倒在其次。」

===Original===
The stranger swerved in his chair to inspect Colet. He frowned at him comically, and his scrutiny was met with a candid conviction of good cheer. This stranger was a man some years older than himself, with a hard but amiable countenance, eyes that were mocking him now in good nature, and a little moustache which might have been cast in iron. There was a scar on his chin, and Colet judged that very likely it had been well earned. That chin would take some breaking, though. The man would take some breaking.

“I don’t see it. Though if you’re not looking for much perhaps you’re right. I’m looking for important metal that goes by troy weight; or by the ton, if it runs to it. When I find it I’m very loving. If I don’t find it I’m not happy at all. Your abstractions wouldn’t turn my scales with a martyr’s crown as makeweight.”

“Well﻿ ﻿… I wonder whether we wouldn’t look twice at the scales, if we know what was in them? Though of all possible things the last I want is martyrdom. It’s too lonely.”

===Chinese===
那生人在椅子里侧过身来打量科利特。他冲他滑稽地皱起眉头，而这番审视迎来的是一脸坦然的好兴致。这位生人比科利特大着几岁，面容刚硬却和善，一双眼睛此刻正善意地挪揄着他，唇上一撇小胡子，像是铁水浇铸出来的。他下巴上有道疤，科利特断定那多半是实打实挣来的。不过，那个下巴可不是轻易打得碎的。这个人也不是轻易折服得了的。

「我可不这么看。不过，要是你求的不多，也许你是对的。我找的是上秤用金衡计算的贵重金属；要是成色够，论吨算也行。找见的时候，我满怀爱意。找不见的时候，我一点儿也不快活。你那些玄想，就是搁上一顶殉道者的冠冕当砝码，也拨不动我的天平。」

「唔……我倒疑心，要是我们晓得秤盘里装的是什么，还会不会把那天平再看上两眼？不过在一切可能的事情里，我最不想要的就是殉道。那太孤单了。」

===Original===
“Here, your talk is quite Christian. I’m afraid to listen to it. Let’s have a merry drink and forget it.”

They waited while a steward, with no sign of emotion but the deepest respect, listened in turn and without a comment to the trivial wishes of that circle of men.

“So you don’t know what you’ll do at Rangoon?”

“Not in the least. I’m a destitute seaman.”

===Chinese===
「喂，你这番话十足是基督徒口气。再听下去我要害怕了。来快快活活喝一杯，把它忘了吧。」

他们等着，一名服务员脸上没有一丝表情、只有最深切的敬意，依次听取那一圈男人们琐碎的意愿，一言不发。

「这么说，到了仰光你打算怎么办，你一点儿也不晓得？」

「一点儿也不。我是个一文不名的海员。」

===Original===
“Um.”

“Eh? I said I am a destitute seaman.”

“I heard you. I only made the noise of a thought. You don’t know anyone at Rangoon?”

“Nobody. Never been there.”

===Chinese===
「嗯。」

「啊？我说了，我是个一文不名的海员。」

「我听见了。我不过是在替一个念头出声。你在仰光什么人也不认识？」

「谁也不认识。从来没去过。」

===Original===
“Well, I do know Rangoon, and anyone destitute there would be about as happy as a hungry dog in a stone yard.”

An elderly neighbour leaned towards them with a gentle contradiction. He had heard Rangoon mentioned, and he and the stranger began a blithe dispute about their destination which left that port uncertain between a dream city of Oriental delights, and a worthy abode of rectangular commercial ideals, in their satisfactory concrete, surrounded by quarters for ministrant coolies. Sinclair extricated Colet, and they began to pace the deck, and gossip of what had been. Colet learned that the end of his voyage was now but a matter of days.

===Chinese===
「我可是认得仰光的。谁要是穷无所依地落到那儿，就跟石头院子里的饿狗差不多快活。」

一位上了年纪的邻座朝他们俯过身来，温和地表示异议。他听见他们提起仰光，便同那生人就此行的目的地展开了一场欢快的争论，把那座港口悬而未决地留在了两可之间：它既是一座满是东方赏心乐事的梦中之城，又是一方方正的正派宅邸，合乎商业理想的直角，浇成叫人满意的混凝土，四下环绕着伺候人的苦力的住区。辛克莱尔把科利特解救出来，两人便沿着甲板踱起步来，闲话已往的种种。科利特这才得知，他这趟航程的尽头，如今不过是几天的事了。

===Original===
The surgeon, on his next visit, was perfunctory.

“I only call because I’m very dutiful. You are fit for treasons, stratagems, and spoils. Only be quite sure,” he added, “that the spoils come safely in at the end. By the way, that passenger you were talking to yesterday, Mr. Norrie. He’s coming here. There’s a book he wants you to look at. And here he is. I’m off to find your friend the engineer.”

Norrie took the settee.

===Chinese===
船医再来巡诊时，只敷衍了事。

「我来一趟，不过是因为我恪尽职守。你如今结实得可以去干叛乱、设谋、劫掠的营生了。只是千万记牢，」他补了一句，「劫掠的油水末了得平安落袋。顺便说一句，你昨天同他说话的那位客人，诺里（Norrie）先生。他要上这儿来。有本书他想请你看看。喏，他来了。我这就去找你那位轮机长朋友。」

诺里在舱室长椅上坐了下来。

===Original===
“I heard the doctor give you my name. It really is my name. And I have brought you a book. As good an excuse as any for walking uninvited into another man’s cabin.”

Colet regarded the book politely. It was not exactly technical, but it appeared to be a serious address to geologists and surveyors, should the thought ever occur to them of exploring the chances Siam held in secret. It was loaded with maps. They bore such legends as Chlorite and Hornblende rocks; Gold Sands; Gem Gravels; Sandstone. What was most attractive about it was its amateurish drawings of temples, boats, camps, and natives. While he turned the pages with respect, looking for something he might say for this kindness, he did, for a moment, become lost in a brief description of a place named Wat Chinareth﻿ ﻿… “far in the jungle, when the eye has become accustomed to green for weeks, the wonderful yellow-red of the tiles of the temple roof, picked off with green borders, and light-red lower buildings of the cloisters, were most striking﻿ ﻿… the entrance to the tower was in its day very prettily panelled and gilded; now, alas, bats and cobwebs are legion.”

===Chinese===
「我听见医生把我的名字告诉你了。那确实是我的名字。我还给你带来了一本书。不请自来闯进别人的舱门，这借口再好不过。」

科利特客客气气地端详这本书。它算不上严格的技术著作，倒像是一篇向地质学家与测量师发出的正经陈词——万一他们动了念头，想去探一探暹罗暗中握着的种种机会。书里塞满了地图。图上标着诸如此类的名目：绿泥石与角闪岩；金砂；宝石砾层；砂岩。书里最惹人喜爱的是那些业余手笔的素描，画着寺庙、船只、营地和土人。他恭恭敬敬地翻动书页，想找一句可说的话来谢这番好意，一时之间却当真看入了迷——那是对一处名叫奇纳雷寺（Wat Chinareth）的地方的一段短短的描写……『在丛林深处，当眼睛一连几个星期看惯了绿色之后，寺庙屋顶瓦片那种奇妙的黄红色，缘着绿边一衬，连同回廊下部浅红色的屋宇，最为夺目……塔的入口当年装镶得颇为雅致，嵌板描金；如今，唉，蝙蝠与蛛网已麇集成群。』

===Original===
“Don’t look for entertainment in it,” he was advised. “Look for facts, Mr. Colet. But not now. I have an idea, but you may not care for it. You’ll find in that book some faint hints of the barbarism of my own life, when poking about for a good substitute for that deer-park. I’m a prospector, that’s what; but I will say for myself that I was passed through the turnstiles of the Royal School of Mines. I won’t flatter you by suggesting that you could be my expert assistant. Field geology is not learned in a day. But, if you’re at a loose end, it would be pleasant if you could come along with me.”

Colet got up on his elbow in his bunk, and considered Norrie, but not his suggestion. His visitor gave no hint of a barbaric life. His dress was only a little less punctilious than the surgeon’s uniform. His intonation was that of one in authority. Colet twisted the point of his beard.

===Chinese===
「别指望在里头找消遣，」人家这样劝他。「要找事实，科利特先生。不过现在不忙。我有个主意，只是你不一定看得上。你会在那本书里找到我自己的生涯何等野蛮的些微端倪——那阵子我正四处扒剔，想替那座鹿园觅一个像样的替代品。我是个探矿的，就是这么回事；不过我可以替自己说一句，我是打皇家矿业学校（Royal School of Mines）的旋转栅门里放出来的。我不会奉承你，说你能当我的专业助手。野外地质学不是一天学得会的。可要是你闲着没事，能同我一道去，倒是件愉快的事。」

科利特在铺位上支起臂肘，端详起诺里来，却没去想他的提议。这位来客身上看不出一丝野蛮生涯的影子。他的衣着只比船医的制服稍稍随便一点。他的语调是发号施令之人的语调。科利特捻着自己胡子的尖。

===Original===
“But﻿—but﻿—of course, I shall be glad to read this book. It’s travel, anyhow. All the same, I can’t see where I could come in.”

“Naturally. You’ve never done it. If you had, you’d refuse. If you can believe me, I’m trying to beguile you into a most damnable passage. For my own sake, of course. Don’t make a mistake about that. I don’t like getting fever in the forest on my lonesome. It’s beginning to frighten me. Then there are the natives. They’re sometimes so volatile. Two could manage them better than one. If they make trouble, you might get it, and not me. That’s one advantage of a partner.”

His manner conveyed that he was explaining but the melancholy truth.

===Chinese===
「可是——可是——当然，我会很乐意读读这本书。不管怎么说，总算游记。只是我看不出我能派上什么用场。」

「那是自然。你从来没干过这一行。要是干过，你就该回绝了。你要是肯信我，我这是在诱你踏进一段最要命的航程。自然是为我自己打算。这一点你别弄错。我不喜欢孤零零一个人在林子里发热病。那开始叫我害怕了。再说还有土人。他们有时候那么反复无常。两个人管束他们总比一个人强。万一他们闹起事来，挨刀的可能是你，不是我。这是搭伙的一桩好处。」

他的神气表示，他无非是在说明一个可悲的事实。

===Original===
“Don’t let me persuade you. I want you to come, but not if you don’t feel like it.”

He began to fill that briar slowly, and once he rubbed it on his cheeks, as though caressing it. Colet considered the man, and thought of road metalling, levelled and hard. But there must be something in a fellow who could choose such a pipe, and treat it like that. Not easy to read that man, but easy to read his pipe. Go by the pipe, and jump for it?

Norrie began to form leisurely clouds, and to watch them unfold; and at his ease, in a reminiscent way, to insult the Orient attractively because he had nothing better to do just then.

===Chinese===
「别让我把你说动。我要你来，可你要是不情愿，就算了。」

他开始慢慢往那只石楠木烟斗里装烟，还一度把它贴在脸颊上蹭了蹭，像是在爱抚它。科利特掂量着这个人，想起筑路碎石，平整，坚硬。可是，一个挑得出这样一只烟斗、又这样待它的人，肚里必有几分货。这个人不易读，他的烟斗却容易读。就凭着烟斗下注，豁出去么？

诺里开始从容地吐出一团团云雾，看它们舒展开来；又趁闲在兴，用追忆的口吻，把东方迷人地糟蹋了一阵，因为那一刻他没有更好的事可做。

===Original===
Colet fumbled in a jacket pocket.

“Before you go any further, for the love of Heaven hand me your tobacco-pouch.”

“Oh, you smoke, do you? I thought you didn’t. That was a count against you.”

“I don’t know when last I had a pipe.” He held his nose over the smoke of his bowl. “Now, if you talk to me, I think I’d agree to go with you either to perdition or the pearly gates.”

===Chinese===
科利特在上衣口袋里摸索。

「趁你还没接着往下讲，看在老天的份上，把你的烟丝袋递给我。」

「哦，你抽烟，是吗？我还当你不抽呢。那可是记在你名下的一条账。」

「不记得上一回抽烟斗是什么时候了。」他把鼻子凑在自己烟斗的烟雾上。「这会儿你只要肯同我说话，我想，随你去沉沦之地还是进天国的珍珠门，我都肯跟你走。」

===Original===
“Then I shan’t say any more about it; not yet. Neither of those two places is in my prospectus.”

“No? Well, the very smell of your mixture warns me that it must be one or the other, and there’s no telling which.”

“Odd; I thought it was a better tobacco than that. I like to listen to you. That’s why I’ve come in. But don’t forget I’m a metallurgist. My only anodynes are the pipe and drink.”

===Chinese===
「那我就不再多说了；暂时不说了。那两个去处，哪一个也不在我的招股章程里。」

「不在？唔，光凭你这种混合板烟的气味，就警告我不是这一个便是那一个，而且没法说准是哪一个。」

「怪了；我还当这板烟比那更好些呢。我喜欢听你说话。我进来就为这个。可你别忘了，我是冶金学家。我唯一的镇痛剂，就是烟斗和酒。」

===Original===
“Is doubt about the nature of things an anodyne?”

Norrie was sadly emphatic. “Rather. That, or an infection. It’s debilitating. It’s worse than dysentery. It takes away a man’s inside. When I want something, I don’t doubt whether I ought to have it or not. The only doubt is, can I get it? That’s the miserable doubt, can I get it? Usually I can’t. No doubt about that.”

===Chinese===
「对事物本性的怀疑，算不算一种镇痛剂？」

诺里沉痛而斩钉截铁。「算。不是镇痛剂，就是传染病。它叫人虚弱。比痢疾还厉害。它把一个人的五脏都掏走。我想要一样东西的时候，从不怀疑自己该不该要。唯一的疑问是，我弄得到手么？那才是叫人难过的疑问——弄得到手么？多半弄不到。这一点倒毫无疑问。」

===Original===
Norrie, Colet thought, was a man worth looking at, large and satisfactorily indolent, but with quick and wary eyes which usually looked down his nose when he was talking, as if his converse was self-communion which you overhead. There was nobody else for him to look at, of course; nobody who mattered so much as himself, anyhow. But the man was kindly; the purring, perhaps, of one of the larger carnivora when it was well fed. Now and then, when he had a question, a sly glance went with it, instant but casual, and you knew your answer would have to be precise; no use to hedge; but, if you hedged, then at least he would sympathise with so human a failing. His taut moustache was dark, but its hint of cruelty was forgotton in his abundant hair, which was careless of definition, and allowed a few trumbles of grey waves to stray over his brow. His eyes and hair gave an assurance that he was often playfully human, while his long thin nose reminded Colet of a knife that would be always prompt to the usual bidding of that firm mouth with its lurking derision of scruples.

“Now, Colet, I’ve been talking of the jungle, not the pearly gates. I don’t like the jungle, but it happens to be a verifiable fact. And please don’t dwell on the thought of perdition. It makes me uncomfortable. Think of valuable deposits, which are much better. They might come in this life. Mine is a business notion. It isn’t metaphysical. Leave metaphysics to the senile, who take to thin joys because they can’t have babies. What I’m looking for is something a bank manager would respect; and yet it might come to nothing but quinine and another shot at the game later. Let me press the button for your steward. Let’s have something to make us hopeful and foolish.”

===Chinese===
诺里这个人，科利特想，值得一看：身材高大，一副叫人心安的慵懒，却有一双敏捷警觉的眼睛，说话时多半顺着鼻梁往下看，仿佛他的言谈是一场自言自语，不过叫你偶然听了去。当然，他也没有别的什么人可看；反正没有谁像他自己那样要紧。可这人是和善的；那或许是一头较大的食肉兽吃饱之后的低鸣。偶尔他提一个问题，便有一道狡黠的目光随之一闪，迅捷却又随意，你就晓得你的回答必须精确；支吾是没有用的；可你若支吾了，他至少会体谅这样一个人之常情的短处。他紧绷的胡子颜色深，但那一点残忍的意味，全被他那头浓发盖了过去——头发不理会什么轮廓分明，任几绺灰白的波浪漫步到额前。他的眼睛和头发叫人确信他常有一种顽皮的人情，而那只又长又薄的鼻子叫科利特想起一把刀，一听见那张坚定的嘴巴惯常的吩咐便立刻应命——那张嘴里对种种顾忌总藏着几分讥诮。

「好了，科利特，我说的可是丛林，不是天国的珍珠门。我不喜欢丛林，可它偏偏是一件可以验证的事实。也请你别老惦记着沉沦，那叫我不自在。想想有价值的矿藏吧，那好得多。它们或许今生就到手。我打的是一桩生意上的算盘，不是玄学。玄学留给老态龙钟的人吧，他们生不出孩子，只好去抓住些稀薄的快乐。我找的东西，是连银行经理都要敬重三分的；可到头来它也可能只剩下一把奎宁，日后从头再赌一局。让我替你揿铃唤你的服务员。来点东西，叫我们又怀着指望、又傻里傻气。」

## XXII / 二十二

===Original===
They arrived at Rangoon before the day had begun; and though that city had a name which had the appeal of a call of the oboe, yet Colet discovered that he was in no hurry to leave his cabin. Something had gone out of him. He could feel no magic in the Gulf of Martaban. He rebuked himself for that. He could not be fully alive, to be in Burma, and yet not to have the sense of a strange occasion. He might be still in Billiter Avenue. But perhaps the ship and the open boat had given to him all he could accept, for a while. There is a limit. Burma could not be much after the old *Altair*; and her modest little master was more significant than all the pagodas and lotus ponds.

Norrie strolled in, to remind him that there were lodgings to be found, that he was concerned with a wreck over which officials would be nosey, and that there was work to be done.

“You are coming with me now, and you’ll know it. Here’s Burma, but you won’t find any lotus about me, except after I’ve fed.”

===Chinese===
天还没有开始，他们便到了仰光；这座城的名字，原带着双簧管一声召唤那样的魅力，可科利特发觉，自己竟不急着离开舱房。他身上的什么东西散掉了。他感觉不出马达班湾（Gulf of Martaban）有什么魔法。他为此责备自己。人身在缅甸，却全没有奇遇临身之感，这不能算真正地活着。他简直还像在比利特大道上。不过，兴许是那条船和那条敞艇，已经把他一时间受得下的一切都给了他。凡事总有个限度。比起旧日的『牵牛星号』，缅甸多不出什么来；而她那位谦逊的小个子船长，比所有的佛塔和莲池都更有分量。

诺里踱了进来，提醒他：住处得去寻；一艘失事的船还牵着他，官员们免不了要在上头东嗅西嗅；再说，还有事要做。

「你这就跟我来，走一走就知道了。这儿是缅甸，可你在我身上找不出半朵莲花——除非等我吃饱了饭。」

===Original===
“I was just thinking of that pond weed. How is it I don’t feel very interested? And on the Irrawaddy, for the first time. Do you think I’m ill?”
