“Not a bit,” Norrie told him. “It shows your sound health. It’s just your blighting divination. You smelt the spicy breeze of this open counterfeit afar off. That’s all. You’ll smell it stronger presently.”

Off the two of them went. And Rangoon was not the expected vision of the Orient, nor was it fully of the West. It was an industrial western city which had got mislaid to sprawl in fatigue in the tropics. Languor and warehouses. Its white people were too hot and bored to get it replaced. They wanted to do it, but they had no will and energy for that. The brown folk had been drawn into the city, but had learned that it was not of their sort. They were enduring the orderly and stagnant heat till they had the means of escape.

===Chinese===
「我方才正想着那种池子里的水草呢。怎么我竟不怎么提得起兴致？何况是头一回待在伊洛瓦底江（Irrawaddy）上。你看我是不是病了？」

「一点儿也不，」诺里对他说。「这恰恰见出你身子康健。那不过是你那股败兴的先见之明。你老远就嗅出了这片露天的赝品那带香料的微风。就这么回事。待会儿你还会嗅得更浓呢。」

两人便出了门。仰光并非意料中那种东方的幻象，也不全然属于西方。它是一座西方的工业城市，被人错放了地方，摊开在热带的倦怠里。慵懒，外加货栈。城里的白人热得、也闷得懒得把它换掉。他们想换，却没了那份心气与精力。棕色的人们被吸进了城，却已明白这不是他们那一类的所在。他们忍着这井然而又死滞的炎热，只等攒下脱身的法子。

===Original===
In a hotel bedroom hot and dim, where the electric light was needed though it was morning, Colet looked at the grey mosquito curtains of the bed and asked, involuntarily, how long they would have to stay there.

“Well, until the proper person within the meaning of the act has discovered to what extent you are stained criminally. You’ve gone and lost your ship, you know. As a seaman you are suspect, naturally. A man of your intelligence isn’t a seaman for nothing, either. It’s against nature. After I’ve bailed you out, you can make for Penang.”

“What shall I do there?”

===Chinese===
在一间又热又暗的旅馆卧室里——虽是清晨也得开着电灯——科利特望着床上灰色的蚊帐，不由自主地问起，他们在这儿还得待多久。

「唔，得等到法令条文所指的那位于事相宜者，查明你沾染的刑事污点到了几分地步为止。你可把船给弄丢了，要知道。身为海员，你自然是嫌疑之人。再说，像你这么有头脑的人，当海员也不会是白当的。这逆着天性。等我把你保出来，你就动身去槟城。」

「我在那儿做什么呢？」

===Original===
“I don’t know. They do funny things there. But I’ll tell you carefully, at the proper time, what I want you to do there.”

Norrie surveyed him plaintively.

“I don’t like your style, now I come to think of it. You can’t go about with me like that. My friends would think I’d given up mining, and was going after locusts and wild honey with a sacred crank who would look more picturesque in goatskins. It would never do. You don’t know how particular we are in the tropics. Come along.”

===Chinese===
「我不知道。他们在那儿干些古怪事。不过到了合适的时候，我会仔仔细细告诉你，我要你在那儿做什么。」

诺里哀婉地打量了他一番。

「如今细想起来，我不喜欢你这副做派。你可不能这样跟着我四处走动。我的朋友们会当我扔下了矿业，跟着一位神圣的怪人去追蝗虫和野蜜，而这位怪人要披上山羊皮才更像一幅画。那可万万使不得。你不晓得我们在热带有多讲究。走吧。」

===Original===
And Norrie, though he affected to be indolent, was casually familiar with the devious city; he wasted no time in his search for what was wanted. The stores, to Colet’s surprise, would have been appropriate in the Brompton Road, and his companion was no better than a shrewd and ironic housewife who had been wearied by the ways of the keepers of shops, yet had to meet them as penance for existence, and so converted them into an opportunity for foolish amusement. He knew too much of the secret motives for the trifling devices of commerce. He sported in a feline way with the disarming candour of salesmen, leading them on with unsmiling humbug till they were sure their skill was happy. Colet began to see that with Norrie he would be protected from the guile of the worldly, because to his companion that was only the cunning of children, simple and endearing. It was enough to make a diverting interlude when not engaged in the really serious task of beguiling the greater powers.

===Chinese===
诺里虽然装出一副慵懒相，对这座曲里拐弯的城市却早随随便便地烂熟于心；他要寻什么，一点工夫也不糟蹋。那些铺面——叫科利特吃惊的是——搁在布朗普顿路（Brompton Road）上也尽不失体面；而他的同伴，说穿了不过是个精明而带讥诮的主妇，早被店主们的做派磨乏了，却还得把他们当作在世修行的苦赎去一一应付，索性把这门苦差使变成一场愚蠢的取乐。买卖场里那些琐碎花招背后藏着什么动机，他知道得太多。他猫儿戏鼠一般，耍弄着店伙那叫人卸下防备的坦白，绷着一张脸把他们往迷魂阵里带，带得他们个个深信自己的手段正得心应手。科利特开始看出，有诺里在，俗世的奸猾近不了他的身，因为在他同伴眼里，那不过是孩童的狡黠，又简单，又招人疼。有这个尽够当一出插科打诨的过场了——只要他还腾得出手，不正在忙着去办那件真正正经的大事：蒙骗那些更大的权势。

===Original===
Colet was released early from the quiet questioning of officials whose knowledge of ships, he realised with a little alarm, would have been infernal, had it been applied with the intent to put gyves on the wicked. They knew too much about ships. He learned, in that brief cross-examination, that no enterprise on earth is under so many jealous eyes as a ship on the high seas. And when, immediately after that episode was closed, the little coaster *Nibong* put out from Rangoon for Penang, and he was its only white passenger, Sinclair said farewell. As the coaster’s siren warned that she was about to depart, Colet had a suspicion that he would have been glad to be returning to London instead, with his old shipmate. Penang had but a minor inducement, and Norrie, though engaging, was new and unpredictable. It was not easy to know for certain which possessions were better worth cherishing, but at least he was reluctant to let Sinclair go. And Sinclair seemed reluctant to go. He was cheered by that. There could be genuine dubiety over the implications of what, in a hurry, people often called good luck, but his friend Sinclair, he was admonished to see, did not enjoy this inevitable break from a chance messmate; there was little to be said about such a trifle of human understanding, and nothing about it at all to Sinclair, but it was of happier augury than the most hopeful hint of valuable deposits in Siam, or wherever they were.

===Chinese===
科利特早早地便脱开了官员们那场安静的盘问。他带着几分惊觉认识到，这些人关于船舶的学问，若是存心用来给恶人上镣铐，那真是地狱一般。他们对船懂得太多了。在那一场简短的盘诘里，他领教了：普天之下，再没有哪一桩事业，像大海上一条船那样，受着这么多双嫉妒的眼睛的盯视。这桩事一收场，小型的近海轮『尼邦号』（*Nibong*）随即驶离仰光前往槟城，船上的白人乘客只他一人，辛克莱尔便来道别。近海轮的汽笛呜呜警告着船就要启程，科利特这时隐隐觉得，自己倒宁愿这会儿是同老同伴一道回伦敦去。槟城那点诱饵无足轻重，诺里虽然招人喜欢，到底是新交，还捉摸不定。哪些身外之物更值得珍惜，一时实在难说；可至少，他不情愿放辛克莱尔走。而辛克莱尔似乎也不情愿走。这一点叫他心里一暖。人们匆忙间唤作好运的那个东西，内里究竟意味着什么，尽有货真价实的可疑之处；可他的朋友辛克莱尔——他像是被人点醒了一句——并不乐享同一位萍水同席者的这场必然的离散；这样一桩人情的小节目，本来说不上几句话，对辛克莱尔更是一个字也提不得，然而它却是吉兆，比暹罗、比随便什么地方的宝贵矿藏所能给出的最有指望的暗示，还要吉祥。

===Original===
Sinclair marched stiffly away in that brisk manner, and he did not look back. Sinclair had gone; but chance had added Sinclair to his store of riches, anyhow, though no bank manager would look at that credit. Perhaps additions to good fortune were always so, imponderable, unaccountable, and of no use to anyone. Yet they were positive. His knowledge of Sinclair and that bunch of men of his old ship gave to an aimless and sprawling world the assurance of anonymous courage and faith waiting in the sordid muddle for a signal, ready when it came. There were men like that. You could never tell where they were. They were only the crowd. There was nothing to distinguish them. They had no names. They were nobodies. But, when they were wanted, there they were; and when they had finished their task they disappeared, leaving no sign, except in the heart. Without the certainty of that artless and profitless fidelity of simple souls the great ocean would be as silly as the welter of doom undesigned, and the shining importance of the august affairs of the flourishing cities worth no more homage than the brickbats of Babylon. Those people gave to God any countenance by which He could be known.

===Chinese===
辛克莱尔迈着僵直的步子，以他那副干脆利落的姿态走远了，没有回头。辛克莱尔去了；可是机缘到底已经把辛克莱尔添进了他的财富库里，尽管哪一位银行经理也不会朝那笔进账看上一眼。也许好运的增添，向来就是这个样子：称不出分量，讲不出缘由，对谁都派不上用场。然而它们确凿是实在的。他对辛克莱尔、对旧船上那一伙人的相识，使这个漫无目标、四下里蔓延的世界有了一重保票：无名的勇气与信义正守在污浊的乱摊子里候一个信号，信号一到，随时应命。这样的人是有的。你永远说不准他们在哪儿。他们不过是人群。没有什么能把他们分辨出来。他们没有名字。他们是无名之辈。可是用得着的时候，他们就在那儿；差事一完，他们便消散了，不留痕迹——除了在心里。若没有这份单纯灵魂不设机心、不图回报的忠贞作凭据，这汪洋大海便同一场无设计的末日翻腾一样地愚蠢，繁华诸城那许多庄严事务的煊赫分量，也值不得比巴比伦（Babylon）的碎砖更多一点的敬意。是这些人，给了上帝一张能教人认出来的脸面。

===Original===
The informal little steamer, the quiet, the radiant day, and the seclusion compelled by his difference from that crowd of noisy native passengers on the deck below, brought him back to a central loneliness. They were off. He began to hear from himself again. His brief glimpse of the East at Rangoon had not been disturbing only because he had gone about in the shadow of Norrie. But he remembered that Rangoon’s slow flux of multitudinous people, like the movement in a nightmare, had been appalling with its reminder of a sightless ooze, without more distinction between fear and laughter in it than there is in the flat expanse of a grey river descending forever to be lost in the deep. A slow pour of viscid life, just like the tide home-going over London Bridge for the trains, moving to a compulsion which was as dark in its nature as a starless patch in the clear sky of night; What was there? Nobody would ever know. If you gazed too long at that abyss of night you would cover your eyes to hide your thoughts. It would not bear looking at. That mob below him on the foredeck was as alien as anything nameless could be. It was a separated puddle, still slowly eddying with the original impulse.

A woman’s voice in it brought his vague survey of the mixture of moving and noisy colours to a focus. A diminutive brown creature in a green sheath, with a silver stud in her nose, was wandering about and calling a name﻿—it sounded like a name. She was seeking somebody in the mob, which took no notice of her, and she appeared to be anxious. She was as though the light of understanding had been let into that ship. She had separated from the agglomerate, and had a personal voice. Then she stopped below his rail. She was bending over a yellow bundle. A child was asleep there in a patch of shadow, with a face as pale and still as a flower upturned to the moon, and its frail hands curled as loosely as fallen petals. Of course, the dark patch in the sky is that only because we cannot do any better with it; not near enough to it; not our place in the sky. That child was as Sinclair, and the woman as himself. There is no such thing as humanity. There are men and women.

===Chinese===
这条不拘形迹的小汽船，这份清静，这个亮晃晃的日子，再加上他同下层甲板上那一群喧闹的土著乘客之间的格格不入——这层幽独是那份差别强加给他的——把他送回到一种居中的孤寂里。船开了。他又开始听得见自己的动静了。在仰光对东方匆匆的一瞥之所以不曾搅扰他，只因他是在诺里的影子里走动的。可是他记得，仰光那万头攒动的人河缓缓的涌流，像噩梦里的动作，曾是骇人的——它叫人想起一片没有视力的淤泥，其间恐惧与欢笑的分辨，并不比一条灰色大河平摊开去的水面更多；那条河永远向下流，流进深处去消失。一注黏稠的生命缓缓倾泻，正像涌过伦敦桥赶火车的回家的人潮，受着一种强制而动——那强制的本性，暗得如同晴朗夜空里一块没有星星的补丁。那里面有什么？永远不会有人知道。你若对那夜的深渊盯视得太久，就得捂住眼睛，藏起自己的念头。它是经不起看的。他下方前甲板上的那群人，同任何无名之物一样隔膜。那是一洼分离出来的水洼，还凭着最初那股劲头缓缓打着旋。

人堆里一个女人的声音，把他对着那一团动着、响着的色彩的茫然扫视，聚成了一个焦点。一个小小的棕色人儿，裹着一袭绿色的紧身筒裙，鼻上嵌一粒银饰，正走来走去地呼唤一个名字——听着倒像个名字。她在人堆里寻什么人，那人堆并不理会她，而她显得焦灼。她这就仿佛是一线领悟之光，透进了那条船。她已从那团聚合物里分离了出来，有了一个属己的声音。随后她停在他栏杆底下。她正俯身向一卷黄色的襁褓。一个孩子睡在那片阴影里，一张脸苍白而安静，像一朵仰向月亮的花，纤弱的小手松松地蜷着，像落下来的花瓣。当然，天上那块黑斑之所以是那副样子，只因为我们拿它想不出更好的办法；离它不够近；那不是我们在天上的位置。那孩子正如辛克莱尔，那女人正如他自己。世上并没有人性这样一个东西。有的是男人和女人。

===Original===
The day glowed and was lazy; it was a blue intensity loosened with a tincture of gold. No land was in sight. Yet, with a sea dreaming in so virtuous a sleep that peace itself would have thought that here was its home, a traveller did not look for land. The sky was gracious, brooding in solicitude over their little company of chattering innocents. Their modicum of a steamer﻿—for her size checked Colet with the idea, when he boarded her, that she was venturesome﻿—was enjoying herself as freely as though she knew nothing ever darkened that favoured region.

Colet began to feel, after the first day, that he had drifted into another sphere. He watched a company of brown people squatting by a hatch, whose happy stoicism and doll-like figures were appropriate to ancient tranquillity and unchanging skies. They did not know harshness. They made on stringed instruments improbable music of a tenuous appeal which was heard by a part of his mind of which he knew nothing. It was music in accord with that aromatic foredeck. That was a smell he did not know, any more than the music, but if they were foreign they were known to a dream that was mislaid somewhere in his memory.

===Chinese===
这日子明艳而懒散；那是一种蓝色的浓烈，掺了一丝金，便松快下来。望不见陆地。然而海上做着那样一场贞静的酣梦，连安宁本身也会认定这儿就是它的家，旅人便也不去寻陆地了。天空是宽厚的，满怀操心，覆在这一小群叽叽喳喳的无辜者头顶。他们那小小一泓汽船——她的尺寸，科利特上船时便叫他起过一个念头：这条船可真敢闯——正自由自在地尽兴，仿佛从不晓得有什么东西曾叫这块受宠的水域变过脸色。

过了头一天，科利特开始觉得，自己漂进了另一重天地。他望着一小群棕色的人蹲在一道舱口旁，他们那种乐天的坚忍、洋娃娃似的身形，正与古老的安宁和不变的天空相称。他们不认得苛酷这回事。他们在弦乐器上弄出一种不像真能有的音乐，那召唤稀薄缥缈，却被他心智里某个部分听见了——他对那个部分一无所知。这音乐同那芳香的前甲板正相合。那是一种他不认得的气味，正如他不认得那音乐；它们纵然是外来的，却被一场梦认得——那梦遗失在他记忆里的什么地方。

===Original===
The *Nibong* had many ports of call. Each place appeared unexpectedly, as though by chance. The ports had rare names. A legend, as it were, would precipitate as a silent appearance, and remain by the ship for an hour. If he came out from his cabin to look at it again, it had gone. It had melted into the sky. He began to suppose that this voyage was one without purpose, as vague as the drowsy afternoons. The course of their toy ship was set to nothing more than a fancy generated like the music, fixed by the whimsicality of a song. They paid calls to get settings for the music: Moulmein, Martaban, Tavoy, Mergui. It did not matter where they went. The company on the foredeck changed from place to place, but its colours were the same, and music was always there.

They had a coast to port, after Moulmein; it was that, or a perennial enchantment. Probably it was but the promise of a kingdom, the auspice of a happier time. It was not the spectacle of solid earth. Beyond the indeterminate green where the foliage met the sea there were heights. They might have been mountains, but when you were not watching them their violet outlines changed. When next they were seen they had risen into stupendous ranges of cobalt and bitumen, too massive for clouds, too high for mountains; and that meridian range was hollowed, as lightning glared and quivered in its body, by profound gulfs, abysms seen for an incredible instant, and then gone. The clouds and the far mountains were of the same nature as the constellation of islands through which the *Nibong* was threading. Norrie, when Colet was leaving him, had an afterthought. He mentioned a place called Mergui.

===Chinese===
『尼邦号』沿途有许多停靠的口岸。每一处都不期而至，仿佛出于偶然。那些港名一个比一个罕闻。一段传说，就这样，会凝成一次无声的显现，在船旁守上一个钟头。他若从舱里再出来看它一眼，它已经去了。它已化进天空里。他开始猜想，这一趟航行是一趟没有目的的航行，同瞌睡的午后一样茫然。他们这条玩具船的航路，不系在别的上头，只系在一个像那音乐一般生出来的奇想，由一支歌的任性定住。他们沿途做拜访，是为给那音乐寻布景：毛淡棉（Moulmein）、马达班、土瓦（Tavoy）、丹老（Mergui）。去哪儿都无关紧要。前甲板上那一伙人一处一换，色彩却总是那几样，音乐也总在。

过了毛淡棉，他们的左舷边有了一道海岸；说它是海岸也罢，不如说是一桩常年不褪的魔法。多半，那不过是一个王国的许诺，一段更幸福时辰的吉兆。它不是坚实大地的景观。在树梢与海相接的那道界限不明的绿色之外，有高地。它们也许是山，可你一不留神，它们紫罗兰色的轮廓便变了。待再看时，它们已经升起，成了钴蓝与沥青色的巍峨山脉，作为云嫌太重，作为山嫌太高；而那条正午的山脉，随着闪电在它躯体里灼灼颤动，被掏空出一道道深谷——深渊只在叫人不敢信的一瞬里望见，随即去了。云与远山，同『尼邦号』正在其间穿行的群岛，是同一副本性。诺里在科利特向他辞行时灵机一动，还提起过一个叫丹老的地方。

===Original===
“Look out for the Mergui Islands. Be careful. Unless it blows hard you’ll want to land on one of them. If you do you’ll forget all that I’ve told you, and I shan’t see you again.”

Here the islands were. But nobody, of course, ever had landed on them. They were unknown to man, except as fond reflections. They were as silent as unspoken thoughts. They were versatile. The thoughts changed. The next was better than the last. As the *Nibong* cruised through the vision Colet saw faceted stones interrupting that polished level of lapis-lazuli, small mounds of emerald on the mirror, bergs of white marble, glimpses of retirements with coral thresholds leading to palms and a forested mountain under a spell. A canoe or two was seen, but they melted round promontories. The secrets were undisclosed. The shades which haunted that seclusion faded when men approached from the outer world.

===Chinese===
「留神丹老群岛（Mergui Islands）。当心。只要不起大风，你就会想在其中的一个岛上登岸。你要是真登了岸，我告诉你的一切你就要忘个干净，我也再见不着你了。」

群岛就在这儿了。可是不消说，从来谁也不曾在它们上头登过陆。它们不为人知，只作为一厢疼爱的倒影存在。它们同没有说出口的心思一样安静。它们是善变的。心思会变。后一个比前一个好。『尼邦号』游过这片幻景时，科利特看见一粒粒琢磨出棱面的宝石，打断那打磨光亮的青金石平面；明镜上一座座翡翠的小丘，一座座白大理石的冰山；还瞥见几处隐居——珊瑚的门限，通向棕榈，通向一座着了魔咒的满山林木的山。望得见一两只独木舟，可它们绕过海岬便化了。秘密未曾泄露。出没于那幽僻里的阴翳，一有人自外面的世界走近，便褪尽了。

===Original===
Colet was told, later, that they were nearing Penang. It was time, he thought, to do that, and to touch reality. The ship should not cruise off the map for too long, or they might never get on to it again. Now, at last, she was close to a proper shore, a high coast solid and brilliant as it looked to the setting sun. There were great ships, and the level light discovered pale houses recessed within the green of the hills. But as he found it, even as he reached it, it went. The sea was transfigured to a shining expanse of carnelian, and the very air was the rosy glow of a heatless fire. Penang and its sea assumed another nature as he watched. Its only affinity to earth was a faint odour of pepper, and the sound of a gong.

===Chinese===
后来有人告诉科利特，船快到槟城了。也该是时候了，他想，该去碰一碰现实。船不该在地图外面游荡得太久，不然也许再也回不到图上去了。如今她终于挨近了一道像样的海岸，一处高岸，在落日的眼中又坚实又辉煌。有大船在那里，平射的光照见一栋栋苍白的房子，深深嵌在山峦的绿意里。可是他一寻到它，就在他够着它的当口，它去了。大海变容，成了一片红玉髓（carnelian）的明晃晃的浩渺，连空气本身也成了一场无火之焰的玫瑰色光晕。他望着，槟城和它的海换上了另一副本性。它与尘世仅有的亲缘，是一缕淡淡的胡椒气味，和一声锣响。

## XXIII / 二十三

===Original===
If he had landed on the further side of the moon he could not have been more in the dark. Those letters with which Norrie had armed him﻿—chits, Norrie called them﻿—they were all very well, but in a way they made the darkness of Penang a bit more questionable; not darker, but more eccentric. One of the letters was to a house called Senang. That suggested the local variety of Clovelly or Bella Vista. To ask for it might be like asking a London policeman for a prayer-meeting. Not worth risking it. One of Norrie’s little jokes, possibly. His fun could be esoteric. His allusions were oblique. And there was another chit to a Mr. Ah Loi. But not likely. Not after dark. Much rather see this Ah Loi first in the daytime. A Chinese friend of Norrie’s should be guardedly approached, in a morning light preferably.

He warned off a loitering Chinaman who attempted to grab his bag. The man did not go away, but stood there watching him, in fierce reproach. Still, if he attempted to carry that bag himself he would sweat till it guttered from his eyebrows. He was sticky already. The night was close. Night in a tropical city, to a stranger new from northern mud, was like the hearthrug to a fish free from the aquarium. It was not according to custom. He watched men trotting past drawing passengers in light go-carts. One of these carts swept close to him, and stopped, its man picked up the bag as though he had come for that, and Colet climbed up after it. The man glided off in silence at a long easy lope. Colet called out to his man that he wanted an hotel, but the beggar swung on without taking any notice; didn’t know what he was talking about, naturally; but of course that fellow had guessed the right thing to do for a newcomer.

===Chinese===
他纵是降落在月亮的背面，也不会比这更两眼摸黑。诺里给他当行头的那几封信——诺里管它们叫「条子」——东西本来不坏，可在某种意义上，它们把槟城的黑暗弄得更可疑了些；不是更黑，而是更邪性。其中一封，是写给一家唤作「瑟南」（Senang）的宅子的。这名字叫人想起本地土产的克洛弗利（Clovelly）或者贝拉维斯塔（Bella Vista）。要去打听它，多半就像拦住一位伦敦警察打听祷告会。犯不上冒这个险。没准儿又是诺里的一个小玩笑。他的玩笑有时刻意冷僻。他的用典向来是斜的。还有一张条子，是给一位阿洛伊先生（Mr. Ah Loi）的。可这靠不住。天黑以后不去。这位阿洛伊，宁可先在白天见。诺里的中国朋友，得提防着接近，最好拣一个上午的光。

他喝退了一个游荡的华人，那人正想抢他的提包。那人不走，就站在那儿望着他，一脸激烈的责备。话虽如此，他若想自己拎那只提包，就得淌汗，一直淌得像烛泪似的从眉毛上挂下来。他身上已经黏糊糊的了。夜气闷热不透风。热带城市的夜，对一个刚从北国泥泞里出来的生客，就像一条出了水族缸的鱼被搁在炉前的地毯上。这不合规矩。他望见人们拉着轻便的小车、载着乘客一路小跑而过。其中一辆车擦着他掠到跟前，停住，那人拎起提包，倒像他专程就是为这个来的，科利特便跟着爬了上去。那人一声不吭，迈着绵长而轻快的大步，滑行而去。科利特朝那人大声喊，说他要找一家旅馆，可那家伙只管晃着走，全不理会；自然，不懂他讲的是什么；不过这家伙当然猜着了，该为一个新来乍到的人做什么。

===Original===
Colet saw that Penang was a city varied and curious enough to mislead a wayfarer easily to an interesting predicament. Its bazaars were involved, continuous, and their wares problematical. Yet it could not matter what was sold by shops illuminated with paper lanterns and advertised by scarlet banners covered with cabalistic symbols. The maze of people moved as would the characters of a ballet with an incomprehensible rhythm, or of a charade of which the secret would never be guessed. They had their ardours in pursuits not to be known by him. Somehow his orbit had coincided with theirs. He felt as absurdly conspicuous there, on his high perch, as though he had chanced on the stage where actors in a privily illuminated masquerade were rehearsing the Lord knew what. It had nothing to do with him, and yet there he was. Luckily, nobody there had an idea he had barged into them. He might have been watching the mystery while protected by the gift of invisibility. He was unseen.

Or else﻿—had these figures agreed to allow him to deceive himself? For occasionally he thought that by some weakening of his protective magic he became faintly and transiently visible. Once a monstrously tall ogre with a black beard parted to hook its inordinate length behind the ears, a sentinel who once might have turned a dark and discerning eye on intruding Sinbad, appeared to have seen him. Yes, certainly that Sikh policeman saw him.

===Chinese===
科利特看出，槟城这座城花样百出，也古怪得很，轻易就能把一个过客引到一个耐人寻味的窘境里去。它的巴扎纠缠不断、连成一片，货色也暧昧可疑。可是那些铺面，一面靠纸灯笼照明，一面靠猩红旗幡招摇，幡上画满天书般的符字——它们卖什么，倒也无关紧要。人的迷宫蠕动着，像一出芭蕾里的人物，踏着一种叫人没法懂的节奏；又像一场字谜戏，谜底永远休想猜着。他们在各自的营生里怀着热焰，那些营生他无从知晓。不知怎么，他的轨道竟同他们的重合了。他坐在高高的座位上，觉得自己蠢头蠢脑地扎眼，仿佛误打误撞闯上了一座戏台，台上一场私下照亮的假面舞会，伶人们正在排演天晓得的名堂。这本与他毫不相干，可他偏偏在那儿。幸而那儿谁也不曾觉出他闯进了他们中间。他尽可以像一个得了隐身法护佑的人那样，旁观这场玄秘。他是看不见的。

再不然——莫非是这些人物商量好了，容他自己骗自己？因为偶尔他觉得，他那护身法术有些松劲，他变得隐约而短暂地可以看见了。有一回，一个高得出奇的妖怪，黑胡子分作两绺，把那过分的长度挂到耳后——这样一位哨兵，当年说不定也曾向擅闯的辛巴德转过一只阴沉而慧眼独具的眼睛——像是瞧见他了。是的，那个锡克（Sikh）警察肯定瞧见他了。

===Original===
They came, beyond the last bazaar, to the design of a palm crown blacked high on the stars. It stood over a grotesque cornice, and under the framing eaves was the golden hollow of a room open to the night. Within that room three girls, three pale sibyls swathed in filmy tissue, sat absorbed in contemplation of the runes. Colet did not descend, though the Chinaman lowered the shafts of the jinrickshaw as though here his service ended. The scene was too much like a picture story without a name to prompt anything more than a puzzled stare. One of the sibyls, whose pale face was not like Europe, but moonlight, and to whom, Colet fancied, had been delegated the announcement of whatever oracle had been resolved for a visiting stranger, languorously rose, looked towards him with eyes which entreated a release from a weird, and spoke at the window in a discreet and mild version of English. And Colet’s Chinaman, though he did not understand that language, knew at once Colet’s peremptory order. He knew he had made a mistake. He picked up his shafts and loped from the shade of that grove, leaving a tinkle of merriment behind.

===Chinese===
出了最后一处巴扎，他们来到一幅图样跟前：一圈棕榈的冠冕，黑黑地高印在星星上。它罩在一道奇形怪状的檐口上，框子似的屋檐底下，是一间向夜敞开的房间的金色凹壁。那房间里坐着三个姑娘，三个苍白的西比尔（sibyl），裹在薄如烟雾的纱衣里，正一心一意参悟着神符。科利特没有下车，虽然那华人把人力车（jinrickshaw）的车把放了下来，仿佛他的差事到这儿就算到了头。这幅光景太像一出没有名字的看图故事，只能引得人茫然瞪视，旁的什么也引不起。其中一个西比尔——那张苍白的脸不像欧洲，倒像月光；科利特猜想，凡是为来访生客议定下的神谕，都派定由她来宣布——慵懒地起了身，朝他这边望过来，一双眼睛恳求着从一道符咒里获释，然后立在窗口，用一种谨慎而温文的英语开了口。科利特的那位华人虽不懂那语言，却立刻会意了科利特斩钉截铁的命令。他明白自己走错了一步。他拾起车把，从那片树荫里小跑着出来，身后留下一串欢快的笑语叮当。

===Original===
Soon he was at a standstill again beside another conjuration of a welcome in the shades, and its light showed several frail idols in embroidered silk, with faces of chalk, their foreheads hidden in black fringes, oblique eyes which had no thoughts, and lips that were little crimson buttons. They resembled, for a moment, the illustration to a beautiful fable of Cathay, but Colet’s loud voice shattered the spell before his man had ever lowered the shafts. The coolie turned about, with wondering disapproval, wiped his shaven crown with his disengaged hand, and tried again. This time he took longer, and when he halted it was before an Indian enticement, some with studs in their ascetic nostrils, their slight bodies bedight for the gaze of idling rajahs.

And then the rickshaw man knew he was right. He did not hesitate. With definition and relief he concluded his journey; and his astonishment was obvious when Colet leaped from the go-cart with savage energy. The Chinaman’s recoil was that of innocence surprisingly attacked. Colet could see, by the light from the room, that his man was now at the end of his resources, and was obviously worried; the fellow was protesting that he had done all he could. Colet pointed to the glow of the city behind them, and made emphatic indicatory noises. The man seemed to understand. He must have guessed that, somehow, he was at fault, though for no reason that he could ever know. He turned about, dolefully.

===Chinese===
不多时他又停了下来，这一回挨着浓荫里另一场迎客的幻术；那灯光照出几位纤弱的偶像，穿着绣花的绸缎，脸是粉笔的白，前额掩在黑色的刘海底下，斜斜的眼睛里没有心思，嘴唇是小小的绛红纽扣。一时间，她们像极了古中国（Cathay）一则美丽寓言里的插图；可是科利特的大嗓门不等他那人放低车把，先已把这道魔咒震得粉碎。那苦力转过身来，带着诧异的不以为然，用闲着的那只手抹了抹剃光的头顶，再试一回。这一回他走得久些，停下时，是在一处印度的魅惑跟前：有几个，苦行者的鼻翼上嵌着饰钉，清瘦的身子妆扮起来，正给闲得发慌的拉贾（rajah）们受看。

于是人力车夫知道他对了。他不再迟疑。他目标既明、如释重负地收了这一程的尾；待到科利特野人似的从那辆小车上蹦将下来，他那吃惊是明摆着的。那华人的一个倒退，是无辜者突遭袭击的姿势。借着房里照出的光，科利特看得出，他那人到这会儿已是智穷力竭，分明在发愁；那家伙正表白呢，说他能想的主意都想到了。科利特指向他们身后城市的光焰，连声发出斩钉截铁的指示。那人像是懂了。他必定猜到了，不知怎么的，错处在他，尽管那缘由他永远无从知晓。他垂头丧气地调转了头。

===Original===
Colet, with his face to those lights, though now disturbed, sustained his faith that Penang somewhere must have a different roof to offer; and down by the waterfront, within a short distance of the quay where the adventure had begun, there it was. The hotel, except that its servants in their white uniforms were of the East, and that the building was adjusted, as well as was possible, to a temperature not altogether within the control of its management, might have been on the Riviera. As far as it could, it excluded the tropics. The hotel used its ingenuity to suggest that it was not where it was, looking out over the heated dark which brooded, with lightning glimmering in its roof, above the Strait of Malacca. When the bluish glare was vivid, then sleeping palms appeared in the foreground like tracings in ink on burnished metal.

===Chinese===
科利特脸朝着那些灯火——这会儿心里虽已着了慌——却仍守着他的信念：槟城总该有个别样的屋檐可投；到了滨水地带，离那场奇遇开头的码头短短一段路的地方，果然有了。那家旅馆，若不是它那些一身白制服的仆役出自东方，若不是那栋楼已尽其所能地迁就了一种管理层全然管束不住的气温，它满可以坐落在里维埃拉（Riviera）。它尽其所能地把热带关在门外。这家旅馆煞费巧思，要叫人相信它并不在它所在的地方；它窗外俯瞰着那片滚烫的黑暗——黑暗蹲踞在马六甲海峡（Strait of Malacca）上空，脊背里曳着电光。蓝幽幽的闪光一亮，沉睡的棕榈便显在眼前，像亮金属上勾的墨线。

## XXIV / 二十四

===Original===
There was no difficulty, after all, in discovering a Mr. Ah Loi. The hotel people knew of him. Even a rickshaw man, when challenged, made almost satisfactory signs of intelligence. Colet viewed him suspiciously, speculating whether this was the genie of the night before, still hanging about in the hope of improved bewitchery. But Penang, on his first morning in Malaya, was superior to all the trickery of mortals. It was as fair as though this were the original daylight of the earth. The morning was certainly heated by a sun with pristine strength, but the air was perfumed, and it sparkled. He thought the sapphire between them and the island of Sumatra was younger, after all, than the tales of Marco Polo and the ancient voyagers. One junk was suspended in it, the first to explore that blue. As he rode through the bazaars and by the shrubberies beyond he was joyously confident that he was equal to the wiles of any Chinaman.

His man, this time, knew where to go, and turned in at a gateway flanked by a pair of porcelain beasts that were not dragons and ought not to have been dogs. Beyond a garden was a large pinkish house, not unlike a temple.

Its door was open, but the house, he was afraid, was deserted. Its interior smelt of teak, or some unusual wood. There was not a sound, except that of an insect making a dry whispering in the garden. The hall subdued its English visitor with its severe integrity, for its sombre panels receded in almost a bare perspective. It was relieved by only a few white silk hangings bearing delicate images of Buddha, water fowl, bamboos, and flowers. The tiled floor was muted with old rugs which made Colet forget, as he looked at them, why he had called. And he had called with the unthinking courage of a fellow bringing a bill of exchange. The fine texture and quiet of this interior began to reduce his confidence with the challenge of another order of things. There was no bell; should he clap his hands?

Apparently his thought had been heard, for a genie in a blue tunic approached him, and kowtowed in perfect gravity though it did not speak. It led him to an inner room and left him.

What at once was seen there, and nothing else, was a bowl of pale jade that appeared to give the silence a faint light, as though it were a lamp. It was honourably isolated and elevated, as though it were the significance of a poet. It was then that Colet noticed that the backs of his hands were not only moist with sweat, but a little hairy. He did not care to approach that luminous fragility; he looked about and saw by his side some shelves of books. They were as unusual as the bowl; perhaps they were even stranger, in that place. They were of European mathematics, philosophy, and theology, and though a chance collection of books on such subjects is placed, usually, where it will not be in the way, the names on the backs of those volumes betrayed a knowledge of the latest mental enterprises of the Occident which shook Colet’s confidence in the range of his own reading.

===Chinese===
要找一位阿洛伊（Ah Loi）先生，到头来竟毫不费力。旅馆的人知道他。连人力车夫，被一问，也做出几乎叫人满意的了然神色。科利特疑心地打量他，心里揣摩：这是不是昨夜那位精灵，还在近旁流连，指望妖法再有长进。可这是他到马来亚（Malaya）的第一个早晨，槟城（Penang）之美，压倒了一切凡人的伎俩。它明净得仿佛这就是大地开天辟地最初的日光。清晨固然有一轮太阳以洪荒之力烘晒，但空气含着香气，而且闪闪发亮。他想，他们与苏门答腊（Sumatra）岛之间的那片蓝宝石色，终究比马可·波罗（Marco Polo）和古代航海家们的传说还要年轻。一艘中国帆船悬在那片蓝色里，是头一个来探这蓝色的。乘车穿过集市、再掠过更远处的灌木林时，他欢欣地自信：任何中国人的伎俩，他都应付得了。

这一回，他的车夫认得路，转进一道大门，门旁蹲着一对瓷兽，不是龙，也不该是狗。园子后面是一座挺大的粉红色宅子，颇像一座庙。

宅门敞着，可他恐怕宅子里空无一人。屋内有一股柚木味，或是某种不寻常木料的气味。四下没有一点声息，只有园子里一只虫子发出干干的絮语。厅堂以它森严的端方镇住了这位英国来客，那些暗色的壁板向后退去，几乎退成一片素净的纵深。只以几幅白丝挂幔稍解其峻，幔上是精巧的佛像、水禽、修竹与花卉。瓷砖地面叫旧地毯衬得悄无声息，科利特望着那些地毯，竟忘了自己为何来访。而他来访时，原带着一个送汇票的家伙那种不假思索的勇气。这内宅精微的质地与宁静，带着另一种秩序的挑战，开始消减他的自信。这里没有铃；他该不该拍拍手？

显然他的心思被听见了，因为一个穿蓝布褂子的精灵向他走来，虽不出声，却端端正正磕了一个头。精灵引他进了一间内室，便退下了。

那屋里第一眼看见、而且除了它什么也看不见的，是一只青白玉碗，它仿佛给寂静添了一脉微光，仿佛是一盏灯。它被恭而敬之地单独供着、垫得高高的，仿佛它便是一位诗人的精义。就在那时，科利特注意到自己的手背不但汗津津，还毛茸茸的。他不愿凑近那发光的脆弱之物；他环顾左右，看见身旁有几架书。那些书与那只碗一样不寻常；在那地方，它们也许更出奇。那是欧洲的数学、哲学与神学书籍，这一类书若是偶然凑成的一堆，通常总搁在不碍事的地方，可这些书脊上的名字，显露出对西方最新心智事业的了解，动摇了科利特对自己读书范围的自信。

===Original===
“You are interested in philosophy, Mr. Colet?”

How had that voice got there? Mr. Ah Loi was behind him. Interested in philosophy! Ah Loi had a friendly smile. He was a smile, but not much more than that; an interrogatory appraisement, tolerant and cheerful. His face was rather like his bowl of jade, delicate, pale, and bright. Colet would have preferred to wipe his hot large hands before taking the one which his host offered to him. This was confusing.

“It interested me to see those books here.”

“Why, Mr. Colet? Are they out of place?”

There it was. Colet knew his first words had been as hairy as his hands.

Ah Loi was not old. He was not young. His years were merely a clarity of the spirit. He spoke English as though his home overlooked an Oxford precinct. Behind him, no doubt the portière through which he had entered the room, were crimson silk hangings; they dropped in heavy folds from the high ceiling and were waved on the floor. They were lettered in gold with Chinese characters, and embroidered figures of men and dragons were ambushed in their coils. The slight figure of Ah Loi, in his western suit of linen, cool and friendly, with that draping of old China for his background, was as noticeable as a gentle word. Of course, Colet thought, this blessed Chinaman had those European books here to learn what the rude children were doing, when out of sight.

===Chinese===
「您对哲学有兴趣，科利特先生？」

那声音是怎么进来的？阿洛伊先生就在他身后。对哲学有兴趣！阿洛伊带着友好的微笑。他就是一副微笑，此外不多什么；一种含问的估量，宽容而愉快。他的脸颇像他那只玉碗，精致、苍白、明亮。主人伸出一只手来，科利特倒情愿先把两只滚烫的大手擦一擦再去握。这真叫人着慌。

「看见这些书在这儿，我觉得有趣。」

「怎么，科利特先生？它们放得不合适吗？」

果然来了。科利特知道，自己头一句话已经跟他的手一样多毛了。

阿洛伊不老。也不年轻。他的年岁只是心神的一种澄澈。他说起英语来，仿佛他的家就俯临牛津的一处院落。他身后——想必是他进屋时经过的那道门帘——是大红的丝幔；它们从高高的天花板沉沉垂下，褶层厚重，末端在地板上漾开。幔上用金字题着汉字，绣出来的人像和龙蟠伏在褶皱间设着埋伏。阿洛伊清瘦的身形，穿一身西式亚麻西装，凉爽而友好，背后衬着那一幅古老中国的垂幔，像一句温厚的话那样惹人注目。当然喽，科利特想，这位有福的中国人把那些欧洲书摆在这儿，是要看看那些粗野的孩子背地里干些什么。

===Original===
“The truth is, Mr. Ah Loi, I only looked at these books because I had not the courage to go closer to your fine bowl there.”

“That? That bowl? Come and handle it. Such things are made to be touched, as well as looked at. The touch should know as much as the eye.”

Colet nerved himself and turned the bowl about. He realised that its frailty was but simplicity and strength, which were unctuous and cool. Ah Loi took it, and replaced it.

“Sometimes,” said the Chinaman, “I have wondered whether Western culture turned into chimney smoke because of a neglected sense of touch. You see, you must pause and weigh it, when you handle an object. You have time to change your mind.”

The man in the blue tunic was there again, and Ah Loi spoke to him. The servant brought in bottles, ice, and a syphon.

“You will have a *stengah*, Mr. Colet?”

===Chinese===
「实话说，阿洛伊先生，我方才看这些书，只是因为没胆量走近您那边那只精美的碗。」

「那个？那只碗？来，上手摆弄摆弄。这样的东西造出来，原是既给人看、也给人摸的。触觉该和眼睛一样懂它。」

科利特鼓起勇气，把碗转了个圈。他这才明白，它的脆弱不过是朴素与坚实，摸上去温润沁凉。阿洛伊接过碗，放回原处。

「有时候，」这位中国人说，「我纳闷，西方文化之所以化成了烟囱里的烟，是不是就因为疏忽了触觉。你要知道，手里掂着一样东西，人就得停一停、称一称。人便来得及回心转意。」

穿蓝布褂子的人又来了，阿洛伊对他吩咐了几句。仆人端进来酒瓶、冰块和一只苏打水瓶。

「您来一杯『司登加』（stengah）吧，科利特先生？」

===Original===
“I don’t know what that is.”

“Then you are certainly new to this country. It is a small whisky and soda, the half of a tonic, as you say. A Malay word. It means ‘half.’ But you English use it, besides for whisky, for a person of mixed blood.”

“Thank you. But no *stengah* for me, if you please. Not now.”

“You have been in Penang only five minutes.” Ah Loi was amused. “Wait till Norrie comes,” he added. “At one time we Chinamen, who find it not easy to understand, kept champagne for our English guests. We heard so much about champagne that we thought it must be the same as your happiness. But now it is whisky. Well, let us talk about Norrie. He is our friend. You know him very well?”

“No. Only a little.”

“A little of him is good.”

===Chinese===
「我不知道那是什么。」

「那您对这个国家必定是初来乍到。那是一小杯威士忌苏打，如您所说，半剂补药。一个马来词。意思是『半』。不过你们英国人拿它，除了指威士忌，还指混血的人。」

「谢谢。可我不要『司登加』了，劳驾。现在不要。」

「您到槟城才五分钟。」阿洛伊给逗乐了。「且等诺里来了再说。」他补了一句。「有一阵子，我们这些中国人——我们觉得这事不容易明白——待英国客人备的是香槟。香槟我们听得太多了，便想它必定和你们的快乐是一回事。如今是威士忌了。好，说说诺里吧。他是我们的朋友。您很了解他？」

「不。只有一点。」

「他这个人，懂一点正好。」

===Original===
“I met him on the voyage out.”

“He is going to Pahang?”

“I don’t know quite what he intends to do.”

Ah Loi looked at his bowl.

“Nobody knows that. But he is going round to the other side of the peninsula, and he will know why. I like Norrie. He would have been the same as a Cantonese. Yet he is a Londoner.”

“He has no place, then?”

===Chinese===
「我是在出来的那趟船上遇见他的。」

「他要去彭亨（Pahang）？」

「我不大清楚他打算做什么。」

阿洛伊望着他的碗。

「那个谁也不知道。不过他要绕到半岛另一边去，到时候他自会知道为什么。我喜欢诺里。他生为广东人也会是这同一个诺里。可他偏偏是个伦敦人。」

「那么，他没有自己的地方？」

===Original===
“Yes. It is all his place. It is all one. He knows.”

“Well, Mr. Ah Loi, I should like to know. What is it one has to know?”

“I think perhaps you know too. But the best things have no name.”

“Not even such as that bowl? What about that?”

“Of course not. That is but a sign.”

“Then we cannot talk about them.”

===Chinese===
「有。处处都是他的地方。处处都是一回事。他懂。」

「那么，阿洛伊先生，我很想知道。人非懂不可的究竟是什么？」

「我想您大概也懂。只是最好的东西没有名字。」

「连那只碗那样的东西也没有？那只碗怎么说？」

「当然没有。那不过是一个征象。」

「那我们就没法谈它们了。」

===Original===
“Oh yes. We do little else, when we are together, but they are not named. What shall we say, shall we say they are the communion? Come and see my porcelain. You will stay to tiffin?”

Colet, for an hour, received glimpses into a past which heartened him with a confirmation of his nebulous and shifting faith. Even a glaze for porcelain could persist, like the thought of an anonymous benevolence. Once he expressed a poignant concern for the safety of these lovely shapes and colours. Ah Loi did not altogether sympathise with him.

“They may all go, some day. There are accidental fires, and men riot. The world is rough, and it is careless. The world is abundant. But you see, Mr. Colet, these things have been done, and so they cannot be lost. They have been added, and they cannot be taken out of the sum. Tell me why it was your Shakespeare did not think it worth the trouble to preserve his poetry? I think that is the strangest thing about Shakespeare. That is why he is the most significant poet. Perhaps that indifference is his greatest gift to us.”

The Englishman supposed that they were alone in the house, but Mrs. Ah Loi met them at tiffin, and Colet’s memory of precious rarities went in a new confusion. She was not a Malay, though slender in a green sarong and a white muslin tunic. The gold buttons of her tunic were her only adornment. She was hardly Chinese, and certainly not English, even with that abundance of brown hair. The simple cordiality and assurance of her greeting meant that she was well used to visitors. She accepted Colet as though he were a frequent guest, but that made her fastidious hand no easier to grasp. She spoke to her husband with a droll mimicry of indifference. Where had he been all the morning? There had been *stengahs*, of course, and before midday, too. Colet saw that her banter disclosed a glint of serious intent. Ah Loi assured her that not a cork had been disturbed. She then gave Colet an innocent glance, not of disbelief, but of surprise. Ah Loi convinced her.

“Mr. Colet has not been long in Penang. He got here only last night. Give him a little more time, my love.”

===Chinese===
「哦，谈得成。我们聚在一起时，别的少谈，谈的差不多全是它们，只是不叫出名字。该怎么说呢——就说它们是圣餐，怎么样？来看我的瓷器吧。您留下用午餐（tiffin）？」

足足一个钟头，科利特得以窥见一段往昔，它印证了他那团云雾般游移的信仰，叫他精神一振。连瓷器上的一层釉也能长存，像一桩无名善举留下的念想。有一回，他对这些可爱形色的安危道出一番深切的忧虑。阿洛伊并不完全与他同感。

「它们总有一天会统统没的。有失火的意外，也有人心的骚乱。这世界粗粝，而且漫不经心。这世界又丰饶。可是您看，科利特先生，这些东西已经做成了，所以就不会失传。它们已经加了进去，再也不能从总和里扣除。告诉我，为什么你们那位莎士比亚，竟不肯费神保存自己的诗？我想那是莎士比亚身上最奇怪的一件事。也正因此，他才是意义最重大的诗人。那份无动于衷，也许是他给我们的最大的礼物。」

英国人原以为宅中只有他们两个，可午餐时阿洛伊太太出来了，科利特对珍稀之物的记忆又陷入一场新的迷乱。她不是马来人，虽然一袭绿色纱笼、一件白色细麻上衣衬得她纤细。上衣的金纽扣是她唯一的妆饰。她算不上中国人，也肯定不是英国人，尽管有那一头浓密的棕发。她问好的那份爽朗与自在，说明她见惯了来客。她接纳科利特，仿佛他是常来的客人，可这并没有让她那只矜持考究的手更容易一握。她跟丈夫说话，装出一副淡漠的滑稽腔调。他一上午都在哪儿？自然是喝了『司登加』，还不到正午就喝了。科利特看得出，她的打趣里闪着一丝认真的锋芒。阿洛伊向她保证，连一个软木塞都不曾动过。她于是给了科利特清白的一瞥，不是不信，而是惊奇。阿洛伊把她说服了。

「科利特先生到槟城不久。他昨天夜里才到。多给他一点时间，我亲爱的。」

===Original===
“Yes, but is he not a friend of Mr. Norrie?” She pronounced the vowels of the name with a comically slow precision. Ah Loi avoided a mention of Norrie.

“We have been talking of Kuan-yin and much else.”

“It is pleasant to talk of her; but Mr. Colet, he does not know her.”

“Oh, but he does.”

===Chinese===
「是，可他不是诺里先生的朋友吗？」她把这个名字的元音念得又慢又准，滑稽可笑。阿洛伊避而不提诺里。

「我们谈了观音，还谈了许多别的。」

「谈谈她是愉快的；可是科利特先生，他不认识她。」

「哦，他认识的。」

===Original===
“He is a collector?” There was a shade of anticipated disappointment in her voice.

“Oh no. He admires.”

“Then I shall like you, Mr. Colet.”

===Chinese===
「他是个收藏家？」她的声音里有一丝预先备好的失望。

「哦，不是。他只是敬慕。」

「那我会喜欢您的，科利特先生。」

===Original===
The more attractive a woman is, the more the resolution needed to look at her; their laughter was freedom to Colet for a candid glance at beauty that was unusual and debateable. Others might not like it. Beauty may cause a little fear. Her dark eyes were large for so small a face, and their soft uncritical light gave Colet a suspicion that she could penetrate to the thoughts at the back of his head. Her eyes, which only seemed slow because their lids were a trifle sleepy, did not rest on one’s face, though they looked at it. She listened, but not so much to what you said as to your reservations; or else she pondered childishly, finding it difficult to understand. (Don’t deceive yourself, Colet thought; she understands very well.) Her face was wide for that delicate chin. But then, of course, her brow had to find room for those eyes. Only the rather high cheekbones were faintly tinctured with colour. She would have been a rarity in the court of Kubla. The nose was the more diminished by the bold curves of her lips, which really looked like the Orient.

Colet began to speak of Norrie, but Mr. Ah Loi smiled uneasily, moving an object or two about the table.

“My wife does not like him. Norrie is a little different for each of us.”

===Chinese===
女人越是动人，端详她就越需要决断；方才的一阵笑，给了科利特自由，叫他得以坦然看一眼那份异样而可议的美。别人未必喜欢。美是会叫人有点害怕的。她那双深色的眼睛，配在这么小的一张脸上显得大，那柔和的、不加分辩的目光，叫科利特疑心她能一直看进他后脑勺里的念头。她的眼睛显得迟缓，只因眼皮带着几分睡意；那双眼睛望着一张脸，却不落在那张脸上。她听着，但与其说听你说出口的，不如说听你没说出口的保留；再不然就是孩子气地出神，觉得不容易明白。（别哄自己了，科利特想；她明白得很。）配着那小巧的下巴，她的脸显得宽。不过，那额头自然要给那双眼睛腾出地方来。只有颧骨略高处淡淡晕着一点血色。若在忽必烈（Kubla）的宫廷里，她也算得一件珍品。鼻子越发显得小了，衬着双唇大胆的曲线——那嘴唇才真像东方。

科利特刚要说起诺里，阿洛伊先生却笑得不安，把桌上一两件东西挪来挪去。

「我太太不喜欢他。我们每个人眼里，诺里都有一点不一样。」

===Original===
“A little? Mr. Norrie is evil,” she said.

Her husband protested ardently.

“No, no. He is not that. I know what you mean. He is naughty, but he is not evil.”

===Chinese===
「一点？诺里先生是恶。」她说。

丈夫热切地抗议。

「不，不。他不是那样。我懂你的意思。他顽劣，可他不恶。」

===Original===
“Yet you tell me he understands.”

“Yes, and for me, that saves him. He is a sad man, for he knows too much, but he had accepted so very little. He is a little amused by all the gods. I am sorry for Norrie.”

“I like him, too, when I am talking to him,” she confessed. “But not after. Then I remember that he knows, yet smiles. He is only polite to you,” she advised her husband, “but he smiles when he goes away.”

===Chinese===
「可你跟我说，他懂。」

「是的，在我这儿，这一点救了他。他是个悲哀的人，因为他懂得太多，接受得太少。他对所有的神明都带着点戏笑。我为诺里难过。」

「我同他说话的时候，也是喜欢他的。」她招认道。「可过后就不了。过后我就想起，他明明懂，却只是笑。他对你不过是客气。」她告诫丈夫，「可他走的时候，脸上带着笑。」

===Original===
“I know he does.” Ah Loi admitted it. “I know him. Yes. There is no ultimate value, for him. Think of that. It has been killed by his science, which is﻿—what is it?﻿—the formulation of dirt. He must pay for that, of course. But he does not understand the penalty.”

“Then he doesn’t understand after all,” she challenged.

“Well, no, not the last things. We must lose all the first good things if we do not understand the last. It is sad not to have ears to hear, especially if one hears so well as Norrie.”

===Chinese===
「我知道他笑。」阿洛伊承认。「我了解他。是的。对他来说，没有终极的价值。想想这个。它已经被他的科学杀死了——他的科学是什么来着？——是给污垢立条目。他自然得为此付出代价。可他不懂那代价。」

「那他到底还是不懂。」她质问道。

「唉，是，最后的事他不懂。我们若不懂最后的事，就必定失去最初的一切好处。没有能听的耳朵，是可悲的，尤其若一个人听得像诺里那样好。」

## XXV / 二十五

===Original===
In his Penang bedroom, alone with what he did not know of the tropics, Colet guessed he was inappropriate to that variety of dark. It was not only a covert dark. Its nature was foreign. It was unlike the nights of the north. The boards of the room were bare, and they were a deep red. The room was too large and high for one small glim, and it contained but a wardrobe, a table, a chair, and a bed enclosed in a muslin box. It resembled a large meat safe, that bed. At a glance by lamplight the gauze did not quite hide the fact that there was a body in his bed; but he saw it was a bolster lying fore and aft, the uninvited Dutch wife. It was there for some reason well known in the locality, of course, but it was a silly mitigation.

The hot night came close up to you. It tried to keep you from moving. It was an obstructing presence, mum and unseen, but heavy. Yet it was full of a sly stirring, though always behind you. Something was going on in it. Nothing there when you looked round. He went to the wardrobe, and the opening of its door surprised whatever was hanging about in that. A crack flashed in a zigzag across the back of the cupboard. He imagined he heard the movement, but when he looked closer there was no doubt the wood was all right. The crack had gone. Nothing in the cupboard. Nothing he could see.

He went to the table and began to write. His moist hands made the paper damp, and then the ink spread into blots; but if you tried to write while poising the hand, to give it air, then the paper became sportive under the pen. It was so ominously quiet that he heard a tiny voice at his ear. A mosquito was about. But that solitary beast had got at his bare foot. He held the light over the floor, and saw a dusky flight of gnats undulating about his ankles. Nothing for it but the inside of the meat safe.

===Chinese===
在槟城的卧房里，独自面对他对热带一无所知的种种，科利特觉得自己与那一种黑暗格格不入。那不只是一种掩藏的黑。它的性情是异邦的。它不像北方的夜。房里的木板光着，是一种深红色。屋子对一盏小灯而言太大太高，屋里只有一座衣柜、一张桌子、一把椅子，和一张封在薄纱匣子里的床。那张床像一只大号的食品纱橱。灯下扫一眼，纱幔没能完全遮住床上有个身体这一事实；可他看出来了，那是一条纵放着的长枕，一位不请自来的「荷兰妻子」（Dutch wife，热带长枕）。它摆在那儿自有本地人人皆知的道理，当然，可这是一种愚蠢的缓解。

炎夜紧贴着你。它存心不让你动弹。它是一种碍事的在场，闷声不响、无形无影，却沉甸甸的。然而它又满是鬼鬼祟祟的窸窣，只是总在你背后。里头有什么事在进行。回头去看，什么也没有。他走到衣柜前，柜门一开，惊动了里头逗留的什么。一道裂缝曲折地闪过柜子背板。他仿佛听见了那动静，可凑近细看，木板分明完好。裂缝没了。柜里空空。他能看见的，什么也没有。

他走到桌前动手写字。汗湿的手把纸洇潮了，墨水便漫作一团团墨渍；可你若悬着手腕写，给手透透气，纸又在笔底下顽皮起来。四下静得凶险，静得他听见耳边一个细细的声音。一只蚊子在近旁。可那独往独来的野兽已经叮上了他赤裸的脚。他把灯举向地板，看见一队昏黑的蠓虫绕着他的脚踝起伏翻飞。没办法，只好钻进食品纱橱里去。

===Original===
Then a creature harsh and green, a sort of gaunt and membraneous moth, if it was a moth and not a heartless joke, plumped on his writing-pad. He immediately surrendered the pad, as that thing wanted it. What name among the bugbears had this beastly object? Its green wings were like a petticoat of leaves about its waist. There was a phantasmal head at the end of a stalk-like neck. It had a chin. It turned its cadaverous face lugubriously towards him, and waved its hands in dispraise. He didn’t like it. It didn’t like him, either. Its long thin arms, which wearily motioned him to keep off, had grappling hooks for hands. One of the Little People maybe; the Malayan sort. Not from the fairy rings and the daisies pied, but out of the jungle. When he moved it flew away to a corner of his ambiguous resting-place.

Better see where this thing went. As he lifted the lamp, shadows from the ceiling came down the walls to go with him. There the thing was, on the floor. Its grappling hooks were raised, as though in the act of malevolent prayer. But it took no notice of him. It had no time for him. It had other business. That triangular face was watching something else, straight before it. He followed its gaze. A shaggy spider, as large as a straddling mouse, with minute eyes like twin starboard lights, was observing the mantid. Colet was glad he was not either of them, glad that he was only the mystified audience of this show. The two horrors sat staring at each other, each waiting for midnight to strike, or else for the other to make the first move. Human life was not the only problem of life. The chimeras on the floor knew that; each of them knew something that is not in Plato. They did not move. Their apprehensions must have been tense enough to snap. Colet moved, and their thoughts snapped. He did not see what happened. There was a mingling of green and black, or else the mantid leaped forward and was caught in the spider’s mouth.

An unusual bedroom. Dreams were there before you slept. A place of torment for Cimmerian eccentricities, a cockpit for boggarts and kobolds. He was sure now that something was in that cupboard, and he wondered, while the floorboards were clear for the journey, how long it would take to get under the mosquito netting once the lamp was blown out. A loud voice addressed him from the wood beams of the ceiling; a clear whistling cluck. He could make out a brief slender shadow up there which was cast by no visible object; and, anyhow, it was too small for so much loud confidence. It was motionless. It was a mistake, that reptilian mark. It was a stain in the wood. The voice spoke again aloft, *chee-chak, chee-chak*; not an unpleasant sound; rather like shameless and noisy kissing. The little shadow writhed forward a yard, as though the lamp had been shifted quietly, and that prompted similar shadows to move above, though the lamp remained still; abrupt divergent wriggles of creatures upside down. The ceiling was populated with lizards; one fell to the floor, rather solidly. That smack knocked the stuffing out of it. No. It was off﻿—going to get into his bed, perhaps. A close night.

===Chinese===
随后一只刺目的绿色生物——姑且算一种瘦削膜翅的蛾子吧，如果它是蛾子、而不是一个狠心的玩笑——扑通落在他的写字垫板上。他立刻把垫板拱手让出，既然那东西想要它。这个可憎的物件，在妖魔鬼怪的名册里唤作什么名字？它的绿翅膀像一圈叶子衬裙拢在腰间。一根梗子似的长颈顶着一颗幻影般的头。它还有下巴。它把那张死人似的脸凄凄惨惨地转向他，摆着双手表示不以为然。他不喜欢它。它也不喜欢他。那两条又长又细的胳膊有气无力地示意他走开，胳膊梢上的手竟是抓钩。说不定是小人族（the Little People）里的一员；马来亚的那一支。不是出自仙人环和斑驳雏菊的那一族，而是从丛林里出来的。他一动，它就飞进他这个身份暧昧的栖身之所的一个角落里去了。

还是去看看这东西去了哪儿。他一举灯，天花板上的影子便顺着墙下来，跟他同行。那东西就在地板上。它的抓钩高高举起，仿佛正做着一场恶毒的祈祷。可它不理会他。它没工夫理会他。它另有正事。那张三角脸正盯着正前方，看着别的东西。他顺着它的目光望去。一只毛茸茸的蜘蛛，大得像一匹叉开腿蹲着的老鼠，一双细小的眼睛像两盏右舷灯，正在观察那只螳螂。科利特庆幸自己不是它们中的任何一个，庆幸自己只是这场戏里莫名其妙的一名看客。两个恐怖之物对坐互瞪，各自等着午夜钟响，或者等对方先动第一招。人的生命并不是生命唯一的问题。地板上那两只奇物懂这个；它们各懂得一样柏拉图（Plato）书里没有的东西。它们不动。它们的惊惧想必绷得就要断了。科利特一动，它们的念头便断了。他没看见发生了什么。只见绿色和黑色混作一处，再不然就是那螳螂向前一跃，落进了蜘蛛口中。

一间不寻常的卧房。人还没睡，梦已先到了。一个供幽冥怪僻受折磨的地方，一个家妖地精的斗场。他如今拿准那柜子里有东西了，便趁地板空着好赶路，琢磨着灯一吹灭之后，要多久才钻得进蚊帐里去。一个响亮的声音从天花板的木梁上对他开腔；一声清脆的、吹口哨似的咯咯。他辨出上头有一道细短的影子，却不见任何投下它的实物；况且那么小的一点，配不上这么响亮的自信。它一动不动。是看错了，那道爬虫似的印子。那是木头里的一块斑。那声音又在上头开腔，『唧喀、唧喀』（chee-chak）；不算难听；倒像不知羞耻的、闹哄哄的亲吻。那小影子扭着向前挪了一码，仿佛灯被悄悄挪动过，这又引得头顶上几道相仿的影子动了起来，虽然灯原是不动的；那是些肚皮朝天的生灵突然四散分岔的扭动。天花板上住满了壁虎；有一只掉到地板上，还挺结实。那一摔叫它散了架。不对。它是跑掉了——多半是想到他床上去。一个闷热的夜。

===Original===
Did anyone ever manage to sleep through a tropic night? Not likely. You turned over, and then found that that side was hotter than the other. You turned over again. Not a sound. The lizards had ceased to kiss aloud, now the light was out. But a swift slithering passed over the boards beneath the bed. The silence was the heat. The heat was muffled. The silence was soft and hot, but heavy. It could not be pushed away. The darkness outside the curtains was waiting. For what? He was waiting too, for sleep, but it was no good waiting for that when the unseen was waiting for something else. The idea of that bolster beside him was to keep him cool, but it was an imbecile lump. He pushed it off with petulance.

That spider. There it was. It was looking at him out of a tunnel. Its eyes were as big as the headlights of a locomotive about to emerge. To emerge at any moment. Its hairy legs filled the tunnel. Its hunched legs made the tunnel dark. He could not move because he could not breathe. He was being held down for that brute. It was coming out. It put a hairy foot on his mouth. Faugh! That released him. Touched him off. The bolster was in his face. It was night still, not morning. Daylight was slow about it.

When next he woke the day was nearly there. The night had thinned; everything in the room could be seen in it, even through the mosquito curtain. It was cool at last, but the silence had not been broken.

===Chinese===
有谁当真睡得过一整个热带之夜？怕是没有。你翻个身，便发现这一面比那一面更热。再翻过去。一点声息也无。灯灭了，壁虎不再高声亲吻。可一道飞快的滑行掠过床底下的地板。寂静就是炎热。炎热裹着棉絮。那寂静又软又热，却沉甸甸的。推不开。帐外的黑暗在等着。等什么？他也在等，等睡着，可当看不见的东西在等别的什么时，干等睡着是白等。身边那条长枕，本意是叫他凉快，可它是个痴愚的呆块。他烦躁地把它推下了床。

那只蜘蛛。它就在那儿。它正从一条隧道里望着他。它的眼睛大得像两盏即将探出的火车头前灯。随时要探出来。它多毛的腿塞满了隧道。它拱起的腿把隧道堵得漆黑。他动不了，因为他喘不上气。他被按住了，留给那畜生享用。它出来了。它把一只毛脚搁上了他的嘴。呸！这一下把他释放了。把他点着了。长枕贴在他脸上。还是夜里，不是早晨。天亮磨磨蹭蹭地不肯来。

再一觉醒来，白昼快到了。夜稀薄了；屋里样样都看得出，隔着蚊帐也看得出。终于凉快了，可寂静还没有破。

===Original===
Now it had gone. A bird was fluting in the garden, trying to remember a morning song. Strange, he felt rather like singing himself, though sleep had been only an intermittent nightmare. In that cool grey light the bare room was merely bare. That reminiscent bird had not yet got the tune right, but he kept at it on his lonesome, a meditative and conscientious little fellow. Quietly trying it over before anyone was about, to be ready for the sun.

Colet opened the shutters of the verandah, and stepped out. The garden below was asleep. Only its familiar spirit was awake, tuning up before sunrise. The garden was still in the mirk. The trees were night itself settling out of the sky, descending to the earth, spreading there unequally while being absorbed. The bathroom was the first door to the left along the verandah. The liquid fluting of that bird was bathing for the mind. It made it fresh and glad. The bathroom tiles were delicious to walk over; another touch or two and they would have been cold. In a corner was an earthenware cistern, with a brass dipper on its ledge. A lizard was stuck to the wall, upside down, a flesh-coloured creature with eager and prominent eyes. It raised its head to watch him. Almost indecent to strip before such an expectant gaze; but it went off, shocked, in a flash, when it saw what he was like. You held the full dipper as high as you could, and tried to imagine you would shrink from the fall of the water. The water was felt, but no more. There was no shock. The water was as soft as new milk.

By his chair on the verandah, when he came out, somebody had left biscuits and tea. These Chinese boys moved about as though they were disembodied spirits, and unless you were watching they were never more than wraiths in the very act of vanishing. At that moment he was sure that a Malayan sunrise, with some tea just after you had bathed, was not to be exchanged for a halo and a harp. This corner of the earth had leisured and regal scope, and its jubilant light, with the musky smell of its lush growth, was good enough for the pleasaunce of an archangel, only he might be upset by a sight of Aphrodite. The crowns of the dominant palms, and the filigree of the upper foliage of the shrubbery, were black against lambent gold, and that tide of fire was plainly welling rapidly to flood the garden. The colours below were already bright; the orange and ruby crotons were separated, and the blossoms on the vines. The sun was so quick that he could be seen moving up behind the screen; he was blazing over the top before the first moment of coolness and calm was forgotten. Wasps arrived with him, to blunder about the joists of the verandah, and they were not ordinary wasps, and knew it. The seething had begun again.

===Chinese===
这时寂静散了。园里一只鸟在吹笛，试着记起一支晨歌。奇怪，他自己倒也想唱上两句，虽然睡不过是断断续续的一场噩梦。在那清凉的灰色光线里，光秃的房间只是光秃。那只怀旧追忆的鸟还没把调门找准，可它独自练个不停，一个沉思而尽责的小家伙。趁四下无人，悄悄先演练一遍，好迎接太阳。

科利特推开临游廊的百叶窗，跨了出去。楼下的园子还睡着。只有它的家宅精灵醒着，在日出前调音。园子仍浸在幽暗里。那些树就是夜本身，正从天上析出、沉降到地面，一边被大地吸去，一边铺展得深深浅浅。浴室在沿游廊过去左手的第一扇门。那鸟流水似的笛音是给心智的沐浴。它叫心智清新而欢畅。浴室的瓷砖踩上去好不舒服；再凉上一两分，就该算冷了。角落里有一口陶缸，缸沿上搁着一把铜勺。一只壁虎倒贴在墙上，肉色的身子，一双热切凸出的眼睛。它抬起头来看他。在这样一副期待的注视下脱衣服，几乎是有伤风化；可它一看清他的模样，便震惊得一闪而逃。你把满勺的水高高举起，尽力想象自己会怕那兜头浇下的一注。水是感觉到了，仅此而已。没有一激灵。那水软得像新挤的奶。

他从浴室出来时，游廊上他的椅子边，有人放下了饼干和茶。这些中国男孩走动起来像没有肉身的魂灵，你若不盯住看，他们永远只是正在隐去的一抹残影。那一刻他确信，马来亚的日出，加上浴后的一口茶，是拿光环和竖琴来也不换的。大地的这一角有悠闲而帝王气象的格局，它欢腾的光，配上草木繁茂的麝香气，拿来做一位总领天使的游苑也尽够了，只怕他见了阿芙罗狄忒（Aphrodite）要心烦意乱。高高在上的棕榈树冠，灌木丛上层枝叶的金丝细工，衬在闪动的金色前都是黑的，那一潮火焰分明正迅速涌起，就要漫过园子。下方的颜色已经鲜亮；橙色与红宝石色的变叶木各自分明，藤上的花也各自分明。太阳快得很，能看见他在屏障后头往上挪；还没等人忘掉最初那一刻的清凉与安静，他已烧过顶了。黄蜂随他一同到来，在游廊的椽木间乱撞，它们不是寻常的黄蜂，自己也知道。沸腾又开始了。

===Original===
This morning he was leaving with Norrie for the other side of the peninsula. What was to come of that was as speculative as being born, for Malaya was to him what the latencies are about a child playing hopscotch, and Norrie was as debatable as poker or immortality. It was as good as just coming into the world. The liveliness of Penang that morning was the celebration of nativity, the perennial birthday, old earth a cherub again and having another cut at it. Their rickshaws had to stop to allow a Chinese wedding to pass. That was the way to do it. No bare certificate of legitimacy, with a registrar’s stamp, for these people, not even for the additional third wife. The regiment of forerunners of the joy were in scarlet, hats and all, as exceptional as the oncoming of an Olympian circus. They cleared the way for musicians in pale blue robes, with stringed instruments wailing bliss. The bride, if it was the bride, was a large doll with dark hypnotic eyes in a face of porcelain, a capricious crown holding her head firm on her neck, and her turquoise silk dress a call to extravagance for the poor in spirit and the homespun.

Norrie was damning his coolie for pausing to watch the procession. He wanted to get aboard; but it was unfair to expect a man to dodge a bit of luck like that. It isn’t a Malay morning every day of the year. Now if all cities were as Penang, then there would be no reason to regret Ithaca and the young days of Ulysses. Our birthright would be as plain as a sign given by the gods. To think this coast had been here always, waiting for whoever doubted the earth was planned for asphalt and regrets, while there he used to be, clanking his chains west of Aldgate Pump, dutiful as an old soldier grateful for the workhouse and skilly. Colet went up the gangway, and saw the leisurely smoke from the funnel of his small coasting steamer as though it were the beckoning of the original Argo.

===Chinese===
今天早晨，他要同诺里动身去半岛另一边。此行会生出什么来，和出生一样无从猜测，因为马来亚之于他，就像一个跳房子的小姑娘身上种种尚未显形的可能，而诺里这个人，和扑克牌或不朽一样可疑可议。这简直等于刚刚来到世上。那天早晨槟城的活泼，是一场诞生的庆典，一个年年常新的生日，老大地的又做了一回小天使，重新再来试一把。他们的人力车不得不停下，让一队中国婚礼通过。这才是办事的样子。这些人不要一纸光秃秃的合法证书、盖一枚登记官的印，就连添娶第三房也不要。开道报喜的前队一身猩红，连帽子带人，气派得像奥林匹斯马戏班驾到。他们为乐师清路，乐师们穿淡蓝色长袍，弦乐一路哀婉着极乐。新娘——如果那真是新娘——是个大玩偶，瓷样的脸上一双深色的、催眠似的眼睛，一顶任性的冠冕把她的头牢牢定在脖子上，那身绿松石色的绸衣，对于心里贫穷的和穿家织粗布的人，是一声豪奢的召唤。

诺里正骂他的苦力，怪他停下来看迎亲的队伍。他要上船去；可是指望一个人躲开这样一场眼福，未免不近情理。一年之中，可不是天天都有一个马来人的早晨。倘若天下的城市都像槟城，那就没有理由再惋惜伊萨卡（Ithaca）和尤利西斯（Ulysses）的年轻岁月了。我们的长子名分就会像诸神给的记号一样一清二楚。想想吧，这片海岸原来一直在这儿，等着任何一个怀疑大地生来就该铺柏油、装悔恨的人；而那边，他从前竟日复一日待在奥德门水泵以西，当啷当啷地拖着锁链，像感激济贫院和稀粥的老兵一样尽职。科利特走上舷梯，望见他那只沿岸小汽船烟囱里悠闲的烟，仿佛那是最初的阿尔戈号（Argo）在向他招手。

## XXVI / 二十六

===Original===
Norrie was taciturn. He had hardly spoken that morning. It was noticeable, too, to his companion, that he was very generous in the confined space of that cabin for two with the broad of his back, which was in no hurry to get out of the way. He was a little testy over the refractory angles of events. Sometimes it was an angle of Colet’s. His white jacket was showing the damp smutch of the heat. He peeled it off and flung it down, and then the cabin became mordant with an alien smell. Colet was aware of a distinct and opposite being, weighty and offensively otherwise, to which all his sympathy did not naturally flow.

Not all, but some, for the leisurely presence of Norrie was a warrant of literal meaning. Norrie was a cunning centre of gravity, never overset in the drift of light chances. You could hold on to him. He accepted and named events, often not vouchsafing them a glance, with melancholy understanding, as though he had known them before Homer. He dismissed occasions which perplexed Colet with droll epithets, though sadly tolerant and broody. All the same, then he was in the way.

They continued to stow their properties for the voyage. Neither spoke. Back to back, they kept impeding each other, forgetfully. Colet wondered whether men were not better apart; might not admire each other more if they were not in contact. Ought to have separate cubicles. Each man had a different aura. What did Norrie think of his? The aura was worse on a close morning. Better to be alone.

They bumped again, and Norrie put out a hand to steady himself. Colet felt aggrieved; it was all Norrie’s fault.

===Chinese===
诺里沉默寡言。那天早上他几乎没有开过口。他的同伴还注意到，在那间狭小的双人舱里，他那宽阔的背脊慷慨大方，一点也不急着让开地方。他对诸事倔强的棱角有点儿恼火。有时那棱角就出在科利特身上。他的白上衣已经洇出了暑热的湿渍。他把它剥下来一摔，舱里顿时辛辣刺鼻，满是生人的气味。科利特意识到眼前是一个截然有别、处处相反的存在，沉甸甸的，咄咄逼人地不像自己，他的同情心并不自然而然地朝那边流去。

也并非全部，只是有一些没有流到，因为诺里那副从容的气度，本身就是字面意义的担保。诺里是个狡黠的重心，在轻浮机缘的漂流中从不翻覆。你可以牢牢抓住他。他接纳种种事件，给它们命名，往往连一眼也不赏给它们，带着忧郁的了悟，仿佛早在荷马（Homer）之前他就同它们相识了。他用滑稽的考语打发那些叫科利特犯难的场合，虽则带着悲哀的宽容，郁郁地出神。话虽如此，这时候他到底碍事。

他们继续把这趟航程的家什归置安放。两个人谁也不说话。背对着背，他们忘乎所以，老是互相碍手碍脚。科利特疑心，人还是分开的好；彼此不接触，说不定还会更互相钦佩些。该一人一间小隔间才对。每个人都有各不相同的气息。诺里觉得他的气息如何呢？闷热的早晨，气息更糟。还是独处好。

两人又撞上了，诺里伸出一只手来稳住自己。科利特觉得受了委屈；这全是诺里的错。

===Original===
“Don’t mind me, Colet. And don’t hit me. I’m simply intolerable most mornings. I couldn’t be civil to the sweetest young thing in the morning.”

He stretched, wiped his face, looked round.

“Now, where’s that blessed bag with my maps? Where is it?”

“What’s it like? If it’s maps, we must find it.”

===Chinese===
「别理我，科利特。也别打我。多半的早晨，我就是这么不可理喻。一大早的，就是天底下最甜的年轻小东西，我也和气不起来。」

他伸了个懒腰，抹了一把脸，四下张望。

「哎，我那装地图的宝贝袋子哪儿去了？在哪儿呢？」

「什么样子？要是装地图的，那非找到不可。」

===Original===
“I should just think we must. It’s got some whisky in it, and I simply can’t drink trade poison. I wouldn’t change it for all the commercial substitutes in the ship.”

“This it?”

“That’s right. Put it in my bunk. We shall want it. No other way to shorten this voyage.”

“Need we shorten it? But you’ve done it before.”

“And before that. In and out of the mangrove swamps. When I die, I shall be shoved into a mangrove swamp with an empty bottle, to sweat forever, and nobody to talk to but the sort of people you meet at a ship’s saloon table. You just think of that. It would make a man virtuous, even if he had a long time to live.”

===Chinese===
「可不是非找到不可。里头还搁着些威士忌呢，那种买卖场上的毒酒我可是咽不下的。拿船上所有的商业代用品来换，我也不换。」

「是这个？」

「就是它。放到我铺位上去。咱们用得着的。要不然，没法把这一程缩短。」

「咱们有必要缩短它吗？不过你从前也这么干过。」

「在那之前也干过。在红树林沼泽里进进出出。等我死了，人家会把我连同一只空瓶子塞进一片红树林沼泽，出汗出到永远，可以说话的伴儿，只剩船上餐厅桌上遇见的那路人。你就想想那个吧。那能把一个男人管教得学好，哪怕他还有大把的年月好活。」

===Original===
Norrie reclined on the settee.

“I wonder whether Ah Loi telegraphed for berths on the Singapore steamer. We shall have to change there. But of course he did.”

“He certainly did, if he said he would. I like your friend the Chinaman.”

“That shows your good taste. That Chink reduces most Europeans to the texture of clay pots. He’s rather too rare, for my taste. Strictly speaking, he oughtn’t to have a body. He’s only a subtle appreciation of refinements. Yet he seems to enjoy life. Did you see his wife?”

===Chinese===
诺里斜倚在舱室长椅上。

「不知道阿洛伊拍了电报去订新加坡汽船上的铺位没有。我们得在那儿换船。不过他当然拍了。」

「他要是说了会拍，那就准拍了。我喜欢你那位中国朋友。」

「这说明你品味不错。那个中国佬能把大多数欧洲人贬损成陶罐的质地。照我的口味，他这人难得得有点过头了。严格说来，他根本不该有一副肉身。他只是对精微事物的一种精微鉴赏力。可他似乎照样活得有味。你见到他太太了吗？」

===Original===
“She was at table.”

“She was? Then you really were honoured. She won’t always eat before me. I’m too coarse. You’ve never seen anything like her, so don’t say you have. I mean alive, and walking about.”

“No. Not in great numbers. I felt large and cumbersome.”

“She doesn’t know it, but I’d go without food just to look at her. I’d be as good as gold. She doesn’t know I’ve got the heart of a poet under a most unlikely outside.”

===Chinese===
「她在席上。」

「在吗？那你可真是受了抬举。她还不一定肯当着我的面吃饭呢。我太粗。你从没见过她那样的人，所以别说你见过。我是说活生生的、还在四处走动的。」

「没有。成群结队的没见过。我只觉得自己又庞大又笨拙。」

「她自己不知道，可为了光看看她，我情愿绝食。我会乖得跟金子似的。她不知道，我这副最不像样的皮囊底下，装着一颗诗人的心。」

===Original===
“What is she?”

“I should call her a masterpiece. But the best people out here, they say she is a *stengah*. That’s what they would do, you know. Her mother was Siamese. There are little bodies in Siam who would make you forget almost anything important. Her father was a Scotchman, and he must have been a forgetful Burns with red hair. I feel almost like a bishop when I look at her. Good job you are not an artist, or you’d be blethering now.”

“That couple surely are not typical here.”

===Chinese===
「她是什么人？」

「我要称她一件杰作。可是这儿最上等的人们说，她是个『司登加』。他们就会来这一套，你知道。她母亲是暹罗人。暹罗有些小人儿，能叫你把差不多顶要紧的事都忘个干净。她父亲是苏格兰人，准是个红头发的、健忘的彭斯。我望着她的时候，几乎觉得自己是个主教。幸亏你不是艺术家，不然这会儿你早絮叨个没完了。」

「那一对儿总不是此地的典型吧。」

===Original===
“What an idea. They wouldn’t be typical in Chelsea. Nothing good is typical. It’s a surprise. I don’t know where my corkscrew is. Where is yours?”

“I haven’t got one. I haven’t got even a stepladder.”

“Now, what a traveller. He hasn’t got a corkscrew. I wonder you’ve got a shirt. Press the button for a boy. There it is, just behind you.”

===Chinese===
「什么念头。他们在切尔西（Chelsea）也算不上典型。凡是好东西都不典型。好东西是个意外。我不知道我的开塞钻哪儿去了。你的呢？」

「我没有。我连一架折梯都没有。」

「瞧瞧，好一个旅行家。连开塞钻都没有。真奇怪你居然还有衬衫。揿铃唤个当差的来。铃就在你背后。」

===Original===
Colet went outside. The island of Penang was already a place apart, and they were leaving fishing stakes, sampans, steamers, and junks behind them. He did not always know what the queer objects signified, those marks of strange human handiwork on another order of nature, but he was satisfied that they were amiable. The waters of the Malacca Strait were the reflections of an upper light, desultory with its display, as though the celestial operator had time to waste, and wanted to see what would happen to the human stage when oriels, seldom used, were opened in the supernal. Norrie was in a hurry to get round to the coast of the China Sea, but there was no need to hurry along this coast on his account.

There were a score of superior passengers at the saloon table. The table was full. Norrie insisted that Colet should sit beside him. He was to talk eagerly whenever the man on the other side showed the least sign of affability.

===Chinese===
科利特走到舱外。槟城这座岛已经成了一个退远的去处，渔栅、舢板、汽船和中国帆船正一件件落在他们身后。那些古怪的物事意味着什么，他并不都清楚——那是人类的手在另一种自然秩序上留下的陌生印记——但他满意于它们都是友善的。马六甲海峡的水面是上方一片光的映像，那光铺陈得散漫无章，仿佛天上那位掌灯的闲得发慌，想看看那几扇久不启用的天上凸窗一旦打开，人间这座戏台会演成什么光景。诺里急着要绕到中国海沿岸去，可是为着他，沿着这一带海岸实在不必赶路。

餐厅的餐桌上有二十来位上等乘客。桌子坐满了。诺里非要科利特坐在他身边不可。交待下来的是：另一边那位只要露出一星半点亲热的意思，都得由科利特热络地陪谈。

===Original===
“I can’t stand it. It isn’t natural. If that planter once begins with his insufferable rubber, I shall have to kill him with the water-bottle, or else sit with the Malays on deck and eat bananas and dried fish.”

Colet thought the deck passengers would be an attractive alternative.

“I’ll squat there with you, if you insist. It’s fun, that crowd on the deck aft.”

“You’ve made a nice start. You do like it?”

“Never seen anything better.”

===Chinese===
「我受不了。这不自然。那个种植园主只要一开口讲他那没完没了的橡胶，我就只好拿水瓶砸死他，要不就到甲板上去跟马来人坐一堆儿，吃香蕉和鱼干。」

科利特倒觉得，去当甲板乘客是个诱人的备选。

「要是你坚持，我就陪你上那儿蹲着。船尾甲板上那一群，挺有意思。」

「你这头开得不错。你真喜欢？」

「再好的没见过了。」

===Original===
“That’s the way to look at it, when you must. But there’s no hurry for it. You’ll smell lots of ripe fish presently, heightened by durians.”

“Let ’em come in their due season. Though I’ve never met durians.”

“You will. They’re as sure as death. It’s a fruit, but you’d think it was a gas escape in a mortuary. Our pleasures are before us, and yet you think I’m too particular now over trifles, like corkscrews and chatty fellow-passengers.”

===Chinese===
「事到非这样不可的时候，就该这么看。可眼下还不用急。待会儿有的是熟透的鱼味儿给你闻，还有榴莲（durian）添彩。」

「该来的时候尽管来。不过榴莲我还没领教过。」

「你会领教到的。它跟死神一样准。那是一种水果，可你会当它是太平间里漏出的煤气。咱们的乐子都在前头呢——可你如今就觉得我鸡毛蒜皮都要挑剔，比如开塞钻，比如爱搭讪的同船客。」

===Original===
“I was down on the deck this morning. Not easy to keep away from it. I’d give a good deal to know what goes on inside those people.”

“The devil you would. All right, Colet, but don’t learn it while you’re with me. There’s an odd chance you would get a real inkling of it. You seem built in that wasteful way.”

Norrie, leaning on the ship’s rail, considered the blue heights and opalescent cloud masses of Malaya.

“No, it’s no good. It’s rather different. They begin their ideas at another mark, where we have too much gumption to begin. I do my best not to see it. It’s disturbing. Dammit, you and I might be wrong after all, and then where should we be? We might have to scrap home and altar, and I can’t bear the thought of it. God bless Clapham Junction. You be careful. The Oriental is dangerous, once you begin to monkey with his notions.”

===Chinese===
「今天早上我就下去过。想不去都难。要能知道那些人肚子里都在转些什么念头，要我拿出许多东西来换，我也愿意。」

「嚯，你倒想。行啊，科利特，可跟我在一起的时候别去学。弄不好你真会窥到一点真章。你天生就是按那种不惜工本的样式造出来的。」

诺里倚着船栏，端详马来亚青蓝的山影和泛着蛋白石光彩的团团云朵。

「不，没用。事情大不一样。他们的念头是从另一道记号上起头的，我们呢，太精明，不肯从那道记号起头。我尽力不去看见它。那叫人心绪不宁。见鬼，说不定到头来错的是你我，那我们可怎么好？没准儿咱们得把家宅和祭坛统统报废，这个念头我想一想都受不住。上帝保佑克拉彭枢纽站（Clapham Junction）。你小心点儿。东方人危险得很，只要你一开始拨弄他的念头。」

===Original===
They got well down the coast. The same things occurred daily, and were getting usual. The loading of the steamer at one of the small ports was nearly completed. Norrie was asleep in the cabin. On the leeward side a few empty sampans and prahus were rocking slowly. The shore was about two miles away, and their port of call a mangrove creek, by the look of it, inhabited only by crocodiles. The hills inland were no more than the lurking masses of a thunderstorm in reserve for the evening. They were distant, whether clouds or mountains, and a warning which need not be heeded before noon. The sea about the estuary was shallow, a level of opaque olive-green, and only the lighters, and the coolies in them who had nothing now to do but to smoke and watch the life of the steamer while waiting for a tow, were an assurance that this anchorage was merited by a veritable and inhabited shore.

===Chinese===
船沿岸南下，走了好一段。同样的光景日日重演，渐渐成了寻常。汽船在其中的一个小港装货，已近尾声。诺里在舱里睡着。下风一侧，几条空的舢板和普拉胡帆船（prahu）在缓缓摇晃。海岸大约在两英里外，他们要挂靠的口岸，看模样是红树林里的一条水湾，住的只有鳄鱼。内陆的群山不过是几团潜伏的雷雨，留着傍晚发作。它们远远的，是云是山分不清，是个正午之前不必理会的警告。河口一带的海水很浅，平展展一片不透明的橄榄绿；只有那几条驳船，和船上无事可做、只能一边抽烟一边观望汽船上的营生、等着拖带的苦力们，才叫人确信，这处锚地底下当真对着一方真实的、有人烟的海岸。

===Original===
From the bridge of the steamer Colet and another saloon passenger watched a derrick manoeuvring the last piece of freight, a motorcar. It was too awkward for the hold, and its bulk made the restricted foredeck of the coaster appear to be dangerously encumbered. Colet remarked to the man beside him that the car was an incongruous interjection. It had no real part in the drama of Chinese, Malays, and Hindus on that deck.

His companion, a young man who had been prompt with knowledge, made his monocle comfortable to regard with kindly amusement that lively huddle of chromatic humanity.

“Oh, hasn’t it a part? That car is as much a part of the East now as the natives. We’re here now, you know.”

Colet ventured to regret that aspect of our presence.

===Chinese===
科利特和另一位头等舱乘客在汽船的驾驶台上，看一根起重吊杆摆弄最后一件货物——一辆汽车。它太大件，进不了货舱，它那副身量把这艘近海轮局促的前甲板堵得看着险象环生。科利特对身边的人说，这辆车是一句格格不入的插话。在甲板上那出中国人、马来人和印度人参演的戏里，它没有真正的角色。

他的同伴——一个见识来得飞快的年轻人——安顿好单片眼镜，怀着善意的兴味，端详底下那一堆闹哄哄、五色斑斓的人。

「哦，没有角色吗？如今这辆汽车跟土著一样，都是东方的一部分了。这会儿我们可就在这儿了，你知道。」

科利特大着胆子说，我们到场竟还有这样一面，他引以为憾。

===Original===
“You must have seen a lot of it?”

“Oh, rather.” The young man freely acknowledged it, “All round this coast and the islands.” He indicated with a generous gesture all that was beyond, in the east. “Travelling here for two years now.”

“Fun?”

“No. Hardware.”

That tickled him. The monocled stranger asked for some news of London. Stood Leicester Square where it did? He hoped to learn that before the year was out.

===Chinese===
「这一带你一定见过不少了吧？」

「哦，可不是。」年轻人爽爽快快地认了，「这一带海岸，还有那些岛子，全转遍了。」他大手一挥，把东面以外的一切都划了进去。「到这儿跑生意，两年了。」

「好玩？」

「不。五金。」

这个字眼把科利特逗乐了。戴单片眼镜的陌生人打听了几句伦敦的消息。莱斯特广场（Leicester Square）还立在老地方吗？他指望年底以前能打听明白。

===Original===
Colet was trying to imagine the Orient in the terms of a captivating prospect for hardware. That was not easy. But this commercial traveller was bright and explicit, if his confidence had not lost all the jaunty indiscretion of youth. His judicious monocle and accent, well maintained in excellent simulation of what was authentic, perhaps made an advance with ironmongery among palm groves the less noticeable. Still, the young man quite evidently knew something of the East. He confessed to a familiarity with Malay. He knew these people.

Colet felt his inferiority. “I must learn to talk with them.”

“It’s really easy to learn their language. And they’re jolly nice people. I get along fine with them.”

===Chinese===
科利特正试着把东方想象成一片对五金有迷人前景的疆土。这可不容易。不过这位旅行推销员倒还爽利直白，只看他那副自信，还不曾把年轻人意气扬扬的口无遮拦丢个干净。他那只措置得宜的单片眼镜，还有那口嗓音——保养得极好，把正宗的做派摹仿得惟妙惟肖——或许让五金生意在棕榈树林间的推进不那么扎眼。话虽如此，这年轻人对东方分明是知道些的。他自认马来话说得熟。他懂这些人。

科利特自惭不如。「我得学会同他们说话。」

「他们的语言真容易学。他们也都是顶好的人。我跟他们处得来着呢。」

===Original===
He adventured into some personal history allusively, but with oiled enjoyment. There was a Malay girl, an apt pupil of love. He sweetened his narrative with a touch of sentiment. He tried to picture the girl for Colet, and Colet realised that she was a female. But she married a chief; only another addition to that populous household. An elderly chief. A friend of his, too. Then the girl ran away from her new home. She came back, in fact, to him. The young man could not help showing how much he appreciated this demonstration of affection; nevertheless, he was frightened.

“So would you have been, if you knew these people.”

“What did you do?”

“Why, you see, that rajah kept a regiment of young men about him. No joke. One of them was the girl’s brother. Real hot stuff. If the chief had nodded﻿—no need to say anything﻿—I should have looked a pretty mess one morning, take it from me.”

===Chinese===
他影影绰绰地闯进自己的一段私史里去，讲得津津有味。有个马来姑娘，是爱情这门功课里一点就通的徒弟。他往叙述里掺了一撮感伤作甜。他试着把那姑娘描画给科利特听，科利特这才明白过来，她是个女的。可她嫁给了一位首领；不过是那户人丁兴旺的宅院里再添的一口罢了。一位上了年纪的首领。还是他的朋友呢。后来姑娘从新家跑了。实际上是跑回了他这儿。年轻人不由得露了形迹，这份情意的表证多么叫他受用；尽管如此，他到底是怕了。

「你要是了解这些人，你也会怕的。」

「那你怎么办的？」

「嗨，你是不知道，那位拉贾身边养着一整团年轻汉子。可不是闹着玩的。其中有一个，就是那姑娘的哥哥。真是个火爆货色。那首领只要点一点头——话都用不着说——不出哪个早晨，我就得横在那儿不成个样子，听我的没错。」

===Original===
When the delectable morsel of girlhood appeared again at his bungalow, therefore, the Englishman, seeking safety, went to the lion’s den. The best thing to do. He went straight to the rajah and said the girl had come to him that morning. But the old man, who knew girls, waived this one aside royally. Too trivial for a dispute between friends. Nothing in it. Colet gathered, too, that the episode was now entirely closed. His fellow-traveller had given up the girl. All right now. One should not keep them too long. They grow fond of you, and it doesn’t do.

===Chinese===
于是，当那块妙龄姑娘的可口甜点再度出现在他的平房里时，这英国人为了自保，索性直闯狮穴。上策莫过于此。他径直去见拉贾，说姑娘那天早上到他那儿去了。可这位深谙姑娘家的老人，一挥手就把这一位王气地拂到了一边。朋友之间犯不着为这点小事起争。里头没什么东西。科利特还听出来，这段公案如今已彻底了结。同船客把那姑娘让了出去。这就没事了。这种姑娘留不得太久。她们会对你生出情分，那可要不得。

===Original===
“There﻿—you see that fellow there?” His companion nudged Colet. He indicated a Malay squatting on his hams, among other native passengers, his back to the bulwarks. Colet remarked him.

“Well, he’s very like that girl’s brother.”

It was a scowling figure notably attired in a bright sarong and jacket, and a black velvet cap.

“They look fine people, don’t they?”

===Chinese===
「喏——看见那边那个家伙了吗？」同伴用胳膊肘碰了碰科利特。他指的是蹲坐在土著乘客中间的一个马来人，背抵着舷墙。科利特端详了他一番。

「呃，他倒真像那姑娘的哥哥。」

那是个满面阴沉的身影，衣着惹眼：鲜亮的纱笼配短上衣，头上扣一顶黑丝绒帽。

「他们看着是体面人，不是吗？」

===Original===
That one was a picturesque example, from what could be seen of him; and while still idly noted so handsome a presentment of a folk strange to him, Colet unlearned much that he had accepted of the East. Not much today in all that. It had all become ordinary. The natives, he was advised, were not difficult to understand. They admired the English. They would do anything for us, take it from him. *Orang puteh*, white men, they call the English, and *orang blanda*, yellow men, the Dutch. That shows you, doesn’t it? These Malays know gentlemen when they see them. When once you were used to their funny little ways the rest was easy. They were only strange until you had lived with them and could talk their language. It was a good thing to take one of their girls. Some of them were very pretty. The girl could soon teach you a lot. Take it from him, a man like himself got to know more of the guts of things, out there, than all the interfering officials who were so touchy over this and that.

===Chinese===
就眼力所及，那一个算得一个入画的样本；科利特一边还懒懒地打量着这个陌生民族的如此俊美的一副形容，一边把许多他先前照单全收的东方见识一一卸下。这样的事，如今已算不得什么。一切都变得寻常了。据人家开导他，土著不难懂。他们钦佩英国人。他们肯替我们做任何事，听他的没错。他们管英国人叫『奥朗布特』（orang puteh），白人；管荷兰人叫『奥朗布兰达』（orang blanda），黄种人。这不就说明问题了吗？这些马来人一打眼就认得出绅士。你一旦习惯了他们那些有趣的小脾气，剩下的都容易。他们只在你还没同他们一起过日子、还说不上他们话的时候，才显得生分。收他们族里一个姑娘，是桩好事。有些姑娘生得很俏。姑娘很快就能教你许多东西。听他的没错，像他这样的人，在那边对事情底细的通透，胜过所有那些样样都要插一手、这也见怪那也见怪的官老爷们。

===Original===
A group of Chinamen below stood bent in appreciation of some cocks, each in its own wicker cage. The cocks stridently challenged each other. One cage was carelessly handled, and the gladiator hustled out. A bystander grabbed at it, but it flustered to the topside of the ship, and another indiscreet clutch at its tail sent it protesting into the sea. For an instant it was a frantic bunch of feathers there, and then an unseen body from below swirled the water ponderously and the cock had gone.

Colet was shocked. This was not in accord with what was familiar. The penalty had come so suddenly, just when he had allowed the motorcar, in spite of its size and appearance, to displace some old conceptions, to occupy with the certainty of a late solid engine the ancient mystery of the East. Yet nobody else seemed affected by the incident. The Chinamen stared indifferently at the place where the water had swirled, but the others might not have been aware that anything had happened. His companion made a humorous reference to the incident, but returned with soft pleasure to some more intimate words on the pleasantries of native ways. Colet, with a divided mind, listened while his eyes still rested absently on the Malay below who had been used to illustrate a little colourful drama of no value except to show to a stranger the delights and simplicity of the things he did not know.

===Chinese===
底下，一群中国人弯着腰，鉴赏几只公鸡，每只各关在一只柳条笼里。公鸡们尖着嗓子互相叫阵。有一只笼子叫人随手一磕，那位角斗士窜了出来。旁边有人一把去抓，它却慌慌张张扑上了船的高处，又有一只冒失的手往它尾巴上一揪，把它一路抗议着送进了大海。有那么一瞬，那儿是一团发狂的羽毛，随后水下一个看不见的躯体沉重地搅出一个漩，公鸡就没了。

科利特震惊了。这跟他素来熟悉的世界对不上号。惩罚来得这么突然，恰恰是在这个当口：他刚刚容许那辆汽车——不论它块头多大、模样多新——顶掉了一些旧观念，以一台迟来而结实的发动机所携带的确凿，进驻了东方那古老的神秘。可对这件事，其余的人似乎无动于衷。中国人漠然地盯着水花打漩的地方，别的人仿佛根本不晓得出了事。同伴就这事打了一句诨，随即又带着绵软的愉快，回到更贴己的话头上去，谈土著风习的种种妙趣。科利特听着，一颗心分作两半，眼睛却还怔怔地落在底下那个马来人身上——方才就是拿他，给一出花花绿绿的小戏作了插图；那戏本身一文不值，不过是演给一个外乡人看看：他所不知道的事物，多么有趣，多么简单。

===Original===
What his fellow-passenger was telling him Colet did not clearly hear, but the narrator was at least cheerful and pert. The heat made one slack. A Chinese cooly was passing the crouching Malay, who rose, and was seized with a sudden convulsion. The Chinaman fell. Colet noticed in disbelief that the man’s head was off. Colet did not move. He had not decided yet that the head was dead, for a little cigarette smoke still moved from its nostrils.

The crowd below, which for a pause as long as Colet’s incredulity had remained still, began to swirl, as had the sea just before. The Malay was running. Several men vaulted into the water. As though it were not real, Colet watched that trotting figure cut down two more men who also were uncertain of its reality, and then disappear into the forecastle. The deck below was now empty, but for several figures in ungainly postures. There were noises in the forecastle. Colet stared at the dark and empty rectangle of its entrance, fixed his attention to those sounds. The Malay came within that frame, cool and leisured, his blade in his hand, the appearance once more of the principal actor in a play, and stared sullenly at his sole audience of two white men in the gallery. With his gaze on them he trotted for the bridge ladder. Colet discovered, as the man began to run, that now he was alone, and that his two hands which gripped the rail did not know they ought to let go. He desired to run, but was held to the spot by himself. How run from a dream?

===Chinese===
同船客后来跟他说了些什么，科利特没有听真切，只晓得那位叙述者自始至终兴致勃勃、轻快活泼。暑热叫人发软。一个中国苦力正打那个蹲伏的马来人身边经过，马来人站起身来，突然浑身一阵痉挛。中国人倒了下去。科利特难以置信地注意到，那人的脑袋已经不在了。科利特没有动。他还没有断定那颗脑袋已经死了，因为还有一小缕香烟从它的鼻孔里袅袅而出。

底下的人群——在与科利特的难以置信等长的一段凝滞里一直木然不动——开始打漩了，正像方才的海水。马来人跑起来了。有几个人翻身跳进了水里。科利特仿佛在看一件不是真的事情，眼睁睁望着那个小跑的身影又砍翻两个人——那两个人也同样拿不准它的真假——随后一头扎进艏楼不见了。底下的甲板如今空了，只剩几具姿势难看的身影。艏楼里传出声响。科利特盯着艏楼门口那个黑黢黢、空落落的矩形，把注意力钉在那些声响上。马来人走进了那个画框，冷静而从容，手里握着刀，再度摆出一出戏里头牌角色的做派，阴沉沉地瞪着他仅有的两名观众——楼座里的两个白人。他一面瞪着他们，一面小跑着奔向驾驶台的梯子。就在那人起跑的当口，科利特才发现，如今只剩他独自一个了，才发现自己攥着栏杆的两只手，还不晓得它们早该松开。他想跑，却被他自己摁在原地。从一场梦里，怎么跑得脱？

===Original===
A gunshot released Colet’s grip. There below him was the tall figure of an engineer, standing at the foot of the ladder in singlet and trousers, his uniform cap on the back of his head. He was pointing a revolver at the Malay. The gun clicked with a silly inconsequence as the coloured figure reached the ladder, and the engineer crumpled. When Colet got to his cabin door the eyes of the Malay on the ladder stared up at him from the deck planking, and were coming higher. The door shook while he was making it fast, and then the face of the man darkened the open port window. The shadow passed. Colet stood holding the handle of the door till the silence told him his arm was aching with an unnecessary effort.

“What are you standing there for? Do open that door. I’m breathing steam.” Norrie was sitting up, looking sleepy.

===Chinese===
一声枪响，把科利特的攥握松开了。他脚底下，梯脚那儿立着一位轮机员高大的身影，汗衫长裤，制帽扣在后脑勺上。他正举着一支左轮枪，对准那马来人。待那彩色的人影扑到梯前，枪却傻里傻气、没头没脑地空响了一下，轮机员便瘫软了下去。科利特赶到舱门口时，那马来人在梯子上，一双眼睛正从甲板板条上瞪上来，而且越瞪越高。他闩门的时候，门震了起来，随后，那人的脸把敞开的舷窗遮得暗了。影子过去了。科利特攥着门把手站在那儿，直到寂静告诉他，他的胳膊一直在白白地发酸。

「你站在那儿干什么？快把门开开。我呼出的都是蒸汽了。」诺里坐起身来，睡意惺忪。

===Original===
“Can’t open it. There’s a johnny outside knifing everybody.”

“Keep the devil out, then. Is it locked?”

“Yes. It’s fast all right, now.”

“I should close the port. He might throw something in.”

===Chinese===
「开不得。外头有个家伙正见人就捅。」

「那就别放魔鬼进来。锁上了吗？」

「锁上了。这会儿闩牢了。」

「依我说该把舷窗关上。他兴许会往里扔东西。」

===Original===
Norrie got up, and made the port fast, and then sat listening, on the couch. He stretched himself again.

“Lucky you got here first. Why doesn’t someone shoot him?”

Colet looked round for a weapon. There was only the water-bottle. The ship was very quiet. It might have been deserted. Were they left aboard with that lunatic?

===Chinese===
诺里起身，把舷窗关牢，然后坐在长椅上听动静。他又伸了一个懒腰。

「幸亏你抢先到了。怎么没人开枪打他？」

科利特四下里找件家伙。只有那只水瓶。船上静极了。简直像已被弃空。他们是被撇在船上，同那个疯子作伴吗？

===Original===
“Norrie, have you got a gun?”
