“Several, in that trunk under the bunk. But no ammunition, of course. But he’ll be downed by someone. Don’t worry. They’re slow about it, though.”

Once cries broke out in the after-part of the ship. Once feet pattered rapidly past their cabin. Once there was a challenge in a strange language, but nobody answered it. Something warm trickled down his nose. He put his hand to his face quickly. Sweat.

===Chinese===
「诺里，你有枪吗？」

「有几支，在铺位底下那只衣箱里。不过子弹当然没有。可他总会叫人撂倒的。别担心。就是他们动手慢了点儿。」

有过一回，船尾那头爆出一阵喊叫。有过一回，一串脚步嗒嗒嗒地疾奔过他们的舱门。有过一回，有人用一种听不懂的话喝问了句什么，可没人应声。一样温热的东西顺着他的鼻子往下淌。他赶紧抬手往脸上一抹。汗。

===Original===
How much more of this? Norrie was a cool customer. Colet peered out of the port. There was nothing to be seen; only the usual patch of deck and the rail. But heading for the ship was a steam launch. He watched its progress. It was bringing four Malay policemen with rifles, and a white officer. The launch reached them and got under the ship, out of sight.

Nothing could be heard. Two of the little noiseless policemen, with their officer, went by the cabin. Then they heard the white man’s voice calling some orders at the head of the ladder. They opened the door and went out. All the police were there, and were beginning to descend to the foredeck. The men advanced cautiously towards the empty forecastle entrance, and one of the policemen chanted to that vacant door words which sounded like taunts. There came the Malay again. He was still cool and leisured, and answered the taunting with dignity. The police halted. The white officer talked quietly to that figure in the doorway and signed for him to drop his parang. But the fellow jumped for the police, toppled into a sliding heap, and was still.

===Chinese===
这还要闹到几时？诺里倒是个冷静角色。科利特凑到舷窗前往外张望。什么也看不见；只有老样子的一块甲板和那道栏杆。可是，有一条汽艇正朝船驶来。他望着它的行进。艇上载着四名挎步枪的马来警察和一名白人警官。汽艇到了船边，钻到船底下去，看不见了。

什么也听不见。两名悄没声儿的矮小警察，跟着他们的警官，从舱门外走过。随后他们听见那白人的声音在梯口下达了几道口令。他们推门出去。警察们全在那儿，正开始下往前甲板。他们小心翼翼地朝空着的艏楼门口推进，其中一名警察朝着那扇空洞的门，用唱诵般的调子抛出几句听来像是叫阵的话。那马来人又出来了。他还是那样冷静而从容，对那叫阵的回答不失尊严。警察们收住了脚。白人警官对着门洞里那个身影平心静气地讲话，打手势叫他放下帕兰刀（parang）。可那家伙朝警察们猛扑过去，栽倒在地，滑作一堆，不动了。

## XXVII / 二十七

===Original===

One day on the China Sea side, well up the coast from Singapore, they reached a hut. It was night, and it rained. Here they would begin their inland journey. It was somewhere near a beach. It had no other description. When coming up from the landing-place Colet had no faith that any roof could have resisted that abrupt smash of rain, even if a roof once had graced that outlandish shore, which did not seem likely. Yet Norrie moved as though he used to believe it, and knew of no reason yet to give up the idea. There was a sleeping-place, cavernous and bare, partly discovered by a mournful lamp; and one of its shadows, and a large one, did not behave normally like the rest. The lamp set it going. It gyrated ceaselessly amid the stationary shadows. Colet was satisfied that it was a bat. It might have been a black cloth circulated about by silent magic.

Then came morning, and the morning when they were to start for the interior of the land. Colet, on the verandah of the house, with the packs for travel about him, did not share Norrie’s annoyance over the delay. Any later time would do to start from there. Don’t let a good thing go too soon. It was folly to hurry from a place like Kuala something or other. By the map, these coastal hamlets were nearly always kualas, or river mouths, and he was not quite sure which one this was. But Norrie knew. There was Norrie now, outside the chief’s house, gossiping with a bunch of men as though he had lived in that kampong for years. Perhaps he had. A character of that place. If anything, Norrie knew too much﻿—more than was good for him. He seemed to be amusing those informal Malays, who somehow gave themselves an air of distinction and good mettle, though Norrie was a head taller. They certainly accepted Norrie as an equal. The village headman was smiling knowingly. Now and then one of the men in the group would glance his way, as though he too had been accepted. A quiet and understanding people. Norrie himself could not hurry them, though from the ease of his manner he did not appear to be attempting it. Women, slight and limber, who walked slowly in a way you had to watch, strolled past the group of gossiping men, but pretended to be unaware even of Norrie’s prevailing shape.

Why fidget over a delay in getting out of that village? It was not likely to come twice in a lifetime. Let’s have the full taste of it, at leisure. We resurrect from the dead only in odd moments﻿—might as well let the moment live itself out. Wasteful to hurry over a sudden flavour of the richness of the earth, as though it were the invasion of a licentious and inappropriate thought. It would take about a week for him to make sure that he was really there. He had had no time to ascertain that. The fact then seemed doubtful. In an unusual fancy dress which anticipated, when its wearer did not, an unaccustomed mode of living, Colet was uncertain of his own identity. This was just a bit absurd. He was only a self-conscious character in an unusual theatrical setting﻿—round about Drury Lane﻿—and the limelight was too bright. A mass of rigid metallic fronds shadowed the house, and formed motionless crenated black patterns on the road. There was a glimpse of the China Sea at the end of the street, a name which suited it. Too much like the China Sea. One could have guessed its name. The Chinese shopkeepers opposite were waiting for custom beside wares which would have been useless, without descriptions, in a museum. Not easy to believe all this. The sun now was full on the street, and Colet began to wonder how he would shape, marching in that white intensity. But he could sit and look at it forever.

===Chinese===

在面向中国海的一侧、新加坡以北沿岸颇远的地方，有一天他们走到了一间棚屋跟前。天黑了，又下着雨。他们将从这里开始内陆的旅程。这地方在某处海滩近旁。除了这个，它再没有别的名目。从登岸处一路上来时，科利特压根不信有什么屋顶扛得住那一阵劈头砸下的骤雨，哪怕那片生荒的海岸上从前真有过屋顶——这看上去也不大像。可诺里走动起来的那副样子，倒像他从来就信，而且至今还不知道有什么理由该放弃这个念头。有一处睡觉的地方，洞窟似的，光秃秃，一盏愁惨的油灯照见了它的一部分；其中有一团影子，还挺大的一团，举止不像其余影子那样安分。是灯把它搅起来的。它在纹丝不动的群影中间不知疲倦地兜着圈子。科利特认定那是一只蝙蝠。也说不定是一块黑布，叫无声的魔法驱得团团转。

然后早晨来了，还是那个他们该动身进入内地的早晨。科利特坐在屋子的游廊上，旅行的行囊围在身边，并不认同诺里对耽搁的不耐。晚些时候再从这里出发也没什么不行。别让一件好东西走得太快。从这样一处叫瓜拉（kuala）什么什么的地方匆匆赶路离开，未免愚蠢。照地图看，这些沿海小村几乎个个都是瓜拉，也就是河口，他拿不太准这一处是哪一个。可诺里知道。瞧诺里这会儿，正在头人的房子外头同一伙人闲磕牙，仿佛他在那座甘榜（kampong）里住了多年似的。说不定真住过。算是那地方的一个人物了。要说有什么毛病，就是诺里知道得太多——多得对他没有好处。他似乎正逗得那些不拘礼数的马来人高兴；这些人不知怎的自有一种身份气派和好筋骨，虽然诺里比他们高出一头。他们确实把诺里当平等的人接纳。村中的头人会心地笑着。那伙人不时有一两个朝科利特这边瞟一眼，仿佛连他也被接纳了。一个安静而善解人意的民族。连诺里也没法子催他们，不过看他那副从容的做派，倒也不像在催。女人们纤瘦柔韧，走路慢得叫你必须盯住了看，从那伙闲谈的男人身边款款走过，却装作连诺里那副压倒众人的身形都没有察觉。

何必为出村的耽搁抓耳挠腮呢？这样的事一辈子未必再来第二回。不妨从容不迫，把它的滋味尝个全。我们只在难得的古怪时刻里从死里还阳——索性让这一刻自己活个尽兴。大地丰饶的滋味蓦地涌上舌尖，急匆匆咽下未免浪费，仿佛那是一桩失宜的邪念来侵。他大约要花一个星期，才能确信自己真到了这儿。他一直腾不出工夫来核实这件事。于是这桩事实看着就可疑了。一身不寻常的行头，预先迎接着一种穿它的人自己尚未迎来的、不惯的生活方式，科利特连自己的身份都拿不准了。这就有点儿可笑了。他不过是身处不寻常布景中的一个自觉的角色——就在德鲁里巷（Drury Lane）一带——而且聚光灯太亮了。一大丛坚硬的金属似的蕨叶给屋子投下荫凉，在路上排出纹丝不动的锯齿形黑纹。街的尽头有一瞥中国海，这名字正配它。太像中国海了。闭着眼也猜得出它的名字。对面的中国店主们守着货色等生意，那些货色进了博物馆，不挂说明牌就全无用处。这一切不容易叫人相信。此刻太阳满满地照在街上，科利特开始寻思，走进那片白炽里行军，自己会走出个什么样子。可是坐着看，他可以永远看下去。

===Original===

Norrie left the men and strolled over to Colet, affecting a complete faith in the outcome of eternity.

“No carriers yet. We’ve got to pay for that entertainment on the ship coming round. We were expected by the last steamer.”

“Shall we get away this morning?”

“We may, as you don’t happen to be able to tell them all about the amok. It’s such a juicy story. They don’t often get one, and they’re so sorry you can’t tell ’em about it. All. Every crimson wound.”

“So am I.”

===Chinese===

诺里撇下那伙人，踱到科利特这边，装出一副对永恒的结局怀着十足信心的神气。

「脚夫还没有呢。绕海岸开过来的那艘船上那出余兴，咱们这会儿得付账了。人家原本掐着上一班汽船等咱们到的。」

「咱们今早走得脱吗？」

「也许走得脱，只因你偏偏没法把那桩发狂乱杀的事儿原原本本讲给他们听。那样一个有嚼头的故事。他们难得碰上一回，你偏又不会讲，他们心里别提多惋惜。全都要听。每一道血红的伤口。」

「我也是。」

===Original===

“They can’t bear to lose us so soon. That’s what it is. We’ve brought bright news﻿—all about a butchery. They’ve no newspapers. Don’t you think we ought to be kind to them?”

“You could make it better for them than it was, Norrie. Let yourself go. You couldn’t make it worse than it was. Give it a little art.”

“I haven’t got the cosy love for it. The story would be prettier if your friend the ass in the eyeglass was one of the coloured exhibits. But it is tame without him.”

“Queer. I’ve been watching them. These villagers don’t seem to be made of the stuff which goes off with a bang, like that Malay on the ship. They’re sane enough.”

“Of course they are. So are you. So was he. We’re all fine, till some button is touched. That Malay was all right. He only wanted to commit suicide, but his God said no to it. So what’s the poor beggar to do? Only one thing in reason, Colet. You can see that yourself. Make others do the dirty work. But don’t let us talk about it any more. It’s such a fine morning. If we begin to chin over the springs of human conduct we should be here when Gabriel tootled, and so intent with enjoyment that we shouldn’t hear him. We’ll surprise God Himself on the Judgment Day. He thinks He knows us, but does He, Colet, does He?”

===Chinese===

「他们舍不得这么快就放咱们走。就是这么回事。咱们带来了鲜亮的消息——一场屠宰的全本细账。他们没有报纸。你说，咱们是不是该对他们发发善心？」

「你可以给他们讲得比实况还好点儿，诺里。放开手脚讲。你总不至于讲得比实况更糟。给它添一点儿艺术。」

「我可爱不出那份劲儿来。要是你那位戴单片眼镜的蠢驴朋友也算有色展品里的一件，这故事本会漂亮些。少了他，未免温吞。」

「古怪。我一直看着他们。这些村民看样子不是那种轰隆一声就炸开的料，不像船上那个马来人。他们清醒得很。」

「他们当然清醒。你也清醒。他从前也清醒。咱们全是好好的，直到哪一颗电钮给按了一下。那马来人本来好好的。他不过是想自杀，可他的上帝不批准。那么这可怜虫还有什么路走？合乎理性的只有一条，科利特。你自己也看得出。让别人去干脏活儿。不过这事儿咱们别再谈了。多好的一个早晨。咱们要是掰扯起人类行为的发条来，等到加百列吹响号角还坐在这儿，而且入迷得兴高采烈，连他吹号都听不见了。审判日那天，咱们要让上帝他老人家吃一惊。他自以为认得咱们，可他真认得吗，科利特，认得吗？」

===Original===

The sun was lord of that country when at last they got going. Beyond the village they entered level rice-fields, and moved towards a dark escarpment of trees, low with distance, which in that torrid light suggested the unapproachable. Norrie led the way, solidly, at an enduring gait; the Malay carriers followed him, and Colet marched at the end of the line, behind the Chinese cook. They had to walk indirectly across that half-dried marsh, one step behind the other arduously along the ridges which parcelled the expanse into square aqueous areas. Some of the ridges were as hard as rock, and others sank under them into a black sludge with a stink of its own. Colet occasionally glanced in hope towards the trees. They took their time over getting higher and plainer. A buffalo bull ahead, broad and black as a rhinoceros, with horns as lengthy as a ship’s yard, snorted at Norrie. Colet heard him, and knew that he must pass the same way. He would be the beast’s last opportunity. The bull had its nose up, waiting, sunk to its belly in mud, just aside from the ridge along which they were looking for a sound patch for each step. If that brute attacked he was done. It snorted again when Colet was level with its nose, and heaved its bulk impatiently. Colet saw the bubbles stir in the mire about it, and realised what moral control is required, at times, for even so simple an act as putting your foot in the right place with care.

The cliff of trees rose over them. There was a clear deep stream bridged by a fallen trunk. When Colet removed his strict attention from a difficult poise over that greasy bole he saw that the rest of the party, even the Chinaman, had disappeared. There was a portal of two great tree trunks, and gloom beyond it. He saw it was the gate to the stuff of the legends. That must be the door through which the party had gone. He entered it, too, and at once relished the coolness of the forest. The track ascended. It was sandy. The twilight was green. Once he was assured by a glimpse of a man ahead that he was not alone in the silence, for the quiet had quickened his pace; the anxious thought had moved him that he would be comforted there by a contiguous human creature. He foresaw that solitude in the jungle might plumb to an unexpected deep in the soul. Only fellowship would keep him buoyed above that dark pit. Well, he would go anywhere with Norrie, over any pit, perhaps into it.

The track was often unmistakable; usually it was compulsory; there could be no divergence. A riot of green cordage as repellent as flourishing cusps, antlers, spines and bodkins, was piled between the trees on either hand; hooked fronds were suspended to catch in the neck and the helmet; the track was reduced to a loose tunnel meandering through edged foliage. Sometimes a prone giant had to be mounted, for it had fallen across the path, a tree that was a hilly jungle in itself, to be climbed, its vegetation forced, and a descent discovered through its bastions to the other side. Roots like low easy walls, or coiled like pythons, made a maze of the track. It descended into a morass where, apparently, monsters had floundered, leaving wallows and pits which compelled Colet into the trees to find a way; and there he paused, while avoiding the taloned raffle, to pick a sound out of the heavy quiet to get the direction of his friends. Nothing moved there but themselves, except flies which hovered and flashed wherever a beam of sunlight fell through a hole in the roof. They waded across streams, and picked leeches off their bodies; as if clothes were meant to be kept constantly wet, and bodies were the normal feeding-ground for worms. Colet compelled his mind to fight against the desire for cleanliness and dryness, and to regard without surprise a bloated and pendant black parasite taking the blood from a white limb. But he was surprised, nevertheless. That worm was the emblem of a world which was new, potent, inimical, and besieging; and not very particular. It waited silently, without respite, for its chances. You couldn’t be always on your guard. It had you, whenever you were not. Colet became wary even of trifles. They stung.

===Chinese===

等到他们终于上路，太阳已经是那一方国土的君主了。出了村子，他们进入平坦的稻田，朝一道黑沉沉的树壁走去——因着距离而显得低矮，在灼热的光里却透出不可亲近的意味。诺里领头，步子扎实，匀而持久；马来脚夫们跟在他身后，科利特行在队尾，排在中国厨子后面。他们不得不迂回着横穿那片半干的泽地，一步跟一步，吃力地沿着一条条田埂走——那些埂把整片水泽划成一方一方的水面。有的埂硬得像岩石，另一些在他们脚下塌陷，陷进一种自有其恶臭的黑泥。科利特不时怀着指望朝那些树望一眼。它们不紧不慢，才慢慢升高、清晰起来。前头一头公水牛，宽阔乌黑得像头犀牛，两支角有船上的帆桁那么长，朝诺里打了个响鼻。科利特听见了，知道自己也必须打那条道上过。他将是那畜生最后的机会。公牛昂着鼻子等着，身子陷进泥里直到肚皮，就在田埂一侧；他们正沿着这条埂，一步一步找结实的地方落脚。那牲口要是扑上来，他就完了。等科利特走到与那鼻子齐平的地方，它又打了个响鼻，不耐烦地鼓起那庞大的身躯。科利特看见它周围的泥沼里翻起了气泡，这才明白，哪怕只是把脚小心地放对地方这样简单的一件事，有时也需要何等的道德自制。

树木的峭壁在头顶耸起。有一条清澈深幽的溪，一道倒下的树干权作桥。科利特把全副的注意力从那段滑腻树干上的艰难平衡中收回来时，才发现一行人连那个中国人都不见了。有一个由两根巨木构成的门阙，门后是幽暗。他看出，那是通往传说之素材的门。一行人必定是从这道门进去的。他也跨了进去，登时尝到森林里的清凉。小径向上。是沙土的路。幽光是绿的。有一回，瞥见前头有个人影，他才放了心，这寂静里并非只有他一个，因为那静把他的脚步催快了；一个焦灼的念头推动着他：但愿在那边有个挨得近近的同类，好宽一宽心。他预见到，丛林里的孤独，在灵魂里可能探到出乎意料的深处。唯有伙伴的情分能托住他浮在那口黑坑之上。也罢，跟诺里去哪儿都行，越过任何一口坑，说不定也栽进坑里去。

小径常常清楚得错不了；通常它是强制性的；无从旁逸。两边树木之间堆满了乱作一团的绿色绳缆，像繁茂的矛尖、鹿角、棘刺和长锥一样拒人；带钩的叶柄悬在半空，专等着钩住人的脖颈和头盔；小径缩成一条松散的隧道，在满是利刃的枝叶间蜿蜒。有时必须登上一具横卧的巨人，因为它倒在了路中间——那是一棵本身就是丘陵丛林的大树，要爬上去，强行穿过它的草木，再从它的座座壁垒间找出下到另一侧的路。树根像低矮平缓的墙，或盘卷如蟒，把小径弄成迷宫。路下到一片沼泽，那里显然有巨怪挣扎过，留下一个个泥塘和深坑，逼得科利特钻进树林找路；他在那儿停住脚，一面避着那带爪的乱堆，一面从沉甸甸的寂静里捡出一点声息，好辨明同伴们的方向。那里除了他们自己什么也不动，除了苍蝇——哪一束阳光从天棚的窟窿里漏下来，它们就在哪儿盘旋、闪光。他们蹚过一道道溪流，从身上摘水蛭；仿佛衣服本就该一直湿着，肉体本就是虫豸正常的觅食场。科利特强迫自己的心去对抗对清洁干爽的渴望，强迫自己毫不诧异地看着一条胀鼓鼓垂挂着的黑色寄生虫，正从一条白皙的肢体上吸血。可他到底还是诧异了。那条虫是一个世界的徽记——一个崭新的、强悍的、怀敌而来、四面围困的世界；而且不很讲究。它无声无息、不休不歇地候着它的机会。你没法永远戒备。你一疏忽，它就得着你了。科利特连细枝末节也警惕起来。它们蜇人。

===Original===

The sun was going when they sighted areca palms ahead. Colet already knew that glad sign. The slender mast of the betel-nut palm, with its cluster of stalked knobs just below its graceful crown, was the sign that fellow-creatures were near. The brown thatches of huts appeared, like haven to seafarers. Nobody was about, but huge shadows rose from the ground as they arrived, and then went walloping loudly among the frail structures, a black torrent of buffalo. Colet felt so limp then that he would not have cared if they had been elephants, so long as they left the house props intact.

A brown little man came out to the verandah of one dwelling to see what all this was about. Like the others, that house stood on stilts, well above the earth, and now, as night was at hand, with a stowage of darkness already underneath it. The old fellow, in a tartan sarong, descended the ladder of his home to them as coolly as though such visits were usual. He conversed with Norrie in a gentle voice which was part of the quietude. Their carriers had disappeared. Except for two children above them on the verandah of the chief’s place, nobody in that hamlet had the curiosity to note this invasion of its solitude. Perhaps the huts stood there to serve as chance sanctuary for wayfarers caught by night in the forest. Or, perhaps, except for that one home, the huts were abandoned, and the old man and the children were alone in the forest.

They went up the ladder of rough beams, its steps slippery with clay, and catchy in their spacing for legs careless through travel. Norrie and Colet reclined on mats of grass, mats which did something towards levelling the floor of separated boughs. They put themselves at ease in native dress. Other men joined the chief; they betrayed no surprise to find white men there. Now and then a newcomer hailed Norrie with mild jocularity. There was a brass lamp on the floor, and brass dishes with fruit about it. The figures murmured to each other. Somewhere, perhaps in the rafters, Colet thought he heard the subdued whispering of women and children. He relished the picture of those men, with their apocryphal background. It was as unconformable as the chiaroscuro of a book belonging to another age. Yet he felt more at home than ever he did at the Gridiron in Soho. He knew only a few words of their speech, but he understood that it could be trusted. They did not move their heads, and scarcely their lips, as they spoke, which was soberly, as men would at night in a house with the wild at its door. Colet, through interstices of one of the walls, could watch sections of palm fronds motionless in moonlight. These fellows had a lot to say to Norrie. If one of them retired but slightly from the lamp, then the shadow almost absorbed him. Several of the men were only bright eyes; or they showed the ridge of a cheekbone when the head turned, and the feeble glow put a polish on bronze skin. But even the figures in the obscurity were not more strange than the smell of the quiet place, a smell faint, but zealous and nameless.

===Chinese===

望见前头的槟榔树时，太阳正在落下去。科利特已经认得那个可喜的记号了。槟榔树那细长的桅杆，正当秀美的树冠之下缀着一簇带柄的圆果——这便是同类生灵就在近旁的记号。棕色的茅草屋顶显现出来，在海客眼里如同港岸。四下不见人，可他们一到，巨大的影子便从地上立起，随即在那片单薄的屋舍间轰然奔突——一股水牛的黑色洪流。科利特这时软得厉害，哪怕它们是大象他也无所谓了，只要它们别撞断撑屋的柱子。

一个棕色皮肤的小个子男人踱到一座屋子的游廊上，来看这是怎么回事。同别家一样，那屋子架在桩柱上，离地老高；这时夜色将临，底下已经屯下了一仓黑暗。老人穿一条格子呢纱笼，从自家的梯子上下来，走到他们跟前，坦然自若，仿佛这样的造访是常事。他用一种轻柔的嗓音同诺里交谈，那嗓音原是这份幽静的一部分。他们的脚夫已经没了踪影。除了头人宅子的游廊上、在他们上方的两个孩子，这村里再没有人好奇地留意这场对孤独的入侵。也许这些棚屋立在那儿，本来就是为夜里困在林中的行路人备下的偶一为之的庇护所。又或者，除了这一户，棚屋都是弃置的，森林里只有老人和孩子们。

他们登上那架粗木梯，梯级沾着泥，滑；级距又刁，专绊走了长路而大意的腿。诺里和科利特在草席上斜倚下来——那席子多少把一条条分散树枝拼成的地板垫平了些。他们换上土人装束，宽舒自在。又有几个男人来同头人作陪；见有白人在座，他们丝毫不露诧异。时不时有个新来的用温和的诙谐向诺里招呼。地板上有一盏铜灯，四周摆着盛果子的铜盘。那些人影彼此低语。什么地方，也许在椽子间，科利特觉得听见了女人和孩子们压低的絮叨。他玩味着这幅图景：这些男人，连同他们真伪莫辨的背景。它像一部隔世古书里的明暗对照，格格难合。然而他竟比在苏荷区那家「烤架」餐馆里的任何时候都更自在。他们的话他只识得几个词，但他懂得那是靠得住的。他们说话时不转动头颅，嘴唇也几乎不动，说得持重，正如人们夜里在门外便是荒野的屋子里说话那样。透过一面墙的孔隙，科利特能望见月光下纹丝不动的一扇扇棕榈叶。这些家伙对诺里有的是话讲。只要有一个往灯外稍稍退开些，暗影几乎就把他吞没了。有几个人只剩一双晶亮的眼睛；或者待头一转，露出一段颧骨的棱，微光便给青铜的皮肤添上一层润泽。可是连幽暗里的人影，也不如这幽静之地的那股气味更奇特——一股淡淡的、却执拗而无名的气味。

===Original===

Norrie, later, was appealed to sombrely by the Malays. They wanted his wise confirmation. Anyhow, he was assuring them, that was plain enough, of his warm agreement that a conclusion of theirs was just and right. His wary and skittish eye, his ironical mouth, his expression of intelligence too drowsy and good-natured to contradict anybody, for he was comfortable, stirred a little curiosity in Colet, who divined this phase in his companion more readily than would these simple folk.

“Now, what’s this you are telling them? What’s the game?”

“Game? Not at all, my son. It’s about a mystery. I love mysteries, and treat them with proper respect. I wish you could have picked up the yarn that the penghulu had been telling me. It’s a true story. All these men know it is true.”

Norrie addressed himself to the penghulu; the chief answered him with gentle explicitness; and Norrie turned again to Colet, grave as the deep shadows and the brooding night. The Malays were watching the two travellers sadly but closely.

===Chinese===

后来，马来人们神色沉沉地向诺里求助。他们要的是他贤明的确认。无论如何，他显然正在向他们担保，自己热诚地赞同他们的某个结论公正而正当。他那只戒备而难驯的眼睛，他那张讥诮的嘴，他那种聪明得过分困倦、过分和善、懒得同任何人抬杠的神情——因为他正舒服着——在科利特心里撩起一点儿好奇；他比这些淳朴的人更轻而易举地窥见了同伴的这一层。

「喂，你这是在给他们讲什么呢？玩的什么把戏？」

「把戏？才不是呢，我的小子。是桩秘辛。我热爱秘辛，并且以应有的敬意待它。可惜你没接住头人（penghulu）刚才讲给我听的那套嗑儿。是个真事儿。这屋里所有的人都知道它是真的。」

诺里转向头人说话；头人用温和而明晰的话回答他；诺里又转回科利特这边，凝重得如同深深的暗影和郁郁沉思的夜。马来人们悲戚而凑近地望着这两个旅人。

===Original===

“It was only last week, Colet. You are now as near as that to the old original once upon a time. You’ve marched all the way back in one day. It only shows you what a fraud time is. You’ll learn something, before we’ve done with you; but don’t you grin, don’t look at all superior, or the magic will pass, and so may you. Keep your face as confiding as if I were flattering your sound moral character. There’s been a tiger about this patch. These men were too artful, just now, to call him that. They don’t want to hear him again, so they gave him polite and allusive names. You can’t be too careful here.

“But they knew he was a tiger, and more. He took their buffalo and chickens. Any tiger might do that, but soon they had doubts about the sort of tiger this one was. They heard him after dark, for he was an insolent thing, and used to prowl under where you are sitting, night after night. They sat and listened to him snarling. They don’t mind tigers; not very much; they don’t mind tigers who keep their place, and eat pigs and deer. But they do dislike what is more than a tiger when all good people are indoors. Are you listening? These foresters are watching you. They know more about tigers than we ever shall. If you think I am trying to be funny you don’t know me. There is more in the forest about us than these people would care to whisper, at this time of night. There’s a woman they know of, for one thing. She is only a lovely head trailing a length of entrails, and it is the end of you to meet her; and there are voices where nobody will ever be seen; and there was this tiger.

“He was only heard; they never saw him; only his pug marks were seen, but they were plain enough. And he was never heard snarling except when a certain old Malay peddler, a fellow from Sumatra, was in the neighbourhood. First they lost two buffalo, and he had the nerve to eat them where these people could hear him enjoying himself. But the buffalo grew wary after that, and bunched, and he didn’t dare to touch them. Then the chickens went. That was when snarling was heard under this house, after dark, and a tiger’s tracks were found in the morning. One day, though, after the peddler had gone beyond the village, they could hear him, being sick. Somebody had to pass that place afterwards, and, you would hardly believe it, but he saw feathers where that fellow had vomited.

===Chinese===

「就在上礼拜，科利特。你现在离那『很久很久以前』的老源头，就近到这个份上了。你只用一天，就一路走回去了。这不过叫你看看，时间这东西是个多大的骗子。等咱们把你调理完，你会长点儿见识；不过你可别龇牙笑，也别露出半点高人一等的样子，要不然魔法就散了，说不定你也跟着散了。你那张脸要摆得信任十足，就像我正在恭维你那端正的品行似的。这一带出过一只老虎。方才这些人乖觉，不肯那样叫它。他们不想再听见它，所以给它起了些客气而有影射的名号。在这儿，多小心也不为过。

「可他们知道那是一只老虎，而且不止。它叼走了他们的水牛和鸡。随便哪只老虎都干得出这种事，可没过多久他们就犯起嘀咕，这一只是哪一路的老虎。天黑以后他们听得见它，因为它是个放肆的东西，惯于一夜一夜地在你正坐着的底下转悠。他们坐着，听它低吼。他们不忌惮老虎；不怎么忌惮；守本分、吃猪吃鹿的老虎，他们不忌惮。可是等好人统统关进了屋，那种比老虎更甚的东西，他们可实实在在厌恶。你在听吗？这些林中人正瞅着你呢。关于老虎，他们懂的比我们往后能懂的还多。你要是以为我在耍俏皮，那你还不认识我。咱们四周这片林子里的名堂，比这些人肯在这种时辰低声说出的还要多。譬如说，有一位女人，他们是知道的。她不过是一颗可爱的头颅，后头拖着一长截肠子，撞见她你就到了头；还有种种声音，出自永远见不到人的地方；还有这只老虎。

「它只是叫人听见；他们从没见过它；只有它的爪印给人看见过，可爪印够分明的。而且，除非某个上了年纪的马来货贩子——一个苏门答腊来的家伙——在左近，从不曾听得见它低吼。起初他们丢了两头水牛，它竟有胆子就在这些人听得见的地方大快朵颐。可打那以后水牛学了乖，聚成了堆，它就不敢碰了。然后丢的是鸡。就是在那之后，天黑以后这所屋子底下听见了低吼，早晨又发现了虎迹。可是有一天，那货贩子出了村子走远之后，他们听得见它在那儿呕吐。过后总得有人打那地方经过，你简直不会相信，他看见了那家伙吐出来的东西上的鸡毛。

===Original===

“Well, you ought not to shoot a man, of course, but a tiger is not a man, is it? Especially when it robs you of cattle and chickens, and might take to cannibalism when the fowls were finished. That sort of thing can’t go on. So these men got a gun, rigged it to the proper bait, and put it where a tiger, in the boldness of his confidence, was likely to find it. The end of the story shows that all the suspicions of these people pointed to the truth of the matter. That night they sat here talking, just as we are now, and they heard the brute snarling again. Nobody dared go out. He was certainly hungry. He insulted these people. Once he sprang on to the verandah here, and shook the flooring. Tigers are heavy brutes. He kept sniffing at the door. The penghulu says he could smell the thing. Presently they heard the gun go off, and the tiger roared; it had got him; and then they thought they could sleep. When daylight came they went to the trap, and sure enough there he was. The gun had shot the peddler. There can be no doubt, after that, as the penghulu says, that some men can turn themselves into tigers when they want to. The village buffalo have returned to their old habits. They know things are all right again. And do you think you will hear snarling under you tonight? No, Colet, the reason has gone.”

Colet nodded his head in sad confession of the dubious nature of things. He glanced at the Malays, as a comrade should to those with him in the midst of dark powers; for the Malays were waiting, watchful, expectant of his full understanding. All wise men know these things are true. Over Norrie’s head, high in the gloom of the opposite wall, was a glimpse of moonlit things without, a panel of luminous silver, with the grotesque black shape of a leaf set in it, like the profile of a leering mask.

===Chinese===

「这个嘛，人当然是不该枪杀的，可老虎不是人，对不对？何况它抢你的牛和鸡，等鸡吃完了，说不定还要开荤吃人。这种事不能容它继续。于是这些人弄来一支枪，装上合宜的饵，安放在一只老虎以其胆大自信极可能寻到的地方。故事的结局表明，这些人的一切疑心都指向了事情的真实。那天夜里他们就坐在这儿说话，正像我们此刻这样，他们又听见那畜生低吼。没有人敢出去。它准是饿了。它羞辱了这些人。有一回它窜上这儿的游廊，把廊板都撼得直晃。老虎是沉重的大畜生。它一个劲儿在门口嗅。头人说，他闻得出那东西。不多时他们听见枪响了，老虎吼了一声；打着它了；然后他们想，这可以睡了。天亮时他们到机关那儿去，可不是嘛，它果然在那儿。枪打中的是那个货贩子。此后再无可疑了，正如头人所说，有些人想要变老虎的时候，是能把自己变成老虎的。村里的水牛恢复了旧习惯。它们知道世道又太平了。你说你今夜还会听见身子底下有低吼声吗？不会了，科利特，那个缘由已经去了。」

科利特点了点头，悲哀地招认了万物之可疑的本性。他朝那些马来人望了一眼，像一个同袍对共处黑暗威力中间的人们应有的那一眼；因为马来人们正候着，警惕着，期待着他全然的领会。一切智者都知道这些事是真的。越过诺里的头顶，在对面墙上高处的幽暗里，有一瞥外间月光下的事物——一方莹亮的银版，嵌着一片树叶的狰狞黑影，宛如一副讪笑面具的侧脸。

## XXVIII / 二十八

===Original===
They had wandered beyond the verifications of the map, which for some time had been little better than the nearest a cartographer could do with what was mainly hearsay. When the country about a camp gave them no hope of gain it was easy to build a house elsewhere; four corner props, some palm leaves laid on cross beams, and a floor of boughs raised well above the ground. A constant fire dried the gear, for the rain, though terse as a rule, made no mistake about it while it was speaking; the fire kept the fanatical leeches at bay, and discouraged the curiosity of night prowlers. Norrie was cleaning his gun. This was one of the mornings when, bent and patient, he sat at a small task suitable for meditation, occasionally pausing to consider the ground before him. His thoughts at such times he did not always avow; they were, usually, but the prelude to packing, and another departure; taking with them again, so far, no more than the hope of a luckier site.

Colet was getting used to it. He had never known what morning was till he saw the dawn from a camp by the side of a jungle stream, a brief inauguration of the earth. He could wake at night now, hear the snarling moan of the tiger on the hill, rise to give the fire a plentiful feed, and forget it. He could work all day and not pass a word to his companion. And that was a good thing. He and Norrie did not have to speak, unless it was necessary, nor even look at each other. There might be a comment from Norrie, late at night, after he put aside the book he had been reading, and began to watch the firelight convulsive on a tree trunk, making the tree move in and out of the forest.

===Chinese===
他们已经漫游到地图无法验证的地方去了；这张图一段时间以来，也不过是制图师拿主要是道听途说的材料所能拼出的最贴近的近似。一处营地四周的地界若叫他们看不出得利的指望，换个地方再起一座房子是容易的：四根角柱，横梁上铺几片棕榈叶，再架一层离地老高的树枝地板。一堆常燃的火烘烤着行装，因为那雨虽说照例言简意赅，可开口的时候却毫不含糊；火拦住了那班狂热的蚂蟥，也挫了夜行兽类好奇的兴头。诺里在擦他的枪。这是这样一个早晨：他弓着身子，耐着性子，坐在一件宜于冥想的小活计跟前，间或停下来，端详面前那片地。这种时候他心里想些什么，他不总肯招认；那些念头照例不过是捆扎行装的序曲，加上又一场拔营；到眼下为止，随他们再度上路的，仍不外是对一块更走运的地的指望。

科利特渐渐习惯了。他从不知道早晨是什么，直到他在丛林溪畔的一处营地看见了黎明——大地一场简短的就职典礼。如今他夜里也醒得来，听得见山上老虎呜呜的低吼，起身给火饱饱添上一顿，然后把它忘掉。他能整天劳作，不同同伴交换一句话。这是件好事。他和诺里不必说话，除非万不得已，甚至连彼此看一眼都不必。深夜里诺里也许会来上一句评点，在他放下手头读着的书、开始望火光在一根树干上抽搐、把那棵树弄得在森林里出出进进之后。

===Original===
“Listen, Colet.”

Colet would listen. The hush was that at the world’s end. No. There was something beneath the silence. Perhaps the sap rising in the trees; the breathing of creatures; the pulse of the forest. But all was dark, the darkness over which had never been pronounced the call to light. The collapse of a little ash in the fire was notable. One looked at it instantly.

===Chinese===
「听着，科利特。」

科利特便听。那种静，是世界尽头的静。不。寂静底下另有点什么。也许是树身里上升的汁液；是生灵的呼吸；是森林的脉搏。可四下里一片黑，一片从来不曾对它宣读过『要有光』的黑暗。火里一小块柴灰的坍落，便成了大事。人立刻便朝它望去。

===Original===
“Listen to what?”

Norrie smiled.

“To what we can’t hear. Suppose we heard begin the Andante from the Fifth Symphony﻿—out in the trees beyond our light. Or if a choir suddenly exploded with ‘Worthy is the Lamb.’ What about it? The leopards would change their spots with fright. And what would you make of it? You’d think it was the Last Day and your number was up.”

===Chinese===
「听什么？」

诺里笑了笑。

「听我们听不见的东西。假如我们听见第五交响曲的行板就在我们火光外头的树林里起了头，那会怎样。又或者一队唱诗班突然爆出一句『羔羊配得颂』。怎么样？豹子都会吓得换了身上的斑点。那你会把它当作什么？你会当那是最后审判的日子，你的大限到了。」

===Original===
Sometimes you considered Norrie as though you had never met him before. He knew that, though, and before you could recognise him he was behind the door.

Now he was cleaning his gun. The Chinaman was squatting by the stream below, washing the dishes. They could hear the Malays cutting firewood. All the immobility of the forest was but the whirr of a grasshopper. The gun was put aside.

===Chinese===
有时候你打量诺里，仿佛从来不曾见过他。不过这个他自己也知道，趁你还没能认出他来，他已经退到门背后去了。

这会儿他在擦枪。那中国人蹲在下面溪边洗着碗碟。他们听得见马来人砍柴的声响。森林全然的纹丝不动，不过是一只蚱蜢的振翅。枪被搁到了一旁。

===Original===
“How long have we been on this pitch?”

“I dunno.” Colet went into the shelter to find a date. “Eight days.”

“Nothing here but signs. Good signs, too. All the bright promises of earth, Colet. Isn’t she kind to her children? But they lead nowhere.”

“But if they were not meant for promises! They may not have been. Not meant for signs at all. What could you expect them to lead to more than they have?”

===Chinese===
「咱们在这块地上扎了多久了？」

「说不上来。」科利特进棚子里去找个日子。「八天。」

「这儿除了征象什么也没有。还尽是好征象。大地所有光明的许诺，都在这儿了，科利特。她待自己的孩子难道不仁慈吗？可它们哪儿也通不到。」

「可要是它们压根就不是许下的诺言呢！兴许本来就不是。压根就不是什么征象。除了它们已经给出的，你还能指望它们通向什么呢？」

===Original===
“Dear old Colet. There he goes. But I’ll tell him again. I want to give the moths and rust a chance to corrupt something that belongs to me. I’ll moth ’em, if they come near it.”

“I don’t feel that way about it. But look here. If you do lift the lid off a hoard, watch me do the Highland fling with the accordant triumphant noises.”

===Chinese===
「亲爱的老科利特。他又来了。不过我还要跟他说一遍。我要给蛀虫和锈坏一个机会，让它们糟蹋一件属于我自己的东西。它们真要凑上前去，我倒要反过来蛀它们一蛀。」

「我对这事没有那种感觉。可是你听好。你当真从一处窖藏上掀开了盖子，就看我跳一支高地舞，配上相称的凯旋喧闹。」

===Original===
“I know. You are like that. But it’s not the right spirit. It’s simply devilish. It’s only your damned playful sympathy. You’d have been a nice Christian all complete with another touch of dreary misfortune. Colet, it makes me doubt you. You’ll come to no good end. You really won’t. I’m inclined to think that you might even fold your hands like a pale martyr, or a skinned rabbit, some day, and let the other fellow have the girl. It’s wicked, you know. It’s unfair to the poor darling. Don’t you ever love your neighbour as yourself, unless you want him to know what a fool you are.”

“I should like to hear your own answer to that.”

===Chinese===
「我知道。你就是这号人。可这不是正经的心术。简直是恶劣。那不过是你那要命的嬉皮笑脸的同情心。只消再添上一场阴惨惨的不幸，你本可以做一个十全的基督徒了。科利特，这叫我犯疑。你不会有好下场。真的不会。我简直认为，说不定哪一天，你会像个脸色苍白的殉道者，或者一只剥了皮的兔子，把两只手交叠起来，让另一个家伙把姑娘得了去。这是作孽，你知道。这对那可怜的宝贝不公平。你千万别爱人如己，除非你想让他知道你是个多大的傻瓜。」

「我倒想听听你自己对这一条的答复。」

===Original===
“Then you’ll have to wait till I’m perfectly safe.”

“No point in it, then.”

===Chinese===
「那你就得等到我万无一失的时候。」

「那可就没意思了。」

===Original===
“Oh, there will be, though. There will be. That is the point. It’s the right time to embrace the sad victims of fate when you have got nothing better to do. No point in being another victim.”

He waited a minute, and then picked up his gun again.

===Chinese===
「哦，到那时会有的。会有的。妙就妙在这儿。等你实在没有更好的事可做了，那正是搂抱命运那些悲苦受难者的良辰。再搭进去一个受难者，可就没有一点意思了。」

他等了一分钟，然后又把枪拿了起来。

===Original===
“I wouldn’t have the nerve to look at the world unless I were sure of a cushioned corner in it. It would be a terror of a hole. There’s no sense in it unless we put it there, so don’t you try to find it. Just think of humanity messing up its planet with progress﻿—shoving things about, piling ’em up, and especially getting cockeyed with deep religious conviction when making its worst muck of its place. It’s enough to bring down on us the Olympian sanitary inspector. I want a clear space in that jolly old riot. Then I shan’t mind the Gadarene rush so much. It might be comic to watch it then, something to pass the time; but I’ve no fancy to be among the hooves.”

===Chinese===
「倘若不能确知这世上有我一块铺垫停当的软角落，我是没有胆量朝这世界看上一眼的。不然它就是个骇人的窟窿。这世上本没有意义，除非由我们自己放进去，所以你也别想去把它找出来。想想人类正拿进步作践它的星球吧——把东西推来搡去，堆了又堆，尤其是在把它的地盘搅得最烂的时候，还怀着深沉的宗教笃诚醉眼惺忪。这足够把奥林匹斯的卫生稽查员招降到我们头上来。在那场热热闹闹的大乱子里，我要一块清净的地方。那样，加大拉的那阵狂奔我也就不很放在心上了。到那时看着它，也许还怪逗的，好歹是桩消磨光阴的事；可要我夹在那群蹄子中间，我没这份兴致。」

===Original===
“Well, by God, Norrie, I never thought of it before. But you’re afraid.”

“I am, when it comes down to it. You’ve given it a name. When I look at life in the eyes, in the hope of finding reason in it, my little inside turns pale. Cast your mind back to the Thames embankment and its outcasts at midnight, and get the horrors. Here, we’ll be off. Let’s go and do a little healthy gravel washing.”

===Chinese===
「嘿，老天在上，诺里，这个我从前可从没想到过。你是害怕。」

「我是怕，刨根问底地说吧。你给它起了名字。每当我直视生活的眼睛、指望从里头找出点道理来，我这点小肚肠就发白。回想回想泰晤士河的堤岸和午夜时分堤上的流落者吧，保你汗毛倒竖。来，咱们走。去做一点有益健康的淘砂。」

===Original===
A shallow stream so clear that its bed of quartz granules appeared to be under glass, came down in an easy glide from a valley head. It coiled about the lower buttresses of the forest. Only in brief stretches of quieter water was its surface open to the sky. The trees enclosed it, and muffled its voice, which was the only one there in the heat of the day. To Colet its bed was but unusually clean and white. The angular grains were displayed by the clarity of the water. Yet for his companion the stones had various names and implications. They were more than stones. Norrie must have known a lot. If he could find in the eternal forest an outlook from a ridge, he could guess the nature of a distant valley by the tone of its foliage, which all appeared to be of a sombre green; an ocean of rounded billows. He could read a spread of gravel in his palm as though it were a page of a book. Show him a lump of local mud in a new place at night and he would tell you what you would see in the morning, with instances of detail according to his humour; what vegetation would be infernal there, whether they would still be as hungry as they were then, and whether the inflammatory patches on their feet would improve or suppurate.

===Chinese===
一条浅溪从一道山谷的谷头舒舒缓缓滑泻而下，清澈得溪底的石英细砂仿佛是隔玻璃陈设的。它在森林低处的板根之间蜿蜒缠绕。只有几小段较为安静的水面，才向天空敞开。树木围拢了它，捂住了它的嗓音——那是白天暑热里那里唯一的声音。在科利特眼里，溪底不过是出奇地干净、出奇地白。水的清澈把有棱有角的砂粒一一陈列出来。可在他的同伴眼里，那些石子却有各色名目与涵义。它们不止是石子。诺里准是懂得极多。倘若他能在无始无终的森林里从一道山梁上寻得一处眺望，他便能凭远处山谷林木的色调猜出那山谷的脾性——那些林木看上去一概是阴郁的绿；一片浑圆怒涛之海。他能把摊在掌心的一捧砂砾读得像书页一样。夜里在一处新地方给他看一块当地的泥团，他便告诉你明天会见到什么，细节如何举例还看他的兴致：那里的草木会有多凶顽，他们到那时还会不会同眼下一样挨饿，还有他们脚上发炎的斑疹会见好还是溃烂。

===Original===
“It’s the nose, Colet. Only the nose. It’s my gross selfishness. I’m so uncomfortable when in ignorance that even an unseen novelty anywhere near will make my nose twitch till I find it. That’s what unwholesome curiosity does for a man. That’s the result of being a dirt washer﻿ ﻿… but there’s a lot in dirt. It tells you what the bedrock may be. Haven’t you ever watched our Chinaman? Doesn’t he ever make your soul curl up at the corners?”

===Chinese===
「是鼻子，科利特。全凭鼻子。这是我不折不扣的自私。一无所知的时候我难受得很，近旁随便什么没露面的新奇东西，都会叫我鼻子直抽动，直到把它寻出来。不健康的好奇心加诸一人，就是这个下场。这就是当淘砂人的下场……可泥砂里名堂多着呢。它能告诉你底下的基岩可能是什么。你从没留意过咱们那个中国人吗？他难道从来不曾叫你的灵魂边儿上都发起卷来吗？」

===Original===
“Johnny? He’s only a shadow. There’s nothing the matter with him. He never even speaks﻿—only makes a gurgle or two.”

“That’s all he can do. If he wasn’t so careful with the stuff I’d be afraid he’d drop some of his opium into the grub. But he loves that more than he hates us. I should like to see a section of the bedrock of that Chink under the microscope. Have you seen him putting little saucers of rice under one of the trees? A devil there he knows about, and we don’t. He keep crackers, to frighten the goblins. A section of his faith would prove unusual, under polarised light. Or of yours, Colet, or of yours. A bit of the bottom of your mind, ground thin, would fascinate me all the evening, with a lens of high power.”

===Chinese===
「那家伙？他不过是一道影子。他没一点毛病。他连话都不说——只咕噜一两声。」

「他也只会这么多。要不是他料理那东西那么经心，我还真怕他会把他的一点鸦片掉进饭食里。好在他对那东西的爱，压过他对我们的恨。我倒想拿那中国佬的基岩切一片，搁显微镜底下瞧瞧。你见过他往一棵树底下摆几小碟米饭吗？那儿有个他认得、我们却不知道的魔鬼。他还存着爆竹，吓那些小妖精。把他的信仰切一片，搁偏振光底下验验，准保异样。你的也一样，科利特，你的也一样。你心底的一小块，磨得薄薄的，够我举着高倍镜足足消遣一个晚上。」

===Original===
“But not me. Nothing there to give me an appetite. That predilection of yours for Beethoven﻿—did you find it in the dirt?”

“Quite right. All my fault. I asked for it. Now we’ll conclude our little inquiry into origins. When a fellow like you grows metaphysical I get lost. But you wouldn’t. Mystics can see anything in a fog, just anything, if only it’s thick enough. The thicker the better. But I loathe fogs. I can’t see so well in a fog.”

===Chinese===
「我可不。那儿没有什么能叫我开胃。你对贝多芬的那份偏爱——是泥砂里淘出来的吗？」

「一点不错。全怪我。是我自找的。好，咱们这就结束这场关于起源的小小考问。像你这样的人一玄起来，我就晕头转向。可你不会。神秘主义者在雾里什么都看得见，什么都行，只要雾够浓。越浓越好。可我讨厌雾。在雾里我可看不大清。」

===Original===
“Well, perhaps it wouldn’t be unfair to ask now whether we may look for gold here. Is there any?”

“That’s better. And there is. But Colet, where does it come from? That’s what beats me. I wish I was a mystic, or had second sight, or inspiration, or the devil’s own luck. Anything to take me where science can’t. The truth is, there’s bright little signs of happiness everywhere in this country. They lure us on like the portrait of a charmer whose favours were all distributed long ago, though we don’t know it. Oh, Colet, to think of it.”

===Chinese===
「那么，这会儿问一声我们在这儿找不找得到金子，也许不算不公平吧。到底有没有？」

「这还差不多。有。可是科利特，它是打哪儿来的？这才是真正难住我的地方。但愿我是个神秘主义者，或者有第二视力，或者有神启，或者有魔鬼看顾的好运。随便什么，只要能带我到科学到不了的地方。实情是，这地方遍地都是幸福的小小亮征。它们引诱我们往前赶，像一位迷人的美人的画像，她的恩宠其实早已散尽给了旁人，只是我们不知道罢了。唉，科利特，想想这个。」

===Original===
They stooped to the stream, whirled the gravel in pans, and when neither perspiration nor another storm could saturate them more would examine the pinch of yellow dust that was all their reward. The metal had a strange loveliness, under the lens. To Colet it did not seem inadequate. For Norrie was near, with his droll comments. There was the apparition of the forest about them, silent and still; you had to touch a leathery leaf of it, to make sure of it, when stretching the back after intent diligence with the stream. Colet would pause in the washing now and then, checked by the only movement, a visiting butterfly, designed and coloured like joy, a flicker of silent mirth in the face of the wild. The butterflies did not object to a close inspection when they settled on a damp hummock of white sand under his nose; if he touched them they merely circused a little, and then came to the same spot, made themselves comfortable, and laid out their wings for inspection again.

===Chinese===
他们俯身到溪边，在淘盘里旋摇砂砾，等到汗水和又一场暴雨都已把他们浸得无可再浸，便细看那一小撮金黄色的尘沙——那便是他们全部的酬报。在放大镜底下，那金属有一种奇异的可爱。在科利特看来，它并不嫌少。因为诺里就在近旁，带着他滑稽的评点。森林的幻影围在他们四周，无声无息；对着溪水专心卖力了一阵、直起腰来舒展背脊的时候，你得伸手去摸一摸它一片皮革似的叶子，才能确信它在。科利特淘着淘着，间或会停下手来，被那里唯一的动静拦住——一只来访的蝴蝶，花纹与颜色设计得像喜乐，一张在荒野面前无声闪现的笑靥。蝴蝶落在他鼻子底下潮湿的白沙丘上时，并不反对有人凑近端详；他若碰一碰它们，它们不过马戏似的兜上一个小圈，随即又回到原处，安顿舒服了，重新摊开翅膀，听凭检视。

===Original===
Norrie declined to eat, when they sat by a tree, at midday. If he spoke, it was captiously. Once or twice his companion looked at him, surprised by a word that was venomous. Here was a corner beyond the hubbub, in a light like glory, and Norrie addressing Heaven, for his want of luck, as though it were the face of a dirty urchin who had soiled his property. Anything the matter with him? His hands were hanging listless over his knees, and he was brooding. His hands seemed queer. The fingers were lemon-colour, and the nails blue. Then Norrie peered over at him, and his jaw was chattering.

Colet became solicitous.

“Anything wrong, old chap?”

“I wondered what was coming. We’ll get back. I’ve got a touch of fever. Cold. It’s damned cold.”

===Chinese===
正午，他们在一棵树底下坐定时，诺里不肯吃东西了。他若开口，话里带着刺。有一两回，同伴望了望他，被一句恶毒的话吓了一跳。这里是喧嚣之外的一隅，光照如荣光，而诺里正为自己时运不济对上天发作，仿佛那是弄脏了他家产业的一个顽童的脏脸。他有什么不对劲吗？他的两手恹恹地垂在膝上，人正郁郁地出神。他那双手显得异样。手指是柠檬色的，指甲发青。随后诺里朝他这边窥过来，牙关咯咯打战。

科利特关切起来。

「怎么啦，老伙计？」

「我正纳闷是什么找上门来了。咱们回去吧。我染上一点热病了。冷。冷得要命。」

## XXIX / 二十九

===Original===
Norrie sank into his hammock, and remained, still and yellow, with his eyes shut, as though dead. The camp that evening suggested a depth in solitude which was more remote than Colet had ever known. The four Malays were apart, conferring together, unheard, almost merged in the wilderness. The Chinaman was nothing; his face always was expressionless and averted. And Norrie, in a sense, had left them. He was with Norrie, but Norrie was not with him. It was lucky he had got that dose of quinine into the poor old fellow before he became lightheaded.

What was he muttering about? Nothing more to be done for him. The natives didn’t seem to care; they only glanced casually at the lumpy hammock, and then forgot it. The day, the last of it, was in the treetops across the stream, and under that lane of upper gold was the unknown, and night already filling its hollows. The cicadas abruptly began their sunset ovation. They knew the signal; the signal was the light on the tree with a dead top. The gaunt antlers became flames, and the jungle instantly was a din, though it never stirred. There ought to be a movement, surely a leaf should shake, when pandemonium broke loose: buzzing of circular saws, hissing of steam, shrill whistling, the husky stridulating of dry membranes, the humming of wires, the verberating of notes inaudible; the exultant celebration of another life in a place not his. It was like triumph over mortal men.

Norrie called out, but when Colet went to him the sick man was moribund, with his eyes closed. The light died. The uproar in the woods instantly ceased. Night put out both day and the paean. The darkness and the silence were the same. Colet sat down on a packing box by the hammock, to wait. This was going to be a night of it. He touched Norrie’s face; it was indifferent; it was hot and dry. What happened to men with malaria?
===Chinese===
诺里陷进他的吊床，一动不动，蜡黄着脸，闭着眼睛，仿佛已经死了。那天傍晚的营地透出一种孤独的深，那份偏远，超出科利特平生所知。四个马来人在一旁，聚在一起商量着什么，听不见声音，几乎融进了荒野。那个中国人算不得什么；他的脸向来没有表情，总转向一边。而诺里，在某种意义上，已经离开了他们。他人还守在诺里身边，诺里却已不在他这儿了。幸亏他赶在这位可怜的老伙计变得神志昏沉之前，让他服下了那一剂奎宁。

他在咕哝些什么？再没有什么可为他做的了。土人似乎并不在意；他们只朝那鼓作一团的吊床随意瞥了一眼，随即把它忘了。白昼，它的最后一线，还在溪对岸的树梢上；那条高处的金色巷道下面是未知，夜已经在灌注它的洼处。蝉骤然开始了它们日落时分的喝彩。它们认得那信号；信号就是落在那棵顶梢枯死的树上的光。嶙峋的鹿角燃成了火焰，丛林霎时成了一片喧声，尽管它纹丝不动。大乱骤起的时候，总该有些动静，总该有一片叶子颤动吧：圆锯的嗡鸣、蒸汽的嘶嘶、尖厉的呼哨、干膜的沙哑摩擦、铁线的嗡嗡、那些听不见的音符的隆隆回荡；那是另一种生命在一个不属于他的地方举行的欢庆。那像是对凡人的得胜。

诺里喊了一声，可等科利特走到他跟前，病人已经垂垂待毙，闭着眼睛。光熄了。林中的喧腾顿然止息。夜把白昼和凯歌一并掐灭。黑暗与寂静是同一个东西。科利特在吊床边一只货箱上坐下，等着。这一夜有得熬了。他碰了碰诺里的脸；那张脸漠然无知；又烫又干。害疟疾的人会变成什么样？

===Original===
The silence stretched out into illimitable leagues of nothing, to a depth where it could never be stirred. The air became cooler, and he packed up Norrie. The Chinaman stretched on the floor. He was only a loose rag on the beams. The Malays were in their own hut. The fire was alive. Only the fire was alive. The hammock had not moved for a long time. All right? Norrie was still hot, anyhow. Colet took his seat again, and waited.

It was queer to watch the feet of the trees. The firelight shaped them. They moved in and out of the forest. Sometimes they vanished. They had retreated into the woods. When a lump collapsed in the fire the flames started again, and the feet of the trees moved in and out of the skirts of the darkness in a noiseless but massive dance.﻿ ﻿…

What was that? He must have been dreaming. Perhaps Norrie had called out. No; the hammock hadn’t moved. Norrie was the same as before. The shadow of the Chink was still like a loose heap of rags. He had not stirred. There was only night, and a hush as though something were lying in wait. Queer. He grinned himself into confidence. This was a rum situation; like being a child at midnight lost in the Tower dungeons.
===Chinese===
寂静向外铺展开去，铺成无穷多少里格的虚无，深到永远无法搅动的地方。空气凉了下来，他把诺里裹了个严实。那个中国人摊开手脚躺在地上。他不过是梁木间一团松垮的破布。马来人们在自家棚屋里。火是活的。只有火是活的。吊床已经很久没有动过了。还好吗？反正诺里还是烫的。科利特重新落座，等着。

看着树的脚，滋味很怪。火光把它们塑了出来。它们在森林里进进出出。有时它们隐没了。它们退进林子深处去了。火里一块柴坍下去，火焰重新窜起，树的脚便在黑暗的裙裾边进进出出，跳一场无声而沉重的舞。……

那是什么？他准是做梦了。兴许是诺里喊了一声。不；吊床没动过。诺里还是先前的样子。中国佬的影子仍像一堆松散的破布。他没有动过。只有夜，和一种仿佛有什么东西正埋伏守候的寂静。怪。他咧嘴一笑，给自己笑出了几分胆气。这处境真是古怪；像个小孩子半夜里迷了路，困在伦敦塔（the Tower）的地牢中。

===Original===
The fire had gone down since he saw it last. About time he made it up. Wanted some resolution to get up and do it, though. You had to move from where you were. What would happen if one moved? Would that set anything going? It felt as if some diabolical business was hanging about. Certainly he heard a sound. It was like the dominant prelude of a Handel march, the music Norrie had told him to listen for, one night. But it was a long way from the croak of a frog in the jungle to Handel. Good God!

Loud in the night he heard the blast of a trumpet. Just beyond the fire. That was no dream. The Chinaman was sitting up. Colet hesitated, rose, and went to peer aside from the hindering of the firelight. He would have felt better if he had known what was there. He could see only a shadow was out of its place beyond. He could make out two white marks like the branches of a tree. But a swamp was there. No tree there. Then he heard a whisper in Malay: “*Gajah.*”

So it was. The shadow was an elephant; what was plain was the gleam of the firelight on its tusks. A flame shifted in the fire, and the beast’s ears then spread out; its trunk was curled over its head. The flame incensed that huge front. It squealed, and advanced a little, squashing and lumbering.
===Chinese===
打他上回看火到现在，火势已经落了下去。也该添柴了。不过，要起身去干这件事，得拿出点决断来。你得从你现在待的地方挪开。挪动一下会出什么事？会把什么东西惊动起来吗？感觉仿佛有什么恶魔的勾当正在近旁盘桓。他分明听见了一个声音。它像亨德尔（Handel）一支进行曲的属音前奏——那种音乐，诺里有一天夜里叫他留心去听的。可是从丛林里的一声蛙鸣到亨德尔，这中间路远着呢。天哪！

夜色里，他听见一声号角的长鸣。就在火堆外面。那不是梦。那个中国人坐了起来。科利特迟疑片刻，起身走到一旁，避开火光的遮挡向外窥望。倘若知道那边有什么，他心里会好受些。他只看得出外面有一团影子，离开了它该在的位置。他辨认出两道白色的印痕，像一株树的枝桠。可那边是一片沼泽。那儿没有树。接着他听见一句马来语的低语：「『加贾』。」

果然是它。那影子是一头象；明明白白的，是火光在它长牙上的闪烁。火里一条火苗晃了晃，那头巨兽的耳朵随之张开；它的长鼻卷在头顶上。火苗激怒了那庞大的前身。它尖啸一声，向前挪近了些，一路碾碾轧轧，步态沉笨。

===Original===
No good trying to shoot dead such a bulk at night. It would have the flimsy show flattened in a rush. He went over to the sick man, helplessly, but Norrie was not interested in anything on earth, not even wild elephants. Colet stood by the hammock while the brute raged and trampled about. He would have to stand there. That beast was trying to make up his mind to come on. Better keep quiet, out of sight, and chance it. Trying to make up its mind, and evidently doing it. Colet snatched up a flaming brand in desperation, and flung it at the uproar. It backed, but worked itself into a worse passion. This couldn’t last.

The Chink had gone. There were no Malays. The burning sticks wouldn’t last long. The beast began to threaten with agile little rushes. Surprisingly quick and light, yet the place shook. What could you do with a man in a hammock? Colet’s eyes were on the huge and noisy shadow, and so he swore when the unseen Chinaman unexpectedly clutched his arm. The fellow was voluble, and had something in his hand. The Chink went to the fire, touched the object with a brand, and flung it at the invader. The spark leaped into a tangle of erratic explosions, and the elephant at once became a series of rapidly diminishing crashes in the forest. Colet began to laugh, but stopped. He recognised that his laughter was pitched in too high a key. A blessed cracker﻿—one of those the Chink used for keeping off devils. The Chinaman stood there with his head solemnly bent, listening to the sounds of an elephantine panic retreating out of hearing. Then he curled up again on the floor without a word.

===Chinese===
夜里想把这样一具庞然大物一枪打死，是没有用的。它一阵猛冲就能把这单薄的一摊夷为平地。他无可奈何地走到病人跟前，可诺里对世间任何东西都不再感兴趣了，连野象也不例外。那畜生暴怒着、践踏着，科利特守在吊床边。他只好站在那儿。那头兽正拿定主意要冲上来。还是不出声、避开它的眼睛、听天由命为好。它正在拿主意，而且显然拿定了。科利特情急之下抓起一支燃着的柴火，朝那片喧腾掷了过去。它退了退，却越发狂怒起来。这样下去是撑不了多久的。

中国佬不见了。马来人一个也不在。燃烧的柴枝撑不了多久。那头兽开始用灵巧的小冲刺示威。快得出奇，轻得出奇，可这地方都震动了。对一个躺在吊床里的人，你还有什么办法？科利特的眼睛盯住那团又大又闹的黑影，所以当那个未曾看见的中国人冷不丁抓住他的胳膊时，他骂了一句。那家伙嘴里滔滔不绝，手里拿着一样东西。中国佬走到火边，用一支燃柴把那物件点着，朝那入侵者掷去。火星跳进一蓬乱纷纷的炸响，那头象当即化作森林里一串迅速远去、迅速减弱的轰隆声。科利特笑出声来，随即打住。他听出自己的笑声拔得太高了调门。一只谢天谢地的爆竹——就是中国佬用来吓魔鬼的那一种。中国人站在那里，庄严地垂着头，听着一场大象的惊慌退到听不见的地方去。然后他一言不发，又蜷起身子躺回地上。

## XXX / 三十

===Original===
The high cliffs of trees around their hut so overhung that the sun never found them till near noon. It was like being at the bottom of a well. Daylight fell to the stream beside the hut as a few long shining rods which leaned on upper shadows and rested on the bottom of the rivulet. The hut was foundered in the forest. None of them ventured far from it alone. In the cool of the morning the calling of birds gave clear depth to the surrounding obscurity. One bird was a tolling bell, and another was a blacksmith at an anvil. There was another who was an idle boy learning to whistle, but who never got the phrase right. But he persevered. No bird was seen. Nothing ever moved there, except themselves.

The leisurely bird was still learning to whistle. It had nothing else to do. Nor had Colet, but to listen to it. Norrie mimicked the bird, and corrected it. A good effort, for Norrie.

“The little beggar always falls off the tune just before the end. I think I make a noise jolly well. Did you notice it?”

===Chinese===
小屋四周树木高崖般交相掩覆，太阳要到将近正午才寻得着他们。那就像置身井底。天光落到屋旁的溪流上，是几竿细长的光柱，斜倚着上层的暗影，息在小溪的底上。小屋沉陷在森林深处。他们谁也不敢独自离屋走远。清晨凉爽时分，鸟鸣给四下的幽暗量出了清亮的深度。有一只鸟是一口缓敲的钟，另一只是铁砧前的铁匠。还有一只是个闲散少年，正学吹口哨，却总也吹不对那个乐句。可他锲而不舍。鸟一只也见不着。那里从来没有什么会动，除了他们自己。

那只从容不迫的鸟还在学口哨。它旁的事没有。科利特也一样，只有听它的份儿。诺里模仿那鸟，还纠正它。对诺里来说，这已经算是卖力了。

「这小要饭的总在收尾前掉调。我觉得我那声响吹得够漂亮。你留意到了吗？」

===Original===
“I did.” Colet cheered the attempt, though his amusement was not quite assured. He was dubious. There was Norrie, but reduced to a framework. His face was not of the colour of life, so when he smiled it was anything but a smile. His sardonic nose was pinched, and with his light-grey eyes, understanding but bleak, and his rumpled grey hair, now too plentiful for his face, he suggested a crested predatory bird.

“My whistle was about as thin as me.”

“Let’s have it again. We could do with a cheerful noise.”

“Don’t shovel out any pity on me, Colet. After a bout of this sort we excite pity, but not enough for a shovel. I’ll walk your legs off in a day or two. You won’t lose me yet, so you needn’t abandon yourself to hope.”

===Chinese===
「留意到了。」科利特给这番尝试捧了场，尽管他心里那点好笑还不大拿得稳。他是将信将疑的。诺里还是诺里，只是瘦得只剩一副架子。他脸上没有活人的颜色，所以他笑起来的时候，那简直怎么都不是笑。他那含讥带诮的鼻子瘪陷着，配上一双浅灰色的眼睛——善解人意，却冷冷清清——还有一头揉乱了的灰发（如今对他的脸来说嫌多了），整个人叫人想起一只竖着冠的猛禽。

「我的口哨跟我这个人差不多一样单薄。」

「再来一声吧。来点快活的响动，咱们不嫌多。」

「别把怜悯一铲一铲往我身上送，科利特。闹过这么一场之后，我们这种人招人怜悯，可还够不上一铲子的量。过一两天，我就走得你两条腿都直不起来。你还丢不下我呢，所以也不必一门心思沉到指望里去。」

===Original===
He made to pass Colet; but paused, rested his hand on Colet’s shoulder, weighed on it perhaps rather too long, and went on. Old Norrie was strange; you couldn’t tell then whether he was sentimental, or only gave way at the knees. He did that sort of thing. He simulated humanity, for a lark, or else he pretended that satire was the best he could do.

But as Norrie said, he soon had them going again. There were new activities. He began to lead them another dance. They left the hut for whoever might want it. Colet had got used to that floor of rough boughs with its roof of brown fronds. He knew the individual bits of the floor, the beam that rolled if you put your foot on it, the catchy knot by Norrie’s hammock, the depression in which he preferred to spread his own sleeping mat. The only bare dry earth he had seen for three humid months was under that floor. Actually, the patch of ground under the hut was dusty; the dust was an experience when you crawled under for a knife which had slipped through. Their fire would go out today. Without a light there, and nobody near, that hut would be worse than the jungle. It would deepen the quietude. He turned, the end man, to see the abandoned camp for the last time. The bare thought of solitude in the old shanty gave Colet a presentiment of the horrors. One would want hardened nerves to face only oneself, in the wilderness.

===Chinese===
他作势要从科利特身边走过去；却顿住，把一只手搭上科利特的肩头，压着的工夫也许略微长了些，然后继续走。老诺里这人古怪；那种时候你说不准他是动了感情，还是只是膝盖撑不住。他就爱来这一手。他装出一副人的心肠，权当逗乐，再不然，就装作冷嘲热讽已是他拿得出的最好本事。

可是正如诺里所言，他很快又让他们动起来了。有了新的营生。他开始领他们跳另一支舞。小屋留给随便哪个想要它的人去。科利特已经惯了那粗糙树枝铺就的地板，和棕褐色棕叶搭成的顶。他认得那地板上的一块块木料：踩上去会滚的那根梁，诺里吊床旁边那个绊脚的树疤，还有他自己更乐意铺睡席的那处凹洼。三个月的潮湿里，他见过的唯一一块裸露的干土，就在那地板底下。说实在的，小屋底下那块地面上还积着灰；爬到底下捡一把掉下去的刀子时，那灰也算得一番阅历。他们的火堆今天就要熄了。那里面一旦没了火光，四近又没有人烟，那小屋会比丛林还糟。它会加深那片沉寂。他走在队尾，回过身，最后望一眼那被撇下的营地。单是想到在那座旧窝棚里独自一人，科利特便预感到恐怖的滋味。在荒野里只面对自己一个人，那得有一副硬起来的神经才成。

===Original===
The regions through which they travelled suggested that they were the first men to see it. They were under an earlier spell of the earth; it was not merely a new country. The sunlight was younger, and sounds were clearer and without fear. Its life, which was its forest, was haunting with its magnitude and extravagant outpouring. It was mute, except at sunset and evening, when it praised the sun, the only god which had yet come to it. When day came, and just as day was departing, the creatures of the woods broke out with that racket which was the sudden release of the pent vehemence of spirits that were without name or shape.

The sun was well down towards the roof of the jungle when they emerged from the twilight of the woods. They were in an open space by a greater river. The men began to build another shelter. It was a relief to see the open sky. Here was full daylight, and the sight could range to distant prospects. Colet wondered how Norrie, still absurdly thin, and bleached by fever and the forest, had maintained so evenly that day’s long hike; even the patient Malays showed they had had enough of it, and they were made of bronze. The Chinaman, of course, wasn’t human. His own body felt as if every length of elastic in it had been stretched; pulled out and snapped all day long. Now he was at ease, fatigued but contented. Norrie was a wonder. He would have given stout Cortez enough for the day, and then have shown even a conquistador, in an evening talk, that there were things he did not know as surprising as the prospect to be seen from a peak in Darien. But in these latter days good men were not conquerors, but navvies, or prospectors, or engineers, or chief mates. Nobody knew them but one or two pals.

The river was low, a shining network about reefs of smooth granite boulders. A beach of white sand under the sombre forest had the shape and pallor of a crescent moon. The water could be heard; it was just audible; but its voice was subdued and stealthy. And it was the only sound, except the occasional slashing of the parangs of the Malays, and that noise was as though the sanctity of an inviolable concealment were being riven. The slash of a heavy knife across that quiet was not quite right. The trees beyond the water, however, took no notice of it. Were they an illusion, or only dumb with astonishment? The front of the jungle opposite ascended into high cupolas and pinnacles, and was draped from its cornice to its base with a dense mesh of vines, green curtains in voluminous folds which sheeted the heights. One palm leaned out from it, its head over the river, as in an attempt at escape, which was checked, from the silent confusion. The sheen of lightning wavering round the coasts of clouds that were the colour of calamity moved and changed the hues of the sunset. The old clearing in which they stood was heavy with the scent of flowers. Over the forest, beyond the corner round which the river came down to them, was the hull of a towering berg, its flat summit dark with trees, but its walls bare and gleaming, as though of white marble. The last of the sun fired the clouds; the isolated hill became a beacon; and at that signal the cicadas and the legion of hidden creatures broke out with their celebrant jubilation. Colet had to raise his voice a little when he spoke to his companion.

===Chinese===
他们一路穿行的那些地界，叫人觉得自己是头一批看见它的人类。他们处在大地更早的一道符咒之下；这不只是一片新的国土。日光更年轻，声响更清亮，而且不带恐惧。它的生命——也就是它的森林——以其浩大与挥霍无度的倾泻萦绕人心。它缄默无言，只有日落与入夜时分例外，那时它赞美太阳——迄今降临到它面前的唯一神明。白昼来临时，还有白昼将逝的那一刻，林间的生灵便爆出那一阵喧闹——那是一群无名无形的精魂被压抑的烈性，骤然得了释放。

他们从林中的暮色里钻出来时，太阳已经沉沉落向丛林的顶盖。他们到了一条更大的河边的一块空地上。人们动手另搭一个棚子。重见敞亮的天空，是一桩宽慰。这里是大亮的天光，目光可以放出去很远。科利特纳闷，诺里还瘦得那么不像话，又叫热病和森林漂得没了血色，是怎么把那天漫长的跋涉撑得那样平稳匀称的；连吃苦耐劳的马来人都显出吃不消了，而他们是青铜铸的人。那个中国人么，当然，算不得血肉凡胎。他自己的身子，感觉像里头每一根橡皮筋都被人抻长了；拽出去、弹回来，闹了一整天。此刻他松快下来，疲乏而满足。诺里是个奇人。同他做伴，够壮实的科尔特斯（Cortez）应付上一整天；到了晚间的闲谈里，他还能源源不断地叫一位征服者（conquistador）开眼：世上也有他不知道的东西，其惊人一如从达里恩（Darien）的山巅望出去的那片景象。可是在近今这些年头，好人不做征服者了，只做挖土工、探矿的、工程师，或者大副。除了三两个伙伴，谁也不认得他们。

河水落了，绕着一丛丛光洁的花岗岩巨石，织成一张发亮的网。阴郁森林底下的一段白沙滩，有新月的形状，也有新月的苍白。水声听得见；勉强才听得见；可那嗓音低抑而隐秘。而且这是唯一的声音，除了马来人的帕兰刀偶尔的劈砍；而那声响，就像一种不容侵犯的隐匿的圣性正被劈开。一记重刀划过那片安宁，总有点不是滋味。不过水那边的树木并不理会。它们是幻象呢，还是只是惊得说不出话？对岸丛林的正面拔起成高高的穹顶与尖塔，从檐口到基脚披挂着密密的藤蔓网，绿色的帷幕褶襞重重，把高处遮蔽成幅。一株棕榈从那里斜探出来，树冠悬在河上，仿佛要从那场无声的纷乱里逃生，却被截住了。闪电的微光在云的海岸边摇曳——那云是灾祸的颜色——闪动间推移着、变换着落日的种种色调。他们站着的那方老林间空地，花香沉沉。森林之上，在河水绕下来找他们的那个拐角再过去，卧着一座巍峨石山的庞大身躯：平顶叫树木衬得幽暗，山壁却光秃秃地莹亮，仿佛白色大理石。太阳最后一线点燃了云；孤山成了一座烽台；得了这信号，蝉和那一军藏着的生灵便爆出它们庆典般的欢腾。科利特跟同伴说话时，不得不稍稍提高一点嗓门。

===Original===
“There are others here beside ourselves. We are not the first.”

“So our men say,” mused Norrie. “They want to go back.”

“Anything wrong? They have seemed moody today.”

“Enough to make them. They tell me this land is full of *hantus*, things that ought not to be about; souls not stowed safely away in Gehenna.”

===Chinese===
「这地方除了我们，还有旁人。我们不是头一批。」

「我们那几个人是这么说的，」诺里沉吟道，「他们想回去。」

「出了什么岔子吗？他们今天像是有点闷闷不乐。」

「够让他们那样的。他们跟我说，这片地界满是『汉图』（hantus）——些本不该在外面游荡的东西；是没给安安稳稳收纳进地狱（Gehenna）里的魂灵。」

===Original===
“It looks rather like it, now you’ve mentioned it. Shall I let off a few crackers, to keep them from crowding us?”

“No good. Something more elaborate than we could think of is wanted. The tobacco is in your pack. You notice how soon the fire is going?”

“They’re not afraid of the people of the woods?”

“No people there. Not the right sort. Nobody lives here. But long ago a prosperous lot of Chinese miners had this clearing. They did rather well, too.”

===Chinese===
「你这一提，倒真有几分像。要不要我放几响爆竹，免得它们挤过来围住我们？」

「不顶事。得有比我们想得出的更周章的法子。烟草在你背包里。你注意到那堆火多快就烧塌下去了吗？」

「他们不怕林子里的人吗？」

「那儿没有人。不是那一路人。这里没人住。不过很久以前，有一伙兴旺的中国矿工占着这片林间空地。他们干得还相当不赖。」

===Original===
“God rest their Chinese souls. They’re not here now.”

“They are. Our Malays say they are. They did too well. The rajah got to hear of it﻿—something he could get for nothing, being a rajah, so naturally he asked for it. The Chinamen forgot where they were, though. They told the rajah to take a carrot. I think that was what it was. And anyhow, Malays don’t regard the Chinese as men. Why should they? Chinamen have a different religion. So the Malays had nothing to argue about, except their honour. When a talk among themselves about honour had sufficiently excited them, they went on an enjoyable and successful outing, without warning, and the country has been like this ever since, except for the *hantus*. It does seem lonely, doesn’t it? The Malays know those Chinamen are still hanging around, though a bit changed in nature; and if I told you all the story you wouldn’t wonder at it. But the end of the yarn is better if heard in daylight.﻿ ﻿… Did you see that lump over there, that high rock with the trees on top? We’ll have a look at that in the morning. If we have to be turned aside by spooks, we’ll try to learn why they are so stuffy about it.”

===Chinese===
「愿上帝让他们的中国灵魂安息吧。他们如今可不在这儿了。」

「在的。我们那几个马来人说他们在。他们干得太好了。拉贾听到了风声——一样他可以白得的东西，既然是拉贾，自然就开口讨了。不过那些中国人忘了自己身在什么地方。他们叫拉贾去拿一根胡萝卜。我想原话大概就是这意思。再说，马来人不把中国人当人看。凭什么要当？中国人信的教不一样。于是马来人便没有别的可计较，除了他们的荣誉。等到他们自己人当中一场关于荣誉的谈话把他们煽动得够了，他们便不打招呼，出发作了一次愉快而大获全胜的郊游，打那以后，这地方就成了眼下这副模样，只除了那些『汉图』。它瞧着确实冷清，是不是？马来人知道那些中国人还在这一带晃荡，虽然性子变了一点；要是我把故事从头到尾讲给你听，你就不会觉得奇怪了。不过这故事的结尾，还是白天听的好。……你瞧见那边那一大块了吗，顶上长着树的高岩？明儿早上咱们去看看它。要是咱们叫鬼魂挡了道，咱们就想法子弄明白，它们在那上头凭什么这么不好说话。」

## XXXI / 三十一

===Original===
The berg rose out of the level forest by the river, and to Colet it was anomalous. It was an isolated mass of white limestone, a lofty island in the ocean of jungle. Its pale cliffs fell sheer to the green billows. Its summit was flat, but was so near to the clouds that its trees were but a dark undulating strip. Its walls, when glimpsed from below through breaks in the roof of the forest, appeared to overhang, but there were scarves and girdles of green on their bare ribs. An eagle soaring athwart its loftier crags was a drifting mote. Stalactites were pendent before the black portholes of caves in upper stories, like corbels over the outlooks of a castle of the sagas. If the number of those dark apertures meant anything, then the berg was hollow, was honeycombed with cavities. This enormity was not inviting, even in a morning light; not in such a land as that. The unexplored dungeons of such a castle might hide anything.

But Norrie judged it with a casual and professional eye. It was curious, but only geologically. He had seen such lumps before, of course. It was only what was left of an earlier skin of Malaya, a fragment of that country’s prehistoric hide. Time and the weather had peeled off all the rest. Unnatural? Well, look at it; was it not there? So how could it be unnatural? What he wanted to do was to get at it.

That was not easy, near and great as it was. The climbing palms, the rattans, flourished about it. Their taloned cables were coiled over the low ground in barriers unfriendly to the haste and impatience of men. Colet, bleeding and perspiring, had forgotten the rock by the time they had reached it. A little journey in that kind of undergrowth, crouching and crawling, while following the sound of a Malay’s parang, leaves room in the mind for but one interest. He crawled into a little clear space beside Norrie and two of the men. The island stood over them. They were at the base of a wall, and almost under a high Gothic porch, the entrance to the retreat, by the look of it, of midnight. Norrie but briefly inspected this rude resemblance of architecture, and was as indifferent to the sinister suggestions of the interior. He was not now discussing the ways of humanity, and so he appeared very cheerful. He declared that he loved caves, and insides that were convoluted and obscure. He was preparing to go in; he was testing some electric torches with a brisk assiduity which had its back to the forbidding fantasies of geological structure. The Malays, so they said, preferred to wait without. Their interest was spent. They went down on their hams and began to roll cigarettes while watching the tuans preparing to disappear on a foolish quest.

===Chinese===
石山从河边的平铺森林里拔起，在科利特眼里，它是个异数。它是一整块孤悬的白色石灰岩，丛林之洋里一座高岛。它苍白的崖壁直直落到绿色的涌浪上。山顶是平的，却离云太近，山上的树木只是一条暗色的、微微起伏的边带。它的山壁，从底下透过森林顶盖的豁口瞥见时，像是向外悬倾，可那赤裸的肋上缠着一道道绿色的披巾和腰带。一只横掠它高处危岩的鹰，只是一粒游尘。钟乳石悬垂在高层一孔孔洞穴的黑舷窗前，像萨迦（sagas）传说里城堡眺台上的梁托。倘若那些黑洞的数目还意味着什么，那便是：这石山是空心的，让空腔蛀成了蜂窝。这庞然巨物并不招人亲近，即便在清晨的天光里也不；在那样一片土地上尤其不。这样一座城堡里未曾探明的地牢，什么都能藏。

可是诺里用一双随随便便的行家眼睛评判它。它是有几分奇，不过奇也只在地质上。这一类的块垒他当然从前就见过。它不过是马来亚更早一层皮剩下的一点东西，是那个国家史前兽皮的一片残角。时间和天气把其余的都剥掉了。不自然？嗐，瞧瞧它；它不就在那儿吗？那它怎么会不自然？他想做的，是到它跟前去。

这可不是容易事，尽管它近在咫尺、巍然可见。攀缘的棕榈——白藤（rattans），在它四周长势兴旺。它们那些带爪的缆索在低地上盘成一圈圈壁垒，对人的性急与不耐颇不友好。等到他们抵达石山跟前时，科利特淌着血、流着汗，早把那岩石忘了个干净。在那样的灌木林里走一小段路，弓着身子爬，耳朵追着一个马来人帕兰刀的声响，脑子里便只容得下一个念头。他爬进一小块空地，挨到诺里和两个伙计身旁。那座岛就耸在他们头顶上。他们立在一面山壁的根脚，差不多正在一道高高的哥特式门廊底下——看那样子，那是午夜退隐之地的入口。对这粗陋的建筑近似物，诺里只草草看了一眼，对内部的种种不祥暗示，他同样漠然。他这会儿不是在讨论人类的风习，所以他显得非常快活。他宣称他爱洞穴，爱那些盘曲幽暗的内里。他正准备进去；正用一股利落的勤快试几支电筒，那股勤快把背对着地质构造拒人的种种奇想。马来人说，他们宁肯在外头等。他们的兴致用尽了。他们蹲踞下去，动手卷烟，一面瞧着两位老爷（tuans）收拾行装，准备为一桩愚蠢的差事钻进去不见了影。

===Original===
The threshold of the cave was of dry sand strewn with fallen rock. The day, venturing within as far as it could, hinted at fretted columns and aisles receding till the last shapes became what Colet chose to see there. The berg was hollow. Its recesses were capricious, and the disturbance of a rock by the invaders awoke echoes in lofty transepts and high vaultings unseen. That sharp sound brought down the dark in flying atoms. Myriads of bats fell like night whirling in shreds. The gloom moved with a screaming rush. Norrie, though, went on as if unaware of it, except that he broke out against the smell of the little beasts. It certainly was lairish, that stench; not to be forgotten.

“Keep close,” said Norrie; “but if you lose me, keep still.”

It was not easy to keep close to such erratic activity in the dark. Norrie, intently inspecting the floor at times, developed an insatiable curiosity and energy. He said little. He kept going. He might have forgotten that such a preferable enjoyment as daylight was now well behind them.

===Chinese===
洞口一带是干沙，散落着崩坠的石块。天光壮着胆子往里探到力所能及的深处，隐约显出雕镂的柱子和一重重退远的廊道，到最末，那些形状便成了科利特愿意在那儿看见的东西。石山是空心的。它的深腔任性无常，入侵者惊动一块石头，便在望不见的高高耳堂和高高的穹顶里唤醒了回声。那尖锐的一响，把黑暗震成了漫天乱飞的微尘。千万只蝙蝠纷纷坠落，像夜被撕成了碎片在打旋。幽暗带着一阵尖叫的奔涌移动起来。不过诺里照直往前，仿佛毫无所觉，只是冲这些小兽的气味发作了一通。那股恶臭确实带着一股兽穴气；叫人忘不掉。

「跟紧，」诺里说，「可要是把我跟丢了，就待在原地别动。」

在黑暗里要紧跟住这么一种飘忽不定的活跃，可不容易。诺里时而专心察看地面，养成出一股不知餍足的好奇与精力。他话不多。他不停步。天光这样一种更可取的享受，如今已落在他们身后老远，这一点他兴许早忘了。

===Original===
“Come here,” he said at last. He stood then, relaxed and indifferent, as though here they would turn back, and with his lamp illuminated black sand at his feet. He idly scraped the ground with his foot.

“Know what that is?”

“Sand.”

“Cassiterite.”

“What’s that?”

===Chinese===
「到这儿来，」他终于说。随即他站定了，松散而淡漠，仿佛到此他们就该折回了，一面用灯照亮脚边的黑沙。他懒懒地拿脚蹭着地面。

「知道那是什么吗？」

「沙子。」

「锡石（cassiterite）。」

「那是什么？」

===Original===
“Haven’t you brought that Highland fling with you? I’m showing you what we came for.”

“This stuff?”

“It’s as ripe as a freehold in Piccadilly. The floor of this hill is tin. It only wants spades.”

Norrie stooped, and poked the grains about with his fingers.

It only wanted spades. Colet felt a little hungry. It was near midday. Besides, Norrie himself was just scooping the sand as if he were a child at the seaside. Norrie twisted round, and turned up his torch to Colet’s face.

===Chinese===
「你没把那支苏格兰高地舞（Highland fling）带在身上吗？我这就给你看，咱们是冲什么来的。」

「这玩意儿？」

「它熟得就像皮卡迪利（Piccadilly）的一宗永久产权。这座山的地面就是锡。只差几把铁锹。」

诺里俯下身，用手指拨弄那些矿粒。

只差几把铁锹。科利特觉得有点饿了。快到正午了。再说，诺里自己也正一捧一捧地舀那沙子，活像海边上的一个孩子。诺里转过身来，把电筒抬起来，照住科利特的脸。

===Original===
“I say, Colet, blast you, you haven’t got the expression of a lucky man. But you might try to behave like one. Sing something agreeable.”

“Me? Hang it, you’re not setting a lively example. I thought it was dirt.”

“So it is. So it is. There’s acres of it. Well, we’ve found it. Let’s go and get something to eat.”

===Chinese===
「我说，科利特，你这该死的，你脸上可没有一个走运人的神情。可你不妨装出一副来。唱点好听的吧。」

「我？见鬼，你自己可没做个快活的榜样。我还当那是脏土呢。」

「本来也是土。本来也是。这样的土有好几英亩呢。好了，咱们找到它了。走，弄点吃的去。」

## XXXII / 三十二

===Original===

Now they had found it, now they stood firmly upon the security which most men desire but usually fail to reach, their campfire, somehow, burned with less of its old companionable light. This was the end of the hunt. Norrie had explained, rather seriously, with hardly a lift of his usual buoyancy, what the law of averages, or something mathematical, had calculated against the chance of good luck coming to men on a rummage like theirs. This good luck, nevertheless, had coincided with their track in space; and to some extent, it appeared, that was not wholly because of blind chance; it had happened, too, because of a little artful designing by knowledge and intelligence. Norrie, with that, then looked round the camp, not perhaps as if his interest in life had gone, but as if that particular day and place had failed in savour for him.

“We shall get used to this scene, Colet. A sort of home.”

Colet followed his friend’s glance. The immense front of the forest on the opposite bank was still majestic and illegible. It was the same forest? Well, when he saw it first it had seemed outside time. Once he had seen it as a symbol of that which does not pass with the episodes of passing men; it was superior to days and nights. The cry of the tiger in the night, while he was sleepless, watching the stars, not knowing what was to happen on the morrow, was only a disturbing but relevant note in a great passage. Yet something hardly definable had happened to his view of it all. Good fortune had changed it. Perhaps the forest itself was no different; maybe he was not exactly the same man, and so could not see things as he did before. What was lost?

It was extraordinary, but the discovery of the hoard afforded them less to talk about than had such a trivial matter as the song of an unknown bird. Yet now the song of the bird passed unremarked. Tin did not prompt Norrie, now he had plenty of it, to a pleasing similitude of his old relish of Malay fables, which have no market value, though they can keep a campfire bright till late. The assurance of much tin induced in Norrie even a certain correctitude. He could no longer abjure their Chinaman with his accustomed histrionic abandon. He was direct, and saved time.

===Chinese===

如今他们找到了它，如今他们稳稳站上了那份多数人渴求却通常无缘企及的安稳，可他们的营火，不知怎的，烧得反倒少了旧日那股可亲的光亮。这场追寻到头了。诺里解释过——说得相当正经，几乎不见他平日那股轻快劲儿——平均律还是什么数学的算法，曾怎样算定了干他们这种四处扒寻营生的人撞不上好运。然而这份好运偏偏与他们在天地间的行踪重合了；而且看来，这在某种程度上并不全出于瞎碰；它之所以成真，也有知识与智力一点巧妙筹划的功劳。说完这个，诺里随即环视营地，那样子未必是他人生的兴味尽了，倒像是那个日子、那个地方，对他失了滋味。

「咱们会习惯这副景致的，科利特。算是某种家吧。」

科利特顺着朋友的目光望去。对岸森林那面浩大的正面，依旧雄伟而不可辨读。还是那片森林吗？嗐，他初见它时，它仿佛置身时间之外。他曾把它看作一个象征：那不随一代代过客的纷纷遭际一同逝去的东西；它凌驾于昼夜之上。夜里他睡不着，望着满天星斗，不知道明日将有什么临头，那时听见的虎啸，在一段宏大的乐章里不过是一个扰人却切题的音符。然而不知怎样，他看待这一切的眼光，起了某种几乎无从界说的变化。好运改变了它。也许森林本身并无不同；也许是他不再恰好是从前那个人，于是不能再像先前那样看事物了。失去了什么？

说来出奇，这宗窖藏的发现，竟不及一只不知名鸟儿的歌声那样一桩小事给他们的话题多。可如今鸟儿的歌声响了也无人留意。锡再也引不动诺里——如今他锡有的是——去重温旧日对马来寓言的那份津津有味；那些寓言没有市价，却能让营火明亮到深夜。大宗锡货在握的那份笃定，甚至让诺里生出了一种端方守礼。他再也不能照老样子，用他那惯常的戏子式的放浪劲，把他们的中国人申斥一通了。他直截了当，倒省了工夫。

===Original===

Colet, reviewing it all, while Norrie was diligently drawing a map, rebuked himself. He ought to feel excited. No good. He didn’t. What does not excite the interest cannot be made to do so by any deliberate concentration of reason. If intelligent discontent is the beginning of progress, is it also the end of happiness? Of all the frauds of the sensational drama, this joy on access of riches, this elation on the discovery of the treasure chest, as though it were wealth, was the silliest. There was nothing in it. More seemed to be lost than was gained. That was hardly fair of the law of compensation. One’s light was not turned up, but down. Colet had hinted to Norrie that there was not so much blithe interest in these abundant and exclusive details of business, this strict adherence to the mining law of the country, as there used to be in his sparkling nonsense. Norrie’s eyebrows moved in surprise at a consequence of good fortune which he had not remarked. Then he assumed a show of his drollery.

“Of course, I’m purged of dross. Fever and the tin have done it. I’m pure now. I’ve got salvation, I feel almost kind. Too kind to be lighthearted.”

Almost impious to say damn the tin, but Colet had that desire.

===Chinese===

诺里正埋头勤勉地绘制一张地图，科利特把这一切前后掂量，责备起自己来。他理应觉得兴奋。没用。他不觉得。凡唤不起兴致的事物，任凭理性怎样刻意地凝神聚力，也逼不出兴致来。倘若智识的不满是进步的开端，它是否也是幸福的终点？煽情戏剧的一切骗局里，数这一桩最蠢：暴得财富之喜、寻获宝箱之狂，仿佛那便是财富本身。里面其实空无一物。失掉的仿佛比得着的还多。补偿律这样行事，未免有欠公道。人的灯火没有被拧亮，反倒拧暗了。科利特曾向诺里暗示，他对这些又多又专的生意细节、这般对当地矿业法的严守，已不及从前对他那些火花四溅的胡言乱语兴味盎然。诺里双眉一动，对好运的这一层后果暗暗吃惊——那是他未曾察觉的。随后他装出了几分他的滑稽。

「当然，我身上的渣滓炼净了。热病和锡联手办成的事。我如今是纯净的了。我得了救恩，觉得自己简直和善起来。和善得过了头，轻快不起来了。」

说一句『该死的锡』几乎近于亵渎，可科利特偏偏有这个欲望。

===Original===

It was night, and Norrie, still at his work, not present enough in the body to notice that his pipe was out, sat beside a lamp. An apparition formed by the campfire.

“Sorry to disturb you. May I come in?”

Norrie scrambled to his feet in quick alarm, but before he was upright he had recovered himself. A glance had satisfied him.

“Come along in.”

The stranger entered, and sat on a box between the friends, looking in appeal from one to the other, as would a child that had been naughty, but was sick. This elderly and bearded man, with the tired, but open and wondering eyes, was sick. His wrecked shirt held to but one shoulder, and its neglect of the other exposed an ugly boil on the upper arm. Only his grizzled beard filled the hollows of his cheeks. He took off a soiled helmet, and arranged it on the floor with what might have been an amusing care for the battered relic, or it was the hesitancy of a man who was preoccupied. His delicate cranium was bald, except for a monk-like but untidy tonsure.

===Chinese===

夜深了，诺里还在做他的活计，人在魂不在，连烟斗熄了都没觉察，坐在一盏灯旁。营火边凝出了一个人影。

「搅扰二位，抱歉。我能进来吗？」

诺里惊得霍然起身，可人还没站直，他已经回过神来。一眼扫过，他心里有了底。

「请进。」

陌生人走进来，在两位朋友之间的一只箱子上坐下，恳求似的看看这个又看看那个，像个淘了气却病着的孩子。这位上了年纪、蓄须的男人，一双眼睛疲惫，却又坦率而带着惊奇，他是病了。他那件破烂的衬衫只剩一边肩膀还挂着，另一边听之任之，露出上臂一个丑恶的疖疮。只有花白的胡须填着他双颊的凹陷。他摘下一顶污脏的盔式遮阳帽，把它在地板上安放停当——那股仔细劲儿，也许是可笑地珍护着这件饱经磕碰的旧物，也许只是一个满腹心事的人的迟疑。他那个纤巧的头颅已经光秃，只剩一圈修士式却蓬乱的边发。

===Original===

“I was very glad to see your campfire. My Malay guide, an exceptionally good man, was lost. Is the river here the Sungei Buloh?”

“No,” said Norrie, “you’ve taken the wrong turning. The Buloh is five miles down stream. What are you making for?”

“Mount Berching. I shall cross the divide into Perak about there. My name, by the way, is Parsell.”

Norrie, astonished, had taken his pipe out of his mouth, and had held it away for an intent inspection of their visitor. Now he put his pipe beside him and leaned forward, with his hands clasped.

===Chinese===

「望见你们的营火，我真高兴。我的马来向导——一个格外好的人——走失了。这儿这条河是双溪布洛（Sungei Buloh）吗？」

「不是，」诺里说，「你拐错了弯。布洛河在下游五英里处。你这是要去哪儿？」

「贝尔钦山（Mount Berching）。我打算在那附近越过分水岭，进霹雳（Perak）去。对了，敝人姓帕塞尔（Parsell）。」

诺里吃了一惊，把烟斗从嘴里取出来，往旁边一搁，专心端详他们的来客。这时他把烟斗放在身侧，俯身向前，两手交扣着。

===Original===

“Parsell the ethnologist, the author of the *Mon-Khmer Influence in Southeastern Asia*?”

The veteran gnome looked quite pleased.

“You know my name, then? Curious, curious!”

Norrie was clearly perplexed. He sang out for the Chinaman, and gave him some instructions. He stroked his nose. He looked with wariness at Colet, as if for a cue.

“No need to ask you, sir, what you are doing here. Didn’t you mention Gunong Berching?”

“I am making for that point.”

===Chinese===

「民族学家帕塞尔，写《东南亚的孟-高棉影响》（Mon-Khmer Influence in Southeastern Asia）的那位？」

这位老地精看上去相当高兴。

「这么说你知道我的名字？稀奇，稀奇！」

诺里分明是一副犯难的神色。他扬声唤那个中国人，吩咐了几句。他摩挲着鼻子。他警惕地瞟了科利特一眼，像在等一句提词。

「您上这儿来做什么，先生，就用不着问了。您方才提到的，可是贝尔钦山（Gunong Berching）？」

「我正要往那儿去。」

===Original===

To Colet it was plain that if Norrie had addressed him in the matter of that mountain, it would have been in a few choice words to demolish a folly.

“Do you think you can manage it, Mr. Parsell?”

“Of course I can; why not? That is part of my plan.”

“A good plan. But here, what with the want of food, and the floods and fevers, we have to alter our plans occasionally. It is rough going to Berching, and I should fancy that beyond it the going would be worse. Hardly anything is known about it.”

“Very likely, very likely.” Mr. Parsell spoke with decision, and a hint of asperity. He was unwell and a little crabbed. Nor did he promise to be the kind of man who would listen at any time to the warnings of common sense, not when he was mounted on his hobby.

===Chinese===

科利特心里有数：要是诺里同他谈起那座山，那也准会是三言两语，把一桩糊涂事拆个粉碎。

「您觉得自己对付得了吗，帕塞尔先生？」

「当然对付得了；为什么对付不了？那是我计划的一部分。」

「计划不错。可是到了这地方，不是缺粮，就是洪水和热病，我们偶尔也不得不改动计划。去贝尔钦山一路难走，我料想过了山，路只会更糟。那一带，几乎什么都没人知道。」

「很可能，很可能。」帕塞尔先生说得斩钉截铁，还夹着一丝火气。他身子不舒服，脾气有些乖戾。而且他也不像那种任何时候听得进常识警告的人——一旦跨上他那匹癖好木马，就更不必提了。

===Original===

Norrie, tactfully, tried to draw from him confessions about his supplies, his guide, his men, and the time he had estimated would be necessary for the journey. But these to Mr. Parsell were only negligible details, of small account compared with the pursuit of truth. He was vague about them; he himself was barely concerned. The Chinaman came to them with dishes, Norrie polished his pipe thoughtfully, and Mr. Parsell addressed himself to food in an attitude of abstraction which allowed him but fitfully to acknowledge the nearness of nourishment. Indeed he would pause with entire detachment, fork held loaded and upright but forgotten, to seek, with the cool and disarming inconsequence of a barrister who knew his case, a betrayal of their own notions of the natives they had met. Had they seen any Sekais or Semangs?

Norrie humoured him. More than once Mr. Parsell sat round to look at Norrie squarely and with the unaffected curiosity of a pundit who is surprised by a suspicion that a layman may be not so ignorant as in fairness could be assumed. Yet, when the subject was not his own, he was, despite his bald head, a ragged and helpless infant one would have been prompted to nurse and cherish, if one had known but the way to hold it. The lifted appeal of his fearless but innocent blue eyes moved the paternal instinct in a man. It was not safe for him to be about.

Then, with talk and food, his nervous energy flagged; would they excuse him? He thought he would rest. He would have to make an early start in the morning. Norrie led him to the hammock, which would be easier for his bad shoulder than the floor, and tended him as carefully as though their guest were a wilful but royal orphan. When Mr. Parsell was out of the way, Norrie stood, for a time, staring into the night; then he turned to Colet with a wry smile.

“We shall have to stop this,” he whispered. “There’s enough *hantus* here.”

===Chinese===

诺里婉转着，想从他口里套出些招认来：他的补给、他的向导、他的人手，还有他估算这趟路程所需的光阴。可这些在帕塞尔先生看来，都只是些无足轻重的枝节，比起追求真理来算不得什么。问到这些他便含糊其辞；连他本人都几乎不上心。中国人端着菜肴过来，诺里若有所思地擦拭烟斗，帕塞尔先生则一心对付食物，那副心不在焉的神态，只容他一阵一阵地意识到滋养就在近旁。他果真会带着全然的出神停顿下来，叉子上满满当当、直直举着却忘了吃，转而去探问——那副冷静而不让人设防的节外生枝，活像一位吃透了案情的出庭律师——想诱使二人泄露自己对所遇土著的观感。他们可曾见过塞凯人（Sekais）或是塞芒人（Semangs）？

诺里迁就着他。帕塞尔先生不止一次转过脸来，正眼端详诺里，带着一位学问家毫不做作的好奇——这位学问家吃惊地起了疑心：一个门外汉，也许并不像按理推断的那样无知。然而一旦题目不是他自己的行当，他尽管顶着那颗秃头，却像个衣衫褴褛、无依无靠的婴孩，人人想抱起来呵护一番，只要知道该怎么个抱法。他那双毫无惧色却天真未凿的蓝眼睛抬起来恳求时，能撩动男人心里做父亲的本能。他这样在外头走动，可不大安全。

随后，话也说了，饭也吃了，他那股神经质的精力低了下去；能不能恕他失陪？他想他要歇息了。明早他还得早早动身。诺里领他到吊床边——对他那侧坏肩膀，吊床总比地板强——照料得细心周到，仿佛这位客人是一位执拗的王室遗孤。待帕塞尔先生安置停当，诺里站了一会儿，凝望夜色；然后他朝科利特转过身来，苦笑了一下。

「这种事可得叫停了，」他压低声音说。「这儿的『汉图』（hantus，鬼怪）已经够多了。」

## XXXIII / 三十三

===Original===
Mr. Parsell did not make an early start. He found their Malays and the situation of the camp too attractive. The awe of the Malays for this eager and energetic little man, who mystified them with his ease among their secrets, was manifest; no doubt they thought he was mad, and the favoured of God. He knew things which were hidden even from Tuan Norrie, and wizards should be carefully reconciled. Norrie watched the play about the hut of his men with amused concern.

===Chinese===
帕塞尔（Parsell）先生没有一早动身。他发觉他们的马来人和这处营地的光景太有吸引力了。马来人对这个热切而精力充沛的小个子满怀敬畏——他在他们的种种秘密之间从容自若，令他们百思不解；毫无疑问，他们认定他是个疯子，又是上帝的宠儿。他知道一些连诺里老爷都瞒着的事，而对巫师，是得当小心笼络的。诺里瞧着自家伙计们棚屋周围上演的这出戏，又觉好笑，又不无牵挂。

===Original===
“Colet, he knows more about those fellows than they know themselves. He has scared them. He isn’t aware of it, but he could order them to heel like dogs. I wish I could.”

“You’ve heard of the old boy before, Norrie?”

“But naturally. Who hasn’t? I knew his work before I could play dominoes. And we meet him here at last. That’s how the surprises are sorted for us.”

“What about this journey of his? Can it be done?”

“Yes. Almost anything can be done, by the right people. What do you think?”

===Chinese===
「科利特，他对那些家伙的了解，比他们对自己的了解还多。他把他们吓着了。他自己并不知道，可他满可以像唤狗似的把他们唤到脚边。我倒希望我也能这样。」

「你从前听说过这位老先生吧，诺里？」
